# Veit Dietrich, *Summaria Christlicher Lehr* (1548)

**The whole book in German and English, arranged by the church year: Dietrich's summaries of the Gospels, with his collects, those of *The Lutheran Hymnary* (1913) revised against them, and the rest newly translated in the same idiom**

For every Sunday and principal feast, Dietrich's *Summaria* gives a saying from Scripture, nearly always from the day's Gospel, a short summary of what is to be learned from it, and a prayer. All of it is given here in the original German, followed by its English. The sayings are given in King James wording, and the summaries and the rest of the book's prose are translated into formal modern English (§2.6). The prayers are given in the idiom of *The Lutheran Hymnary* (TLHy). Where TLHy has the prayer, its English has been checked clause by clause against the German, corrected where it departs from it, and printed as **Revised TLHy**, with every change listed beneath it. Where TLHy lacks the prayer, a **New translation** in TLHy's idiom is given instead. The index in §3 lists every collect in church-year order and links to it.

## 1. Source and scope

**Source.** Veit Dietrich, *Summaria Christlicher lehr/für das junge volck/Was auß eim yeden Sontags Euāgelio zu mercken sey/Sampt angehenckten Gebeten. durch Vitum Dietrich. M.D.XLVIII.* [Summaries of Christian Doctrine for the Young People: What Is to Be Noted from Each Sunday's Gospel, Together with Appended Prayers. By Vitus Dietrich. 1548.] Google Books digitization, 306 PDF pages. The book has signatures but no page numbers, so every page reference below is to the **PDF page** of the scan (PDF p. 3 = title page). The colophon (PDF p. 304) reads *Gedruckt zu Nürmberg/durch Johañ vom Berg/vnd Vlrich Newber.* [Printed at Nuremberg by Johann vom Berg and Ulrich Neuber.]

PDF pp. 1–2 are Google's digitization notices. The book opens with the title page (PDF p. 3; its verso, p. 4, is blank) and Dietrich's dedicatory letter to Johann Neudörffer, dated 2 September 1546 (pp. 5–12). The temporale runs PDF pp. 13–228 (First Sunday in Advent through the Twenty-fifth Sunday after Trinity, with St. Stephen's Day placed after Christmas). The sanctorale, headed *Sum̃aria Christlicher ler auß den Euangelien der fürnembsten Fest durchs Jar* [Summaries of Christian doctrine from the Gospels of the principal feasts through the year], runs pp. 229–296 (St. Andrew through Sts. Simon and Jude). After the sanctorale come Dietrich's address *Dem Christlichen Leser* [To the Christian Reader] (pp. 297–298), two appended prayers (pp. 299–303) and the colophon (p. 304).

**The sections.** Every day has a section in four parts: a heading naming the day and the chapter of its text; a saying (*spruch*) of a few verses, given here as **Verse**; Dietrich's summary of what is to be learned from it; and a prayer headed *Gebet*. Dietrich's address to the reader (Part III) says how the book was meant to be used: children were to learn the saying by heart each Sunday and recite it daily through the week, and at times to read the summary and the prayer with it. Most sayings are taken from the day's Gospel; nine come from elsewhere in Scripture (Appendix D, item 11).

**Scope.** This edition gives the whole book: the front matter; every section of the temporale and the sanctorale, with its heading, saying, summary and prayer; and the end matter. The prayers are the ones Stratman marks (S), meaning they come from the *Summaria*: 80 numbered collects. Because [86] and [87] are the same prayer printed twice, there are 79 distinct texts. Bracketed numbers are Stratman's throughout.

- **54 collects are in TLHy.** Each is printed as *Revised TLHy*, with its changes listed. They include [10], which TLHy prints at Quinquagesima and which is restored here to the Sunday after New Year, and Maundy Thursday, where TLHy's collect ([33]) proves to be a free adaptation of Dietrich's [32].
- **26 collects are not in TLHy.** Each is given in a *New translation* in TLHy's idiom. Among them is Dietrich's own Quinquagesima collect [23].
- **TLHy's other collects**, which do not come from Dietrich, are given in Appendix A, so that every TLHy day can be found. Thirteen of them belong to TLHy's first series and are English versions of Norwegian collects: twelve that Stratman marks (H), and TLHy's second collect for St. Stephen's Day, which he omits. Each is given in the Norwegian of 1887 and revised against it, as Dietrich's collects are revised against his German. Appendix A also gives, for reference, TLHy's own texts of [24] and [33] (Dietrich's [10] and [32]) and its harvest collect [77], so that it holds every collect Stratman marks (H).
- **The four collects Stratman marks (ELH)**, [73], [75], [76] and [78], are given in Appendix B with their origins and a version in TLHy's idiom. Two of them, [75] and [78], prove to be in TLHy after all, in its second series of collects.
- The front matter (title page and dedicatory letter) precedes Part I. Part III gives the end matter: Dietrich's address to the reader, the two prayers appended at the end of the book, and the colophon.

---

## 2. Conventions

### 2.1 Transcription

- **Semi-diplomatic.** Original spelling, capitalization and punctuation are kept, including the virgule (/). Abbreviations stay as printed: vñ = vnd, dz = das, d͛ = der, and a bar or tilde over a letter stands for a following m or n (lebē = leben, stim̃ = stimm). Long ſ and r rotunda (ꝛ) are normalized to s and r. Superscript-e vowels are given as ä, ö, ü. Words broken at a line end are joined without comment. The print's question mark, a point with a curved stroke above it, is given as ?.
- **Decorated initials.** The initial is printed as part of the first word (HErr, HERR, ALmechtiger, O Jesu).
- **j-forms.** The printer sets the pronoun forms *jn, jm, jhn, jren* with a descending j, distinct from the preposition *in*. This was checked at 400 dpi and is followed here.
- **Misprints and damage.** Misprints are kept and marked [sic], with the intended reading. Damaged type is noted where it occurs, and letters that have failed to print are supplied in square brackets, as in *bl[i]ben*.
- **Page breaks.** The title page is given line by line. In the dedicatory letter, the sayings, the summaries and the address to the reader, [p. 6] and the like mark where a new PDF page begins; when a page ends inside a divided word, the marker follows that word. Catchwords and signatures are omitted, and ꝛc., the sign for *et cetera*, is kept as printed.
- **Paragraphs.** Most summaries are printed as a single paragraph. Where the print breaks one, the break is kept, and the entry says so.
- **Checking.** Every transcription was read from the page images at 200 dpi, and doubtful points were checked at 400 dpi or more. The scan's OCR layer was used only to find pages and, afterwards, to cross-check that no words or lines had been skipped; for the sayings and summaries this was done by aligning each transcription with the OCR text of its pages.

### 2.2 TLHy's idiom, and how it has been revised

Every TLHy collect that comes from the *Summaria* was set beside Dietrich's German and compared clause by clause. The comparison shows how TLHy worked:

1. **Register and syntax.** TLHy uses Thee/Thou/Thy/Thine, -est/-eth verbs and capitalized divine pronouns. It shapes each prayer as a collect: invocation, a relative clause ("who hast…"), a colon, then the petition ("We beseech Thee…"). Scripture echoes are given in King James wording.
2. **Plural for singular.** TLHy recasts Dietrich's first-person-singular prayers in the plural (Epiphany 2; Trinity 11, 12, 19 and 20).
3. **Conclusions.** TLHy adds its full conclusion to every prayer, whether Dietrich ends with no conclusion at all, with a short "durch Christum…" (e.g., Easter 1, Trinity 8), or with a Trinitarian close of his own (Epiphany 4; Trinity 22 and 25).
4. **Free paraphrase.** TLHy often paraphrases. It drops clauses (Epiphany 4's "to hear our prayer in all temptation and need"; Trinity 13's "and sooner forsake all things"), adds others (Maundy Thursday's "remember Thine agony and death"; Trinity 8's prayer to "send us now and ever pious pastors"), and at times reverses the sense (Trinity 18 blames "the weakness of our flesh and blood," where Dietrich says that flesh and blood are too *strong* in us). At Maundy Thursday it readdresses Dietrich's prayer to Christ. Not all of this is TLHy's own work: TLHy translated the Norwegian collects of 1887 (Appendix A), and the Maundy Thursday prayer is already addressed to Christ there, with "remember Thine agony and death" (*komme din Pine og Død ihu*).

**How TLHy is revised here.**

- *Kept:* TLHy's register, syntax, collect shape and "We beseech Thee"; its settled equivalents wherever they render the German without loss (table below); King James wording wherever Dietrich's German follows the same verse ("overcharged with surfeiting and drunkenness, and cares of this life," Advent 2; "an honest and good heart," Sexagesima; "besides women and children," Trinity 7); and TLHy's conclusions (§2.4).
- *Corrected:* omissions are restored; additions are removed; changed sense is given literally; Dietrich's address, person and number are restored, and for the singular prayers a plural "congregational form" is supplied, as TLHy's practice requires; *dein liebes wort* is "Thy dear word," not "Thy holy word"; and "Holy Ghost" replaces "Holy Spirit" (§2.3). Slips in the Orthodox Lutheran Ministerium's web text of TLHy, such as "Holy Gaost" or a closing "Amen,", are corrected and noted.
- *Recorded:* every change is listed under **Changes from TLHy**, in the form "TLHy wording" → "revised wording," followed by the German that requires it.

The new translations follow the same rules, so that the two kinds of text can stand side by side. The prose of the front matter, the summaries and the end matter is translated in formal modern English instead, with Scripture still in King James wording (§2.6).

| Dietrich (1548) | TLHy, where it translated Dietrich | Adopted here |
|---|---|---|
| HErr Gott himlischer Vatter | Lord God, heavenly Father | same |
| Allmechtiger ewiger Gott | Almighty and everlasting God (Trinity 12, 21) | same |
| Wir bitten dich/du wöllest … | We beseech Thee (so) to … | same |
| ewig selig werden | obtain eternal salvation (Advent 1; Septuagesima; Easter 2) | same |
| erwecke vnsere hertzen | quicken our hearts (Trinity 3) | same |
| regieren vnd füren | govern and direct (Advent 1) | same; also where TLHy has "rule and govern" or "guide and direct" |
| leyten vnd füren | guide and direct (Trinity 11) | same |
| sicherheyt; sicher werden | carnal security (Epiphany 5) | same ("grow secure," Trinity 11) |
| anfechtung | temptation (Christmas; Epiphany 5) | same |
| vnaußsprechliche gnad | ineffable grace (Easter 1) | same |
| (Väterlich) bedacht | (of Thy fatherly goodness) hast been mindful of (Easter 2) | same |
| vns … trösten | comfort ourselves with (Christmas) | same |
| geytz | covetousness (Trinity 15) | same |
| sorgen der narung | the cares of this life (Lent 4; Trinity 7) | same (Luke 21:34) |
| Christum Jesum (also Christus Jesus, Christo Jesu, Christi Jhesu) | Jesus Christ (TLHy's regular order) | same |
| Jesu Christe/du Son des lebendigen Gottes | O Jesus Christ, Thou Son of the living God (Trinity 5) | same |
| Sun des Allmechtigen Gottes | Thou almighty Son of God (Ascension; Pentecost) | **Thou Son of the almighty God** |
| (sünde) erkennen | confess (Easter 4; Trinity 22) | **acknowledge** |
| *selig werden* | obtain *eternal* salvation (Trinity 3) | **be saved** (TLHy adds "eternal") |
| seligklich sterben | through a blessed death (Trinity 18) | **die blessedly** |
| *dein liebes wort* | Thy *holy* word (Septuagesima; Sexagesima; Trinity 2, 5, 20) | **Thy dear word** |
| *gib deinen heyligen Geyst in vnsere hertzen* | *send* … into our hearts (Septuagesima); *grant us* … (Easter 1) | **give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts** (cf. 2 Corinthians 1:22) |
| *grundlose barmhertzigkeyt* | Thy *great* mercy (Trinity 19); "most merciful Father" (Trinity 13) | **Thy fathomless mercy** |
| *für vnsere sünde bezalen* | *atone for* our sins (Trinity 16) | **pay for** our sins |
| *vns versündigen* | defile ourselves (Trinity 1, 15) | **sin** |

### 2.3 "Holy Ghost"

"Holy Ghost" is used throughout for *heyliger Geyst*, as in TLHy's own conclusions and Trinitarian formulas; "Holy Spirit" is not used in any rendering. "Spirit" alone, as in TLHy's "Thy Spirit and grace" (Epiphany 1) or "the power of Thy Spirit" (Day of Humiliation and Prayer), stands as it is. In the revised TLHy texts each such change is recorded under "Changes from TLHy." In the new translations, the congregational forms and Part III, the earlier draft of this edition had "Holy Spirit"; all of these now read "Holy Ghost."

### 2.4 Conclusions (TLHy endings)

Dietrich usually ends with "Amen" alone, whereas TLHy closes every prayer with its full conclusion. Every collect here carries a conclusion, chosen by the rules below. **Words not in Dietrich's German are set in italics**, so that his own text can always be told apart; his "Amen" stands after them. The congregational forms carry the same endings.

- **(a) Dietrich gives no conclusion, and the Son has not been named.** In the revised TLHy texts, TLHy's own conclusion for the day is kept as TLHy prints it, with its variants (e.g., "ever one God" at Sexagesima). In the new translations: *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Used at [13], [81], [92].
- **(b) Dietrich gives no conclusion, and the Son has already been named.** Revised TLHy: as in (a), e.g., [46]. New translations: *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Used at [6], [16], [23], [83], [89], [90], [94], [95], [96], [97], [98].
- **(c) Dietrich's last clause already contains "through Christ," "through Him" or "through Thy Son."** That phrase is moved to the end of its clause, as TLHy does at Christmas and Easter 2, and the conclusion follows it: "…through Him, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.*" Only the word order changes. Revised TLHy at [2], [3], [26], [34], [35], [37], [38], [40], [52], [56], [63], [64], [67], [70]; new translations at [8], [30], [79], [84], [85].
- **(d) Dietrich already closes with Christ.** His close is kept and continued, as TLHy does at Easter 1 and Trinity 8: "…through Jesus Christ, Thy Son and our Lord, *who liveth and reigneth…*". Revised TLHy at [5], [15], [27], [28], [32], [36], [42], [44], [53], [54], [61]; new translations at [29] ("for the sake of Thy dear Son and our Redeemer, Jesus Christ") and [91].
- **(e) Prayers to Christ.** *Thou who livest and reignest with the Father and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* This is TLHy's form at Trinity 5 ([51]). Used also at [93], and at [80], whose last petition is addressed to the Son.
- **(f) The prayer to the Holy Trinity, [45].** *who livest and reignest, one God, world without end.* This is the prayer-book ending for a prayer addressed to the Holy Trinity, as in the Book of Common Prayer's collect for Trinity Sunday. It reads "one God" where TLHy's other endings read "one true God"; the prayer-book wording is kept.
- **(g) Dietrich's own Trinitarian conclusion.** Nothing is added. Where TLHy had replaced or enlarged his close, Dietrich's words are restored ([18], [68], [71]); at Pentecost ([43]) TLHy's rendering of his close is accurate and is kept. At [86]/[87] the prayer closes "…through Him live eternally with Thee and Thy Holy Ghost, and be saved."

The two appended prayers in Part III end with their own conclusions, and nothing is added to them.

### 2.5 How each entry is laid out

1. **Heading:** Stratman's number and the day in TLHy's calendar.
2. **Dietrich's heading:** the section heading in the *Summaria*, with an English rendering in brackets.
3. **Verse:** the saying set at the head of the section, in German and in English.
4. **Summary:** Dietrich's exposition, in German and in English, followed by **Notes on the verse and summary**. Where an entry needs a note on how its collect is assigned in TLHy's calendar (**Assignment** or **Relation to TLHy**), it comes next.
5. **Prayer:** the prayer as printed, with its PDF page.
6. **Revised TLHy** (TLHy's collect, corrected to the German) or **New translation** (where TLHy lacks the prayer).
7. **Congregational form**, where Dietrich prays in the first person singular.
8. **Changes from TLHy** (revised texts) or **Notes** (new translations).

Stratman's modern versions are compared with the German in Appendix C.

### 2.6 The sayings and summaries

- **Sayings.** Each saying is given in King James wording. Where Dietrich's German differs from the King James text in a way that matters, because the summary turns on the word or because a clause is missing, the English follows Dietrich and the notes say so. Dietrich's own introductions, such as "Christ said unto His disciples," are translated as they stand.
- **Summaries.** The summaries are translated into formal modern English, close to the German but in English sentences: Dietrich's long chains of clauses joined by virgules are divided where English needs it. Scripture that he quotes is given in King James wording and identified in the notes.
- **Capitals and spelling.** Pronouns referring to God are capitalized in Dietrich's own words, as in the collects; the King James wording keeps its lower case and its spelling (*Saviour*, *neighbour*). Otherwise the translations use American spelling.

Recurring words are rendered consistently:

| Dietrich | Rendered |
|---|---|
| *selig*, *selig werden* | saved, be saved; "blessed" where the saying has it ([3], [59]) |
| *sein geniessen* | have the benefit of Him |
| *anfechtung* | temptation |
| *sicher*, *sicherheyt* | secure; carnal security |
| *Euangelion* | the gospel (the message); the Gospel (the day's reading) |
| *beruff* | calling |
| *glück*, *vnglück* | good fortune, misfortune |
| *Buß thun* | repent |
| *Kirchendiener* | ministers of the Church |
| *Pfaffen* | priests (a disparaging word for the Roman clergy) |

"Section" gives the PDF pages of a day's whole section in the *Summaria* (heading, verse, summary and prayer), and "Prayer" those of the prayer alone. "Revised TLHy" marks a TLHy collect corrected against the German or, in Appendix A, against the Norwegian of 1887 from which TLHy translated it; "New translation" marks a Dietrich collect that TLHy lacks. TLHy's Quinquagesima collect (Stratman's [24]) is [10], and its Maundy Thursday collect ([33]) is revised under [32]; both are also given unrevised in Appendix A. "(TLHy)" marks the collects that Stratman marks (H), all of which are in Appendix A, and "(ELH)" the four that he assigns to ELH alone (Appendix B). Every number in Stratman, [1] to [99], is listed. The one row without a number is TLHy's second collect for St. Stephen's Day, which Stratman does not include.

---

## FRONT MATTER (PDF pp. 3–12)

PDF pp. 1–2 are Google's digitization notices and are not part of the book. The book itself opens with the title page (p. 3), whose verso (p. 4) is blank, and Dietrich's dedicatory letter to Johann Neudörffer (pp. 5–12). The First Sunday in Advent begins on p. 13.

These pages are prose, not prayers, so they are translated in formal modern English rather than in TLHy's prayer idiom; Scripture is still given in King James wording.

### Title page (PDF p. 3)

The title stands in a cartouche inside a woodcut architectural border. Above it is a vault with a bearded figure among foliage in the lunette; on either side a robed figure stands in a niche; at the foot a bearded man sits enthroned among children, several of them holding books. The title is printed in red and black, line by line as follows. A later hand has written "2." at the top right.

| Line | German | Ink |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Summa- | red |
| 2 | ria Christlich- | red |
| 3 | er lehr/für das junge | red |
| 4 | volck/Was auß eim yeden | black |
| 5 | Sontags Euāgelio zu mer | black |
| 6 | cken sey/Sampt ange- | black |
| 7 | henckten Gebeten. | black |
| 8 | durch | red |
| 9 | Vitum Dietrich. | black |
| 10 | M.D.XLVIII. | red |

**Translation**

> Summaries of Christian doctrine for the young people: what is to be noted from each Sunday's Gospel, together with appended prayers. By Vitus Dietrich. 1548.

**Notes**
- **"Summaries."** *Summaria* is a Latin plural, "summaries."
- **"Vitus."** *Vitum* is the Latin accusative of *Vitus*, Dietrich's Latin name (German Veit), governed by *durch* ("by").

---

### Dedicatory letter to Johann Neudörffer (PDF pp. 5–12)

The letter runs on without a break in the print. The paragraph divisions (¶1–¶10) are editorial and are the same in the German and the English. Each paragraph gives the German first (indented) and the translation after it. [p. 6] and the like mark where a new PDF page begins.

**Address (PDF p. 5)**

> Dem Ersamen/vnd namhafften Johann Newdörffer/deutscher Schulmeyster in Nürmberg/Meinem sonder lieben freunde.

To the honorable and renowned Johann Neudörffer, German schoolmaster in Nuremberg, my especially dear friend.

**¶1** (p. 5)

> DEr heylige Simeon/da er zu Jerusalem im Tempel/das kindlin Jesus in seinen armē hat/weyssagt er/Es werde nicht yederman diß kindlin annemen/noch für seinen heyland haben/sonder der meyste theyl/werde jm widersprechen/Vnd sein lere/so die eynige rechte seligkeyt ist/lestern/vnnd alß Ketzerey verdammen/Ob nun solches den Christen sehr werde wehe thun/soll es doch dazu dienen/das viler hertzen gedancken offenbar werden/vnnd wir die leut recht erkennen lernen/vnd durch falschen scheyn/nicht betrogen werden.

Holy Simeon, when he has the child Jesus in his arms in the Temple at Jerusalem, prophesies that not everyone will receive this child or hold Him for his Savior, but that the greater part will speak against Him, and will blaspheme His doctrine, which is the one true salvation, and condemn it as heresy. Now although this will grieve Christians sorely, it shall nevertheless serve to this end: that the thoughts of many hearts may be revealed, and that we may learn to know people rightly and not be deceived by a false appearance.

**¶2** (pp. 6–7)

> [p. 6] Solche weyssagung gehet für vnnd für vnter den Christen/Bald/das liebe Euangelion sich lest hören/ist es allenthalb vol zungen/die jhm widersprechen/vnd alß Ketzerey vnd verfürische lehr es verdammen/Ja es ist an dem widersprechen nit gnug/man nimbt auch das schwert in die faust/vñ wil es mit gewalt dempffen. Werden derhalb die armen Christen mit zweyen schwerten geschlagen/Das eine durchdringet jnen jr hertz/Denn es ye einen grossen grewlichen schmertzen geberē muß/das die welt so blind ist/den gnedigen Gott im himel so lestert/dem Teuffel so willig diener/dem wort/da doch all jr seligkeyt anligt/so bitter feynd/vnnd der lügen/dem ergsten seelengifft/so vber alle massen zugethon vnnd hold ist/Das ander schwert/Nemlich/die verfolgung/mattet/verwundet/vnd würget den leyb/Solches soll mit disem kind/mit seim wort/dem heyligen Euangelio/vnd darnach mit allen Christen/für vnd für practiciert vnd geübt werden.

This prophecy comes to pass continually among Christians. As soon as the dear gospel makes itself heard, everywhere is full of tongues that speak against it and condemn it as heresy and seductive doctrine. Indeed, the speaking against is not enough; men also take the sword in hand and would stifle it by force. Poor Christians are therefore struck with two swords. The one pierces through their heart. For it must surely bring forth a great and dreadful pain that the world is so blind, so blasphemes the gracious God in heaven, is so willing a servant to the devil, so bitter an enemy to the word, on which all its salvation yet depends, and is so devoted and attached beyond all measure to the lie, the worst poison of souls. The other sword, namely persecution, wearies, wounds, and slays the body. This shall be practiced and carried out continually upon this child, upon His word, the holy gospel, and thereafter upon all Christians.

**¶3** (p. 7)

> [p. 7] Was aber für ein ergetzligkeit in yenem leben werde folgen/wissen wir auß Gottes wort lautter vnd gewiß/deñ es wirt ein vberauß herrlich/frölich/vnd wonreychs wort sein/wenn der Son Gottes sagen wirt: Kombt her jr gesegneten meines Vatters/ererbet das Reych/das euch bereytet ist/von anbegin der welt. Die hunde aber/vnnd die Zauberer(wie inn der Offenbarung am 22. stehet) Vnd die Hurer/vnd die Todschleger/vnd die Abgöttischen/vnd alle die lieb haben vnd thun die lügen/werden hinunder müssen inn das hellische fewer/vnnd ewige flammen/Da wirt sich alß denn der rechte wechssel finden.

But what delight will follow in the life beyond, we know clearly and certainly from God's word. For it will be an exceedingly glorious, joyful, and blissful word when the Son of God shall say: "Come, ye blessed of my Father, inherit the kingdom prepared for you from the foundation of the world." But the dogs, and the sorcerers (as it stands in Revelation 22), and the whoremongers, and the murderers, and the idolaters, and all who love and make a lie, will have to go down into the fire of hell and the eternal flames. There, then, the right exchange will be found.

**¶4** (pp. 7–8)

> Hie aber auff erden/sagt Simeon/sollen die Feynde des worts offenbar werden/Das alle Christen jhnen in das hertz hinein sehen/vnd gründlich sie vrteylen können/wer sie sind/wie sie leben vnd sterben/vnd was für ein vrtheyl sie für Gott in ewigkeyt tragen müssen/Solchs ist nicht ein geringes/damit die Christen inn der verfolgung [p. 8] sich mögē trösten.

But here on earth, says Simeon, the enemies of the word shall be revealed, so that all Christians can see into their heart and judge them thoroughly: who they are, how they live and die, and what judgment they must bear before God in eternity. That is no small thing, with which Christians may comfort themselves in persecution.

**¶5** (p. 8)

> Wir haben in deutschen land nun ein lange zeyt das widersprechen/starck hören vnd dulden müssen/Yetzundt kombt das schwert mit gewalt dazu/das der Antichrist zu Rom/mit seim Anhang vnsere kirchen gar will tilgen/vnd verwüsten/vnd durch sein Antichristisch Conciliabel zu Trient/alle abgötterey vnd lügen wider anrichten. Solches fürnemen/schmucken sie zierlich vnnd fein/vnd ist gewiß/wer jhren worten will glauben/der ist schon betrogen. Denn anderst hörest nicht von jnen/denn das sie die strittigen Religion/vergleychen/eynigkeyt anrichten/vñ die Scheflein in den rechten stal bringen wöllen.

We in the German land have now for a long time had to hear and endure the speaking against in full force. Now the sword comes with force besides, in that the Antichrist at Rome, with his adherents, would utterly destroy and lay waste our churches, and through his antichristian conciliabulum at Trent set up again all idolatry and lies. This undertaking they adorn elegantly and finely, and it is certain that whoever will believe their words is already deceived. For you hear nothing else from them than that they would settle the disputed religion, establish unity, and bring the little sheep into the right fold.

**¶6** (pp. 8–9)

> Vnd kan wol sein/das jr vil/so zu solchem blutdürstigen fürnemen helffen/jres wandels vnd lebens halb from̃ sind/Aber was saget Simeon/bald du das widersprechen hörest/das sie des Herrn Christi wort nicht wöllen leyden/noch war lassen sein/so frag nicht weyter/jr hertz stehet dir so glat vnd gar offen/alß hettest [p. 9] du es in deiner hand/Kanst derhalb vrtheylen/das es eytel Heucheley mit jnen ist/das der Teuffel jhnen im hertzen sitzt/sie treybet vnd füret/Vnd in summa/das sie gar des Teuffels sind/wie sie stehen vnd gehen/vnangesehen jres ampts/stands/wandels/zucht/vñ was sonst mehr für tugend an jnen mögen gerhümet werden. Denn das Euangelion verfolgen/den Son Gottes nicht allein für vnsern erlöser halten/Andere mittel der seligkeyt den leuten fürhalten/vnd suchen/Abgötterey verteydigen ꝛc. solches ist ein sünde/die nicht allein hie auff erden mit allerley plagen/sonder in yener welt/mit dem hellischen ewigen fewer/muß gestraffet werden.

And it may well be that many of those who help in such a bloodthirsty undertaking are upright as regards their conduct and life. But what does Simeon say? As soon as you hear the speaking against, that they will not suffer the word of the Lord Christ nor let it be true, then ask no further: their heart stands as plainly and wide open to you as if you had it in your hand. You can therefore judge that it is sheer hypocrisy with them, that the devil sits in their heart, drives and leads them, and, in sum, that they are wholly the devil's, as they stand and go, without regard to their office, station, conduct, discipline, and whatever other virtue may be praised in them. For to persecute the gospel, not to hold the Son of God alone for our Redeemer, to set other means of salvation before people and to seek them, to defend idolatry, etc.: this is a sin which must be punished not only here on earth with all manner of plagues, but in the world beyond with the eternal fire of hell.

**¶7** (pp. 9–10)

> Da sehen wir/die wir sonst des Creutzes/vnd der verfolgung halben/kleinmütig vnnd verzagt sind/was für einen grossen treffenlichen vortheyl/wir für der welt haben/ob gleich die selb mit gewalt/ehren/gut/vnd anders vns vberlegē ist. Sollen derhalb dem genedigen Gott im Hymel/ [p. 10] nicht vndanckbar für solche gnade sein/vnd sonderlich weyl die welt/mit dem widersprechen nicht will ablassen/sonder noch das schwert dazu in die faust nimbt/sollen wir beden/dem Teuffel vnnd der welt zu trotz/Vnnd darnach dem Son Gottes zu ehren/vnnd vnseren nachkommen zu nutz/mit dem bekentniß in alle weg/so vns müglich/getrewlich vnd fleyssig anhalten/vnd vnsere rechte Christenliche lehr/auff alle weyse zu pflantzen/vnd erweytern versuchen.

Here we, who are otherwise fainthearted and despondent on account of the cross and of persecution, see what a great and excellent advantage we have over the world, although it is superior to us in power, honors, wealth, and other things. We should therefore not be unthankful to the gracious God in heaven for such grace. And especially, since the world will not leave off the speaking against, but takes the sword in hand besides, we should, in defiance of both the devil and the world, and further to the honor of the Son of God and for the good of our descendants, persevere faithfully and diligently in the confession in every way possible to us, and try in every manner to plant and extend our true Christian doctrine.

**¶8** (pp. 10–11)

> Sonderlich aber sollen wir auff das junge volck sehen/das es inn zeyt zu Gottes wort gehalten/vnnd trewlich damit vnterrichtet werde. Solche vrsach hat mich bewegt/dise kurtze Euangelien Summaria/so ich für meine kindlein zuschreyben newlich angefangen/yetzt diser zeyt/da ich schwachheyt halb/mein ampt in der Kirch/nicht hab selb verwalten können/gar zuuerfertigen/vnnd der jugend zu gut inn druck zu geben/auff das/wer lust dazu hat/meiner arbeyt [p. 11] möchte geniessen/ob ich gleych stetter kranckheyt halb/das predigampt hab müssen anstehen lassen.

But especially we should look to the young people, that they be kept to God's word early and faithfully instructed in it. This reason has moved me, now at this time when on account of weakness I have not been able to administer my office in the church myself, to finish completely these short summaries of the Gospels, which I recently began to write for my little children, and to give them to the press for the good of the youth, so that whoever has a desire for it might have the benefit of my labor, although on account of continual illness I have had to let the office of preaching rest.

**¶9** (p. 11)

> Jch hab aber günstiger vnnd lieber Newdörffer/euch vnd ewer schule/damit verehren wöllen/weyl jr/alß ein Christlicher trewer Schulmeyster/ewr gesindlin sonderlich fleyssig zu Gottes wort haltet/das ich nicht allein zu solchem gutem werck euch hülffe/sonder durch ewr Exempel/auch andere deudsche vnnd lateynische Schulmeyster/reytzete/ewrem Exempel nach/hie vnd an andern orten/die jugend fleyssig zu Gottes wort zu halten. Denn da müssen wir anheben/so wir anders Christliche lehr erhalten/vnnd Gottes namen heyligen/Ja auch fromme/gefölgige/züchtige/vnd glückselige Kinder haben wöllen.

But, kind and dear Neudörffer, I have wished to honor you and your school with it, because you, as a Christian and faithful schoolmaster, keep your little household especially diligently to God's word; so that I might not only help you in such a good work, but through your example might also stir up other German and Latin schoolmasters, after your example, here and in other places, to keep the youth diligently to God's word. For there we must begin, if indeed we would preserve Christian doctrine and hallow God's name, yes, and also have godly, obedient, well-disciplined, and happy children.

**¶10** (pp. 11–12)

> Bey den Papisten soll diß klein Büchlein/so vnansehenlich es ist/sein frucht auch reychlich mit bringen/das es zum ewigen zeuckniß/sampt andern der vnsern schrifften offentlich da stehen sol/das sie vñ yederman sehen/was doch [p. 12] mangels an vnser lehr sey/die sie bißher mit lestern so hart angefochten/vnd yetzundt mit dem schwert gar zu tilgen sich vnterfangen haben. Aber sie sollens nicht enden/Dise lehr/soll vnd muß bleyben/Sie aber/wenn sie es nit wöllen leyden/müssen drüber zu grund gehen/vñ soll sie kein macht noch herrligkeyt helffen. Der Allmechtige/wölle euch/vnd alle fromme Christen/sonderlich zu diser kömmerlichen zeyt/gnedig erhalten vnd behüten/Amen.

Among the papists too this little book, unimpressive as it is, shall bring its fruit abundantly, in that it shall stand publicly, together with other writings of ours, as an everlasting testimony, so that they and everyone may see what fault, after all, there is in our doctrine, which they have hitherto assailed so hard with blasphemy and have now undertaken to destroy utterly with the sword. But they shall not accomplish it. This doctrine shall and must remain. But they, if they will not suffer it, must perish over it, and no power or glory shall help them. May the Almighty graciously preserve and protect you and all godly Christians, especially in this sorrowful time. Amen.

**Date and subscription (p. 12)**

> Geschriben den andern September/in meiner studier vnd siech stube. Anno 1546.
>
> E. W. Vitus Dietrich.

Written on the second of September, in my study and sickroom, in the year 1546.

Your willing [servant], Vitus Dietrich.

**Notes**
- **Neudörffer (Address).** Johann Neudörffer the Elder (1497–1563) was Nuremberg's celebrated writing and arithmetic master. *deutscher Schulmeyster* means the master of a German school, one that taught in German rather than in Latin; Dietrich draws the same distinction in ¶9 ("German and Latin schoolmasters"). *sonder lieben* is "especially dear."
- **Simeon (¶1, ¶4).** The letter is built on Luke 2:34–35. *widersprechen* ("speak against") is the verb of Luke 2:34 in Luther's Bible ("ein Zeichen, dem widersprochen wird"; King James: "a sign which shall be spoken against"), and the noun *das widersprechen* is rendered "the speaking against" throughout. "That the thoughts of many hearts may be revealed" is Luke 2:35 in King James wording, which Dietrich's German follows closely.
- **The two swords (¶2).** The first sword "pierces through" the heart, applying to all Christians Simeon's word to Mary, "a sword shall pierce through thy own soul also" (Luke 2:35). The second is persecution.
- **"So willing a servant to the devil" (¶2).** The print reads *diener* ("servant"), checked at 400 dpi. *dienet* ("serves") would also make sense, but the noun matches the parallel *feynd* ("enemy") in the next clause.
- **Scripture in ¶3.** The words of the Son of God are Matthew 25:34. The list of the condemned is Revelation 22:15, in King James wording; the rest of that sentence, that they "must go down into the fire of hell," is Dietrich's own (compare Revelation 21:8).
- **"The right exchange" (¶3).** *wechssel* means exchange or reversal: in the life beyond, present fortunes are turned around.
- **Trent (¶5).** The Council of Trent had opened in December 1545. *Conciliabel* (Latin *conciliabulum*) is the term for an illegitimate council; Dietrich denies Trent the name of a council. *vergleychen* ("settle") is the vocabulary of the religious colloquies that sought to reconcile the two parties; Dietrich had himself taken part in the one held at Regensburg in 1546.
- **The sword "now" (¶5, ¶7, ¶10).** The letter is dated 2 September 1546, during the Schmalkaldic War, which had broken out that summer. This is the sword that, Dietrich says, has now been added to the speaking against.
- **"As they stand and go" (¶6).** An idiom for "in all that they are and do."
- **Dietrich's illness (¶8, subscription).** He says he can neither administer his office nor preach, because of continual illness, and dates the letter from his "study and sickroom." He died at Nuremberg on 25 March 1549.
- **"Honor you with it" (¶9).** *verehren* here has its older sense of honoring someone with a gift, that is, presenting the book to him.
- **"Your little household" (¶9).** *gesindlin* is the diminutive of *Gesinde*, a household; here it evidently means Neudörffer's pupils.
- **Subscription.** *E. W.* is taken as the usual abbreviation for *Ewer Williger*, "your willing [servant]."
- **Two-word forms (¶7).** *ob gleich* ("although") and *die selb* ("the same") are printed as two words and are kept so.

---

## PART I. TEMPORALE

St. Stephen's Day, which the *Summaria* prints after Christmas, is given with the saints' days in Part II ([81]).

### Advent

#### [1] First Sunday in Advent

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 13):** *Am ersten Sontag des Aduents/wie Christus zu Jerusalem auff eim Esel einreytet/Euang. Matth. 21.* [On the First Sunday in Advent: how Christ rides into Jerusalem on an ass. Gospel, Matthew 21.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 13–14):** *Auß dem Propheten Zacharia. 9.* [From the prophet Zechariah 9.]

> DV tochter Zion/frewe dich sehr/Vñ du tochter Jerusalem jauchtze. Sihe dein König kombt zu [p. 14] dir/ein gerechter vñ ein helffer/Arm/vnd reytet auff einem Esel/vnd auff eim Füllen der Eselin.

Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; and shout, thou daughter of Jerusalem: behold, thy King cometh unto thee, a righteous one and a helper, poor, and riding upon an ass, and upon a colt the foal of an ass.

**Summary (PDF pp. 14–15)**

> DJser spruch lehret/was wir von dem Herren Christo sollen glauben/vnnd wie wir sein sollen geniessen/Er ist ein König/das ist/er will ein volck vnnd Regiment haben auff erden. Aber nicht wie andere welt König/die herrlich prangen/vnd mechtig sind/Denn diser König Christus/ist für der welt arm/wie man auch an dem mercket/das er nur auff einem entlehneten Esel einreytet/da er zu Jerusalem/alß ein König sich will sehen lassen/Das aber ist sein herrligkeyt/das er gerecht ist/vnd von sünden allen den wil helffen/so jn für einen König annemen/vnd an jn glauben/Vnd das er ein helffer ist/der von dem ewigen tode erlösen will/Wie er denn solcher vrsach halb/das vns von sünd/vnd todt geholffen [p. 15] würde/am Creutz sich auffgeopffert hat/vnd gestorben ist/Solches Königes sollen wir vns frewen/vnd jn mit hertzen annemen/vber jm jauchtzen/vnd guter ding sein/vnnd Gott für solche gnad dancken/wie der Prophet saget.

This saying teaches what we are to believe of the Lord Christ, and how we are to have the benefit of Him. He is a King; that is, He will have a people and a kingdom on earth. But not like other kings of the world, who make a splendid show and are mighty. For this King Christ is poor in the sight of the world, as one may see also in this, that He rides in upon nothing but a borrowed ass when He would show Himself at Jerusalem as a king. But this is His glory: that He is righteous, and will help out of sins all who receive Him as their King and believe in Him; and that He is a helper, who will redeem from eternal death. As indeed, for this very reason, that we might be helped out of sin and death, He offered Himself up on the cross and died. In such a King we are to rejoice, and receive Him with our hearts, shout for joy over Him and be of good cheer, and thank God for such grace, as the prophet says.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse.** Zechariah 9:9, which the Gospel quotes (Matthew 21:5). Where the King James Version has "he is just, and having salvation; lowly," the German, as in Luther's Bible, has *ein gerechter vñ ein helffer/Arm* ("a righteous one and a helper, poor"), and the summary expounds those three words in turn, so they are translated literally. A stroke stands over the *m* of *Arm*; it has no meaning here.
- **"Have the benefit of Him."** *sein … geniessen* (genitive) means to enjoy something, to have the use and benefit of it.
- **"Kingdom."** *Regiment* is rule or government; with *König* it is rendered "kingdom."

**Prayer (PDF pp. 15–16)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/wir dancken dir ye billich/vnd loben/vnd preysen dich inn ewigkeyt/das du vns armen menschen/so wir der sünden halb/vnter des todtes vnnd Sathans Tyranney haben müssen sein/deinen Son zu einem König gesetzet hast/der ein gerechter König vñ Heyland sein/das ist/vns von vnsern sünden/durch seinen todt ledig machen/vnnd wider den todt retten vnd helffen soll/Wir bitten dich auch/du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst vns erleuchten/regieren vnnd füren/das wir an disen gerechten König vnd Heyland vns alleine [type damaged] halten/vnd an seiner armen gestalt vñ verachtetem wort vns nit/wie die welt/ergern/vnd also durch ein festes vertrawen auff jn/ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee, as is meet and right, we bless and praise Thee forever, that over us poor men, who because of sin had to be under the tyranny of death and Satan, Thou hast set Thy Son as King, to be a righteous King and Savior, that is, by His death to set us free from our sins, and to rescue and help us against death: We beseech Thee also so to enlighten, govern and direct us by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may hold to this righteous King and Savior alone, and not, after the manner of the world, be offended at His humble form and despised word, and so, through a firm trust in Him, obtain eternal salvation; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "we thank Thee" → "we thank Thee, as is meet and right," restoring *ye billich*.
- TLHy's "that Thou didst send Thy Son to rule over us poor sinners, who for our transgressions did justly deserve to remain in the bondage of sin and Satan, and didst give us in Him a meek and righteous King, who by His death became our Savior from sin and eternal death" is replaced by a rendering of the German. Dietrich speaks of "poor men" (*armen menschen*), not "sinners"; of men who "had to be" (*haben müssen sein*), not who "did justly deserve to remain"; and of "the tyranny of death and Satan" (*des todtes vnnd Sathans Tyranney*), not "the bondage of sin and Satan". "Meek" is not in the prayer. The Son is set as King "to be" Savior, "that is," to free us from sin by His death and to help us against death (*der ein gerechter König vñ Heyland sein/das ist/vns von vnsern sünden/durch seinen todt ledig machen/vnnd wider den todt retten vnd helffen soll*).
- "We beseech Thee so" → "We beseech Thee also so" (*auch*).
- "ever remain faithful to this righteous King and Savior" → "hold to this righteous King and Savior alone" (*vns alleine halten*).
- "with His humble form" → "at His humble form" (idiom only).
- "but, firmly believing in Him," → "and so, through a firm trust in Him," (*vnd also durch ein festes vertrawen auff jn*).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [2] Second Sunday in Advent

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 16):** *Am andern Sontag des Aduents/was für zeychen vor dem Jüngsten tag kommen/vnd wie die Christen dazu sich schicken sollen/Luce am 22.* [On the Second Sunday in Advent: what signs come before the Last Day, and how Christians should prepare themselves for it. Luke 22 [sic, for 21].]

**Verse (PDF p. 17).** The Gospel (Luke 21:34–36) stands without a source line.

> HVtet euch/das ewre hertzen nit beschweret werden/mit fressen vnd mit sauffen/vnd sorgen für die Narung/vnnd komme diser tag schnell vber euch. Denn wie ein fallstrick wirdt er kommen/vber alle die auff erden wohnen/So seydt nun wacker alle zeyt/vnnd bettet/das jr wirdig werden möget/zu empfliehen disem allem/vnnd zu stehen für des menschen Son.

Take heed to yourselves, lest your hearts be overcharged with surfeiting and with drunkenness, and cares of this life, and so that day come upon you unawares. For as a snare shall it come on all them that dwell on the earth. Watch ye therefore at all times, and pray, that ye may be accounted worthy to escape all these things, and to stand before the Son of man.

**Summary (PDF pp. 17–18)**

> VNser HERr Christus/weissaget erstlich/wie ein jüngster tag komē/himel vnd erden zergehen/vñ was für zeychen vor her gehen werden/Vnd tröstet darnach seine Christen/sie sollen vor solchem tag nit erschrecken/Denn alß denn sollen sie erlöset werden/von der sünde/der welt/vnd des leydigen Teuffels anfechtung vnd allem vbel/vnd soll ewige seligkeyt folgen. Jtem [p. 18] er warnet/wie wir auff solchen tag vns sollen schicken. Nemlich das wir nicht sicher sein/vns mit fressen vnd sauffen nicht beladen/sonder wacker/munder vnd nüchtern sollen sein/vñ ymmerdar inn Gottes forcht leben/vnnd für sünden vns hütten/vnnd alß denn mit dem Gebet auch anhalten/das Gott durch seinen heyligen Geyst vns regieren/vnd für sünden vnd aller anfechtung bewaren wölle/Wer solches thut/den soll diser tag nicht vbereylen/es soll jm ein tag der erlösung sein/vnnd ein anfang des ewigen lebens.

Our Lord Christ first prophesies how a Last Day will come, heaven and earth will pass away, and what signs will go before it. And then He comforts His Christians: they are not to be terrified before such a day, for then they shall be redeemed from sin, from the world, and from the temptation of the wretched devil and from all evil, and eternal salvation shall follow. He also warns us how we are to prepare ourselves for such a day: namely, that we are not to be secure, nor burden ourselves with surfeiting and drunkenness, but are to be watchful, wakeful and sober, and live evermore in the fear of God, and guard ourselves against sins, and then also continue in prayer, that God would govern us by His Holy Ghost and keep us from sins and from all temptation. Whoever does this, that day shall not overtake him; it shall be to him a day of redemption and a beginning of eternal life.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is the end of the Gospel, Luke 21:34–36, in King James wording. Dietrich's text lacks "on the face of the whole earth" and "that shall come to pass," and puts "at all times" with "watch." *sorgen für die Narung* (cares for one's livelihood) is "cares of this life," as in the collect.
- **"Secure."** *sicher* has its old sense of careless, unconcerned; compare "carnal security" in the table in §2.2.
- **"Jtem."** Latin *item*, "likewise, further," common in German prose of the time; rendered "also."

**Prayer (PDF pp. 18–19)**

> HERR Gott hymlischer Vatter/der du durch deinen Son vnns hast offenbaret/wie Hymel vnd erden zergehn/vnser leybe wider aufferstehen/vnd wir alle für Gericht sollen gestellet werden/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest durch deinen heiligen geyst vns in deim wort/vnd rechtem glauben erhalten/für sünden genedig behüten/vnd in aller anfechtung erhalten/das wir vnsere hertzen mit fressen vnd sauffen/vnd sorgen der Narung nicht beschweren/sonder allzeyt wacker sein/vnd beten/vnd in gewiser zuuersicht deiner gnad/der zukunfft deines Sons mit frewden erwarten/vnd durch jn ewig selig werden/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who by Thy Son hast revealed to us that heaven and earth shall pass away, that our bodies shall rise again, and that we all shall appear before the judgment seat: We beseech Thee, keep us by Thy Holy Ghost in Thy word and in the true faith, graciously defend us from sin and preserve us in all temptations, that our hearts may not be overcharged with surfeiting and drunkenness, and cares of this life, but that we may ever watch and pray, and in sure confidence of Thy grace await with joy the coming of Thy Son, and obtain eternal salvation through Him, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "keep us by Thy Holy Spirit in Thy word; establish us in the true faith" → "keep us by Thy Holy Ghost in Thy word and in the true faith." The German has one verb for both (*vns in deim wort/vnd rechtem glauben erhalten*); "establish" is not in it.
- "trusting fully in Thy grace" → "in sure confidence of Thy grace" (*in gewiser zuuersicht deiner gnad*).
- "the glorious coming" → "the coming"; "glorious" is not in the German.
- "and at last obtain eternal salvation, through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord," → "and obtain eternal salvation through Him," (*vnd durch jn ewig selig werden*; "at last" is not in the German). TLHy's conclusion follows (§2.4c).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

**Note.** "Overcharged with surfeiting and drunkenness, and cares of this life" is TLHy's King James wording of Luke 21:34, which Dietrich's *fressen vnd sauffen/vnd sorgen der Narung* follows in Luther's version; it is kept.

#### [3] Third Sunday in Advent

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 20):** *Am dritten Sontag des Aduents/wie Johannes seine Jüngern zu Christo schicket/Matth. 11.* [On the Third Sunday in Advent: how John sends his disciples to Christ. Matthew 11.]

**Verse (PDF p. 20)**

> CHristus sprach zu Johannis jüngern/Die Blinden sehen/die Lamen gehen/die Aussetzigen werden reyn/vnd die Tauben hören/vñ die todten stehen auff/vnnd den armen wirdt das Euangelion gepredigt/Vnd selig ist der sich an mir nit ergert

Christ said to John's disciples: The blind receive their sight, the lame walk, the lepers are cleansed, and the deaf hear, and the dead are raised up, and the poor have the gospel preached to them; and blessed is he, whosoever shall not be offended in me.

**Summary (PDF pp. 21–22)**

> DJß ist auch ein lehr/vom Ampt vnsers lieben Herren Christi/das wir lernen sollen/wozu wir sein bedürffen/vnd wie wir sein geniessen sollen. Nemlich/das er darumb auff erden kombt/das er vns menschen helffen will/wider den Sathan/vnd alles vnglück wenden/so durch die sünde auff vns kommen ist/Derhalben sollen wir inn allem vnglück bey dem Herrn Christo hilffe suchen/vnd hoffen/Vnd sonderlich sein wort/das heylig Euangelion mit hertzen annemen vñ glauben/Denn durch das selbe/werden wir von sünden ledig/vnd kommen zum ewigen leben. Darumb ist solches die höchste vnd gröste wolthat/vnnd das herrlichste wunderwerck/das er den armen das Euangelion prediget/das ist/die jre sünde sehen vnd fülen/vnd lassen ins hertzlich leyd sein/Dise tröstet Christus/sie sollen zu friden sein/er wölle für die sünde bezalen/vnnd sie dauon ledig machen. Solche zusagung nimme mit [p. 22] glauben an/vnd ergere dich an Christo nicht/das ist/zweyffele an solcher zusagung nicht/so bist du selig. Wer aber zweifflet/vnd sich an Christo ergert/der wirdt nicht selig.

This too is a teaching of the office of our dear Lord Christ, that we may learn for what we need Him and how we are to have the benefit of Him: namely, that He comes upon earth for this purpose, that He will help us men against Satan, and turn away all the misfortune that has come upon us through sin. Therefore in every misfortune we are to seek help from the Lord Christ and hope in Him, and above all receive His word, the holy gospel, with our hearts and believe it; for through it we are set free from sins and come to eternal life. Therefore this is the highest and greatest benefit, and the most glorious miracle, that He preaches the gospel to the poor, that is, to those who see and feel their sins and are heartily sorry for them. These Christ comforts: they are to be at peace; He will pay for their sin and set them free from it. Receive such a promise with faith, and be not offended in Christ, that is, do not doubt such a promise, and you are saved. But whoever doubts and is offended in Christ will not be saved.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 11:4–6, introduced by Dietrich's own "Christ said to John's disciples," in King James wording.
- **"Saved."** *selig* means both "blessed," as in the verse, and "saved." Dietrich takes the verse's "blessed is he … not offended" as a promise of salvation (*so bist du selig*), so the summary has "saved."
- **"Heartily sorry for them."** *vnd lassen ins hertzlich leyd sein*, literally "and let it be heartily grievous to them."
- **"Pay for."** *für die sünde bezalen*, as in the table in §2.2.

**Prayer (PDF p. 22)**

> HErr Got hymlischer Vatter/der du deinen Son vnsern herrn Jesum Christum mensch werden/vnd darumb inn dise welt hast kommen lassen/das er des Teuffels Tyranney wehren/vns armen menschen wider sünd vnd tod helffen/vnd ewig soll selig machē/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest mit deinem heiligen Geist/vnsere hertzen also füren vnd leyten/das wir auff anders nichts/denn auff sein wort sehen/vnd also allem ergernuß/da vnser vernunfft vnns auffweyset/empfliehen/vnnd vnter dem heufflein mögen gefunden werden/welcher sich an deinem Son Christo Jesu nicht ergert/sonder durch jn selig wirdt/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who didst suffer Thy Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, to become man, and for this cause to come into this world, that He should restrain the tyranny of the devil, help us poor men against sin and death, and save us eternally: We beseech Thee so to lead and guide our hearts with Thy Holy Ghost, that we may look to nothing else but His word, and so avoid all offense whereunto our reason directeth us, and may be found among the little company which is not offended in Thy Son, Jesus Christ, but is saved through Him, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "and to come into the world" → "and for this cause to come into this world" (*vnd darumb inn dise welt*).
- "that He might destroy the works of the devil" → "that He should restrain the tyranny of the devil" (*des Teuffels Tyranney wehren*).
- "deliver us poor offenders from sin and death" → "help us poor men against sin and death" (*vns armen menschen wider sünd vnd tod helffen*).
- "and give us everlasting life" → "and save us eternally" (*vnd ewig soll selig machen*).
- "so to rule and govern our hearts by Thy Holy Spirit" → "so to lead and guide our hearts with Thy Holy Ghost" (*mit deinem heiligen Geist ... füren vnd leyten*).
- "that we may seek no other refuge than His word" → "that we may look to nothing else but His word" (*auff anders nichts/denn auff sein wort sehen*).
- "and thus avoid all offense to which, by nature, we are inclined" → "and so avoid all offense whereunto our reason directeth us" (*da vnser vernunfft vnns auffweyset*).
- "in order that we may always be found among the faithful followers of Thy Son, Jesus Christ, and by faith in Him obtain eternal salvation" → "and may be found among the little company which is not offended in Thy Son, Jesus Christ, but is saved through Him" (*vnter dem heufflein ... welcher sich an deinem Son Christo Jesu nicht ergert/sonder durch jn selig wirdt*). "Offended in" is the King James idiom of Matthew 11:6, the Gospel of the day.
- "through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord," is dropped, since Dietrich's last clause already ends "through Him"; TLHy's conclusion follows it (§2.4c).

#### [4] Fourth Sunday in Advent

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 23):** *Am vierdten Sontag des Aduents/wie die Juden von Jerusalem zu Johanne schicken/vnd jhm das tauffen verbieten wöllen/Johannis am 1.* [On the Fourth Sunday in Advent: how the Jews send from Jerusalem to John and would forbid him to baptize. John 1.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 23–24)**

> JOhannes sprach: Jch tauffe mit wasser/Aber er (Christus) ist mitten vnter euch getreten/den jr nicht kennet/Der ists/der nach mir kommen wirdt/welcher vor mir gewesen [p. 24] ist/des ich nicht werd bin/das ich seine schuchryemen aufflöse.

John said: I baptize with water: but He (Christ) standeth among you, whom ye know not; He it is, who cometh after me, who was before me, whose shoe's latchet I am not worthy to unloose.

**Summary (PDF pp. 24–25)**

> DJse Predig Johannis des Tauffers/lehret/wie wir den Herren Christum/seines ambts vnd person halben/sollen vnterscheiden/von allen andern menschē/Johannes der tauffer/die Aposteln/vnd alle Kirchendiener/tauffen nur mit wasser/Das ist/ob wol der heylig Geyst/durch solches wassertauffen den leuten geben wirt/so geben doch sie nit mehr denn das wasser/Den Geyst können sie nit geben/Aber vnser lieber Herr Christus/der verdienet vns durch den gehorsam/welchen er seinem Vatter leystet/den heyligen Geyst/vnnd ist der Herr vber solche gaben/das er sie außtheylet nach seinem willen/allen die sich in seinem todt lassen tauffen/vnd sein wort mit glauben annemen. Darumb so lerne es bedes/Wir menschen können den heyligen Geyst niemandts [p. 25] geben/Christus allein kan jn geben/Er will jn aber durch der menschen ampt geben/Weñ menschen das Euangelion predigen/wenn sie tauffen/wenn sie das Nachtmal außspenden/durch solche menschen werck/will Christus den heyligen Geyst geben/Derhalb soll yederman die predigt des Euangelij/die heylige Tauff/das gnadenreyche Abendmal des Herrn suchen vnd brauchen/Vnnd alß denn durch Christum/die gabe des heyligen Geystes hoffen/vnd jn auch darumb bitten.

This sermon of John the Baptist teaches how we are to distinguish the Lord Christ, as to His office and person, from all other men. John the Baptist, the apostles, and all ministers of the Church baptize only with water; that is, although the Holy Ghost is given to people through such baptizing with water, yet they themselves give no more than the water; the Spirit they cannot give. But our dear Lord Christ merits the Holy Ghost for us by the obedience which He renders to His Father, and is Lord over such gifts, so that He distributes them according to His will to all who let themselves be baptized into His death and receive His word with faith. Therefore learn both of these things. We men can give the Holy Ghost to no one; Christ alone can give Him. But He will give Him through the office of men. When men preach the gospel, when they baptize, when they administer the Supper, through such works of men Christ will give the Holy Ghost. Therefore everyone is to seek and use the preaching of the gospel, holy baptism, and the gracious Supper of the Lord, and then hope through Christ for the gift of the Holy Ghost, and also ask Him for it.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 1:26–27, with Dietrich's "(Christ)" in the text, in King James wording. The German has "who was before me" (*welcher vor mir gewesen ist*), where the King James Version has "is preferred before me," and is translated accordingly.
- **"Ministers of the Church."** *Kirchendiener*, literally "servants of the Church."
- **"Into His death."** *in seinem todt … tauffen* echoes Romans 6:3.
- **"Supper."** Dietrich uses both *Nachtmal* and *Abendmal* for the Lord's Supper; both are rendered "Supper."

**Prayer (PDF pp. 25–26)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/wir dancken dir ye billich/das du die selige gnadenreyche Tauf/durch den heyligen Tauffer Johannem hast erstlich angerichtet/vnnd vns auch dazu lassen kommen/In welcher du vns vmb deines Suns Christi Jesu willen zusagest vergebung der sünden/den heyligen Geyst/vnd das ewige leben/Wir bitten dich/erhalte vns inn solchem vertrawen auff dein gnad vnd barmhertzigkeyt/das wir an solcher zusagung nymmer mehr zweyffelen/sonder der selben inn allerley anfechtung vns trösten/Vnd gib durch deinen heyligen Geyst/das wir für sünden vns hüten/vnnd in solcher vnschuld/dazu wir durch die Tauffe kommen sind/bleyben mögen/Oder ja wo wir fallen/vnnd menschlicher schwachheyt halb nicht können bestehen/das wir inn sünden nicht ligen bleyben/sonder durch rechtschaffene Buß vns bekeren/vnnd deiner gnad vnnd zusagung/vns wider trösten/vnd also durch dein genad ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, it is meet and right that we should give thanks unto Thee, that Thou didst first establish the blessed baptism, rich in grace, through holy John the Baptist, and hast caused us also to come unto it, wherein Thou dost promise us, for the sake of Thy Son, Jesus Christ, the remission of sins, the Holy Ghost, and everlasting life: Preserve us, we beseech Thee, in such trust in Thy grace and mercy, that we may never doubt Thy promise, but be comforted by the same in all temptations; and grant by Thy Holy Ghost that we may guard ourselves against sin, and continue in the innocence unto which we have come through baptism; or, if we fall, and by reason of human weakness cannot stand, that we may not lie still in sin, but turn again through true repentance, and comfort ourselves again with Thy grace and promise, and so through Thy grace obtain eternal salvation; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "that Thou hast given us a more glorious baptism than that of John the Baptist" → "that Thou didst first establish the blessed baptism, rich in grace, through holy John the Baptist, and hast caused us also to come unto it" (*das du die selige gnadenreyche Tauf/durch den heyligen Tauffer Johannem hast erstlich angerichtet/vnnd vns auch dazu lassen kommen*). Dietrich does not set our baptism above John's; he says it was first instituted through John.
- "and hast therein promised us the remission of sins, the Holy Spirit, and everlasting life through Thy Son, Jesus Christ" → "wherein Thou dost promise us, for the sake of Thy Son, Jesus Christ, the remission of sins, the Holy Ghost, and everlasting life" (*vmb deines Suns Christi Jesu willen*, "for the sake of," not "through").
- "in such faith in Thy grace" → "in such trust in Thy grace" (*vertrawen*).
- "and grant us Thy Holy Spirit that we may renounce sin, and ever continue in the righteousness bestowed upon us in baptism" → "and grant by Thy Holy Ghost that we may guard ourselves against sin, and continue in the innocence unto which we have come through baptism" (*das wir für sünden vns hüten/vnnd in solcher vnschuld/dazu wir durch die Tauffe kommen sind/bleyben mögen*).
- Restored the clause TLHy omits: "or, if we fall, and by reason of human weakness cannot stand, that we may not lie still in sin, but turn again through true repentance, and comfort ourselves again with Thy grace and promise" (*Oder ja wo wir fallen ... vns wider trösten*).
- "until by Thy grace we obtain eternal salvation" → "and so through Thy grace obtain eternal salvation" (*vnd also durch dein genad ewig selig werden*).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit" (twice).

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

### Christmas

#### [5] Christmas Day

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 27):** *Am heyligen Christage/von der geburt Christi/Luce am 2.* [On holy Christmas Day: of the birth of Christ. Luke 2.]

**Verse (PDF p. 27)**

> DEr Engel sprach zu dem [sic, for den] hirten/Fürchtet euch nit/Sihe/ich verkündige euch ein grosse frewde/die allem volck widerfaren wirdt. Denn euch ist heut geborn der Heyland/welcher ist Christus der Herr/in der stadt Dauid.

The angel said unto the shepherds, Fear not: behold, I bring you good tidings of great joy, which shall be to all people. For unto you is born this day the Saviour, which is Christ the Lord, in the city of David.

**Summary (PDF pp. 28–29)**

> DJse Engelpredigt lehret vns/wie wir von der geburt/vnsers lieben Herrn Christi halten/vñ vns dagegen stellen sollē. Er heist die Hirten frölich sein/vnnd sich gar nichts förchten/vnd sagt des vrsach/Darumb das der heyland geborn sey/der helffen vnnd retten soll/nicht wie weltliche König) [sic: ")" for virgule] wider zeytliches vnglück/sonder wider die sünde/den tod vnd alles vnglück/so auß der sünden herfleust. Denn solches zeuget das wörtlein Christus/das dises kind der gesalbte Gottes sey/dauon Gott im Paradiß verheyssen hat/Er soll der Schlangen den kopff zertretten/vnd dem Sathan all seine macht vnd gewalt nemen. Darumb sollen wir dises kindlins vns auch von hertzen frewen/vnnd mit den Engeln singen: Ehr sey Gott in der höhe/vnnd fride auff erden/Vnnd/den menschen ein hertzliche freud vnd wolgefallen. Deñ wir sollen Gott von hertzen darumb loben vnnd dancken/der durch dises [p. 29] kind aff [sic, for auff] erden frid gemacht/vns mit Gott versünet/Vnnd des Sathans Tyranney gewehret hat/Sollen derhalb frölich sein vnd guter ding/vnd an disen trost in allem vnglück vns halten.

This sermon of the angel teaches us what we are to hold concerning the birth of our dear Lord Christ, and how we are to conduct ourselves toward it. He bids the shepherds be joyful and fear nothing at all, and gives the reason: because the Savior is born, who is to help and to save, not as worldly kings do, against temporal misfortune, but against sin, death, and all the misfortune that flows out of sin. For this is what the little word "Christ" testifies: that this child is God's Anointed, of whom God promised in Paradise that He should bruise the serpent's head and take from Satan all his might and power. Therefore we too are to rejoice from our hearts in this little child, and sing with the angels: "Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, and to men a hearty joy and good pleasure." For we are to praise and thank God from our hearts that through this child He has made peace on earth, reconciled us with God, and restrained the tyranny of Satan. We are therefore to be joyful and of good cheer, and to hold to this comfort in every misfortune.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 2:10–11 in King James wording, introduced by "The angel said unto the shepherds." The German reads "great joy" without "good tidings" (*ich verkündige euch ein grosse frewde*); the King James phrase is kept because Dietrich's German follows the same verse.
- **"Christ."** *Christus* is the Greek for "Anointed" (*der gesalbte*). The promise in Paradise is Genesis 3:15.
- **The angels' song.** Luke 2:14, with Dietrich's own enlargement: Luther's Bible has *vnd den Menschen ein wolgefallen*; Dietrich writes "and to men a hearty joy and good pleasure."
- **"Restrained."** *wehren* (with genitive or dative) is to check or restrain, as in the Advent 3 prayer ("restrain the tyranny of the devil").

**Prayer (PDF p. 29)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/wir dancken dir deiner grossen gnad vñ barmhertzigkeyt/das du deinen eingebornen Sun inn vnser fleisch kommen/vnd durch jn/vns von sünden vnd dem ewigen tod/genediglich hast helffen lassen/Vnd bitten dich/erleuchte vnsere hertzen/durch deinen heyligen Geyst/das wir für solche deine genad dir danckbar sind/vnnd der selben vns in allerley not vnnd anfechtung trösten/vnd also durch deinen Sun/vnseren Herren Jhesum Christum/ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we give thanks unto Thee for Thy great grace and mercy, that Thou didst suffer Thine only-begotten Son to come into our flesh, and through Him hast graciously helped us out of sin and everlasting death: We beseech Thee, enlighten our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may be thankful unto Thee for such Thy grace, and comfort ourselves with the same in all tribulation and temptation, and so obtain eternal salvation through Thy Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "that of Thy mercy and compassion" → "for Thy great grace and mercy, that" (*deiner grossen gnad vñ barmhertzigkeyt*).
- "Thy dear Son to become incarnate" → "Thine only-begotten Son to come into our flesh" (*deinen eingebornen Sun inn vnser fleisch kommen*).
- "and didst through Him redeem us from sin and everlasting death" → "and through Him hast graciously helped us out of sin and everlasting death" (*durch jn/vns von sünden vnd dem ewigen tod/genediglich hast helffen lassen*).
- "that we may ever be thankful for such grace" → "that we may be thankful unto Thee for such Thy grace" ("ever" is not in the German; *dir* restored).
- "and at last obtain eternal salvation through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord," → "and so obtain eternal salvation through Thy Son, our Lord Jesus Christ," which is Dietrich's own close (*vnd also durch deinen Sun/vnseren Herren Jhesum Christum*); TLHy's conclusion continues it (§2.4d).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [6] First Sunday of Christmas (Sunday after Christmas)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 33):** *Am Sontag nach Weyhenachten/wie Simeon im Tempel Marien vnnd das Kindlin Jesus segnet/vnd von Christo weyssaget/Luce am 2.* [On the Sunday after Christmas: how Simeon in the Temple blesses Mary and the Child Jesus, and prophesies of Christ. Luke 2.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 33–34)**

> SJmeon sprach zu Maria der Mutter Jesu: Sihe/diser wirt gesetzt zu eim fall/vnd aufferstehung viler in Jsrael/vñ zu eim zeychen dem widersprochen wirt/Vnd es wirdt ein schwert durch sein [sic, for dein] Seele [p. 34] dringen/auff das viler hertzen gedancken offenbaret werden.

Simeon said unto Mary the mother of Jesus, Behold, this child is set for the fall and rising again of many in Israel; and for a sign which shall be spoken against; and a sword shall pierce through thy soul, that the thoughts of many hearts may be revealed.

**Summary (PDF pp. 34–35)**

> DJß ist ein weyssagung/wie es vnserm Herrn Christo/vnd hernach dem heyligen Euangelio/vñ allen Christen in der welt ergehn sol. Nemlich/das nit alle Christum annemen/noch durch jn selig werden/sonder an jm sich ergern vnd fallen werden/vnnd das/was inn der Welt/herrlich/mechtig/vnnd groß ist/jm widersprechen/das Euangelion für Ketzerey halten/es lestern vnnd schenden werde. Solches thut den Christen hertzlich wehe/vnd ist gleich wie ein Schwert/welches das hertz druchschneytet [sic, for durchschneytet]. Aber es dienet dennoch zu etwas/Nemlich/das die hertzen dadurch offenbaret werden. Deñ da sonst die leut sich schmucken/vnnd für die besten/frümbsten Christen köndten verkauffen/geben sie sich durch verfolgung des worts an tag/ [p. 35] das yederman sehen vnnd bekennen muß/der Teuffel so ein Mörder ist/sitze jnen im hertzen drinn/vnd das es nur eytel heucheley mit jnen sey/sintemal sie das Euangelion/nicht dulden noch leyden mögen.

This is a prophecy of how it shall go with our Lord Christ, and afterward with the holy gospel and with all Christians in the world: namely, that not all will receive Christ or be saved through Him, but will be offended in Him and fall; and that what is glorious, mighty and great in the world will speak against Him, hold the gospel for heresy, blaspheme it and revile it. This grieves Christians to the heart, and is just like a sword that cuts through the heart. But it nevertheless serves a purpose: namely, that hearts are revealed by it. For whereas people otherwise adorn themselves and could pass themselves off as the best and most godly Christians, by persecuting the word they bring themselves to light, so that everyone must see and confess that the devil, who is such a murderer, sits within their hearts, and that it is nothing but sheer hypocrisy with them, since they can neither tolerate nor bear the gospel.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 2:34–35 in King James wording, introduced by "Simeon said unto Mary the mother of Jesus." The print reads *durch sein Seele* ("through his soul"); Luther's Bible has *durch deine Seele* ("through thy soul"), and *sein* is taken as a misprint.
- **Simeon's prophecy** is also the theme of Dietrich's dedicatory letter (front matter, ¶1–¶4), where "the speaking against" (*das widersprechen*) and the sword through the heart are developed at length.
- **"Pass themselves off as."** *sich … verkauffen für*, literally "sell themselves for."
- **"Bring themselves to light."** *geben sie sich … an tag*, they reveal themselves.

**Assignment:** TLHy's First Sunday of Christmas. TLHy's only collect for that day is not Dietrich's ("O almighty and everlasting God, mercifully direct our ways…"; Appendix A, [7]).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 35–36)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/der du durch den heyligen Simeon hast weyßgesaget/wie Christus dein Sun zum fall vnnd aufferstehung viler in Jsrael gesetzt sey/wir bitten dich/erleuchte vnsere hertzen/durch deinen heyligen Geist/das wir deinen Sun Christum Jhesum recht erkennen/vnnd vns in allerley not vnnd fahr/an jn halten/vnd an jm auffstehen/vnd nicht mit der sichern blinden welt vns an jn stossen/auch in allerley leyden vnnd anfechtung/so deines worts vnnd bekentnuß halb vns begegnet/getröst vnnd mütig sein/sintemal gewiß ist/das die/so dein wort verfolgen/zu deim Reych nicht gehören/Die aber dran glauben vnd dabey bleyben/ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who by holy Simeon didst prophesy that Christ, Thy Son, is set for the fall and rising again of many in Israel: We beseech Thee, enlighten our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may rightly know Thy Son, Jesus Christ; in all manner of need and danger hold fast unto Him and rise again in Him, and not, with the secure and blind world, stumble at Him; and also, in all manner of suffering and temptation which befalleth us for the sake of Thy word and the confession thereof, be of good comfort and courage: since it is certain that they who persecute Thy word belong not to Thy kingdom, but they who believe it and abide by it shall obtain eternal salvation; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Wordplay on the text.** The opening follows Luke 2:34 and is given in King James wording ("set for the fall and rising again"). Dietrich then plays on the verse with *an jm auffstehen* ("rise again in Him") and *vns an jn stossen* ("stumble at Him"). Both are kept.
- **"Secure."** *sicher* has the sense TLHy renders elsewhere as "carnal security."
- **"Confession thereof."** *deines worts vnnd bekentnuß halb* is literally "for the sake of Thy word and confession." "The confession thereof" makes the reference explicit.
