#### [8] The Circumcision / New Year's Day

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 36):** *Am newen Jarstag/wie das Kindlin beschnitten/vnd Jesus ist genennet worden/Luc. 2.* [On New Year's Day: how the Child was circumcised and named Jesus. Luke 2.]

**Verse (PDF p. 37)**

> DA acht tage vmb waren/das das Kind beschnitten wurde/da ward sein Nam genennet Jesus/welcher genennet war/ehe denn er in Mutter leyb empfangen war.

When eight days were accomplished for the circumcising of the child, his name was called Jesus, which was so named before he was conceived in the womb.

**Summary (PDF pp. 37–38)**

> WAs des Herren Christi ampt sey/vnnd warumb er mensch worden/vnnd auff erden kommen sey/haben wir gehöret/Jm heutigen Euangelio aber stehet von seinem namen/das/ehe er in Mutter leyb ist empfangen worden/der Engel auß Gottes befelh jm den namen geben hat/er soll Jesus heyssen. Solchen namen legt der Engel selb auß/Matth. 1. Vñ sagt: Er soll Jesus heissen/Denn er wirt sein volck selig machen von jren sünden/Hilfft er aber von sünden/so hilfft er auch vom tod/vnd gibt das ewige leben/Denn wo keine sünde ist/da kan auch kein todt sein. So lerne nun dises Kindlins [p. 38] recht brauchen/vnd dich sein trösten. Engsten dich deine sünde/fürchtest du dich fur dem todt/wo wilt du anders wohin/denn zu disem Jesu/der darumb den namen hat/das er von sünden/seinem volck/das ist/allen so jn für jren Herrn annemen vnnd bekennen/helffen will: Wie er auch im werck heut beweyset/das er sich lest beschneyden/so er doch ein Herr vber das Gesetz war/Aber er gibt sich darum̃ mit der beschneydung vnter das Gesetz/das er vns vom fluch des Gesetzes will ledig/vnd also von sünden frey machen. Des sollen wir dem genedigen Gott von hertzen dancken/vnnd widerumb seines willens vns fleissen/vnnd für sünden vnd vngehorsam hüten.

What the office of the Lord Christ is, and why He became man and came upon earth, we have heard. But in today's Gospel we are told of His name: that before He was conceived in His mother's womb, the angel by God's command gave Him the name, that He should be called Jesus. This name the angel himself explains, Matthew 1, and says: "He shall be called Jesus: for he shall save his people from their sins." But if He helps from sins, He helps also from death, and gives eternal life; for where there is no sin, there can be no death either. Learn now, then, to use this little child rightly and to comfort yourself with Him. If your sins distress you, if you are afraid of death, where else will you go but to this Jesus, who has the name for this reason, that He will help His people from sins, that is, all who receive Him as their Lord and confess Him? As He also shows today in deed, in that He lets Himself be circumcised, although He was Lord over the law. But He puts Himself under the law with circumcision for this reason, that He would set us free from the curse of the law, and so make us free from sins. For this we are to thank the gracious God from our hearts, and in return apply ourselves to His will, and guard ourselves against sins and disobedience.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 2:21 in King James wording. Dietrich's text lacks "of the angel" (Luther: *von dem Engel*), and the translation follows him.
- **Matthew 1:21** is quoted in King James wording.
- **"Curse of the law."** Galatians 3:13; compare Galatians 4:4–5, "made under the law, to redeem them that were under the law."
- **"Apply ourselves to His will."** *seines willens vns fleissen*: *sich fleissen* (with genitive) is to be diligent in something.

**Assignment:** TLHy's Circumcision of Christ. TLHy's only collect for that day is not Dietrich's ("O merciful and eternal God, heavenly Father, who didst cause Thy Son to endure circumcision…"; Appendix A, [9]).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 38–39)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/wir dancken dir für dein Väterliche gnad/das du der armen sünder dich angenommen/vnd deinen Sun/vns zu gut/vnter das gesetz hast gethan/auff das er durch seinen volkommen gehorsam/deinen zorn stillete/vnnd vnsern vngehorsam heylete/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst/vnsere hertzen also erleuchten/das wir solches gehorsams vns wider vnsere sünde vnnd böses gewissen trösten/vnd durch hilff deines heyligen Geystes/auch anfahen gehorsame kinder zu sein/vnd endlich durch Christum ewig selig werden. Amen.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee for Thy fatherly grace, that Thou hast had compassion upon poor sinners, and for our good hast put Thy Son under the law, that by His perfect obedience He might still Thy wrath and heal our disobedience: We beseech Thee so to enlighten our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may comfort ourselves with this obedience against our sins and an evil conscience; and that by the help of Thy Holy Ghost we may also begin to be obedient children, and at last obtain eternal salvation through Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Under the law.** *vnter das gesetz hast gethan* echoes Galatians 4:4 (Luther: "vnter das Gesetz gethan"; King James: "made under the law"). The subject is the Father: it is the Father who puts the Son under the law.
- **"Had compassion upon."** *sich … annehmen* means to take up someone's cause or care for them. "Had compassion upon" gives the sense.
- **Whose obedience.** *solches gehorsams vns … trösten* means to comfort ourselves *with this obedience*, that is, Christ's, set against our sin.
- **Ending.** Dietrich's closing "through Christ" is moved to the end of its clause so that TLHy's "who liveth and reigneth…" can follow it; only the word order changes (§2.4c).

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [10] Second Sunday of Christmas (Sunday after New Year), restored from Quinquagesima

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 40):** *Am Sontag zwischē dem Newen jar vnd Obersten/wie Christus am Jordan von Johanne ist getauffet worden/Matth. 3.* [On the Sunday between New Year and Epiphany: how Christ was baptized by John at the Jordan. Matthew 3.] *Oberster* (Tag) is Dietrich's name for Epiphany.

**Verse (PDF pp. 40–41)**

> DA Jesus getauffet war/steyg er bald herauff auß dem wasser/Vnd sihe/da thet sich der Himel auff vber jm/vñ Johannes sahe den Geyst Gottes/gleych als ein Taube herab faren/vnd vber jhn kommen/ [p. 41] Vnd sihe ein stim̃ vom Himel herab sprach: Das ist mein lieber Sun/an dem ich wolgefallen hab.

When Jesus was baptized, he went up straightway out of the water: and, lo, the heavens were opened unto him, and John saw the Spirit of God descending like a dove, and lighting upon him: and lo a voice from heaven said, This is my beloved Son, in whom I am well pleased.

**Summary (PDF pp. 41–42)**

> DAs vnser lieber Herr Christus/selb sich von Johanne im Jordan lest tauffen/solches geschicht erstlich/das (wie er sagt) er alle gerechtigkeyt erfülle/das ist/das er nichts dahinden lasse/das da gehört zu vnser gerechtigkeyt/Denn weyl Gott durch Johannem die tauff geordnet hette/für die armen sünder/sie damit zu trösten/Gott wölte jhnen gnedig sein/kombt vnser lieber Herr Christus als ein armer sünder/denn er war Gottes Lamb/das der gantzen welt sünde auff sich träge/vnnd begerte der heyligen Tauff auch/vñ Johannes tauffet jn auch/vnd geschicht/das der heylig Geyst auff den Herren Christum sitzet/in gestalt einer Tauben/vnnd Gott von himel herunder lest sich hören/vnnd spricht: Das ist mein lieber [p. 42] Sun/da ich hertzliche freud an hab/Solches ist nun vnser sonderlicher trost/weil Christus vnser sünde tregt das wir sehen/das Gott vnser sünden halb jm nit vngnedig ist/sonder vns durch jn zu genaden annimbt/wenn anders wir vns in seim namen tauffen lassen/vnd seiner zusagung glauben. Zum andern/sollen wir lernen die Tauff hoch halten/sintemal sie der Sun Gottes selb mit seim leyb geheyligt/vnnd die gantze Gottheit sich da sichtigklich offenbaret hat/als bey eim solchem werck/da Gott nicht anders denn gnedig bey könne sein.

That our dear Lord Christ Himself lets Himself be baptized by John in the Jordan happens, first, so that (as He says) He may "fulfil all righteousness," that is, that He may leave nothing undone that belongs to our righteousness. For since God had ordained baptism through John for poor sinners, to comfort them with it that God would be gracious to them, our dear Lord Christ comes as a poor sinner (for He was the Lamb of God, who was to bear the sin of the whole world) and desires holy baptism too; and John baptizes Him too; and it comes to pass that the Holy Ghost rests upon the Lord Christ in the form of a dove, and God lets Himself be heard from heaven, and says: "This is my beloved Son, in whom I have heartfelt joy." Now this is our special comfort: since Christ bears our sin, we see that God is not ungracious to Him on account of our sins, but receives us into grace through Him, if only we let ourselves be baptized in His name and believe His promise. Secondly, we are to learn to hold baptism in high esteem, since the Son of God Himself hallowed it with His body, and the whole Godhead revealed itself there visibly, as at a work at which God cannot be present otherwise than graciously.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 3:16–17 in King James wording. With Luther's Bible, Dietrich's text has "John saw" where the King James Version has "he saw," and the translation follows him.
- **"Fulfil all righteousness."** Matthew 3:15. "The Lamb of God, which taketh away the sin of the world" is John 1:29.
- **"In whom I have heartfelt joy."** *da ich hertzliche freud an hab*: in the summary Dietrich paraphrases the Father's word "in whom I am well pleased."
- **"Rests upon."** *sitzet*, literally "sits."
- **"Cannot be present otherwise than graciously."** *da Gott nicht anders denn gnedig bey könne sein*: where the whole Trinity is revealed in baptism, God can be there only as gracious.

**Assignment.** TLHy prints this collect at Quinquagesima. It is restored here to the Sunday after New Year, which TLHy calls the Second Sunday of Christmas, and Quinquagesima receives Dietrich's own collect [23]. TLHy followed the Dano-Norwegian pericopes, in which Quinquagesima (*Fastelavn*) carries the Baptism of Christ, Matthew 3:13–17; in the German pericopes Dietrich follows, Quinquagesima has Luke 18:31–43, and the Baptism falls on the Sunday after New Year. TLHy's own collect for the Second Sunday of Christmas is not Dietrich's (Appendix A, [11]). TLHy's unrevised text of this prayer, Stratman's [24], is given in Appendix A.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 42–43)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/der du dich mit deim heyligen Geyst/inn allen genaden bey der Tauffe deines lieben Suns/der vnser aller sünde auff sich geladen/hast offenbaret/vnnd vns mit deiner stim̃ zu jm gewisen/das wir durch jhn vergebung der sünde/vnd alle gnad haben sollen/Wir bitten dich/erhalt vns in solcher zuuersicht/vñ weil wir nach dem Befelh vnd Exempel deines Suns auch getauffet sind/Stercke vnsern glauben durch deinen heiligen Geist/vnd hilff vns zum ewigen lebē/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who didst manifest Thyself, with Thy Holy Ghost, in all grace at the baptism of Thy dear Son, who hath taken upon Himself the sins of us all, and with Thy voice didst direct us to Him, that through Him we should have forgiveness of sins and all grace: Keep us, we beseech Thee, in this confidence; and inasmuch as we also have been baptized according to the command and example of Thy Son, we pray Thee to strengthen our faith by Thy Holy Ghost, and to help us unto eternal life; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "with the Holy Ghost" → "with Thy Holy Ghost" (*mit deim heyligen Geyst*).
- "in the fullness of grace" → "in all grace" (*inn allen genaden*).
- "and with Thy voice didst direct us to Him who hath borne our sins" → "who hath taken upon Himself the sins of us all, and with Thy voice didst direct us to Him" (*der vnser aller sünde auff sich geladen ... vnnd vns mit deiner stim̃ zu jm gewisen*). The clause is restored to its place and to its full wording (cf. Isaiah 53:6).
- "that we might receive grace and the remission of sins" → "that through Him we should have forgiveness of sins and all grace" (*das wir durch jhn vergebung der sünde/vnd alle gnad haben sollen*).
- "in the true faith" → "in this confidence" (*in solcher zuuersicht*).
- "inasmuch as we have been baptized in accordance with Thy command, and the example of Thy dear Son" → "inasmuch as we also have been baptized according to the command and example of Thy Son" (*weil wir nach dem Befelh vnd Exempel deines Suns auch getauffet sind*). Both the command and the example are the Son's.
- "and lead us to everlasting life and salvation" → "and to help us unto eternal life" (*vnd hilff vns zum ewigen lebē*).
- Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics. "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit"; the OLM web text's "Amen," corrected.

### Epiphany

#### [12] The Epiphany of Our Lord

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 43):** *Am Oberstag/Wie die weysen auß dem morgenland gehn Hierusalem kom̃en/vnnd nach dem newgebornen kindlin fragen/Luc. 2.* [On Epiphany: how the wise men come from the East to Jerusalem and ask after the newborn child. Luke 2 [sic, for Matthew 2].]

**Verse (PDF p. 44)**

> DJe weisen kamen von dem morgenland geen Hierusalem vnnd sprachē/wo ist der Newgeborn könig/der Juden/wir haben seinen stern gesehen im Morgenland/vnnd sind kom̃en jn anzubeten.

There came wise men from the east to Jerusalem, saying, Where is he that is born King of the Jews? for we have seen his star in the east, and are come to worship him.

**Summary (PDF pp. 44–50).** The paragraph breaks follow the print.

> GLeich wie der heylig Simeon im Euangelio des negst vergangnē Sontagß von dem kindlin weissaget/wie es dazu gesetzt sey/dz ettliche an jm auffstehen/ettliche fallen sollē. Also sehen wir im heutigē Euangelio solcher weissagung zwey exēpel/die seer wunderberlich vñ schier gar vnglaublich sind/Denn da sind erstlich die Juden/welche die verheissung von Christo haben/das er jr heiland/jr König/vnnd Hertzog sein sol/die wissen nicht allein/wo Christus sol geborn werdē/Sonder leren es auch dise Heiden/Sey er geborn/so werdē sie jhn zu Bethlehem finden/da sollen sie hin ziehen. Aber eben solches/das sie wissen/ [p. 45] vnd andere lehren/glaubē sie selb nit/das es war sey/sonst solten ye der König Herodes/die hohen Priester/vnd schrifftgelerten/entweder selb mit den weysen gehn Bethlehem gezogen sein/oder zū wenigsten ettliche diener mitgeschickt haben/die sachen zu erkundē. Aber sie thun es nicht/halten es fur ein fabel/gedencken so es war were/sie soltē es auch wissen/als die so im weltlichen vnnd geistlichen ambt die höchsten waren/es müste jnen ein ewige schand sein/das sie es nicht solten wissen/vnnd die Heiden solten es wissen. Dise/wie du sihest/heben an zufallen/durch hoffart vnd vnglauben/das sie das wort haben/wöllen aber jm nicht folgen noch glauben.

As holy Simeon, in the Gospel of the Sunday just past, prophesies of the little child that it is set for this, that some shall rise again by Him and some shall fall, so in today's Gospel we see two examples of that prophecy, which are very strange and almost wholly incredible. For there are, first, the Jews, who have the promise of Christ, that He is to be their Savior, their King and their Leader. They not only know where Christ is to be born, but also teach it to these heathen: if He is born, they will find Him at Bethlehem; thither they are to go. But this very thing that they know and teach to others, they themselves do not believe to be true. Otherwise King Herod, the chief priests and scribes would surely either have gone to Bethlehem with the wise men themselves, or at the least have sent some servants along to find out the matter. But they do not do it. They hold it for a fable, and think that if it were true they would know it too, as those who were highest in the secular and the spiritual office; it would have to be an everlasting shame to them that they should not know it, and the heathen should know it. These, as you see, begin to fall, through pride and unbelief, in that they have the word but will neither follow it nor believe it.

> Dagegen aber sind dise weysen noch Heiden/vnnd predigt jnen nicht/wie den hirtē auff dem felde ein engel/Es predigt jnen nicht Simeon noch Anna/frome/alte/warhaffte leutlin. Ein sum̃er [sic, for stum̃er] stern leß sich am himel sehen/auß dem selben lernen sie/auß eingebung des heiligen Geistes/wie der Juden [p. 46] König geborn sey/auff dises blosses gesicht machen sie sich auff/vnd ziehen disem König nach/einē so weiten weg/auß Persia biß mitten in Judeam/mit so grosser gefahr. Dise nu stehn an dē Herrn Christo auff/vnd glauben der göttlichen offenbarung. Da ist ferner wol zu mercken/wie der vnglaub/so zum fall sich angehoben/gestigen vnd ye lenger ye grösser worden/widerum̃ der glaub in den fromen Heiden auch gewachssen sey.

But over against them, these wise men are still heathen, and no angel preaches to them as to the shepherds in the field; neither Simeon nor Anna, godly, old, truthful folk, preaches to them. A mute star let itself be seen in the sky; from it they learn, by the inspiration of the Holy Ghost, that the King of the Jews is born. On the strength of this bare sight they set out and follow this King, so long a way, from Persia into the very midst of Judea, with such great danger. These now rise again by the Lord Christ, and believe the divine revelation. Here it is further to be well noted how the unbelief which had begun to fall rose and grew ever greater, and how, on the other hand, faith in the godly heathen grew as well.

> Denn da dise Heyden die offenbarung durch den stern annemen/vnnd dem kindlin Jesu nach zihen/ob sie es gleich zu Hierusalem nicht finden/so finden sie doch das zeugniß des Propheten Miche/vñ lernen drauß/das die Juden von disem könig ein gewisse verheissung haben/das er sol zu Bethlehem geborn werden. Darumb faren sie flugs fort auff Bethlehem zu/vnd wie sie nahe hinbey komen/leßt der stern sich wider sehen/da wurden sie sehr fro/vnnd erkennen aller erst das sie nicht jrren/noch vergebens vmb [p. 47] ziehen/darnach da sie zu Bethlehem komen/leßt der stern sich so gewaltig herunder/das er jnen auch die thür vñ das hause weyset/da Christus innē ware. Sihe also wo man anhebt recht zu glauben/wird der glaube jm̃er ye lenger ye gewisser/Vnd folget/das sie disen könig finden vnd sehen/vñ betten jn an/nicht als ein welt könig/deñ sie sehen wol/das kein welt pracht da war/vnd westen auß Daniele/der in jrem land sein weissagung geschriben hette/Er wurde ein solcher könig sein/der sünde vergeben/vñ ewige gerechtigkeit damit wurde bringen/das er sich selb wurde in den tod zum opffer gebē. Nach solchem anbetten/thun sie auch jre schetz auff/vnnd schencken jm gold/weyrauch/vnd myrrhen/Vñ ziehen als denn wider heim. Also sind nu dise Heyden am Herrn Christo aufferstanden/haben sich an jm nicht geergert/sind derhalb durch jn selig wordē.

For when these heathen accept the revelation through the star and follow after the child Jesus, although they do not find Him at Jerusalem, they nevertheless find the testimony of the prophet Micah, and learn from it that the Jews have a sure promise of this King, that He is to be born at Bethlehem. Therefore they journey on at once toward Bethlehem; and as they come near to it, the star shows itself again. Then they rejoiced greatly, and only now recognize that they are not going astray nor wandering about in vain. Afterward, when they come to Bethlehem, the star comes down so mightily that it shows them even the door and the house where Christ was. See, then: where one begins to believe rightly, faith grows ever more certain. And it follows that they find and see this King, and worship Him, not as a king of the world, for they see well enough that there was no worldly pomp there; and they knew from Daniel, who had written his prophecy in their land, that He would be a King who would forgive sins and bring everlasting righteousness, and would do so by giving Himself into death as a sacrifice. After such worship they also open their treasures, and give Him gold, frankincense, and myrrh, and then go home again. So these heathen have now risen again by the Lord Christ, have not been offended in Him, and have therefore been saved through Him.

> Aber Herodes vnd die Juden/bald sie anfangen Gotes wort zu verachtē/bleibt es nicht dabey/Herodes vñ das [p. 48] gantze Hierusalē mit jm/spricht Mattheus 1. [sic, for 2.] erschrecken/Warumb doch? ist doch das jr könig/der jn vergebūg der sünde vnd ewige seligkeit sol bringen? Wie deñ/das sie sich sein nicht frewen? Es ist den armen leuten darumb zu thun/das jn ein zeitlicher fride lieber ist/denn das ewige. Deñ sie gedencken es werde nu an ein rauffen gehn/der new könig werde das sein wöllen haben/so werde es Herodes nicht gestatten/da müsse es zum vnfrid geratten. Auff dise sünd/die da sehr schwer ist/folget ein andere/die ist noch grösser/dz sie gedencken diß kind vmbzubringen/weil es noch klein vnd vnuermöglich sey/Vñ sonderlich Herodes decket diß sein fürnemen/sagt die weysen sollen das kindlin suchen/so wölle er auch komen vnnd es anbetten. Aber er redet mit einer wolffstim̃/vnd nicht mit einer menschen stim̃/deñ anbetten heißt er mörden vnd würgen. Darumb feret er endtlich zu/da er sihet/das die weysen sein wolffssprach verstanden haben/vnd würget alle kindlin in/vñ [p. 49] vmb Bethlehem/die zwey jerig vnd drunder sind/vñ helffen jm die hohen priester dazu. Also sind sie fur vnd fur ye lenger ye tieffer in den fall geratē/vnd entlich in das ewige verdamniß kommen.

But with Herod and the Jews, as soon as they begin to despise God's word, it does not stop there. Herod, and all Jerusalem with him, says Matthew 2, are troubled. Why so? Is He not their King, who is to bring them forgiveness of sins and eternal salvation? How then is it that they do not rejoice in Him? The poor people's concern is that a temporal peace is dearer to them than the eternal one. For they think it will now come to blows: the new King will want to have what is His, Herod will not allow it, and so it must come to strife. Upon this sin, which is very grievous, follows another, which is greater still: that they think to kill this child while it is still small and helpless. And Herod in particular cloaks this design of his, saying that the wise men are to seek the little child, and he too will come and worship it. But he speaks with a wolf's voice and not with a man's voice; for to worship, with him, means to murder and to slay. Therefore, when he sees that the wise men have understood his wolf's language, he finally sets to work and slays all the little children in and around Bethlehem who are two years old and under, and the chief priests help him in it. So they have fallen continually, ever deeper, and at last come into everlasting damnation.

> Solches sollen wir lernen vnd vns fur dem fall hüten/vnd mit den fromen Heyden an Christo auffstehen. Deñ vns ist auß Gottes gnaden/der selige stern der auff Christum weyset/vnd zu jm füret/das heilige Euangelion/zu diser zeit auch erschinen/Den sollen wir nicht verachten/wie die vnglaubigē Juden/sonder jm nachgehn/vnd kein fahr fliehen noch mühe vnnd vnkost sparen/so werdē wir das kindlin Jesus sehen vnd finden/vnd es anbetten/das ist/vnser hoffnung vnnd trost auff jn setzen/er werde vns selig/vnd von sünden ledig machen.

This we are to learn, and guard ourselves against the fall, and with the godly heathen rise again by Christ. For by God's grace the blessed star that points to Christ and leads to Him, the holy gospel, has appeared to us also in this time. We are not to despise it, as the unbelieving Jews did, but follow it, and flee no danger and spare no labor and expense. So shall we see and find the child Jesus, and worship Him, that is, set our hope and comfort on Him, that He will save us and set us free from sins.

> Darnach weyl diß kindlin klein vñ arm ist/sollen wir vnsere schetze auch auffthun/das ist/wir sollen mit vnserm zeitlichen segen da zu helffen/das das kindlin möge erzogen werden vñ [p. 50] nicht allein arme leut vnser geniessen/sonder wir vnser gut gern dahin geben/das fleissige gelerte Kirchen diener/erzogen vnd erhalten werden.

Next, since this little child is small and poor, we too are to open our treasures; that is, with our temporal blessings we are to help the little child to be brought up, and not only let poor people have the benefit of us, but gladly give our goods to this end, that diligent, learned ministers of the Church may be trained and maintained.

> Ob es aber gleich nicht wird felen/wir werden mit disen Heyden auch etwas des worts halb müssen leiden/so will doch Gott bey vns sein/vnd fur dem Herode warnē/das wir jm nicht in die hende kom̃en. Solche lehr vnnd trost sollen wir auß dem heutigen Euangelio mercken/vnd an dem Herrn Christo mit den weysen aufferstehen zum ewigen leben/Amen.

And although it will not fail that we too, with these heathen, must suffer something for the word's sake, yet God will be with us, and warn us against Herod, so that we do not fall into his hands. Such doctrine and comfort we are to note from today's Gospel, and with the wise men rise again by the Lord Christ to eternal life. Amen.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 2:1–2 in King James wording. The heading's reference, "Luc. 2.", is a misprint (Appendix D).
- **Rising and falling.** Dietrich reads the Epiphany Gospel through Simeon's prophecy in the Gospel of the Sunday after Christmas (Luke 2:34, "set for the fall and rising again of many"). *an jm auffstehen* and *am Herrn Christo aufferstanden* are rendered "rise again by Him," after the King James "rising again."
- **"A mute star."** The print reads *sum̃er* (*summer*), which gives no sense; *stum̃er* ("mute, silent") is taken as intended. The star preaches no word, unlike the angel to the shepherds and Simeon and Anna in the Temple.
- **"Leader."** *Hertzog* (duke), as in Luther's Matthew 2:6, "aus dir sol mir komen der Hertzog" (King James "a Governor").
- **Micah and Daniel.** The testimony of Micah is Micah 5:2, quoted in Matthew 2:5–6. Dietrich's appeal to Daniel, "who had written his prophecy in their land," refers to Daniel 9:24–26 (to "make an end of sins … and to bring in everlasting righteousness," and the Anointed who "shall be cut off"); Daniel wrote in Babylon and Persia, whence Dietrich brings the wise men ("from Persia").
- **"They rejoiced greatly."** *da wurden sie sehr fro*: Dietrich here borrows the past tense of Matthew 2:10, "they rejoiced with exceeding great joy," within a passage otherwise in the present tense.
- **"Herod and all Jerusalem … are troubled."** Matthew 2:3. The print reads "Mattheus 1."
- **"Two years old and under."** Matthew 2:16 in King James wording.
- **"Wolf's voice."** The image is Dietrich's: Herod's "worship" (Matthew 2:8) means murder. Compare Matthew 7:15.
- **"Ministers of the Church."** *Kirchen diener*; as in Advent 4, they are to be "trained and maintained" (*erzogen vnd erhalten*), that is, educated and supported.
- **"Amen."** The summary itself ends with "Amen," before the prayer, as do those of the Third, Tenth, Twelfth and Twenty-fifth Sundays after Trinity and of Sts. Simon and Jude ([49], [56], [58], [71], [98]).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 50–51)**

> HErr Gott himlischer vatter/der du dein liebes wort den rechtē Stern/der das kindlin Jesus zeyget/vns hast lassen leuchten/wir bitten dich/gib deinen heiligen Geist in vnsere hertzen/das wir ja solches liecht annemen/vñ seligklich brauchen/das wir/wie die Weysen/solchem Stern nachziehen/kein mühe noch fahr vns lassen schrecken/sonder mit gantzē hertzen vns an deinen Sun Christum Jesum/als den eynigen Heyland halten/vnd vnnser zeitliches auch dazu wenden/das dein arme Christenheyt dadurch gebessert/vñ deinem Sun Christo Jesu gedienet werde/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who hast caused Thy dear word, the true star which showeth the child Jesus, to shine upon us: We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts, that we may indeed receive this light and make use of it unto our salvation; that we, like the wise men, may follow this star, and suffer no toil nor peril to affright us, but with our whole heart hold to Thy Son, Jesus Christ, as the only Savior, and turn our temporal goods also to this end, that Thy poor Christendom may thereby be bettered, and Thy Son, Jesus Christ, be served, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "who hast given us the light of Thy holy word, the guiding star, which leadeth us to the Christ-child" → "who hast caused Thy dear word, the true star which showeth the child Jesus, to shine upon us" (*der du dein liebes wort den rechtē Stern/der das kindlin Jesus zeyget/vns hast lassen leuchten*).
- "Send, we beseech Thee, Thy Holy Spirit into our hearts" → "We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts" (*gib deinen heiligen Geist in vnsere hertzen*; §2.2).
- "receive this light" → "indeed receive this light" (*ja*).
- "and that we, like the wise men, when they were seeking the star, may not be afraid because of any hardship or peril" → "that we, like the wise men, may follow this star, and suffer no toil nor peril to affright us" (*das wir/wie die Weysen/solchem Stern nachziehen/kein mühe noch fahr vns lassen schrecken*). In the German it is we who follow the star.
- "but put all our trust in Thine only-begotten Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, as our only Savior" → "but with our whole heart hold to Thy Son, Jesus Christ, as the only Savior" (*mit gantzē hertzen vns an deinen Sun Christum Jesum/als den eynigen Heyland halten*).
- "devote our earthly possessions to the advancement of Thy kingdom, and in all things serve Him, Thine only-begotten Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord," → "and turn our temporal goods also to this end, that Thy poor Christendom may thereby be bettered, and Thy Son, Jesus Christ, be served," (*vnd vnnser zeitliches auch dazu wenden/das dein arme Christenheyt dadurch gebessert/vñ deinem Sun Christo Jesu gedienet werde*). TLHy's conclusion is attached to the Son's name, as TLHy itself attaches it here.
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [13] First Sunday after Epiphany

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 51):** *Am ersten Sontag/nach Obersten/wie das Kindlin Jhesus zu Jerusalem bleybt/vnd Maria jn im Tempel findet/Luc. 2.* [On the First Sunday after Epiphany: how the Child Jesus stays behind in Jerusalem, and Mary finds Him in the Temple. Luke 2.]

**Verse (PDF p. 52)**

> VNd es begab sich nach dreyen tagen/funden Maria vnd Jpseph [sic, for Joseph] das Kindlin Jesus im Tempel sitzen/mitten vnter den Lerern/das er jnen zuhörete/vnd fragete.

And it came to pass, that after three days Mary and Joseph found the child Jesus in the temple, sitting in the midst of the doctors, both hearing them, and asking them questions.

**Summary (PDF pp. 52–53)**

> DAs ist ein sehr feines Exempel/welches junge leut mercken/vnd jm fleyssig in jhr jugend/vnd durch jr gantzes leben sollen folgē/Das sie/wie dz kindlin Jesus/da es zwölff Jar alt war/gern in der Kirche sein/Gottes wort hören/vnnd lernen sollen. Denn Gottes wort ist der eynige rechte Same/da Gottes forcht vnnd allerley tugend herwachssen/Vnd folget ferner/das Gott solchen Kindlin glück vnd segen geben/für kranckheyt vnnd allerley vnfall behütten/vnnd erhalten will/wie vom kindlin Jesu hie stehet: Er nam zu/an Weyßheyt/Alter/vnd gnad bey Gott vnd den menschē. Da dagegen die kinder/so der kirchen vnd Gottes wort nicht [p. 53] achten/rochloß vnnd frech werden/Menschen vnd Got wirt jnen feind/Vnd ist weder glück noch heyl bey jnē/wie die Exempel vor augen sind/Darumb lerne yederman sich fleyssig zu Gottes wort/vnd der kirche halten.

This is a very fine example, which young people are to note, and follow diligently in their youth and through their whole life: that, like the child Jesus when He was twelve years old, they are to be glad to be in church, and to hear and learn God's word. For God's word is the one true seed from which the fear of God and every kind of virtue grow. And it follows further that God will give such children good fortune and blessing, keep them from sickness and every kind of mishap, and preserve them, as is written here of the child Jesus: "He increased in wisdom and stature, and in favour with God and man." Whereas, on the other hand, children who care nothing for the church and God's word become reckless and insolent; men and God become their enemies, and there is neither good fortune nor well-being with them, as the examples are before our eyes. Therefore let everyone learn to keep diligently to God's word and to the church.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 2:46 in King James wording, with "Mary and Joseph" and "the child Jesus" from Dietrich's German. The print's *Jpseph* is a misprint for *Joseph*.
- **"He increased in wisdom and stature."** Luke 2:52 in King James wording; Luther's *Alter* is "age," for which the King James Version gives "stature."
- **"Good fortune."** *glück*, here and in the next sentence, is prosperity, things going well.
- **"Reckless."** *rochloß* (*ruchlos*), heedless, wicked.

**Assignment:** TLHy's First Sunday after Epiphany. TLHy's only collect for that day is not Dietrich's ("…who in mercy hast established the Christian home among us…"; Appendix A, [14]).

**Prayer (PDF p. 53)**

> HERR Got himlischer Vater/Jch dancke dir für deine gnad/das du zur seligen Tauff vñ zum erkentnuß deines göttlichē worts vnd willens mich hast lassen kommen/Jch bitte dich/gib deinen heyligen geyst in mein hertz/das ich mich deines worts fleyssige/es nicht versaume noch verachte/sonder wol mercke/vnnd es in mir die rechte frucht schaffe/das ich in deinem willen lebe/im glauben vnd der forcht Gottes zuneme vnd wachse/vnd endlich seligklich in deinem wort sterbe/vnnd selig werde/AMEN.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, I thank Thee for Thy grace, that Thou hast caused me to come to blessed baptism and to the knowledge of Thy divine word and will: I beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into my heart, that I may diligently apply myself to Thy word, neither neglect nor despise it, but mark it well, and that it may work in me the right fruit; that I may live in Thy will, increase and grow in faith and in the fear of God, and at last die blessedly in Thy word, and be saved; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Congregational form (TLHy practice)**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee for Thy grace, that Thou hast caused us to come to blessed baptism and to the knowledge of Thy divine word and will: We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts, that we may diligently apply ourselves to Thy word, neither neglect nor despise it, but mark it well, and that it may work in us the right fruit; that we may live in Thy will, increase and grow in faith and in the fear of God, and at last die blessedly in Thy word, and be saved; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Person and wording.** The German is in the first person singular. *mich hast lassen kommen* is rendered after TLHy's own "Thou hast caused us to come to the knowledge of Thy word" (Trinity 8). *sich … fleyssigen* (with genitive) means to apply oneself diligently to.

#### [15] Second Sunday after Epiphany

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 54):** *Am andern Sontag/wie vnser Herr Jesus mit seiner Muter/vnd seinen Jüngern auff einer Hochzeyt/zu Cana in Galilea gewesen ist/vñ da es an weyn gebrach/liesse wasser auß dem brunnen schöpffen/vnnd einschencken/da war es der beste weyn/Johannis am 2.* [On the Second Sunday: how our Lord Jesus was at a wedding at Cana in Galilee with His mother and His disciples, and, when the wine failed, had water drawn from the well and poured out, and it was the best wine. John 2.]

**Verse (PDF p. 54)**

> DAs ist das erste zeychen/das Jesus thet/vñ offenbaret seine herrligkeyt/vnd seine Jünger glaubten an jn.

This is the first sign which Jesus did, and manifested forth his glory; and his disciples believed on him.

**Summary (PDF pp. 55–56)**

> DAs vnser lieber Herr Christus auß wasser weyn machet/solches wunderwerck ist ein starcke beweysung/das er ein Herr vber die Creatur/vnd ewiger Gott sey/Denn etwas besser vnnd anders machen/denn es von Gott erschaffen ist/solches kan niemand denn Gott. Wir sollen aber solches wunderwercks vns trösten/auff das/wenn wir mangel an der narung/am gesind/oder anderm befinden/wir derhalb nit verzagen/sonder vns zum Herren Christo finden/mit der hoffnung/wie er hie auff der Hochzeyt den mangel gewendet/vnd auß wasser weyn gemachet hat/das er der gleichen mit vns auch thun könne vnnd wölle/sonderlich/wenn wir jn drumb bitten. Denn es kostet jn schlecht ein wort oder einen gedancken/so muß wasser wein werden/kranckheyt muß zur gesundheyt werden/Tod muß zum leben werden. Jn sonderheit aber/sollen die Eheleut den trost haben/wenn sie Gottselig [p. 56] vnd from̃ sind/vnd den Herren Christum/zu sich auff die Hochzeyt bitten/er werde gnedig allen mangel/vnd felh durch seinen segen wenden.

That our dear Lord Christ makes wine out of water: this miracle is a strong proof that He is Lord over the creature and eternal God. For to make something better and other than it was created by God, that no one can do but God. But we are to comfort ourselves with this miracle, so that, when we find ourselves in want of food, of servants, or of anything else, we do not despair on that account, but betake ourselves to the Lord Christ, in the hope that, as He here at the wedding turned away the want and made wine out of water, He can and will do the like with us also, especially when we ask Him for it. For it costs Him no more than a word or a thought, and water must become wine, sickness must become health, death must become life. But married people in particular are to have this comfort: if they are godly and upright, and invite the Lord Christ to their wedding, He will graciously turn away all want and lack by His blessing.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 2:11 in King James wording, without "in Cana of Galilee," which Dietrich omits. *zeychen* is "sign"; the King James Version has "This beginning of miracles."
- **"Servants."** *gesind* is the household staff, the servants and hired hands.
- **"No more than."** *schlecht* has its old sense, "merely, simply."
- **"Want and lack."** *mangel vnd felh*.
- **The prayer** that follows is headed "For those who are in the estate of marriage," and the summary's last sentence leads into it.

**Prayer (PDF p. 56).** Headed *Für die/so im Ehestand sind.* [For those who are in the estate of marriage.]

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/ich dancke dir/das du mir dein gnad geben/vnd mich in den heyligen Ehestand kommen lassen/vnd dadurch für sünden behütet hast/Vñ bitte dich/gib deinen heyligen Geyst/der mich also drinn leyte vñ füre/das ich mit meinem Ehegemahel eynig sein/kein vrsach zu widerwillen vnnd zorn geben/sonder ein freundliches/Gottseliges leben füren möge/Auff das ob gleich anfechtung nicht aussen wirt bleyben/wir dennoch jmmerdar dein gnedige hilff vnd segen spüren/vnsere kindlein nach deinem willē erziehen/vnd in aller Gottseligkeyt vnter einander leben/vnd endlich durch deinen Sun Christum Jhesum ewig selig werden/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, I thank Thee, that Thou hast given me Thy grace, and hast caused me to come into holy matrimony, and thereby hast kept me from sin: And I beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost, who may so lead and direct me therein, that I may be of one mind with my spouse, give no occasion for ill will and anger, but lead a friendly and godly life; that, although temptation will not stay away, we may nevertheless evermore perceive Thy gracious help and blessing, rear our little children in accordance with Thy will, and live together in all godliness, and at last obtain eternal salvation through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Congregational form (TLHy practice)**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee, that Thou hast given us Thy grace, and hast caused us to come into holy matrimony, and thereby hast kept us from sin: And we beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost, who may so lead and direct us therein, that we may be of one mind with our spouses, give no occasion for ill will and anger, but lead a friendly and godly life; that, although temptation will not stay away, we may nevertheless evermore perceive Thy gracious help and blessing, rear our little children in accordance with Thy will, and live together in all godliness, and at last obtain eternal salvation through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Rubric after the prayer (PDF p. 57)**

> Das junge gesind/so im Ehestand noch nicht ist/soll sein Gebet dahin richten/das Gott/wenn sie zun jaren kommen/glück vnnd segen zu disem stand geben/vnnd in des für vnzucht gnedig behüten wölle.

The young household folk who are not yet in the estate of marriage should direct their prayer to this: that God, when they come of age, would give good fortune and blessing for this estate, and meanwhile graciously keep them from unchastity.

**Changes from TLHy.** TLHy recasts this prayer freely, so most of it is changed.
- Person: Dietrich's prayer is spoken by a married person ("I," turning to "we" for the household) and headed "For those who are in the estate of marriage." TLHy makes it a general intercession for "every husband and wife." The revised text follows Dietrich, and a congregational form is supplied (§2.2).
- "that of Thy grace Thou hast instituted holy matrimony, in which Thou keepest us from unchastity, and other offenses" → "that Thou hast given me Thy grace, and hast caused me to come into holy matrimony, and thereby hast kept me from sin" (*das du mir dein gnad geben/vnd mich in den heyligen Ehestand kommen lassen/vnd dadurch für sünden behütet hast*).
- "We beseech Thee to send Thy blessing upon every husband and wife, that they may not provoke each other to anger and strife, but live peaceably together in love and godliness" → "And I beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost, who may so lead and direct me therein, that I may be of one mind with my spouse, give no occasion for ill will and anger, but lead a friendly and godly life" (*gib deinen heyligen Geyst/der mich also drinn leyte vñ füre/das ich mit meinem Ehegemahel eynig sein/kein vrsach zu widerwillen vnnd zorn geben/sonder ein freundliches/Gottseliges leben füren möge*).
- "receive Thy gracious help in all temptations" → "that, although temptation will not stay away, we may nevertheless evermore perceive Thy gracious help and blessing" (*Auff das ob gleich anfechtung nicht aussen wirt bleyben/wir dennoch jmmerdar dein gnedige hilff vnd segen spüren*).
- "and rear their children" → "rear our little children" (*vnsere kindlein*).
- "grant unto us all to walk before Thee, in purity and holiness, to put all our trust in Thee, and lead such lives on earth, that in the world to come we may have everlasting life" → "and live together in all godliness, and at last obtain eternal salvation through Thy Son, Jesus Christ" (*vnd in aller Gottseligkeyt vnter einander leben/vnd endlich durch deinen Sun Christum Jhesum ewig selig werden*). TLHy's "through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord" gives way to Dietrich's own close, which TLHy's conclusion continues (§2.4d).

#### [16] Third Sunday after Epiphany

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 57):** *Am dritten Sontag/wie Christus einen aussetzigen reyn/Vnd zu Capernaum des Haubtmans Knecht gesundt machet/Matthei am 8.* [On the Third Sunday: how Christ makes a leper clean, and at Capernaum makes the centurion's servant whole. Matthew 8.]

**Verse (PDF p. 58)**

> DER Aussetzige sprach: Herr so du wilt/kanst du mich wol reynigen/Vnnd Jesus strecket seine hand auß/vnd rüret jn an/vñ sprach: Jch wils thun/sey gereyniget/Vnnd alß bald war er von seinem Aussatz reyn.

The leper said, Lord, if thou wilt, thou canst make me clean. And Jesus put forth his hand, and touched him, and said, I will; be thou clean. And immediately he was clean of his leprosy.

**Summary (PDF pp. 58–59)**

> DJse zwey wunderzeychen/lehren erstlich das vnser lieber Herr Christus geren helffen will/vnd wol helffen kan/wider allerley leibliche not/sonderlich aber wider die sünde/auß welcher aller jammer herfleust. Doch so fern/das wir erstlich jn vmb hilff bitten/vñ zum andern/das wir an solcher hilff nicht zweyfflen/sonder im glauben fest vñ gewiß sind. Wie wir aber/wenn es zeytliche not belanget/betten sollen/lernen wir fein am Aussetzigen/der spricht: Herr so du wilt/kanst du mich wol reynigē/Denn ob wir wol keins weges sollen [p. 59] zweyffeln/es drucke vns für not was da wölle/Gott werde helffen/so sollen doch wir vnsern willen/in Gottes willen setzen/Vnnd so es Gott so haben wolt/das Creutz gedultig leyden/Solchen gehorsam sind wir Gott schuldig/vnnd Gott lest jm den selben sonderlich gefallen/Wir müssen aber auch mit dem Haubtman alle hoffnung/allein auff das wort vnsers Herrn Christi setzen/alß das allmechtig ist/vnnd auß nichte alles gemacht hat.

These two miracles teach, first, that our dear Lord Christ is glad to help and well able to help against every kind of bodily need, but above all against sin, out of which all misery flows. Yet only on these terms: first, that we ask Him for help, and secondly, that we do not doubt such help, but are firm and sure in faith. But how we are to pray where temporal need is concerned, we learn well from the leper, who says: "Lord, if thou wilt, thou canst make me clean." For although we are by no means to doubt that, whatever need may press upon us, God will help, yet we are to set our will in God's will, and, if God would have it so, bear the cross patiently. Such obedience we owe to God, and God is especially well pleased with it. But we must also, with the centurion, set all our hope on the word of our Lord Christ alone, as the word that is almighty and has made all things out of nothing.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 8:2–3 in King James wording, introduced by "The leper said." The second miracle of the heading, the centurion's servant (Matthew 8:5–13), is not quoted, but the summary closes with it.
- **"Glad to help."** *geren* is *gern*.
- **"Whatever need may press upon us."** *es drucke vns für not was da wölle*.
- **"Made all things out of nothing."** Compare Psalm 33:9 and Hebrews 11:3. The centurion trusts the bare word (Matthew 8:8, "speak the word only").

**Assignment:** TLHy's Third Sunday after Epiphany. TLHy's only collect for that day is not Dietrich's ("O almighty and everlasting God, mercifully look upon our infirmities…"; Appendix A, [17]).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 59–60)**

> HERR Gott himlischer Vater/der du auß sonderlicher lieb vnd barmhertzigkeyt vns deinen Sun geben/vnnd für vns mensch werden/vnnd am Creutz hast sterben lassen/Gib deinen heyligen Geyst in vnsere hertzen/das wir all vnser vertrawen auff jn setzen/vnd durch jn vergebung vnser sünde/vnnd ewiges leben ja so fest glauben alß der Hauptman hie glaubt/er werde sein krancken Knecht mit eim wort helffen. Wir haben ye sein wort/Wer an mich glaubt soll den todt nit sehen ewigklich/Gibe nur/lieber Vatter im himel/das wirs mit vnzweyffenlichem hertzen glaubē/vnd an vnserm letzten ende fest dabey bleyben. Amen.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who of especial love and mercy hast given us Thy Son, and hast suffered Him to become man for us and to die upon the cross: Give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts, that we may put all our trust in Him, and through Him believe the forgiveness of our sins and eternal life even as firmly as the centurion here believeth that He will help his sick servant with a word. We have indeed His word: "He that believeth on Me shall never see death." Only grant, dear Father in heaven, that we may believe it with undoubting hearts, and at our last end abide firmly thereby; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **The quotation.** The quoted word combines John 8:51 and John 11:26 and is given in King James idiom. *sein wort* is Christ's word, not the Father's.

#### [18] Fourth Sunday after Epiphany

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 60):** *Am vierden Sontag/wie der Herr Christus seine Jünger errettet/in eim grossen vngewitter auff dem Meer/Matth. 8.* [On the Fourth Sunday: how the Lord Christ rescues His disciples in a great storm on the sea. Matthew 8.]

**Verse (PDF p. 61).** The heading stands alone on p. 60; the woodcut heads p. 61.

> ES erhub sich ein groß vngestüm̃ auff dem Meer/Also das das schifflein mit wellen bedecket war/Jesus aber schlieff/vnd die jünger tratten zu jm/wecketen jn auff/vnnd sprachen: Herr hilff vns/wir verderben/Er aber sagt zu jhn: Jr kleinglaubigen warumb seydt jhr so forchtsam/Vnd er stund auff/vnnd bedrowete den wind/vnd das meer/da war es gantz still.

There arose a great tempest in the sea, insomuch that the ship was covered with the waves: but Jesus was asleep. And the disciples came to him, and awoke him, saying, Lord, help us: we perish. And he saith unto them, O ye of little faith, why are ye so fearful? Then he arose, and rebuked the wind and the sea; and there was a great calm.

**Summary (PDF p. 62)**

> DJß ist ein tröstliche Histori/da wir erstlich lernen/wer mit Christo ins Schif will/dae [sic, for das] ist/wer Christum kennen/vnd in seim Reych begert zu bleyben/der muß sich einer vngestümme/oder eines vngewitters erwegen/Wie Paulus sagt: Wer inn Christo will Gottselig leben/der muß verfolgung leyden. Da findets sich aber allweg/das Christus schlefft/das ist/er stellet sich/alß wisse er nicht wie es vns gehe/vnnd gedencke nicht an vns/Aber wir sollen/wie die Jüngern hie/zu Christo tretten/vnd mit ernstem gebet jn auffwecken/so will er mit seim allmechtigen wort Meer vnd wind stillen/Das sollen wir gentzlich glauben/Vnd derhalb/ob die hilff sich ein zeytlang verzüge/mit dem Gebet nicht ablassen/Denn es heyst/wer verharret biß ans ende/der wirt selig.

This is a comforting history, in which we learn, first, that whoever will go into the ship with Christ, that is, whoever desires to know Christ and to remain in His kingdom, must make up his mind to a tempest or a storm, as Paul says: "All that will live godly in Christ must suffer persecution." But then it always turns out that Christ is asleep; that is, He acts as though He did not know how it goes with us, and does not think of us. But we are to come to Christ, as the disciples do here, and wake Him with earnest prayer; then He will still the sea and the wind with His almighty word. This we are to believe wholly, and therefore, even if help should be delayed for a time, not leave off praying. For it is written: "He that endureth to the end shall be saved."

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 8:24–26 in King James wording. The disciples' cry is given as "Lord, help us" (*Herr hilff vns*, as in Luther's Bible; King James "Lord, save us"), because the summary takes up the word "help."
- **"Make up his mind to."** *sich … erwegen* (with genitive) is to resign oneself to, to reckon on.
- **Paul's word** is 2 Timothy 3:12, freely quoted; "He that endureth to the end" is Matthew 10:22 and 24:13.

**Prayer (PDF p. 63)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/der du auß vätterlichem vnnd gutem rath/vns deine Kinder hie auff erden/vnter das Creutz stellest/vnnd allerley vngewitter lest vber vns kommen/der sünde damit zu wehren/vnnd vns im glauben/hoffnung vnd gebet zu üben/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest dich vnser erbarmen/vnser gebet in aller anfechtung vnnd not hören/vnd gnedige hilff schaffen/auff das wir dein gnad vnd vätterlichen beystand erkennen/vnd dich mit all deinen heyligen in ewigkeyt loben vnnd preysen/der du mit deim Sun vnnd heyligen Geyst/allein der ewige almechtig Got bist/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who of fatherly and good counsel dost set us, Thy children, here on earth under the cross, and sufferest all manner of storms to come upon us, thereby to restrain sin, and to exercise us in faith, hope, and prayer: We beseech Thee to have mercy upon us, to hear our prayer in all temptation and need, and graciously to grant us help, so that we may perceive Thy grace and fatherly help, and with all Thy saints forever praise and glorify Thee, who with Thy Son and the Holy Ghost alone art the eternal, almighty God. Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "who in Thy divine wisdom and fatherly goodness" → "who of fatherly and good counsel" (*auß vätterlichem vnnd gutem rath*).
- "makest Thy children to bear the cross" → "dost set us, Thy children, here on earth under the cross" (*vns deine Kinder hie auff erden/vnter das Creutz stellest*).
- "and sendest divers afflictions upon us to subdue the flesh, and quicken our hearts unto faith, hope and unceasing prayer" → "and sufferest all manner of storms to come upon us, thereby to restrain sin, and to exercise us in faith, hope, and prayer" (*allerley vngewitter lest vber vns kommen/der sünde damit zu wehren/vnnd vns im glauben/hoffnung vnd gebet zu üben*). "Storms" keeps Dietrich's link to the Gospel of the day (Matthew 8:23–27).
- Restored "to hear our prayer in all temptation and need" (*vnser gebet in aller anfechtung vnnd not hören*), which TLHy omits.
- "and graciously deliver us out of our trials and afflictions" → "and graciously to grant us help" (*vnd gnedige hilff schaffen*).
- "and with all saints forever praise and worship Thee" → "and with all Thy saints forever praise and glorify Thee" (*mit all deinen heyligen ... loben vnnd preysen*).
- TLHy's "through Thy dear Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end" → Dietrich's own Trinitarian close, "who with Thy Son and the Holy Ghost alone art the eternal, almighty God" (*der du mit deim Sun vnnd heyligen Geyst/allein der ewige almechtig Got bist*). No TLHy ending is added, as at [86]/[87] (§2.4g).

#### [19] Fifth Sunday after Epiphany

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 64):** *Am fünften Sontag/vom Feynd/der vnkraut in den guten acker seet/weyl die leut schlieffen/Matthei am 13.* [On the Fifth Sunday: of the enemy who sows tares in the good field while the people slept. Matthew 13.]

**Verse (PDF p. 64)**

> DAs Himelreych ist gleych einem menschen/der guten samen auff seinen acker seet/Da aber die leute schlieffen/kam sein feynd/vnd seete vnkraut zwischen den Weytzen/vnd gieng dauon.

The kingdom of heaven is likened unto a man which soweth good seed in his field: but while men slept, his enemy came and sowed tares among the wheat, and went his way.

**Summary (PDF pp. 65–66)**

> DJß Euangelion ist ein nötige lehr wider das gemeyne ergernuß/das auch vnter dem heufflein/welchs Christen heyst/so vil ergernuß vnnd böser buben sind. Denn die welt/wenn sie es sihet/kan sie anders nicht vrtheylen/denn solcher vnrath kome auß der lehr her/Sie gedenckt/wenn die lehr reyn vnnd gut were/so solten die leut wol frömmer sein/Aber vnser lieber Herre Christus straffet solches vrtheyl/vnnd sagt: Das wort Gottes vnnd Christliche lehr/sey ein recht guter Same/da anders nichts denn alles gutes könne außwachsen. Das aber in dem acker/da diser gute same geseet ist/vnkraut wechst/vnnd vnter dem heufflin/so Gottes wort reyn vnnd lautter hat/vil böser buben sind/solches spricht er/ist des feyndes/des Sathans schuld/der lauret/wie ein böser Bawer/vnd wenn die leut schlaffen/seet er vnkraut in den guten acker. Darumb soll yedermand wacker vnd mundter sein/vnd fleyssig [p. 66] auff Gottes wort achtung haben/vnnd für dem feind sich hütten. Die aber schlaffen/vnd sich in ergernuß lassen füren/ob wol die selben hie auff erden der straff entlauffen/müssen sie doch am Jüngsten tag herhalten/vnnd ewiges vrtheyl darumb tragen.

This Gospel is a necessary teaching against the common offense, that even in the little flock that bears the name of Christians there are so many offenses and so many wicked knaves. For when the world sees it, it can judge no otherwise than that such mischief comes from the doctrine. It thinks: if the doctrine were pure and good, people would surely be more godly. But our dear Lord Christ rebukes such a judgment, and says that the word of God and Christian doctrine is a truly good seed, from which nothing can grow but what is good. But that tares grow in the field where this good seed is sown, and that there are many wicked knaves in the little flock that has God's word pure and clear, this, He says, is the fault of the enemy, of Satan, who lies in wait like a wicked husbandman, and when people sleep, sows tares in the good field. Therefore everyone is to be watchful and wakeful, and diligently give heed to God's word, and guard himself against the enemy. But those who sleep and let themselves be led into offense, although they may escape punishment here on earth, must nevertheless answer for it on the Last Day, and bear eternal judgment for it.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 13:24–25 in King James wording. *vnkraut* (weeds) is the King James "tares."
- **"Knaves."** *böser buben*, wicked fellows, scoundrels.
- **"Answer for it."** *herhalten*, to be made to pay.

**Prayer (PDF p. 66)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/wir dancken dir/das du den guten Samen dein heyliges wort inn vnsere hertzen hast außgeseet/vñ bitten dich/du wöllest durch dein heyligen Geyst/solchen samen in vns lassen lebendig werden/vnd frucht bringen/vnd für dem bösen feind vns behüten/das der nicht vnkraut mit ein see/Behüt für sicherheyt/vnd erhalt in deiner forcht/vnd hilff in aller anfechtung/biß wir ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee, that Thou hast sown the good seed, Thy holy word, in our hearts: We pray Thee that by Thy Holy Ghost Thou wilt cause this seed to become living in us and bring forth fruit, and defend us from the evil enemy, that he may not sow tares therein. Keep us from carnal security, preserve us in Thy fear, and help us in all temptations, until we obtain eternal salvation; *through Thy beloved Son, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "cause this seed to grow" → "cause this seed to become living in us" (*solchen samen in vns lassen lebendig werden*).
- "from the enemy" → "from the evil enemy" (*für dem bösen feind*).
- Restored "preserve us in Thy fear" (*vnd erhalt in deiner forcht*), which TLHy omits.
- "and give us at last eternal salvation" → "until we obtain eternal salvation" (*biß wir ewig selig werden*).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

### Pre-Lent

#### [21] Septuagesima

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 67):** *Das Gleychnuß von den arbeytern im Weinberg/die vngleich arbeyten/vnnd doch gleichen lohn empfangen/Matth. 20.* [The parable of the labourers in the vineyard, who labour unequally and yet receive equal pay. Matthew 20.] The heading names no Sunday.

**Verse (PDF p. 67)**

> ALso werden die letzten die ersten/vnnd die ersten die letzten. Denn vil sindt beruffen/aber wenig außerwelet.

So the last shall be first, and the first last: for many be called, but few chosen.

**Summary (PDF pp. 68–69)**

> VNser lieber Herr Christus vergleichet das himelreich oder die Christliche Kirch einem Weyngarten/da etliche vil/etliche wenig stunde jnne arbeyten/vnd doch gleychen lohn bekommen/Denn ob wol alle Christen/im Weinberg/ein yeder seinem beruff nach/arbeyten sollen/vnd keins wegs müssig gehen/so wil doch Got mit vns nicht nach vnserm verdienst noch arbeyt/sonder nach seiner gnaden handelen. Aber da finden sich die Juden/welche das Gesetz/vnnd einen geordneten Gottesdienst hetten/die wöllen einen vorteyl vor den Heyden haben/vnd verdreust sie/das Gott gnedig ist/vñ die Heyden halten will wie sie/Geschicht derhalb/da sie hetten sonst können die ersten sein/wo sie trewlich gearbeytet/vnd an Gottes gnad sich gehalten hetten/das sie die letzten werden/vnnd in Gottes tieffe vngnad fallen. Denn Gott will nach gnaden mit vns menschen handelen/vnnd nicht nach verdienst/Solches [p. 69] sollen wir zu höchstem danck annemē/vnd dennoch darumb nicht müssig gehen/sonder vns in Gottes gehorsam/vnnd willen nach bestem vermögen vben/vnd doch allein auff sein genad sehen/vnd der selben vns trösten.

Our dear Lord Christ likens the kingdom of heaven, or the Christian Church, to a vineyard, in which some work many hours and some few, and yet receive the same wage. For although all Christians in the vineyard are to work, each according to his calling, and are by no means to stand idle, yet God will deal with us not according to our merit or our work, but according to His grace. But here the Jews come forward, who had the law and an ordered worship of God. They want to have an advantage over the heathen, and it vexes them that God is gracious and will treat the heathen as He treats them. So it comes about that, whereas they could otherwise have been the first, had they worked faithfully and held to God's grace, they become the last, and fall into God's deep displeasure. For God will deal with us men according to grace and not according to merit. This we are to receive with the highest thanks, and yet not be idle on that account, but exercise ourselves in obedience to God and in His will to the best of our ability, and nevertheless look to His grace alone, and comfort ourselves with it.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 20:16 in King James wording.
- **The heading** names no Sunday (Appendix D); the Gospel is that of Septuagesima.
- **"Worship of God."** *Gottesdienst*, the ordered service of God under the law.
- **"Displeasure."** *vngnad*, the opposite of *gnad*.

**Prayer (PDF p. 69)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du durch dein liebes wort/vns in dein weingarten beruffen hast/Wir bitten dich/gib deinen heyligen Geist in vnser hertz/das wir in solchē weingarten trewlich arbeyten/für sünden vnd allem ergernuß vns hüten/vnd deines worts vnd willens vns fleyssigen/vnnd alle hoffnung allein auff dein gnad/so du vns durch deinen Son Christum Jesum reychlich erzeyget hast/setzen/vnd also ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who through Thy dear word hast called us into Thy vineyard: We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts, that we may labor faithfully in Thy vineyard, shun sin and all offense, diligently apply ourselves to Thy word and will, and set all our hope on Thy grace alone, which Thou hast bestowed upon us so plenteously through Thy Son Jesus Christ, and so obtain eternal salvation *through Him, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "Thy holy word" → "Thy dear word" (*dein liebes wort*; §2.2).
- "Send, we beseech Thee, Thy Holy Spirit into our hearts" → "We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts" (*gib deinen heyligen Geist in vnser hertz*; §2.2).
- "obediently keep Thy word and do Thy will" → "diligently apply ourselves to Thy word and will" (*deines worts vnd willens vns fleyssigen*).
- "put our whole and only trust in Thy grace" → "set all our hope on Thy grace alone" (*alle hoffnung allein auff dein gnad ... setzen*).
- "that we may obtain eternal salvation" → "and so obtain eternal salvation" (*vnd also ewig selig werden*). TLHy's "through Him" is part of its conclusion and is italicized with it.

#### [22] Sexagesima

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 70):** *Am Sontag Sexagesima/vom Samen der eins theyls an weg/ein theyls auff das felsicht/vnd eins teyls vnter die dorn fellet/Luce am 8.* [On Sexagesima Sunday: of the seed, which falls partly by the way, partly upon the rock, and partly among the thorns. Luke 8.]

**Verse (PDF p. 70)**

> DEr Same auff dem guten lande/sind die das wort hören/vnd behalten inn einem feinen gutten hertzen/vnnd bringen frucht inn gedult.

The seed on the good ground are they which hear the word, and keep it in an honest and good heart, and bring forth fruit with patience.

**Summary (PDF p. 71)**

> MJt disem gleichnuß/vom Same zeigt der Herr an/was die vrsach sey/dz Gottes wort nicht allenthalb frucht bringt/Nemlich/dz der mangel nit am wort/sonder an dē hertzen/vñ darnach am Sathan sey/Etliche habē leichtfertige vnachsame [sic, for vnachtsame] hertzen gegen das wort/den mag der Teuffel leicht etwas in weg werffen/so ist der predigt gar vergessen/Etliche sind blöd vñ zart/wöllen nichts vmb des worts willen leyden/noch wagen/Etliche lassen jnen gelt vnd gut so sehr lieben/das sie des worts nit wartē mögen/darumb bleibt die frucht bey solchen hertzen aussen. Aber wo die hertzē fein achtsam sind/vnnd Gottes wort fleissig hören/vñ gewenen sich zur gedult/das sie etwas leyden/vnd lassen die weltsorg sich nit vberwachsen/noch das hertz jnen gar einnemen/da wirt die rechte frucht/Nemlich/glaub vnd lieb/vñ darnach das ewige leben folgen. Darum̃ lerne ein yeder Christ sich also gegen das wort schicken.

With this parable of the seed the Lord shows what the reason is that God's word does not bring forth fruit everywhere: namely, that the fault lies not in the word, but in the hearts, and then in Satan. Some have frivolous, heedless hearts toward the word; for them the devil can easily throw something in the way, and the sermon is quite forgotten. Some are faint-hearted and soft; they will suffer nothing for the word's sake, nor venture anything. Some let money and goods be so dear to them that they cannot attend to the word. Therefore the fruit fails to appear in such hearts. But where hearts are properly heedful, and hear God's word diligently, and accustom themselves to patience, so that they suffer something, and do not let the cares of the world grow up over them nor take their heart wholly captive, there the right fruit will follow, namely faith and love, and after that eternal life. Therefore let every Christian learn to conduct himself so toward the word.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 8:15 in King James wording, introduced by "The seed on the good ground"; "an honest and good heart" renders *einem feinen gutten hertzen*, as in the collect.
- **"Heedless."** The print's *vnachsame* is taken as a misprint for *vnachtsame*; the contrast is *achtsam* ("heedful") below.
- **The four kinds of ground.** Wayside, rock and thorns (Luke 8:12–14) are the frivolous, the faint-hearted and the lovers of money; *weltsorg* ("cares of the world") echoes the thorns.
- **"Fails to appear."** *bleibt … aussen*, literally "stays outside," stays away.

**Prayer (PDF p. 72)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/wir dancken dir/das du dein liebes wort/durch deinen Son Christum Jesum vnter vns hast außgeseet/Vnnd bitten dich/du wöllest durch dein heyligen Geyst vnsere hertzen dermassen zubereyten/das wir solches wort mit fleyß hören/vnd in eim feinen guten hertzen behalten/vñ in gedult frucht bringen/das wir der sünde nit nachhengen/sie durch dein hilff dempffen/vnnd inn allerley verfolgung deiner gnad/vnd ewigen hilffe/vns gewiß trösten vñ hoffen/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee, that through Thy Son Jesus Christ Thou hast sown Thy dear word among us: We pray that Thou wilt so prepare our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may diligently hear Thy word, keep it in an honest and good heart, and bring forth fruit with patience; and that we may not incline to sin, but subdue it by Thy help, and in all manner of persecution comfort ourselves surely with Thy grace and everlasting help, and hope therein; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, ever one God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "Thy holy word" → "Thy dear word" (*dein liebes wort*; §2.2).
- "prepare our hearts" → "so prepare our hearts" (*dermassen*).
- "diligently and reverently hear" → "diligently hear" (*mit fleyß hören*; "reverently" is not in the German).
- "keep it in good hearts" → "keep it in an honest and good heart" (*in eim feinen guten hertzen*; King James, Luke 8:15).
- "subdue it by Thy power" → "subdue it by Thy help" (*durch dein hilff*).
- "and in all persecutions comfort ourselves with Thy grace and continual help" → "and in all manner of persecution comfort ourselves surely with Thy grace and everlasting help, and hope therein" (*deiner gnad/vnd ewigen hilffe/vns gewiß trösten vñ hoffen*).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."
- TLHy's conclusion reads "ever one God" here; it is kept as TLHy prints it.

#### [23] Quinquagesima

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 73):** *Am Faßnacht Sontag/wie Christus von seim leyden weyssaget/vnd einen Blinden sehen macht/Luce am 18.* [On Shrovetide Sunday: how Christ foretells His suffering and makes a blind man see. Luke 18.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 73–74)**

> VNser Herr Christus sprach: Des menschen Son wirdt vberantwortet werden den Heyden/Vnd er wirdt verspottet/vnnd geschmehet/vñ verspeyet werden/vnd sie werden jhn geysseln vnd tödten/ [p. 74] vnd am dritten tag/wirdt er wider aufferstehen/Aber seine Jünger vernamen der keines.

Our Lord Christ said: The Son of man shall be delivered unto the Gentiles, and shall be mocked, and spitefully entreated, and spitted on: and they shall scourge him, and put him to death: and the third day he shall rise again. And his disciples understood none of these things.

**Summary (PDF pp. 74–75)**

> WArumb vnser lieber Herr Christus solches hab müssen leyden/wissen wir/Denn er hat darumb sich angenommen/vñ ist darumb mensch worden/das er für aller welt sünde hat bezalen wöllen. Darumb sollen wir erstlich vns hie erinnern/wie ein grewliche last es vmb die sünde sey/vnd die selben mit höchstem fleiß meyden/sintemal niemand denn der Son Gottes/durch so ein hefftige marter/vnd hefftigen todt dafür hat bezalen können. Zum andern/sollen wir dises leydens vnd sterbens/vns auch wider die sünde trösten/vnd nit zweyfflen/ob gleych wir den ewigen todt/durch die sünde verwircket haben/das doch Gott vmb seines Sones willen gnedig sein/vnd die schuld an vns ferner nicht wölle fordern/so durch Christum [p. 75] bezalet ist. Zum dritten/sollen wir vns auch drein ergeben/vnnd nicht dran ergern/ob wir auch müssen mit leyden vnd mit sterben/Denn der Jünger ist nit vber seinen Meyster/noch der Knecht vber seinen Herrn. Solches wustē die Jüngern dazumal nit/aber hernach lerneten sie es.

Why our dear Lord Christ had to suffer this, we know. For He took it upon Himself, and became man, for this very reason, that He would pay for the sin of all the world. Therefore we are, first, to consider here what a dreadful burden sin is, and to shun it with the greatest diligence, since no one but the Son of God could pay for it, through so grievous a torment and so grievous a death. Secondly, we are also to comfort ourselves with this suffering and dying against sin, and not doubt that, although we have deserved eternal death by sin, God will nevertheless be gracious for His Son's sake, and will no more require of us the debt that has been paid by Christ. Thirdly, we are also to resign ourselves to it, and not be offended, if we too must suffer and die with Him; for the disciple is not above his master, nor the servant above his lord. This the disciples did not know at that time, but afterward they learned it.

> Das Christus den blinden sehend machet/merck das wort: Dein glaub hat dir geholffen/Denn wer vmb hilf den Herrn Christum anschreyet/vnd glaubt/dem soll hilff widerfaren.

That Christ makes the blind man see: mark the word, "Thy faith hath helped thee." For whoever cries to the Lord Christ for help, and believes, to him help shall come.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 18:32–34 in King James wording, introduced by "Our Lord Christ said."
- **"Deserved eternal death."** *verwircken* here is to bring a penalty on oneself by one's deeds.
- **"The disciple is not above his master."** Matthew 10:24 in King James wording.
- **"Thy faith hath helped thee."** Luke 18:42, where the King James Version has "hath saved thee." Luther's *geholffen* is kept, because Dietrich's point is the help (*hilff*) that comes to faith.
- **The paragraph break** before the last sentence follows the print.

**Assignment:** Quinquagesima (Luke 18:31–43). It replaces the baptism collect [10] that TLHy prints on this Sunday.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 75–76)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du dem Blinden menschen/durch deinen Son Christum Jesum gnedigklich die augen geöffnet/vnd das liecht hast sehen lassen/Wir armen sünder bitten dich/du völlest [sic, for wöllest] durch dein liebes wort vnsere blinde hertzen erleuchten/das wir dich durch Christum deinen Son/der für vnns am Creutz gestorben/vnd für vnsere sünde bezalet hat/recht lernē erkennen/vnd in allen nöten vnd anfechtungen allein auff dein gnedige hilff vñ barmhertzigkeyt sehen/die selbe durch ein glaubiges gebet suchen/vnd also trost vnd rettung wider Teuffel/sünd/vñ todt finden/Amen.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, didst graciously open the eyes of the blind man, and didst suffer him to see the light: We poor sinners beseech Thee, enlighten our blind hearts by Thy dear word, that through Christ, Thy Son, who died for us upon the cross and paid for our sins, we may rightly learn to know Thee; and that in all necessities and temptations we may look only to Thy gracious help and mercy, seek the same by believing prayer, and so find comfort and deliverance against the devil, sin, and death; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Rendering choices.** *dein liebes wort* is TLHy's "Thy holy word" elsewhere, given literally here (§2.2). *rettung* means rescue or deliverance.
