### Lent and Holy Week

#### [25] First Sunday in Lent (Invocavit)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 76):** *Am ersten Sontag in der Fasten/wie Christus in der Wüsten vom Teuffel versucht wirt. Ma[tth]. 4. [partly blotted]* [On the First Sunday in Lent: how Christ is tempted by the devil in the wilderness. Matthew 4.]

**Verse (PDF p. 77)**

> DEr versucher trat zum Herren Jesu/vnd sprach: Bist du Gottes Son/so sprich/das dise stein brod wreden [sic, for werden]. Aber er antwortet/vñ sprach: Es stehet geschriben/der mensche lebt nicht allein vom brod/sonder von eim jegklichen wort/das durch den mund Gottes gehet.

The tempter came to the Lord Jesus, and said, If thou be the Son of God, command that these stones be made bread. But he answered and said, It is written, Man shall not live by bread alone, but by every word that proceedeth out of the mouth of God.

**Summary (PDF pp. 77–79)**

> DJse Histori/wie Christus vom Teuffel versucht sey/ist darumb geschriben/das erstlich wir gewarnet würden/vnd für sicherheyt vns hüteten/Denn so der Sathan an dem Son Gottes selb sich hat dürffen versuchen/wirdt er vnser nicht schonen/das ist gewiß. Zum andern/das wir auch lerneten/wie allein durch Gottes wort/wir vns wider den Sathan weren vñ auffhalten können. Nun sinds aber dise anfechtung/damit der Sathan die Christen gemenigklich plaget. Die erst/wenn es jnen vbel geht/ [p. 78] treybt er dahin man soll verzweyffelen/Gott hab vnser vergessen. Aber Christus lehret/wir sollen Gottes genad nicht nach dem vrtheylen/wie es vns hie auff erden gehet/sonder nach seim wort/vnnd wie er vnns zusaget durch Christum/Denn allein im wort Gottes stehet das rechte leben/vnnd sonst in nichte/es heisse brod/gelt/gut/ehr ꝛc. Die ander anfechtung ist/wenn der Sathan mit verzweyfflung den glauben nicht kan dempffen/versucht ers/ob er vns vermessen köñ machen/das wir mer glauben vnd wagen deñ Gott befolhen hat/Solchs saget der Herr hie/heysse Gott versuchen/vnd sey vnrecht. Die dritte/das er durch gelt/gut/ehr/vnd anders die hertzen vom wort auff Abgötterey abfüret. Aber da sollen wir wissen/das wir nit dem gelt/noch der welt/sonder allein Gott dienen/vnd jhn allein anbeten sollen. Wer also mit dem wort wider des Sathans anfechtung sich wehret/der siget/vnd muß der Sathan ablassen. Ja die lieben Engel werden kommen/ [p. 79] retten/trösten/vnd dienen wo nit leyblich/wie zu Christo/doch geystlich vnd durchs wort.

This history of how Christ was tempted by the devil is written, first, so that we should be warned and guard ourselves against carnal security; for if Satan dared to try his hand on the Son of God Himself, he will not spare us, that is certain. Secondly, so that we should also learn how we can defend ourselves against Satan and withstand him by God's word alone. Now these are the temptations with which Satan commonly plagues Christians. The first: when things go ill with them, he drives them to despair, as though God had forgotten us. But Christ teaches that we are not to judge God's grace by how it goes with us here on earth, but by His word, and by what He promises us through Christ; for in the word of God alone stands the true life, and in nothing else, whether it be called bread, money, goods, honor, etc. The second temptation is this: when Satan cannot quench faith with despair, he tries whether he can make us presumptuous, so that we believe and venture more than God has commanded. This, the Lord says here, is called tempting God, and is wrong. The third: that he leads hearts away from the word into idolatry by money, goods, honor and other things. But here we are to know that we are to serve neither money nor the world, but God alone, and worship Him alone. Whoever thus defends himself with the word against Satan's temptation conquers, and Satan must leave off. Yes, the dear angels will come, rescue, comfort, and minister, if not bodily, as to Christ, then spiritually and through the word.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 4:3–4 in King James wording, introduced by "came to the Lord Jesus." The print's *wreden* is a misprint for *werden*.
- **The three temptations** follow Matthew 4:3–10: bread (despair of God's care), the pinnacle of the Temple (presumption, "tempting God," 4:7), and the kingdoms of the world (idolatry, "him only shalt thou serve," 4:10). The angels who "came and ministered unto him" are 4:11.
- **"Try his hand on."** *sich … versuchen an*, to make an attempt on someone.
- **"Presumptuous."** *vermessen*, rashly overconfident.

**Prayer (PDF p. 79)**

> HERR Gott himlischer Vater/wir bitten dich/weil der arge feind/für vnd für vns nachschleichet/vnnd wie ein brüllender Löwe vmbher gehet/vnd suchet/ob er vns köndte fressen/Du wöllest vmb deines Sones Christi Jesu willen/mit dem heyligen Geyst zu vns setzen/vnnd durch dein wort vnsere hertzen stercken/auff das der feinde vns nicht obsiege/vnnd wir in deiner gnade für vnnd für bleyben/vnd endlich selig werden/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, inasmuch as the evil enemy doth continually creep after us, and as a roaring lion doth walk about, seeking to devour us: We beseech Thee for the sake of Thy Son, Jesus Christ, to stand by us with the Holy Ghost, and to strengthen our hearts by Thy word, that our enemy may not prevail over us, but that we may evermore abide in Thy grace, and at last be saved; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "the adversary doth continually afflict us" → "the evil enemy doth continually creep after us" (*der arge feind/für vnd für vns nachschleichet*).
- "for the sake of the suffering and death of Thy Son" → "for the sake of Thy Son" (*vmb deines Sones Christi Jesu willen*; "the suffering and death" is not in the German).
- "to help us by the grace of the Holy Spirit" → "to stand by us with the Holy Ghost" (*mit dem heyligen Geyst zu vns setzen*, literally "set [Thyself] by us").
- "and be preserved unto everlasting life" → "and at last be saved" (*vnd endlich selig werden*).

#### [26] Second Sunday in Lent (Reminiscere)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 80):** *Am anderen Sontag inn der Fasten/vom dem [sic, for von dem] Heydnischen Frewlin/des Dochter besessen war/Matthei am 15.* [On the Second Sunday in Lent: of the heathen woman whose daughter was possessed. Matthew 15.]

**Verse (PDF p. 80)**

> VNser Herr Jesus sprach zu dem Heydnischen Freulin/O Weyb dein glaub ist groß/dir geschehe wie du wilt/vnd jr Dochter war gesund zur selbigen stund.

Our Lord Jesus said unto the heathen woman, O woman, great is thy faith: be it unto thee even as thou wilt. And her daughter was made whole from that very hour.

**Summary (PDF pp. 81–82)**

> DAs ist ein sehr fein Exempel/wie wir im Gebet anhalten/vnnd ymmer auff dem gedancken fest bleyben sollen/Gott sey gnedig/er werde alß ein Vatter sich vber vns/seine kindlein erbarmen/vnnd in allen nötten helffen. Denn wie wir hie am Herren Christo sehen/das er sich lang lest bitten/vnd stellet sich alß wölle er nicht helffen/Also widerferets vns auch/das vns gedunckt/es sey vmb sonst/Gott wölle nicht hören/vnd hindern vns sonderlich vnsere sünd/vnd das wir wissen/wir haben es vmb Got nit verdienet. Aber da können wir mehr nicht/sollen auch mehr nicht thun/denn das wir vns mit disem Heydnischen Freulin von hertzen demütigen/vnd sprechen: Es ist doch ye war/das ich ein sünder/vnd nicht werdt byn/das Gott das oder yenes mir gebe/Vnd dennoch weyl Gott gnedig vnd barmhertzig ist/soll ich an seiner genad vnd güte nicht zweyfflen. Das es also bedes beysam̃ stehe/das wir vnser [p. 82] sünd halb für Gott vnns demütigen/vnd vmb seiner barmhertzigkeyt willen/die er in Christo Jesu vns bewisen hat/glauben/er werde vns nit verlassen. Diser glaub erlanget alles/wie wir hie sehen/da Christus spricht: Dein glaub ist groß/dir geschehe wie du wilt.

This is a very fine example of how we are to persevere in prayer, and ever hold fast to the thought that God is gracious, that He will have mercy on us, His little children, as a father, and help in every need. For as we see here in the Lord Christ, that He lets Himself be asked for a long time and acts as though He would not help, so it befalls us also: it seems to us that all is in vain and God will not hear; and above all our sins hinder us, and our knowing that we have not deserved it of God. But there we can do no more, and are to do no more, than humble ourselves from our hearts with this heathen woman, and say: "It is indeed true that I am a sinner, and not worthy that God should give me this or that; and yet, since God is gracious and merciful, I am not to doubt His grace and goodness." So both stand together: that for our sin we humble ourselves before God, and for the sake of His mercy, which He has shown us in Christ Jesus, believe that He will not forsake us. This faith obtains all things, as we see here, where Christ says: "Great is thy faith: be it unto thee even as thou wilt."

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 15:28 in King James wording, introduced by "Our Lord Jesus said unto the heathen woman." *Freulin* (*Fräulein*, little woman) is Dietrich's word for the Canaanite woman, as in the heading (*Frewlin*); the prayer calls her *das Cananeisch Weyblin*.
- **"Have deserved it of God."** *vmb Got … verdienet*, to have earned something from God.

**Prayer (PDF p. 82)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/wir biten dich/du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst/den glauben vnnd hoffnung auff dein gnad vnnd barmhertzigkeyt in vnserm hertzen fest vñ gewiß machen/auff das/ob wir gleich vnsers gewissens/sünde/vnd vnwirdigkeyt halb vrsach haben vnns zu förchten/wir dennoch an deiner genade/wie das Cananeisch Weyblin/fest hangen bleyben/vnd durch Christum hilff vnnd rettung in allerley anfechtung vnd nöten bey dir finden/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we beseech Thee, by Thy Holy Ghost make faith and hope in Thy grace and mercy firm and sure in our hearts, so that, although we have reason to fear because of our conscience, our sin, and our unworthiness, we may nevertheless, like the woman of Canaan, hold fast to Thy grace, and in all manner of temptation and need find help and deliverance with Thee through Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "grant us, we beseech Thee, by Thy Holy Spirit, that He may strengthen our hearts and confirm our faith and hope in Thy grace and mercy" → "we beseech Thee, by Thy Holy Ghost make faith and hope in Thy grace and mercy firm and sure in our hearts" (*du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst/den glauben vnnd hoffnung auff dein gnad vnnd barmhertzigkeyt in vnserm hertzen fest vñ gewiß machen*).
- "with the woman of Canaan" → "like the woman of Canaan" (*wie das Cananeisch Weyblin*).
- "and in every trial and temptation find Thee a very present help and refuge" → "and in all manner of temptation and need find help and deliverance with Thee through Christ" (*vnd durch Christum hilff vnnd rettung in allerley anfechtung vnd nöten bey dir finden*).
- TLHy's "through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord," gives way to Dietrich's "through Christ," which the conclusion follows (§2.4c).

#### [27] Third Sunday in Lent (Oculi)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 83):** *Am dritten Sontag/wie Jesus einen Teuffel außtreibet/der stum̃ war. Luce am 11.* [On the Third Sunday: how Jesus casts out a devil that was dumb. Luke 11.]

**Verse (PDF p. 83)**

> VNser Herr Jesus sprach zu den vnglaubigē Juden: So ich durch Gottes finger die Teuffel außtreybe/so kombt ye das Reych Gottes zu euch.

Our Lord Jesus said unto the unbelieving Jews: If I with the finger of God cast out devils, no doubt the kingdom of God is come upon you.

**Summary (PDF pp. 84–85)**

> MJt disen worten lehret vnser lieber Herr Christus/wie wir dises wunderwercks vns trösten/vnd was wir drauß lernen sollen/das er dem armen stummen menschen hilfft/vnd jn vom Teuffel ledig macht/Nemlich/dz wir sollen wissen/wie der gnedige Vater im Himel sich vnser not angenommen/vnd hilff wider den Sathan vñ sein Tyranney/vns durch seinē Sun genedigklich verschaffet hab/auff dz/wer disen Jesum Christum mit glauben annimbt/der soll fürt an in Gottes Reych sein/vnnd der Sathan soll jn nicht mehr/wie zuuor/treyben vnd zwingen. Denn durch Christum empfangen wir den heyligen Geyst/der hilfft vns wider den bösen feind streyten vnnd siegen/welchen vnser lieber Herr Christus/alß der stercker/allgereyt vberwunden/jm seinen harnisch außgezogen/vnd seinen Christen den raub außgetheylet hat/das sie solcher vberwindung auch geniessen sollen. [p. 85] Aber gleych wie Christus vber solchem gnedigen werck sich muß lassen vrteylen vnd lestern: Also geht es sein wort noch heutigs rages [sic, for tages] in der welt/das manß ein Teuffels lehr schillt vnd verdam̃et als ketzerey.

With these words our dear Lord Christ teaches how we are to comfort ourselves with this miracle, in which He helps the poor dumb man and sets him free from the devil, and what we are to learn from it: namely, we are to know that the gracious Father in heaven has taken our need to heart, and has graciously procured for us, through His Son, help against Satan and his tyranny; so that whoever receives this Jesus Christ with faith shall henceforth be in God's kingdom, and Satan shall no longer drive and compel him as before. For through Christ we receive the Holy Ghost, who helps us to fight against the wicked enemy and to conquer. Him our dear Lord Christ, as the stronger, has already overcome; He has taken from him his armor and divided the spoils among His Christians, so that they too may have the benefit of that victory. But just as Christ must let Himself be judged and blasphemed over this gracious work, so it goes with His word in the world even today: men call it the devil's doctrine and condemn it as heresy.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 11:20 in King James wording, introduced by "Our Lord Jesus said unto the unbelieving Jews." Luther's *ye* ("surely") is the King James "no doubt."
- **"The stronger … his armour … the spoils."** Luke 11:21–22: "when a stronger than he shall come upon him, and overcome him, he taketh from him all his armour … and divideth his spoils."
- **"Judged and blasphemed."** Luke 11:15: "He casteth out devils through Beelzebub."
- **"Even today."** The print reads *heutigs rages*, a misprint for *tages*.
- **"Henceforth."** *fürt an* is *fortan*.

**Prayer (PDF p. 85)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/der du deinen Sun vnsern Herren Christum Jesum darumb hast lassen mensch werden/das er des Teuffels Tyranney wehren/vñ vns menschen wider den argen feind/schützen soll/wir dancken dir erstlich für solche gnedige hilff/vnd bitten darnach du wöllest in allen anfechtungen genedig zu vns setzen/für sicherheyt vns behüten/vñ in deim wort vñ deiner forcht/durch deinen heyligen Geyst genedig erhalten/auff das wir biß ans ende für dem bösen feinde befridet bleyben/vnd durch deinen Sun ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who for this cause didst suffer Thy Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, to become man, that He should restrain the tyranny of the devil, and defend us men against the evil enemy: We give thanks unto Thee first for such gracious help, and then we beseech Thee graciously to stand by us in all temptations, to preserve us from carnal security, and by Thy Holy Ghost graciously to keep us in Thy word and Thy fear, that unto the end we may remain safe from the evil enemy, and obtain eternal salvation through Thy Son, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "who hast sent Thy Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, to take upon Himself our flesh" → "who for this cause didst suffer Thy Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, to become man" (*darumb hast lassen mensch werden*).
- "that He might overcome the devil" → "that He should restrain the tyranny of the devil" (*des Teuffels Tyranney wehren*), as at [3].
- "and defend us poor sinners against the adversary" → "and defend us men against the evil enemy" (*vns menschen wider den argen feind/schützen*).
- "We give thanks unto Thee for Thy merciful help" → "We give thanks unto Thee first for such gracious help" (*erstlich für solche gnedige hilff*).
- "and we beseech Thee to attend us with Thy grace in all temptations" → "and then we beseech Thee graciously to stand by us in all temptations" (*vnd bitten darnach du wöllest in allen anfechtungen genedig zu vns setzen*), as at [25].
- "to keep us" → "graciously to keep us" (*genedig erhalten*).
- "that unto the end we may be delivered from the enemy" → "that unto the end we may remain safe from the evil enemy" (*biß ans ende für dem bösen feinde befridet bleyben*).
- "and obtain eternal salvation, through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord," → "and obtain eternal salvation through Thy Son," which is Dietrich's own close (*vnd durch deinen Sun ewig selig werden*); TLHy's conclusion continues it (§2.4d).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [28] Fourth Sunday in Lent (Laetare)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 86):** *Am Sontag zu Mitfastē/wie der Herr Christus fünfftausent Man/weyb vnnd kind nicht mit gerechnet/mit fünff brodten vnd zwen fischen speyset/vnd zwölff körb vberblieben/Johan. 6.* [On the Sunday of Mid-Lent: how the Lord Christ feeds five thousand men, not counting women and children, with five loaves and two fishes, and twelve baskets were left over. John 6.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 86–87)**

> VNser Herr Jesus nam die brodt vnd dancket/vnnd gab sie den Jüngern. Die Jüngern aber/denen die sich gelägert hetten/Desselben [p. 87] gleichen auch von den Fischen/wie vil er wolt.

Our Lord Jesus took the loaves; and when he had given thanks, he distributed to the disciples, and the disciples to them that were set down; and likewise of the fishes as much as he would.

**Summary (PDF pp. 87–88)**

> DJß ist ein schöne tröstliche Histori/das wir vns keinen mangel sollen anfechten lassen/sonder nur allein dahin sehē/das wir bey dem Herren Christo vnnd seim wort bleyben/Denn so wir das thun/ob gleich mangel fürfelt/so wissen wir/das vnser Herr Christus die macht hat/es sey so wenig für der hand alß jmmer wöll/so gibt er doch dauon alß vil er will/vnnd muß durch seinen segen alles jm vnter den henden wachsen vnnd zunemen. Wo nun er seinen segen also gibt/da soll ferner folgen/das man jn schon auffheben/vnd nicht vnnützlich vmbbringē soll/wie er hie befilhet/das man die vbrigen brocken soll auffheben. Darumb lerne yederman diser Histori sich trösten/vnd für dem geytz hüten/Der selbe sihet nur nach dem grossen hauffen/Ein Christ aber soll [p. 88] nach Gottes wort vnnd dem Herren Christo sehen/vnnd alß denn hoffen er habe einen reychen Gott im himel/der werde jn nicht lassen not leyden.

This is a beautiful, comforting history: that we are to let no want trouble us, but only see to it that we abide with the Lord Christ and His word. For if we do that, even if want befalls us, we know that our Lord Christ has the power: however little there may be at hand, He nevertheless gives from it as much as He will, and through His blessing everything must grow and increase under His hands. Now where He gives His blessing in this way, it is further to follow that one is to keep it carefully, and not squander it uselessly, as He commands here that the fragments that remain are to be gathered up. Therefore let everyone learn to comfort himself with this history and to guard himself against covetousness. Covetousness looks only to the great heap; but a Christian is to look to God's word and to the Lord Christ, and then hope that he has a rich God in heaven, who will not let him suffer want.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 6:11 in King James wording, introduced by "Our Lord Jesus." Dietrich has "as much as he would" (*wie vil er wolt*), where the King James Version has "as much as they would"; the translation follows him.
- **The heading** says "five thousand men, not counting women and children" (*Man/weyb vnnd kind nicht mit gerechnet*), from Matthew 14:21; John 6:10 has only "about five thousand."
- **"Gathered up."** John 6:12, "Gather up the fragments that remain."
- **"Keep it carefully."** *jn schon auffheben*: *schon* (*schonend*) is carefully, sparingly, and *jn* refers to the blessing.

**Prayer (PDF p. 88)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/der du durch deinen Sun in der wüsten fünff tausent Mañ/weyb vnnd kind nit mitgerechnet/nur mit fünff brodt vñ zwen fischen/reychlich/gespeyset hast/Wir bitten dich/sey mit deinem segen auch gnedig bey vns/behüte für dem geytz vnd sorgen der narung/das wir dein Reych vnd dein gerechtigkeyt am ersten süchen/vnd deine milte Väterliche güte in allem/was wir zu leyb vnd seel bedürffen/gnedigklich spüren durch Jesum Christum vnsern Herren/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who by Thy Son didst richly feed in the desert five thousand men, besides women and children, with but five loaves and two fishes: We beseech Thee to abide graciously also with us with Thy blessing. Preserve us from avarice and the cares of this life, that we may seek first Thy kingdom and Thy righteousness, and graciously perceive Thy bountiful fatherly goodness in all that we need for body and soul; through Jesus Christ our Lord, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "didst feed five thousand men in the desert with five loaves and two fishes" → "didst richly feed in the desert five thousand men, besides women and children, with but five loaves and two fishes" (*fünff tausent Mañ/weyb vnnd kind nit mitgerechnet/nur mit fünff brodt vñ zwen fischen/reychlich/gespeyset*; "besides women and children" is the King James wording of Matthew 14:21).
- "in the fullness of Thy blessing" → "with Thy blessing" (*mit deinem segen*; "fullness" is not in the German).
- "and in all things perceive Thy fatherly goodness" → "and graciously perceive Thy bountiful fatherly goodness in all that we need for body and soul" (*deine milte Väterliche güte in allem/was wir zu leyb vnd seel bedürffen/gnedigklich spüren*).
- "through Jesus Christ" → "through Jesus Christ our Lord," which is Dietrich's own close (*durch Jesum Christum vnsern Herren*); TLHy's conclusion continues it (§2.4d).
- The OLM web text omits the comma before "world without end"; restored.

**Note.** "The cares of this life" is TLHy's King James wording (Luke 21:34) for *sorgen der narung*, as at [2]; it is kept.

#### [29] Fifth Sunday in Lent (Judica)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 89):** *Am Sontag nach Mitfasten/Johannis am 8.* [On the Sunday after Mid-Lent. John 8.]

**Verse (PDF p. 89)**

> VNser Herr Jesus sprach: Warlich warlich ich sage euch/so yemand mein wort wirdt halten/der wirdt den todt nicht sehen ewigklich.

Our Lord Jesus said: Verily, verily, I say unto you, If a man keep my word, he shall never see death.

**Summary (PDF pp. 90–91)**

> DAs ist ein treffenliche/nütze/vnnd nötige/aber doch bey den Juden/vñ der welt sehr ergerliche ler/das kein ander weg zum ewigen leben sey/deñ das wort vnsers Herren Christi halten. Denn vernunfft helts dafür/man werde selig/weñ man gute werck thue/vnnd dem gesetz Gottes folge/Aber hie hörest nicht ein wort/das Christus vom Gesetz oder guten wercken sagte/Wer den todt nicht will sehen/das ist/wer da will das ewige leben haben/der wirts mit dem nicht erlangen/das er Mosen helt/diß oder yenes thut/Mein wort muß er halten. Was ist denn des Herren Christi wort? oder/was predigt er/das Moses nicht predigt? Dises/das er Gottes Sun sey/das er für vnsere sünde am Creutz sich opffere vnd bezale/vnd Gott vmb seiner willen vns gnedig sein/vnd zu kindern vns wölle annemen/Wer das wort helt/das ist/mit vnzweyffenlichem glauben annimbt/ [p. 91] der soll den tod nicht sehen ewigklich. Denn ob wol der leyb muß sterben/so soll doch der Geyst lebendig bleyben in Gott/vnd der leyb durch Christum am Jüngsten tag auch zu eim newen vnd ewigen leben aufferwecket werden. Da hilfft vns des Herren Christi wort zu/Moses wort hilfft vns nicht dazu.

This is an excellent, profitable and necessary teaching, but one that gives great offense to the Jews and to the world: that there is no other way to eternal life than to keep the word of our Lord Christ. For reason holds that one is saved if one does good works and follows the law of God. But here you do not hear a word from Christ about the law or good works. Whoever would not see death, that is, whoever would have eternal life, will not obtain it by keeping Moses or doing this or that: he must keep My word. What then is the word of the Lord Christ? Or what does He preach that Moses does not preach? This: that He is the Son of God; that He offers Himself on the cross for our sins and pays for them; and that God for His sake will be gracious to us and receive us as children. Whoever keeps this word, that is, receives it with undoubting faith, shall never see death. For although the body must die, the spirit shall nevertheless remain alive in God, and the body too shall be raised through Christ on the Last Day to a new and eternal life. To this the word of the Lord Christ helps us; Moses' word does not help us to it.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 8:51 in King James wording, introduced by "Our Lord Jesus said," except that "my word" (*mein wort*) is kept where the King James Version has "my saying," because the summary turns on the word.
- **"Keeping Moses."** *das er Mosen helt*, that he keeps the law of Moses.
- **"He must keep My word."** Dietrich slips into Christ's own voice.

**Assignment:** Fifth Sunday in Lent. TLHy has no collect at all for this Sunday.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 91–92)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/dein lieber Sun vnnd vnser erlöser Christus Jesus sagt: Wer mein wort helt/wirdt den todt nicht sehen ewigklich/Wir bitten dich gibe deinen heyligen Geyst in vnsere hertzen/weyl du zu deines Suns wort vns kommen/vnnd das selb hast hören lassen/das wirs auch halten/von gantzem hertzen vns drauff verlassen/vnd mitten im tod desselben vns trösten/vnnd den todt in ewigkeyt nicht sehen/vmb deines lieben Suns/vnnd vnsers erlösers Christi Jhesu willen/AMEN.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, Thy dear Son and our Redeemer, Jesus Christ, saith: "Whosoever keepeth My word shall never see death." We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts, that, since Thou hast brought us to the word of Thy Son and hast caused us to hear it, we may also keep it, rely upon it with our whole heart, comfort ourselves therewith in the midst of death, and never in eternity see death; for the sake of Thy dear Son and our Redeemer, Jesus Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **The quotation.** It is John 8:51.
- **A reason, not a request.** *weyl du … vns kommen/vnnd das selb hast hören lassen* gives the ground of the petition ("since Thou hast…"); it is not itself a petition.
- **Ending.** Dietrich's own close, "for the sake of Thy dear Son and our Redeemer, Jesus Christ," is continued with TLHy's "who liveth and reigneth…" (§2.4d).

#### [30] Palm Sunday

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 92):** *Am Palm Sontag/vom leyden vnsers Herren Christi/Esa. 53.* [On Palm Sunday: of the suffering of our Lord Christ. Isaiah 53.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 92–93)**

> FVRwar er trug vnser kranckheyt/vnnd lud auff sich vnsere schmertzen/wir aber hielten jhn für den/der von Got geplagt vñ geschwechet [p. 93] wer/Aber er ist vmb vnser missethat willen verwundet/vnd vmb vnser sünde willen zuschlagen/Die straff ligt auff jm/auff das wir fride hetten/vnd durch seine wunden sind wir geheylet/Wir giengen jrre/wie die schaffe/ein yegklicher sahe auff seinen weg/Aber der Herr warff all vnser sünde auff jn.

Surely he hath borne our griefs, and carried our sorrows: yet we did esteem him smitten of God, and afflicted. But he was wounded for our transgressions, he was bruised for our iniquities: the chastisement of our peace was upon him; and with his stripes we are healed. Like sheep we went astray; we turned every one to his own way; and the Lord hath laid on him the iniquity of us all.

**Summary (PDF pp. 93–95).** The paragraph breaks follow the print.

> DAs leyden vnsers liebē Herren Christi sollen wir dazu brauchē/das wir drauß lernen Gott fürchten/Gott trawen/vñ Gottes willen gedultig tragen/Deñ weyl die Sünd durch kein Creatur hat können versönet werden/der Sun Gottes selb/hat dafür leyden vnd bezalen müssen/solchs soll vns dahin treyben/das wir Gott fürchten/vñ vns für der sünde/alß für dem vntreglichstem last/vnd schedlichstem vnnd ewigem gifft/mit höchstē fleyß hüten/Aber die welt/wil [p. 94] leyder solches nicht glauben/sie achtet die sünd für gering/vnd hat noch lust dazu/Das ist ein anzeygung/das sie vnsers Herren Christi leyden nie recht bedacht hat.

We are to use the suffering of our dear Lord Christ to this end, that we learn from it to fear God, to trust God, and to bear God's will patiently. For since sin could not be atoned for by any creature, but the Son of God Himself had to suffer and pay for it, this should drive us to fear God, and to guard ourselves with the utmost diligence against sin, as against the most unbearable burden and the most harmful and everlasting poison. But the world, alas, will not believe this; it counts sin a small thing, and even takes pleasure in it. That is a sign that it has never rightly considered the suffering of our Lord Christ.

> Zum andern/sollen wir auch lernen Gott trawen/vnd vns aller genad zu jm versehen/sintemal sein eyniger Sun/vnser Gott/für vnsere sünd mit seinem eygnen leyb vnd blut bezalet/vnd durch solches opffer Gottes zorn gestillet/vnd vns mit seinem Vatter versönet hat/Darumb/wen seine sünde engsten/der sehe den Sun Gottes am Creutz an/vnnd bedencke warumb er doch da also ellendigklich hange/so wirt er finden/wie der Prophet sagt/das es vmb vnsert willen geschehen sey/das vns dadurch würde geholffen/wie er spricht: Durch seine wunden sind wir heyl worden.

Secondly, we are also to learn to trust God and to look to Him for every grace, since His only Son, our God, has paid for our sin with His own body and blood, and by this sacrifice has stilled God's wrath and reconciled us with His Father. Therefore whoever is distressed by his sins, let him look upon the Son of God on the cross, and consider why He hangs there so wretchedly. Then he will find, as the prophet says, that it happened for our sake, that we might be helped by it, as he says: "With his stripes we are healed."

> Zum dritten/sollen wir auch lernen gedultig sein/vnd Gottes willen tragē/Denn so der Sun Gottes selb/vmb vnsert willen das Creutz auff sich genommen hat/der doch keiner sünde [p. 95] schuldig war/wie können wir vns des Creutzes beschwerē/die wir durch eygne sünd für vnnd für dazu vrsach geben/vnd der sünden halb/der ruten oder straff sehr wol bedürffen? Wir sollen aber auch im leyden dise hoffnung fest halten/gleich wie Christus auß dem leyden zu seiner herrligkeyt ist gangen/also soll vnser leyden auch zur freude werden/wenn wir nur gedultig sind/vnnd in festem glauben vmb hilff bitten.

Thirdly, we are also to learn to be patient and to bear God's will. For if the Son of God Himself, who was guilty of no sin, took the cross upon Himself for our sake, how can we complain of the cross, we who by our own sin continually give occasion for it, and on account of sin have great need of the rod or of punishment? But in suffering we are also to hold fast this hope: as Christ went through suffering into His glory, so our suffering too shall be turned to joy, if only we are patient and pray for help in firm faith.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Isaiah 53:4–6 in King James wording. Dietrich's text has "plagued and weakened by God" (*von Got geplagt vñ geschwechet*), rendered with the King James "smitten of God, and afflicted," and lacks "all" in "All we like sheep have gone astray."
- **The text.** Like Pentecost (Joel 2), this section takes a prophecy as its text rather than the Gospel of the day; its subject is the Passion (Appendix D).
- **"Look to Him for every grace."** *vns aller genad zu jm versehen*: *sich versehen zu* is to expect from someone.
- **"Into His glory."** Luke 24:26: "Ought not Christ to have suffered these things, and to enter into his glory?"

**Assignment:** Palm Sunday. TLHy's collect for that day is not Dietrich's ("Almighty and everlasting God, who hast caused Thy beloved Son to take our nature upon Himself…"; Appendix A, [31]).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 95–96)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/der du auß väterlicher gnade/deines eingebornen Sunes nit verschonet/sonder in den todt vnd an das Creutz hinan geben hast/Wir bitten dich/gib deinen heyligen Geyst in vnsere hertzen) [sic: ")" for virgule] das wir solcher gnad vns hertzlich trösten/für sünden ferner vns hüten/vnnd was du vns zu leyden zuschickest/gedultig tragen mögen/auff das wir durch jhn/mit dir ewigklich leben/Amen.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who of fatherly grace didst not spare Thine only-begotten Son, but didst deliver Him up unto death and to the cross: We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts, that we may heartily comfort ourselves with such grace, guard ourselves henceforth against sin, and patiently bear whatever Thou sendest us to suffer; that we may live with Thee eternally through Him, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **The text.** Dietrich's Palm Sunday text is Isaiah 53. *nit verschonet … hinan geben* echoes Romans 8:32.
- **Ending.** Dietrich's closing "through Him" is moved to the end of its clause so that TLHy's "who liveth and reigneth…" can follow it; only the word order changes (§2.4c).

#### [32] Maundy Thursday

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 96):** *Von dem Abendmal des Herren 1. Cor. 11.* [Of the Lord's Supper. 1 Corinthians 11.]

**Verse (PDF p. 97)**

> JCh habe es von dem Herren empfangen/das ich euch gegeben hab. Denn der Herr Jesus inn der nacht da er verrathen war/nam er das brod/dancket vnd brachs/vnd sprach: Nemet hin vnnd esset/das ist mein Leyb/der für euch gebrochen wirdt/Solches thut zu meinem gedechtnuß. Desselben gleychen auch den Kelch nach dem Abendmal/vnd sprach: Diser Kelch ist das newe Testament inn meinem blut (Das für euch vnd für vil vergossen wirdt/zu vergebung der sünden) Solches thut so offt jhrs drincket/zu meinem gedechtnuß.

I have received of the Lord that which I delivered unto you. For the Lord Jesus the same night in which he was betrayed took bread, gave thanks, and brake it, and said, Take, eat: this is my body, which is broken for you: this do in remembrance of me. After the same manner also the cup, when he had supped, and said, This cup is the new testament in my blood (which is shed for you and for many, for the remission of sins): this do ye, as oft as ye drink it, in remembrance of me.

**Summary (PDF pp. 97–100).** The paragraph breaks follow the print.

> VOn des Herrn Abendmal/soll ein yeder Christ dise stück wissen/Erstlich das es der Herr Christus befolhen/vnd zum Testament seiner kirchen vermeinet [p. 98] hat/das mans brauchen soll/Derhalb sind dise nicht Christen/so es können haben/aber doch nicht wöllen brauchen.

Of the Lord's Supper every Christian should know these points. First, that the Lord Christ has commanded it, and appointed it as His testament to His Church, that it should be used. Therefore those are not Christians who could have it and yet will not use it.

> Zum andern/hat der Herr diß Abendmal also geordnet/das man das brod vnd den Kelch den Christen soll reychen. Vnd dise klare wort mit angehengt/wer diß brod esse/der esse seinen Leyb/vnnd wer disen Kelch trincke/der trincke sein blut. Solche wort müssen wir nicht in wind schlagen/alß weren es allein wort. Wie der Herr Christus redet/also ist es in der warheyt/vnd ist ein grewliche sünde von disem Sacrament halten wie Zwingel/vnd die Sacramentschwirmer/das es nur brod vnd weyn sey.

Secondly, the Lord has so ordained this Supper that the bread and the cup are to be given to Christians, and has added these clear words: whoever eats this bread eats His body, and whoever drinks this cup drinks His blood. Such words we must not cast to the wind, as though they were mere words. As the Lord Christ speaks, so it is in truth; and it is a dreadful sin to hold of this sacrament, as Zwingli and the sacramentarian fanatics do, that it is only bread and wine.

> Zum dritten/hat Christus solches Abendmal geordnet/das man sein dabey soll gedencken/das ist/wir sollen vns seines leydens trösten/vnnd wie die wort klar mit bringen/nit zweyflen/sein leyb sey für vns geben/vnnd sein blut für vnsere sünde vergossen. Denn auff das solcher glaub fest vnd [p. 99] gewiß wer/ordnets Christus/das wir nicht allein solches im wort sollen hören/sein leyb sey für vns geben/vnd sein blut für vnsere sünde vergossen/sonder der leyb vnd das blut Christi/wirt vns in vnsern mund gelegt/vnd zu essen vnd drincken geben/das wir ja nicht zweyfflen/vnsere sünde sind bezalet/vnnd sollen ferner vns nicht zugerechnet werden.

Thirdly, Christ has ordained this Supper so that we should remember Him in it; that is, we are to comfort ourselves with His suffering, and, as the words clearly declare, not doubt that His body is given for us and His blood shed for our sins. For, in order that such faith might be firm and certain, Christ ordained that we should not only hear in the word that His body is given for us and His blood shed for our sins, but the body and blood of Christ are laid in our mouth and given us to eat and to drink, so that we may by no means doubt that our sins are paid for and are no longer to be imputed to us.

> Zum vierdten/wer nun diß Abendmal will brauchen/soll zuuor sich prüffen/das ist/sein hertz erforschen/ob er sich für einen sünder erkenne/vnd dauon beger ledig zu werden. Wo solches ein rechter ernst ist/vnd du dich des leydens vnsers Herrn Christi tröstest/so bist du zu disem Abendtmal recht vnd wol geschickt/Es soll aber ferner folgen/weil du darumb zu disem Abendtmal kombst/das du von sünden begerest ledig zu werden/das du fürt an für sünden dich hütest/vñ in allerley guten wercken übest/vnd sonderlich deines beruffs fleyssig wartest/Da gehöret aber auch stettiges [p. 100] gebet zu/das wir Got vmb seinen heiligen Geyst bitten/das er vns regiere/vud [sic, for vnd] für sünden beware.

Fourthly, whoever would now use this Supper is first to examine himself, that is, to search his heart, whether he acknowledges himself a sinner and desires to be set free from it. Where this is in true earnest, and you comfort yourself with the suffering of our Lord Christ, then you are rightly and well prepared for this Supper. But it is further to follow, since you come to this Supper because you desire to be set free from sins, that henceforth you guard yourself against sins, exercise yourself in every kind of good works, and above all attend diligently to your calling. But to this belongs also continual prayer, that we ask God for His Holy Ghost, that He may govern us and keep us from sins.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is 1 Corinthians 11:23–25 in King James wording, fitted to Dietrich's sentences ("For the Lord Jesus …"; "gave thanks, and brake it"). Into the word over the cup Dietrich inserts, in parentheses, "which is shed for you and for many, for the remission of sins," combining Luke 22:20 ("which is shed for you") with Matthew 26:28 ("which is shed for many for the remission of sins").
- **"Testament."** *zum Testament … vermeinet*: Dietrich takes up "the new testament in my blood": the Supper is Christ's last will, bequeathed to His Church.
- **"Whoever eats this bread eats His body."** Dietrich's own summary of the words of institution, not a quotation.
- **Zwingli.** Huldrych Zwingli (1484–1531), the reformer of Zurich, taught that the bread signifies Christ's body. *Sacramentschwirmer* ("sacramentarian fanatics") is the Lutheran name for those who denied the presence of Christ's body and blood in the sacrament.
- **"Examine himself."** 1 Corinthians 11:28.
- **"Attend diligently to your calling."** *deines beruffs fleyssig wartest*: *warten* (with genitive) is to look after, to attend to.
- **Misprint.** *vud* has a turned n, for *vnd*.

**Relation to TLHy.** TLHy's Maundy Thursday collect (Stratman's [33], which he marks as TLHy's alone) is a free adaptation of this prayer, readdressed to Christ: its thanksgiving for the sacrament, "that we may not receive this gift unworthily," the confession of sins, faith in forgiveness and growth "day by day … in faith and love, until we obtain eternal salvation" all come from Dietrich. It is therefore revised here against Dietrich's German, and the revision serves as the English of [32]. The adaptation was not made by TLHy: TLHy translates the Norwegian collect of 1887, which already has it. TLHy's unrevised text is given in Appendix A, [33].

**Prayer (PDF p. 100)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/wir dancken dir für dein grosse gnade/das du durch deinen Son diß Abendmal hast verordnet/da wir sein Leyb essen/vnnd sein Blut trincken/Wir bitten dich/gib durch deinen heiligen Geyst/das wir solche gabe nicht vnwirdig brauchen/sonder vnsere sünde erkennen/vnd dauon ablassen/vñ durch Christum vergebung der selben hoffen vnd glauben/vnd also im glauben vñ liebe von tag zu tag zunemen/biß wir ewig selig werden/Durch Christum Jesum deinen Son vnnd vnseren Herren/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee for Thy great grace, that through Thy Son Thou hast instituted this Supper, in which we eat His body and drink His blood: Grant us, we beseech Thee, by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may not receive this gift unworthily, but that we may acknowledge our sins and forsake them, through Christ hope for and believe the forgiveness of the same, and so day by day grow in faith and love, until we obtain eternal salvation; through Jesus Christ, Thy Son and our Lord, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- Address: "O Lord Jesus Christ" → "Lord God, heavenly Father" (*HErr Got himlischer Vatter*). Dietrich prays to the Father, and the rest of the prayer is turned accordingly.
- "that of Thine infinite mercy Thou hast instituted this Thy sacrament, in which we eat Thy body and drink Thy blood" → "for Thy great grace, that through Thy Son Thou hast instituted this Supper, in which we eat His body and drink His blood" (*für dein grosse gnade/das du durch deinen Son diß Abendmal hast verordnet/da wir sein Leyb essen/vnnd sein Blut trincken*).
- "but that we may confess our sins, remember Thine agony and death, believe the forgiveness of sin" → "but that we may acknowledge our sins and forsake them, through Christ hope for and believe the forgiveness of the same" (*sonder vnsere sünde erkennen/vnd dauon ablassen/vñ durch Christum vergebung der selben hoffen vnd glauben*). "Remember Thine agony and death" is not in the German.
- "and day by day grow" → "and so day by day grow" (*vnd also ... von tag zu tag zunemen*).
- "until we obtain eternal salvation through Thee, who livest and reignest with the Father and the Holy Ghost" → "until we obtain eternal salvation; through Jesus Christ, Thy Son and our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost," which continues Dietrich's own close (*Durch Christum Jesum deinen Son vnnd vnseren Herren*; §2.4d).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

**Note.** *Brauchen* is "use," as in the Lutheran "use" of the sacrament; TLHy's "receive … unworthily" (cf. 1 Corinthians 11:27) is kept as an equivalent.

### Easter and Ascension

#### [34] Easter Day

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 101):** *Am Oster tag/Rom. iiij.* [On Easter Day. Romans 4.] The text expounded is Romans 4:25.

**Verse (PDF p. 101)**

> CHRJstus ist vmb vnser sünde willen hingeben/vnnd vmb vnser gerechtigkeyt willen aufferwecket.

Christ was delivered for our offences, and was raised again for our justification.

**Summary (PDF pp. 102–103)**

> DAs ist/durch das leyden vnd sterben/vnsers lieben Herren Christi/sind wir von all vnsern sünden ledig vnd loß worden/Seiner aufferstehung aber geniessen wir dazu/das wir gerecht werden/das ist/das wir inn solcher genad bleyben/vnd die sünde fort an nicht mehr vber vns herrschen noch vns schaden möge. Deñ Christus ist vom todt erstanden/vnnd sitzt zur gerechten hand Gottes/das er vnns vertrette/vnnd mit seinem heyligen Geyst/nit allein den glauben in vns anzünde vnd erhalte/sonder auch der sünde in vns wehre/Vnnd vns leyte im willen Gottes vnnd heyligen gehorsam/biß wir von disem argem/mühseligem leben abscheyden/vnnd durch Christum zum ewigē leben aufferwecket/vnd ewig selig werden/wie er ist. Solches alles fasset die fröliche aufferstehung vnsers lieben Herren Christi in sich/vnd wir sollens mit rechtem glauben annemen/vñ solcher gaben vnd gnaden aller gewiß warten/ [p. 103] Gott dafür teglich dancken/vnd für sünden vns hüten/das wir solche genad vnd hoffnung/nit wider verlieren/sonder mit Christo der welt absterben/vnd in Gott vnnd nach seim wort leben.

That is: through the suffering and death of our dear Lord Christ we have been set free and loosed from all our sins; but from His resurrection we have this benefit besides, that we become righteous, that is, that we remain in this grace, and that sin may henceforth no longer reign over us or harm us. For Christ is risen from the dead and sits at the right hand of God, that He may intercede for us, and by His Holy Ghost not only kindle and preserve faith in us, but also restrain sin in us, and lead us in God's will and in holy obedience, until we depart from this evil and toilsome life, are raised through Christ to eternal life, and are blessed forever, as He is. All this the joyful resurrection of our dear Lord Christ contains within itself. We are to receive it with true faith, wait for all such gifts and graces with certainty, thank God for them daily, and guard ourselves against sins, so that we never lose such grace and hope again, but die to the world with Christ, and live in God and according to His word.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Romans 4:25 in King James wording, with "Christ" for "Who." *vnser sünde* ("our sins") is the King James "our offences," as in the collect.
- **"Justification."** *gerechtigkeyt*; the summary explains it as "that we become righteous" (*das wir gerecht werden*).
- **"Reign over us."** Romans 6:12–14. "Intercede for us" (*vertrette*) is Romans 8:34: "who is even at the right hand of God, who also maketh intercession for us."
- **"Die to the world with Christ."** Compare Romans 6:8–11 and Galatians 6:14.

**Prayer (PDF p. 103)**

> HERR Gott himelischer Vatter/der du deinen Son vmb vnser sünde willen hingeben/vnnd vmb vnser gerechtigkeit willen aufferwecket hast/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest deinen heiligen Geyst vns schencken/durch jn/vns regiern vnd füren/im waren glauben erhalten/vnd für allen sünden in eim newen reynen leben behüten/vnnd durch deinen Son Christum Jesum/vns nach disem leben aufferwecken zum ewigen leben/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who didst deliver Thy Son for our offenses, and didst raise Him again for our justification: We beseech Thee, grant us Thy Holy Ghost, and through Him govern and direct us, keep us in the true faith, and defend us from all sins in a new and pure life, and after this life raise us unto eternal life through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "that He may rule and govern us according to Thy will" → "and through Him govern and direct us" (*durch jn/vns regiern vnd füren*; "according to Thy will" is not in the German).
- "graciously keep us" → "keep us" ("graciously" is not in the German).
- "defend us from all sins" → "defend us from all sins in a new and pure life" (*in eim newen reynen leben*).
- "through the same, Thy beloved Son," → "through Thy Son, Jesus Christ," which is Dietrich's own phrase (*durch deinen Son Christum Jesum*), moved to the end of its clause (§2.4c).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

**Note.** The opening is Romans 4:25 in King James wording, as in TLHy.

#### [35] Easter Monday

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 103):** *Am andern Oster tag/Luce am 24.* [On the second day of Easter. Luke 24.]

**Verse (PDF p. 104)**

> VNser Herr Jhesus sprach zu den zweyen Jüngeren: O jhr thoren vñ treges hertzē zu glauben alle dem das die Propheten gered haben/Muste nicht Christus solches leiden/vnd zu seiner herrligkeyt eingehn.

Our Lord Jesus said unto the two disciples: O fools, and slow of heart to believe all that the prophets have spoken: ought not Christ to have suffered these things, and to enter into his glory?

**Summary (PDF pp. 104–106)**

> ES ergerten sich nit allein die vnglaubigen Juden/sonder auch des Herren Christi Jüngern selb an seinem schmehlichen todt/vnd war jnen vnmüglich zu glauben/das der gestorbne Christus widerumb solte lebendig werden/Denn sie sahen auff ander leut Exempel/der war nie keiner erwider kommen/ausserhalb etliche die Christus hette von todten aufferwecket/Aber der selben war diser zeyt auch vergessen/wie anderer herrlichen wunderwerck mehr. Solches war nicht allein ein grosser vnglaub/sonder wie es der Herr hie nennet/ein grosse thorheyt vnd sündhaffter [p. 105] vnuerstand. Deñ so sie gewist hetten/was des Herrn Christi vnd verheyßnen Messie eygnes ampt wer gewest/würden sie ob solchem todt sich nit geergert habē. Deñ die Propheten sagen klar/der Messias soll der gantzen welt sünde tragen/wie Esai. 53. stehet/Nun gehört aber auff die sünde der todt. Folget derhalb/weil Christus aller welt sünde auff sich genommen/vnd dafür bezalet hat/Das er auch hat müssen schmehlich sterben/Aber weil er nicht allein ein mensch/sonder auch ewiger Gottes Son war/hat der todt jn nit können halten/er hat selb sich am drittē tag auß dem tod erwecket. Hie sollen wir erstlich lernen/weyl der Herr mit den vnglaubigen Jüngeren so vätterliche gedult tregt/er werde mit vns auch so thun. Darnach sollen wir an seim todt vns nit ergern/sonder Got für solch gnedige erlösung dancken/vñ wissen/dz wir solchem exempel auch folgen/vnd mit dem Herren Christo leyden müssen/ehe wir mit jm herrlich werden/wie [p. 106] Paulus sagt 2. Cor. 1. das gleych/wie wir des leydens theylhafftig werden/also sollen wir auch des trostes theylhafftig sein.

Not only the unbelieving Jews but even the Lord Christ's own disciples were offended at His shameful death, and it was impossible for them to believe that the dead Christ should come to life again. For they looked to the example of other people, and of these none had ever come back, except some whom Christ had raised from the dead; but those too they had forgotten at this time, like many other glorious miracles. This was not only great unbelief, but, as the Lord calls it here, great folly and sinful want of understanding. For if they had known what the proper office of the Lord Christ, the promised Messiah, was, they would not have been offended at such a death. For the prophets say clearly that the Messiah is to bear the sin of the whole world, as it stands in Isaiah 53; and death belongs to sin. It follows, then, that since Christ took upon Himself the sin of all the world and paid for it, He also had to die a shameful death. But because He was not only a man but also the eternal Son of God, death could not hold Him; He raised Himself from death on the third day. Here we are to learn, first, that since the Lord bears with His unbelieving disciples with such fatherly patience, He will do the same with us. Next, we are not to be offended at His death, but to thank God for such gracious redemption, and to know that we too must follow this example and suffer with the Lord Christ before we are glorified with Him, as Paul says, 2 Corinthians 1: that as we are partakers of the sufferings, so shall we be also of the consolation.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 24:25–26 in King James wording, introduced by "Our Lord Jesus said unto the two disciples."
- **"Come back."** *erwider kommen*: *erwider* is a form of *herwider*, "back again."
- **"Messiah."** *Messie* is the Latin genitive (*Messiae*).
- **"Death belongs to sin."** Romans 6:23, "the wages of sin is death." "Death could not hold Him" echoes Acts 2:24, and "He raised Himself" John 2:19 and 10:18.
- **"Suffer with … glorified with Him."** Romans 8:17. The words of 2 Corinthians 1:7 are given in King James wording, in the first person as Dietrich has them.

**Prayer (PDF p. 106)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du deinen Son Christum Jesum den zweien jüngern zu Emaus hast offenbaret/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest durch dein wort vñ heiligen Geist vnsere hertzen auch erleuchten/das wir im glauben starck vnd fest werden/An deim wort trewlich halten/dauon gern reden/vnnd fleyssig mit vmbgehen/Auff das/ob wir gleych dem Exempel Christi nach/allerley hie auff erden müssen leyden/dennoch durch dein wort den rechten trost haben/vnd behalten/biß wir durch deynen Son nach disem leben zum ewigen leben aufferwecket werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who didst reveal Thy Son, Jesus Christ, to the two disciples at Emmaus: We beseech Thee to enlighten our hearts also by Thy word and by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may become strong and steadfast in the faith, hold faithfully to Thy word, delight to speak thereof, and diligently occupy ourselves therewith; that although, according to the example of Christ, we must suffer all manner of things here on earth, we nevertheless may have and retain the true comfort through Thy word, until, after this life, we shall be raised unto eternal life through Thy Son, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "Thy Son" → "Thy Son, Jesus Christ" (*deinen Son Christum Jesum*).
- "on the way to Emmaus" → "at Emmaus" (*zu Emaus*).
- "become established in the faith" → "become strong and steadfast in the faith" (*im glauben starck vnd fest werden*).
- "hold fast to Thy word" → "hold faithfully to Thy word" (*An deim wort trewlich halten*).
- "diligently meditate thereon" → "diligently occupy ourselves therewith" (*fleyssig mit vmbgehen*).
- "much evil on earth" → "all manner of things here on earth" (*allerley hie auff erden*).
- "a sure comfort in Thy word" → "the true comfort through Thy word" (*durch dein wort den rechten trost*).
- "through the same, Thy Son," → "through Thy Son," Dietrich's own words at the end of the clause (*durch deynen Son*; §2.4c).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [36] First Sunday after Easter (Quasimodogeniti)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 107):** *Am ersten Sontag nach Ostern/wie Christus seinē Jüngern nach seiner aufferstehung/das predigampt befilhet/Johan. 20.* [On the First Sunday after Easter: how Christ after His resurrection commits the office of preaching to His disciples. John 20.]

**Verse (PDF p. 107)**

> GLeich wie mich mein Vatter gesendet hat/so sende ich euch. Nemet hin den heiligen Geyst/welchen jr die sünde erlasset/den sind sie erlassen/vnd welchen jr sie behaltet/den sind sie behalten.

As my Father hath sent me, even so send I you. Receive ye the Holy Ghost: whose soever sins ye remit, they are remitted unto them; and whose soever sins ye retain, they are retained.

**Summary (PDF pp. 108–109)**

> DAs ist ein kurtze ler/aber da trefflich vil an gelegen ist/Nemlich/das wir zur vergebung der sünden/durch vnsere werck/vnd verdienst nit können komen. Allein im wort Gottes finden wirs/Das selb wirt vns nit allein in der predigt/sonder auch in der heyligen Tanf [sic, for Tauf]/vñ dem abendmal verkündet/vnd fehlet ferner nichts/deñ das du solchem wort/vnd zusagung Gotes glaubest. Jn der tauff sagt man dir/wer glaubt vñ getaufft wirt/der wirt selig werdē/Jm Abendmal sagt man dir/der leyb Christi sey für dich gebē/vñ sein blut zu vergebung deiner sünden vergossen/So sagt das Euangeon [sic, for Euangelion] allenthalb/Durch Christum sollen vns vnser sünd vergeben werdē. Wer nun solcher zusagung glaubt/der hat vergebung all seiner sünde/Wer aber nit glaubt/dē sol weder tauf noch Sacrament/noch wort helffen/seine sünde sind jm in ewigkeyt behalten/vnd muß verdampt werden/er fange an/vnd thu sonst was er wölle/Also lösen [p. 109] vnd bindē die schlüssel/das ist/das heylig Euangelion vnd predigampt/welches alß denn anfangen vñ in schwang hat gehn sollē/da Christus von todten aufferstanden/für die sünde mit seim tod bezalet/vñ den Satan vberwundē hat. Für solche frucht der aufferstehung Christi/das vergebung der sünde in seinem namen gepredigt wirdt/sollen wir Got teglich dancken.

This is a short teaching, but one on which a very great deal depends: namely, that we cannot come to the forgiveness of sins by our works and merit. In the word of God alone do we find it. This is proclaimed to us not only in the sermon but also in holy baptism and in the Supper, and nothing further is lacking but that you believe this word and promise of God. In baptism you are told: "He that believeth and is baptized shall be saved." In the Supper you are told that the body of Christ is given for you and His blood shed for the forgiveness of your sins. So the gospel says everywhere: through Christ our sins shall be forgiven us. Whoever now believes this promise has forgiveness of all his sins. But whoever does not believe, neither baptism nor sacrament nor word shall help him; his sins are retained against him forever, and he must be damned, whatever else he may undertake and do. So the keys loose and bind, that is, the holy gospel and the office of preaching, which was to begin and to come into full force at the time when Christ had risen from the dead, had paid for sin with His death, and had overcome Satan. For this fruit of Christ's resurrection, that forgiveness of sins is preached in His name, we are to thank God daily.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 20:21–23 in King James wording, without "Peace be unto you" and "when he had said this, he breathed on them."
- **The keys.** Binding and loosing are the Office of the Keys (Matthew 16:19; 18:18), which Dietrich identifies with the gospel and the office of preaching.
- **Scripture.** "He that believeth and is baptized shall be saved" is Mark 16:16 in King James wording. "Forgiveness of sins … preached in His name" echoes Luke 24:47.
- **Misprints.** *Tanf* has a turned u, for *Tauf*; *Euange-/on* omits a syllable at the line break, for *Euangelion*. Both were checked at 600 dpi; the page is smudged.

**Prayer (PDF p. 109)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/wir danckē dir für deine grosse vnaußsprechliche gnad/das du durch deinen Son vnsern lieben Herrn Christum/das heylige Euangelion/vnd die heyligē Sacrament verordnet hast/vns zum trost/das wir vergebung der sünden da sollen finden/Wir bitten dich/gib deinen heyligen Geyst in vnsere hertzen/auff das wir deim wort von hertzen glauben/vnd durch die heyligen Sacrament von tag zu tag den glauben stercken/biß wir endtlich selig werden/Durch Jesum Christum deinē Son/vnd vnsern Herren/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee for Thy great and ineffable grace, that through Thy Son, our dear Lord Christ, Thou hast ordained the holy gospel and the holy sacraments for our comfort, that we should find therein the forgiveness of sins: We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts, that we may heartily believe Thy word, and through the holy sacraments day by day strengthen our faith, until we at last obtain salvation; through Jesus Christ, Thy Son and our Lord, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "that of Thine ineffable grace" → "for Thy great and ineffable grace, that" (*für deine grosse vnaußsprechliche gnad*).
- "for the sake of Thy Son, Thou hast given us the holy gospel, and hast instituted the holy sacraments, that through the same we may have comfort and forgiveness of sin" → "that through Thy Son, our dear Lord Christ, Thou hast ordained the holy gospel and the holy sacraments for our comfort, that we should find therein the forgiveness of sins" (*durch deinen Son vnsern lieben Herrn Christum/das heylige Euangelion/vnd die heyligē Sacrament verordnet hast/vns zum trost/das wir vergebung der sünden da sollen finden*).
- "grant us Thy Holy Spirit" → "give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts" (*gib deinen heyligen Geyst in vnsere hertzen*; §2.2).
- "establish our faith" → "strengthen our faith" (*den glauben stercken*).
- "through Jesus Christ our Lord" → "through Jesus Christ, Thy Son and our Lord," which is Dietrich's own close (*Durch Jesum Christum deinē Son/vnd vnsern Herren*); the conclusion continues it (§2.4d).

#### [37] Second Sunday after Easter (Misericordias Domini)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 109):** *Am andern Sontag nach Ostern/wie vnser Herr Christus tröstet/er wölle vnser rechter Hirt sein/vnd vns wider den Sathan vnd todt retten/Johannis 10.* [On the Second Sunday after Easter: how our Lord Christ comforts us that He will be our true Shepherd and rescue us against Satan and death. John 10.]

**Verse (PDF p. 110)**

> JCh bin ein guter Hirt/ein guter hirt lest sein leben für seine schaff. Jch erkenne die meinen/vnd bin bekandt den meinen/Wie mich mein Vatter kennet/vnnd ich den Vatter kenne. Vnd ich laß mein leben für die Schaff.

I am the good shepherd: the good shepherd giveth his life for the sheep. I know my sheep, and am known of mine. As my Father knoweth me, even so know I the Father: and I lay down my life for the sheep.

**Summary (PDF pp. 110–111)**

> WJr menschen alle sind wie ein armes Schefflein/gegen eim Wolff/Denn wir können vnns wider den Teuffel nicht schützen/Aber vnser lieber Herr Christus sagt vnns zu/er wölle vnser Hirt sein/vnd sein leben für vns lassen/das ist/er wölle sich lassen würgen vnnd fressen/wie am Creutz geschehen/vnnd also vns vom todt vnnd des Teuffels Reych ledig machen/das wir durch jn sollen kinder sein des ewigen lebens/Denn er kennet vns/vnd nimbt sich vnser an/wie jn sein Vatter kennet/das ist/ob er vns hie gleich lest leyden/so will er doch vns endlich selig machen. Darumb [p. 111] sollen wir solchen trewen Hyrten auch erkennen/alß vnsern erlöser/vnd seim wort gern folgen/Denn wer disen Hyrten nicht hören/vnnd vergebens will rüffen lassen/der gibt sich selb willig in fahr/vnd ist jm ferner nicht zu helffen. Wer aber bey disem Hirten bleybt/ob gleych der Sathan mit anfechtung vnnd allerley vnglück jmmerdar anhelt/der soll ewigen schutz vnd rettung haben/wider sünd/todt/vnd Teuffel.

All of us men are like a poor little sheep before a wolf, for we cannot protect ourselves against the devil. But our dear Lord Christ promises us that He will be our Shepherd and lay down His life for us; that is, He will let Himself be slain and devoured, as happened on the cross, and so set us free from death and from the devil's kingdom, so that through Him we may be children of eternal life. For He knows us and takes care of us, as His Father knows Him; that is, although He lets us suffer here, yet He will save us in the end. Therefore we too are to know this faithful Shepherd as our Redeemer, and gladly follow His word. For whoever will not hear this Shepherd, but lets Him call in vain, gives himself willingly into danger, and there is no more help for him. But whoever abides with this Shepherd, although Satan presses on continually with temptation and all manner of misfortune, shall have eternal protection and deliverance against sin, death, and the devil.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 10:11 and 10:14–15 in King James wording. Dietrich has "my Father" (*mein Vatter*) in verse 15.
- **"Know … as our Redeemer."** *erkennen* answers the verse's "I know my sheep": as Christ knows His own, they are to know Him.
- **"Lets Him call in vain."** *vergebens will rüffen lassen*: compare John 10:3–5, 27.
- **Damaged type.** The page is smudged in several places, but every word can be read.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 111–112)**

> HERR Gott himlischer Vatter/der du vns ellende menschen Vätterlich bedacht/vnd deinen Son zum Hirten vber vns gesetzet hast/das er nit allein mit seinem wort vns weidē/sonder auch durch seinen schutz/wider sünd/todt/vnnd Teuffel/vns retten vnnd erhalten soll/Wir bitten dich/gib durch deinen heyligen Geyst/das/gleich wie diser Hirt vns kennet/vnd vnser not sich annimbt/wir widerum̃ in allerley anligen jn auch erkennen/vns an jn halten/hilff vnnd trost bey jm suchen vnd gewarten/vnd seiner stimmen mit hertzen folgen/vnd also durch jn ewig selig werden/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who of Thy fatherly goodness hast been mindful of us miserable men, and hast set Thy Son as Shepherd over us, not only to feed us with His word, but also by His protection to rescue and preserve us against sin, death, and the devil: We beseech Thee, grant by Thy Holy Ghost that, even as this Shepherd doth know us and taketh our need upon Him, we in turn may know Him also in all our concerns, hold to Him, seek and await help and comfort from Him, from our hearts obey His voice, and so obtain eternal salvation through Him, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "us poor, miserable sinners" → "us miserable men" (*vns ellende menschen*).
- "and hast given Thy beloved Son to be our shepherd" → "and hast set Thy Son as Shepherd over us" (*deinen Son zum Hirten vber vns gesetzet*).
- "not only to nourish us by His word, but also to defend us from sin, death, and the devil" → "not only to feed us with His word, but also by His protection to rescue and preserve us against sin, death, and the devil" (*nit allein mit seinem wort vns weidē/sonder auch durch seinen schutz/wider sünd/todt/vnnd Teuffel/vns retten vnnd erhalten*).
- "grant us Thy Holy Spirit, that" → "grant by Thy Holy Ghost that" (*gib durch deinen heyligen Geyst/das*).
- "and succor us in every affliction" → "and taketh our need upon Him" (*vnd vnser not sich annimbt*).
- "we also may know Him, and, trusting in Him, seek help and comfort in Him" → "we in turn may know Him also in all our concerns, hold to Him, seek and await help and comfort from Him" (*wir widerum̃ in allerley anligen jn auch erkennen/vns an jn halten/hilff vnnd trost bey jm suchen vnd gewarten*).
- "and obtain eternal salvation, through the same, Thy Son Jesus Christ," → "and so obtain eternal salvation through Him," which is Dietrich's own phrase (*vnd also durch jn ewig selig werden*); the conclusion follows (§2.4c).

#### [38] Third Sunday after Easter (Jubilate)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 112):** *Am dritten Sontag nach Ostern/wie Christus warnet vnnd tröstet/der künfftigen verfolgung halb/Johannis am 16.* [On the Third Sunday after Easter: how Christ warns and comforts concerning the persecution to come. John 16.]

**Verse (PDF p. 113)**

> WArlich warlich/ich sage euch: Jr werdet weynen vnnd heulen/Aber die welt wirdt sich frewen/Jr aber werdet traurig sein/doch ewer trawrigkeyt soll zur frewde werden.

Verily, verily, I say unto you, That ye shall weep and lament, but the world shall rejoice: and ye shall be sorrowful, but your sorrow shall be turned into joy.

**Summary (PDF pp. 113–114)**

> VNser lieber Herr Christus warnet erstlich alle seyne Christē/weil sie in der welt sind/das sie allerley widerwertigkeyt vnnd vnglück müssen gewarten vnnd leyden/Vnd tröstet darnach solches leyden soll ein kleines sein/Sintemal es Gott durch sein hilff bald wenden/vnd in yenem leben mit ewiger frewde will bezalen. An solchen trost sollen wir gedencken/wenn es vns vbel gehet/vnnd der besserung vnd der gnedigen hilffe gewarten/vnd ja nicht verzweyflen/ob gleich vns vnmüglich dunckt/das wirs erdulden/oder zur hilffe mögen kommen. Denn gleich wie ein schwangeres Weyb/nichts denn angst vnnd [p. 114] not fület/vnnd dennoch Gott in eim augenblick rath vnd hilff schaffet/Also soll es mit den Christen auch gehen/sonderlich wenn sie mit dem gebet vñ glauben fest anhalten/Es muß rettung vnd hilff folgen/wo nicht zeytlich/doch ewig/das ist gewiß. Denn die verheyssung hie leugt vns nicht/Ewer trawrigkeyt soll zur frewde werden. Gleich aber wie es vns in der anfechtung vnmüglich ist/das wir solten glauben/es würde nur vmb ein kleines zu thun sein. Also wenn die anfechtung hinüber ist/findets sich selb/das wir müssen bekennen/es sey ein kleines gewest/sonderlich wenn wirs gegen der verheyssung des ewigen lebens halten.

Our dear Lord Christ first warns all His Christians that, because they are in the world, they must expect and suffer all manner of adversity and misfortune; and then He comforts them that such suffering shall be but a little while, since God will soon turn it away by His help, and in the life beyond will repay it with eternal joy. This comfort we are to remember when things go ill with us, and wait for the amendment and for gracious help, and by no means despair, even if it seems impossible to us that we can endure it or come to help. For just as a woman with child feels nothing but anguish and distress, and yet God in a moment provides counsel and help, so it shall go with Christians too, especially when they hold fast with prayer and faith. Deliverance and help must follow, if not in time, then in eternity; that is certain. For the promise here does not lie to us: "Your sorrow shall be turned into joy." But just as in temptation it is impossible for us to believe that it will be only a matter of a little while, so, when the temptation is over, it turns out of itself that we must confess it was but a little while, especially when we set it against the promise of eternal life.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 16:20 in King James wording.
- **"A little while."** *ein kleines* is Luther's word for Christ's "a little while" (John 16:16–19), the beginning of this Gospel; Dietrich applies it to the Christian's suffering.
- **"A woman with child."** John 16:21: "A woman when she is in travail hath sorrow … but as soon as she is delivered of the child, she remembereth no more the anguish, for joy."
- **"Counsel and help."** *rath vnd hilff*, as in the St. Thomas prayer ([80]).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 114–115)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du auß väterlichē wolmeinen/vns deine kinder hie auff erden vnter der ruthen wilt halten/auff das wir deinen eingebornen Son bedes im leyden/vnd hernach in der herrligkeyt sollen gleich sein/Wir bitten dich/tröste vns in der anfechtung vnd Creutz/mit dem heyligen Geyst/das wir nicht verzagen/sonder nach der zusagung deines Sons fest an disem trost halten/es soll das leyden nur ein kleines sein/vñ darnach die ewigen freude folgen/auff das wir also durch dise hoffnung alles vnglück in gedult vberwinden/vnd durch Christum selig werden Amen/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who of Thy fatherly goodness dost suffer Thy children to come under Thy chastening rod here on earth, that we may be like unto Thine only-begotten Son both in suffering and hereafter in glory: We beseech Thee, comfort us in temptation and cross by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may not fall into despair, but, according to the promise of Thy Son, hold fast to this comfort, that the suffering shall be but a little while, and shall then be followed by eternal joy; that we thus, through this hope, may overcome all misfortune in patience, and be saved through Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen, Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "in suffering" → "both in suffering" (*bedes*).
- "in temptations and afflictions" → "in temptation and cross" (*in der anfechtung vnd Creutz*).
- "but that we may continually trust in Thy Son's promise, that our trials will endure but a little while" → "but, according to the promise of Thy Son, hold fast to this comfort, that the suffering shall be but a little while" (*sonder nach der zusagung deines Sons fest an disem trost halten/es soll das leyden nur ein kleines sein*).
- "that we thus, in patient hope, may overcome all evil, and at last obtain eternal salvation, through the same, Thy Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord," → "that we thus, through this hope, may overcome all misfortune in patience, and be saved through Christ," (*auff das wir also durch dise hoffnung alles vnglück in gedult vberwinden/vnd durch Christum selig werden*). The conclusion follows Dietrich's "through Christ" (§2.4c).
- "Amen, Amen": the print doubles the Amen (*Amen/AMEN.*), as at [86].
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [39] Fourth Sunday after Easter (Cantate)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 115):** *Am vierdten Sontag/wie Christus den heiligen geyst verheist/vnd lehret was er in der welt soll außrichten/Johan. 16.* [On the Fourth Sunday: how Christ promises the Holy Ghost, and teaches what He shall accomplish in the world. John 16.]

**Verse (PDF p. 116)**

> CHristus spricht: Wenn ich hingehe/will ich den tröster zu euch senden/der wirdt die welt straffen vmb die sünde/vnd vmb die gerechtigkeyt/vnnd vmb das gerichte/Das ist.

Christ saith: If I depart, I will send the Comforter unto you, and he will reprove the world of sin, and of righteousness, and of judgment. That is:

**Summary (PDF pp. 116–118).** The paragraph breaks follow the print.

> DEr heylige Geyst lehret vnns im Euangelio drey ding/Das erste/das alle/so an Christum nicht glauben/sünder sind/vnd jnen selb nicht können helffen/sie müssen in sünden vnd dem tode bleyben.

The Holy Ghost teaches us three things in the gospel. The first: that all who do not believe in Christ are sinners and cannot help themselves; they must remain in sins and in death.

> [p. 117] Das ander/das wir allein dadurch gerecht/vnd von sünden ledig werden/das Christus zum Vatter gehet/das ist/das er am Creutze/für vns bezalet mit seinem todt/vnd wir jn nit sehen/das ist/wir glauben seinē wort vnd zusagung/vnd trösten vns sein/das er vnser erlöser sey/vnd sitze zur rechten Gottes/vnd vertrette vns.

The second: that we become righteous and free from sins solely by this, that Christ goes to the Father, that is, that He pays for us on the cross with His death; and that we see Him not, that is, we believe His word and promise, and comfort ourselves with Him, that He is our Redeemer, and sits at the right hand of God, and intercedes for us.

> Zum dritten/das wir für dem Sathan vñ seiner gewalt/vns nit sollen fürchten/sonder wissen/das er gerichtet/vnd von dem Herrn Christo vberwunden sey. Derhalb kan er denē nit schaden/so an Christum glauben. Das ist nu der schatz/den wir Christē durch den heyligen geyst haben/das erstlich wir vnser sünde erkennen/vnnd für Got vns demütigen/vñ darnach vns des verdiensts Christi trösten/vñ wider den Sathan vñ die welt einē mut fassen/alß die vns in ewigkeit nit schaden können/sie seyen so böß/mechtig/vnd witzig sie wöllen/Christus hat sie schō gerichtet. Weil aber solch lehr vñ trost d͛ wenigst teil mit glaubē añimbt/ [p. 118] die andern verachten oder verfolgen es/Straffet der heilig geyst solchē vnglauben durch das predigampt/vnd lest die verächter vñ verfolger in sünden/vnd vnter des Teuffels tyrañey stecken vnd verderben/deñ sie wöllen jnen nit lassen raten noch helffen.

Thirdly: that we are not to fear Satan and his power, but to know that he is judged and overcome by the Lord Christ. Therefore he cannot harm those who believe in Christ. This, now, is the treasure that we Christians have through the Holy Ghost: that first we acknowledge our sin and humble ourselves before God, and then comfort ourselves with the merit of Christ, and take courage against Satan and the world, as those who can never harm us, however wicked, mighty and cunning they may be; Christ has already judged them. But since only the smallest part receives such doctrine and comfort with faith, and the others despise or persecute it, the Holy Ghost reproves such unbelief through the office of preaching, and leaves the despisers and persecutors stuck fast in sins and under the devil's tyranny, to perish; for they will not let themselves be counselled or helped.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 16:7–8, shortened, in King James wording. "That is" (*Das ist*) stands at the end of the verse and introduces the exposition.
- **The three points** expound John 16:9–11: "Of sin, because they believe not on me; Of righteousness, because I go to my Father, and ye see me no more; Of judgment, because the prince of this world is judged."
- **"Reprove."** *straffen*, as in the verse, is "reprove" in the summary's last sentence too.
- **Abbreviation.** *d͛* is the printer's abbreviation for *der* (a d with the er-hook above it).

**Prayer (PDF p. 118)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du durch deynen Son vns den heiligē geist verheissen hast/das er die welt vmb die sünde/gerechtigkeyt/vnd das gericht straffen soll/Wir bitten dich/erleuchte vnsere hertzen/das wir vnsere sünde erkennen/vnnd durch den glauben an Christum zur ewigen gerechtigkeyt kummen/vnd in allerley vnser not vñ anfechtung disen rechten trost fassen/das Christus ein Herr vber Teuffel/todt/vnd alles sey/vnnd vns mit genaden von allem vnglück helffen/vnd ewig wölle selig machen/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who didst through Thy Son promise us the Holy Ghost, that He should convince the world of sin, of righteousness, and of judgment: We beseech Thee, enlighten our hearts, that we may acknowledge our sins, through faith in Christ obtain everlasting righteousness, and in all our needs and temptations lay hold on this true consolation, that Christ is Lord over the devil and death, and all things, and that He will graciously help us out of all misfortune, and save us eternally; *through the same, Thy Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "Thy Holy Spirit" → "the Holy Ghost" (*den heiligē geist*).
- "confess our sins" → "acknowledge our sins" (*vnsere sünde erkennen*).
- "in all our trials and temptations retain this consolation" → "in all our needs and temptations lay hold on this true consolation" (*in allerley vnser not vñ anfechtung disen rechten trost fassen*).
- "that He will graciously deliver us out of all our afflictions, and make us forever partakers of eternal salvation" → "that He will graciously help us out of all misfortune, and save us eternally" (*vns mit genaden von allem vnglück helffen/vnd ewig wölle selig machen*).
