#### [40] Fifth Sunday after Easter (Rogate)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 119):** *Am fünfftē Sontag nach Ostern/verheyssung vnd vermanung zum gebet/Johan. 16.* [On the Fifth Sunday after Easter: a promise of, and an exhortation to, prayer. John 16.]

**Verse (PDF p. 119)**

> WArlich warlich/ich sage euch: So jhr den Vatter etwas bitten werdet inn meinem namen/so wirdt ers euch geben. Bittet/so werdet jr nemen/das ewer freude volkommen sey.

Verily, verily, I say unto you, Whatsoever ye shall ask the Father in my name, he will give it you. Ask, and ye shall receive, that your joy may be full.

**Summary (PDF pp. 120–121)**

> VNser lieber Herr Christus vermanet seine jüngern zum Gebet/Darumb das sie so vil not vñ ellend inn der welt gewarten vnnd außstehen müssen/da sonst kein hilff noch trost ist/deñ das Got vns erhören/vñ vns helffen wölle/Wie denn vnser lieber Herr Christus vns gewiß vertröstet/der Vatter im himel/werde vns seine kinder nicht lassen vmb sonst betten. Es lehret aber daneben Christus auch wie wir recht sollen beten/Nemlich/in seinem namen/vnnd nicht inn vnserm namē/das ist/wir sollen hoffen/gleich wie Gott durch Christum vnser Vatter worden ist/also wölle er auch vmb Christus willen/vns erhören/vnd auß allem vnglück helffen. So mercke nun bedes/erstlich den befelh vnd die verheissung/das wir sollen betten/vnd Gott das gebet wölle hören/vnnd darnach den vnterricht wie wir allein in Christus namen/das ist/auff sein verdienst vnd gnad sollen [p. 121] beten/Denn sonst werden vnser sünde vnns am gebet hindern. Wer aber weyß/das Got allein vmb seines Sones willen erhören will/der tröstet sich solcher verheyssung mehr/denn er seine sünde sich lest schrecken.

Our dear Lord Christ exhorts His disciples to prayer, because they must expect and endure so much need and misery in the world, where there is no other help or comfort than that God will hear us and help us. As indeed our dear Lord Christ gives us the sure assurance that the Father in heaven will not let us, His children, pray in vain. But at the same time Christ also teaches how we are to pray rightly: namely, in His name, and not in our own name; that is, we are to hope that, as God has become our Father through Christ, so He will also hear us for Christ's sake and help us out of all misfortune. Mark both, then: first, the command and the promise, that we are to pray and God will hear the prayer; and then the instruction, that we are to pray in Christ's name alone, that is, relying on His merit and grace. For otherwise our sins will hinder us in prayer. But whoever knows that God will hear only for His Son's sake comforts himself with this promise more than he lets his sins terrify him.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 16:23–24 in King James wording, without "Hitherto have ye asked nothing in my name," which Dietrich omits.
- **"Gives us the sure assurance."** *vertröstet* is to give someone a firm hope.
- **"Christ's sake … Christ's name."** The print has *Christus* in both places where the genitive would be expected (*vmb Christus willen*, *in Christus namen*); this is the printer's spelling and is kept.

**Prayer (PDF p. 121)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du durch deinen Son vns verheissen hast/was wir in seinem namen bitten/das wöllest vns geben/Wir biten dich/erhalt vns in deim wort/gib deinen heyligen Geyst/der vnns regiere/vnd füre nach deinem willen/Behüte für des Teuffels Reych/für falscher lehr vnd vnrechtem Gottesdienst/Bewar auch vnser leyb vnd leben/für allem vnglück/Gib deinen segen vnd frid/auff das wir allenthalb deine gnedige hilff erfaren/vnd dich hie vnd dort in ewigkeyt/alß vnsern gnedigen Vater/durch Christum preisen vnd loben/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who through Thy Son didst promise us that whatsoever we ask in His name Thou wilt give us: We beseech Thee, keep us in Thy word, and grant us Thy Holy Ghost, that He may govern and direct us according to Thy will; protect us from the kingdom of the devil, from false doctrine and worship; preserve also our body and life from all misfortune; grant us Thy blessing and peace, that we may in all things perceive Thy gracious help, and here and hereafter, forever, praise and glorify Thee as our gracious Father through Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "that He may govern us" → "that He may govern and direct us" (*der vnns regiere/vnd füre*).
- "from the power of the devil" → "from the kingdom of the devil" (*für des Teuffels Reych*).
- "also defend our lives against all danger" → "preserve also our body and life from all misfortune" (*Bewar auch vnser leyb vnd leben/für allem vnglück*).
- "Thy merciful help" → "Thy gracious help" (*deine gnedige hilff*).
- "and both now and forever praise and glorify Thee" → "and here and hereafter, forever, praise and glorify Thee" (*hie vnd dort in ewigkeyt*).
- "through our Lord Jesus Christ, Thy Son," → "through Christ," which is Dietrich's phrase (*durch Christum*); the conclusion follows (§2.4c).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [41] The Ascension of Our Lord

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 122):** *Am tag der Himelfart Christi/Ephe. 4. Psal. 68.* [On the Day of Christ's Ascension. Ephesians 4; Psalm 68.]

**Verses (PDF p. 122)**

> CHristus ist auffgefaren in die höhe/vnnd hat das gefengnuß gefangen genommen/vnd hat den menschen gaben geben/Ephe. 4.
>
> Christus ist von todten aufferwecket/vnd sitzt zur rechten Gottes/vnd vertrit vns/Rom. 8.

Christ is ascended up on high, and hath led captivity captive, and hath given gifts unto men. Ephesians 4.

Christ is raised from the dead, and sitteth at the right hand of God, and maketh intercession for us. Romans 8.

**Summary (PDF pp. 123–124).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> DVrch die Himelfart Christi Jesu/habē wir dise zwo herrliche gaben/Das erstlich/der Teuffel/sünd/vnd todt/alß gefangene feynde/dem Herrn Christo zun füssen müssen ligen/Vnd können wider jhn vnd seine Kirch mehr nicht außrichten/denn was er/alß jr Herr jhnen henget vnd nachlest/nicht zum schaden oder nachtheyl der Christen/sonder das es jnen soll nützen/vñ zum besten dienen/wenn es gleich der feyndt am ergsten meyndt.

Through the ascension of Christ Jesus we have these two glorious gifts. First: that the devil, sin, and death must lie at the Lord Christ's feet as captive enemies, and can accomplish nothing more against Him and His Church than what He, as their Lord, permits and allows them; and that not to the harm or detriment of Christians, but so that it shall profit them and serve them for the best, even though the enemy means it for the very worst.

> Zum andern/gibt er den menschen gaben/das ist/er schenckt vns den heiligen Geist/der lehret vns Got durch das Euangelion recht erkennen vnd anrüffen/vnd tröstet in allerley vnglück/Darumb sollen wir vns der himelfart vnsers lieben Herren Christi trösten/vnd getrost in gewiser hoffnung vmb solche gabe bitten/das er den heyligen Geyst vnns schencken/vnd dem Teuffel/vnd seim reich wehren wölle/Alß der zur rechten Gottes [p. 124] sitzet/ein Herr vber alle Creatur/im himel vnd auf erden. Wir sollen aber der himelfart Christi auch inn disem fall zum trost vñ vnterricht brauchen/das wir erstlich vnser hoffnung dahin setzen/vnd nit zweyffeln sollen/wo er ist/da wöllen wir durch sein genad vnd hilff auch hin/Wie er im letzten Abendmal selb bitet: Vater ich wil wo ich bin/das sie auch sind. Zum andern/das wir vnser lebē darnach richten/vnd nach dem himlischen vñ ewigen trachten/vñ das zeitliche vns nit verfüren lassen/wie Paulus sagt: Wandelt in dem himlischen/vnd nit in dem jrdischen.

Secondly, He gives gifts to men; that is, He gives us the Holy Ghost, who teaches us to know God rightly through the gospel and to call upon Him, and comforts us in every kind of misfortune. Therefore we are to comfort ourselves with the ascension of our dear Lord Christ, and confidently, in sure hope, pray for this gift: that He would give us the Holy Ghost and restrain the devil and his kingdom, as He who sits at the right hand of God, Lord over every creature in heaven and on earth. But we are also to use the ascension of Christ in this respect for comfort and instruction: first, that we set our hope there, and are not to doubt that where He is, there we too shall go by His grace and help, as He Himself prays at the Last Supper: "Father, I will that where I am, they also may be." Secondly, that we order our life accordingly, and seek after what is heavenly and eternal, and do not let what is temporal lead us astray, as Paul says: "Walk in heavenly things, and not in earthly."

**Notes on the verses and summary**
- **The verses** are Ephesians 4:8 (Psalm 68:18) and Romans 8:34, both recast by Dietrich as statements about Christ and both given in King James wording. In the second, *von todten aufferwecket* ("raised from the dead") stands where the King James Version has "risen again." The two references are printed in the verses themselves.
- **"Permits and allows."** *henget vnd nachlest*: *verhängen* is to permit (something that God allows to befall).
- **"Means it for the very worst."** Compare Genesis 50:20, "ye thought evil against me; but God meant it unto good."
- **"Father, I will …"** John 17:24, from Christ's prayer at the Last Supper, shortened by Dietrich.
- **"Walk in heavenly things."** Not a quotation but a summary of Paul; compare Colossians 3:1–2 ("Set your affection on things above, not on things on the earth") and Philippians 3:19–20.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 124–125)**

> O Jesu Christe/du Son des allmechtigen Gottes/der du fürt an nicht mehr auff erden/arm vnnd ellend/sonder zur rechten deines Vatters/vnd ein Herr vber alles bist/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest deinen heyligen Geyst vns senden/fromme Kirchendiener geben/ob deinem wort halten/Dem Sathan vnd allen Tyrannen wehren/vnnd dein Reych auff erden gewaltig erhalten/biß alle deine feinde zu deinen füssen ligen/vnnd wir durch dich vber sünd/todt/vnd alles siegen/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> O Jesus Christ, Thou Son of the almighty God, who art henceforth no more on earth, poor and wretched, but art at the right hand of Thy Father, and Lord over all things: We beseech Thee, send us Thy Holy Ghost; give Thy Church pious pastors, preserve Thy word, restrain Satan and all tyrants, and mightily uphold Thy kingdom on earth, until all Thine enemies shall have been put under Thy feet, and that we may triumph over sin, death, and all things through Thee, *who livest and reignest with God the Father and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "Thou almighty Son of God" → "Thou Son of the almighty God" (*du Son des allmechtigen Gottes*).
- "who art no longer in humiliation here on earth, but sittest at the right hand of Thy Father, Lord over all things" → "who art henceforth no more on earth, poor and wretched, but art at the right hand of Thy Father, and Lord over all things" (*der du fürt an nicht mehr auff erden/arm vnnd ellend/sonder zur rechten deines Vatters/vnd ein Herr vber alles bist*).
- "control and restrain the devil and all who would oppress us" → "restrain Satan and all tyrants" (*Dem Sathan vnd allen Tyrannen wehren*).
- "mightily uphold Thy kingdom" → "mightily uphold Thy kingdom on earth" (*auff erden*).
- "that we may hold the victory over sin, death, and the devil, through Thee" → "and that we may triumph over sin, death, and all things through Thee" (*vnnd wir durch dich vber sünd/todt/vnd alles siegen*). Dietrich's list ends with "all things," not "the devil."
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit"; the OLM web text's "Amen," corrected.

**Note.** "Until all Thine enemies shall have been put under Thy feet" is TLHy's scriptural wording (1 Corinthians 15:25; Psalm 110:1) for *biß alle deine feinde zu deinen füssen ligen* ("until all Thine enemies lie at Thy feet"); it is kept.

#### [42] Sixth Sunday after Easter (Exaudi)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 125):** *Am sechsten Sontag nach Ostern/warnung der künfftigen verfolgung halb/Johan. 16.* [On the Sixth Sunday after Easter: a warning concerning the persecution to come. John 16.]

**Verse (PDF p. 126)**

> CHristus sprach zu seinen Jüngeren: Solches hab ich zu euch geredt/das jr euch nit ergert/Sie werden euch in den Bann thun/es kombt aber die zeyt/das/wer euch tödtet/wirdt meinen/er thue Gott einen dienst dran. Vnd solches werden sie euch darumb thun/das sie weder meinen Vatter/noch mich erkennen.

Christ said unto His disciples: These things have I spoken unto you, that ye should not be offended. They shall put you out of the synagogues: yea, the time cometh, that whosoever killeth you will think that he doeth God service. And these things will they do unto you, because they have not known my Father, nor me.

**Summary (PDF pp. 126–127)**

> DJß ist ein weyssagung/wie die Christen vmb des wortes willen/auff der welt müssen verfolget werden. Weil es aber für der vernunfft scheynet/alß sey Gott denen feind/die also vnter dem Creutz ligen/vnd sich leyden müssen/zeyget der Herr die vrsach an/wo solche verfolgung vñ haß wider die Christen herkomme/vnnd spricht: Es geschicht nit darumb/das euch Gott feynd sey/oder niches [sic, for nichts] guttes gönne/Sonder das die welt weder meinen vatter/noch mich kennet. [p. 127] Diß vrtheyl sollen wir fleyssig mercken/Denn ye vnd ye haben dise/die Christen am hefftigsten verfolget/so für der welt für from̃ vnd heylig geachtet sind. Zu Jerusalem waren es die Phariseer vnnd Schriff[t]gelerten vnd Hohenpriester/Heutigs tages ist es der Bapst/die Bischoffe/Münche vnd Pfaffen/die sich rhümen/sie sind allein die rechte Kirch. Aber gewiß ist es/sie kennen weder Christum noch seinen Vatter/sonst würden sie sein wort nicht verfolgen. Darumb erger sich niemandt an dem grossen scheyn vnd herrlichen namen/den die verfolger des worts bey der welt haben. Widerumb beschwere sich auch nyemandt des leydens vnnd der verfolgung/sintemal wir den schatz haben/vnd den ewigen Vatter vnnd seinen Son Christum Jesum recht kennen/Solches erkentnuß bringt vns ye den höchsten trost/das wir an Gottes hilff vnd gnad nit können zweyfflen/vnd macht vns endlich gar selig.

This is a prophecy of how Christians must be persecuted in the world for the word's sake. But since it seems to reason as though God were the enemy of those who thus lie under the cross and must suffer, the Lord shows the reason, whence such persecution and hatred against Christians comes, and says: It does not happen because God is your enemy, or begrudges you anything good, but because the world knows neither My Father nor Me. This judgment we are to mark diligently. For always it is those who are reckoned godly and holy before the world that have persecuted Christians most fiercely. At Jerusalem it was the Pharisees and scribes and chief priests; today it is the Pope, the bishops, monks and priests, who boast that they alone are the true Church. But it is certain that they know neither Christ nor His Father; otherwise they would not persecute His word. Therefore let no one be offended at the great show and glorious name that the persecutors of the word have before the world. On the other hand, let no one complain of suffering and persecution either, since we have the treasure, and rightly know the eternal Father and His Son, Jesus Christ. Such knowledge brings us the highest comfort, so that we cannot doubt God's help and grace, and at last makes us wholly blessed.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 16:1–3 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ said unto His disciples." Luther's *in den Bann thun* is "put under the ban," that is, excommunicate; the King James Version has "put you out of the synagogues." Dietrich's text has "my Father" (*meinen Vatter*) for the King James "the Father."
- **"Priests."** *Pfaffen*, a disparaging word for the Roman clergy.
- **"Life eternal."** Compare John 17:3: "this is life eternal, that they might know thee the only true God, and Jesus Christ, whom thou hast sent."
- **Print.** *niches* is a misprint for *nichts*. In *Schriff[t]gelerten* the second t is damaged, leaving only a speck.

**Prayer (PDF p. 128)**

> HERR Gott himlischer Vatter/wir dancken dir das du vns durch deinen heyligen Geyst zu zeugen deines Sones/vnsers lieben Herren Christi gesetzet hast/Vnd bitten dich/weyl die welt solches zeucknuß nicht kan dulden/vnd vns drüber zusetzet/du wöllest vns mut vnd trost verleyhen/das wir am Creutz vns nicht ergern/sonder bey deinem zeucknuß fest bleyben/vnd für vnd für bey dem hauffen gefunden werden/der dich vnd deinen Son kennet/biß wir endlich gar selig werden/durch Jhesum Christum deinen Son/vnd vnseren Herren/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we give thanks unto Thee, that by Thy Holy Ghost Thou hast appointed us to be witnesses of Thy Son, our dear Lord Christ: We beseech Thee, inasmuch as the world cannot endure such testimony, and assaileth us for it, grant us courage and comfort, that we may not be offended because of the cross, but continue steadfastly in Thy testimony, and be found always among those who know Thee and Thy Son, until at last we be wholly saved; through Jesus Christ, Thy Son and our Lord, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "to bear witness of Thy dear Son, our Lord Jesus Christ" → "to be witnesses of Thy Son, our dear Lord Christ" (*zu zeugen deines Sones/vnsers lieben Herren Christi*).
- "and persecutes us in every way" → "and assaileth us for it" (*vnd vns drüber zusetzet*).
- "until we obtain eternal salvation through the same, Thy Son, Jesus Christ our Lord," → "until at last we be wholly saved; through Jesus Christ, Thy Son and our Lord," which is Dietrich's own close (*biß wir endlich gar selig werden/durch Jhesum Christum deinen Son/vnd vnseren Herren*); the conclusion continues it (§2.4d).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit"; the OLM web text's "Holy Gaost" corrected.

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

### Pentecost and Trinity

#### [43] Pentecost

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 129):** *Am heyligen Pfingstag/Joel am 2.* [On Holy Pentecost Day. Joel 2.]

**Verse (PDF p. 129)**

> ES soll geschehen inn den letzten tagen/spricht Got: Jch will außgiessen von meinem Geyst/auff alles fleysch/vnd soll geschehen/Wer den namē des Herrn anrüffen wirt/soll selig werden.

It shall come to pass in the last days, saith God, I will pour out of my Spirit upon all flesh: and it shall come to pass, that whosoever shall call on the name of the Lord shall be saved.

**Summary (PDF pp. 129–131)**

> AM heyligen Pfingstag hat vnser lieber Herr Christus sehē lassen/wazu wir seiner aufferstehung vnd Himelfart [p. 130] geniessen sollen/Wir arme menschen sind durch die sünd so gar verderbet/das nichts gutes an vnns ist/So nun wir wöllen selig werden/müssen wir newe hertzen haben/vnd gar andere menschen werden. Dazu sendet vnser lieber Herr Christus seinen heyligen Geyst/der mit dem Vatter/vnd Son ewiger Gott ist/Das er vnsere hertzen endern/vnd andere menschen auß vns machen soll. Vnd richtet der heylige Geyst solchs auß/durch das heylige Euangelion/vnnd die selige Tauff/Denn er zündet die hertzen durch das wort an/das sie an Christum glauben/vnnd vergebung aller sünden durch den glauben empfangen/vnnd darnach lust vnd lieb haben an Gottes wort vnnd willen/Vnd dem bösen Geyst nicht hengen/sonder seinem eingeben widerstreben/vnd die sünd nicht lassen herschen/vñ der welt zorn vnd haß/auch alle fahr verachten. Solches alles sind des heiligen Geystes werck/die er in der menschen hertzen anrichtet/vnnd on des [p. 131] heyligen Geystes würckung/vns menschen gantz vnd gar vnmüglich sind/Denn von natur können wir mehr nicht/deñ das wir vns fur Gott fürchten/wir haben lust vñ liebe zu der sünde/vnd werden im leyden kleinmütig vnd trawrig/Solche angeborne vnart vnser hertzen endert der heylige Geyst/vnd erregt sonderlich die hertzen/das sie in der not vnd anfechtung auß kindtlichem vertrawen zu Gott vmb hilff schreyen vnd flehen/vñ für sünden von hertzen sich hüten. Für solche gnad sollen wir Gott von hertzen dancken/vnnd teglich jn darumb bitten/das er sie vns mehren/vnd nicht entziehen wölle.

On holy Pentecost our dear Lord Christ has let us see what benefit we are to have from His resurrection and ascension. We poor men are so wholly corrupted by sin that there is nothing good in us. If, then, we would be saved, we must have new hearts and become altogether different men. For this purpose our dear Lord Christ sends His Holy Ghost, who with the Father and the Son is eternal God, that He may change our hearts and make other men of us. And the Holy Ghost accomplishes this through the holy gospel and blessed baptism. For through the word He kindles hearts, so that they believe in Christ and receive the forgiveness of all sins through faith, and thereafter have delight and love in God's word and will; and do not follow the evil spirit, but resist his promptings, and do not let sin reign, and despise the wrath and hatred of the world, and every danger besides. All these are works of the Holy Ghost, which He brings about in men's hearts, and which without the working of the Holy Ghost are utterly impossible for us men. For by nature we can do no more than be afraid before God; we have delight and love for sin, and in suffering become fainthearted and sad. This inborn perversity of our hearts the Holy Ghost changes, and above all He stirs up hearts, so that in need and temptation they cry and plead to God for help out of childlike trust, and guard themselves against sins from their hearts. For such grace we are to thank God from our hearts, and pray Him daily that He would increase it in us and not withdraw it.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Joel 2:28 and 2:32 (Luther 3:1, 5), as Peter quotes them at Pentecost (Acts 2:17, 21, whence "saith God"), in King James wording. The section takes the prophecy as its text rather than the Gospel of the day (Appendix D).
- **"Follow."** *hengen* (with dative) is to hang on someone, to adhere to him.
- **"Perversity."** *vnart*, a bad or unnatural disposition.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 131–132)**

> O Herr Jhesu Christe/du Sun des Allmechtigen Gottes/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest durch dein liebes wort deinen heyligen Geyst in vnsere hertzen geben/das der selbe vns regier vnnd füre nach deinem willen/vnnd in allerley anfechtung vnd vnglück vns tröste/vnd in deiner warheyt/wider alle jrrthumb vns leyte/auff das wir im glaubē fest bestehen/inn der liebe vnnd allen guten wercken zunemen/vnd durch die gewisse hoffnung deiner gnad/so du vns mit deim sterben erworben hast/ewig selig werdē/der du regierest mit dem Vatter/vnnd heyligem Geyst in ewigkeyt/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> O Lord Jesus Christ, Thou Son of the almighty God: We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts through Thy dear word, that He may govern and direct us according to Thy will, comfort us in every temptation and misfortune, and lead us in Thy truth against every error, so that we may continue steadfast in the faith, increase in love and all good works, and through the sure hope of Thy grace, which by Thy death Thou hast purchased for us, obtain eternal salvation, Thou who reignest, with the Father and the Holy Ghost, world without end. Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "Thou almighty Son of God" → "Thou Son of the almighty God" (*du Sun des Allmechtigen Gottes*), as at [41].
- "send Thy Holy Spirit into our hearts, through Thy word" → "give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts through Thy dear word" (*durch dein liebes wort deinen heyligen Geyst in vnsere hertzen geben*).
- "rule and govern us" → "govern and direct us" (*vns regier vnnd füre*), as at [34] and [40].
- "and defend us by Thy truth against every error" → "and lead us in Thy truth against every error" (*vnd in deiner warheyt/wider alle jrrthumb vns leyte*).
- "and firmly trusting in Thy grace, which through death Thou hast purchased for us" → "and through the sure hope of Thy grace, which by Thy death Thou hast purchased for us" (*vnd durch die gewisse hoffnung deiner gnad/so du vns mit deim sterben erworben hast*).
- The conclusion is Dietrich's own (*der du regierest mit dem Vatter/vnnd heyligem Geyst in ewigkeyt*). TLHy's rendering of it is kept, and no ending is added (§2.4g).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [44] Monday after Pentecost

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 132):** *Am andern Pfingstag* [On the Second Day of Pentecost.] No Scripture reference is printed; the text expounded is John 3:16–21.

**Verse (PDF p. 133).** The heading (p. 132) has no reference, and the woodcut beneath it is the Crucifixion.

> ALso hat Gott die welt geliebet/das er seinen eingebornen Sun gab/Auff das alle/die an jn glauben/nit verlorn werden/sonder das ewige leben haben. Denn Gott hat seinen Sun nicht gesendet/das er die welt richte/sonder das die welt durch jn selig werde. Wer glaubt/wird nit gerichtet/wer aber nicht glaubt/ist schon gerichtet/Darumb das er nicht glaubt an den namen des eingebornen Sun Gottes.

God so loved the world, that he gave his only begotten Son, that whosoever believeth in him should not perish, but have everlasting life. For God sent not his Son to condemn the world; but that the world through him might be saved. He that believeth is not condemned: but he that believeth not is condemned already, because he hath not believed in the name of the only begotten Son of God.

**Summary (PDF pp. 133–134)**

> DJß ist gleich wie ein summa des gantzen Euangelij/oder der predigt/welche der heylig Geyst in der welt füret/vnd dadurch die hertzen endert/Denn wo das inn den hertzen gewiß ist/das Gott vns vnser sünden halb/nit feinde sey/sonder er habe vns lieb/vnnd wölle vns durch seinen Sun selig machen/Da fürchten sich vnsere hertzen nicht mehr für Gott/sonder [p. 134] halten sich zu jm/gewarten hilff vnd trost von jm/vñ sind willig/alles das zu thun/was sie wissen das es jm wolgefelt/vnd er haben will. Sie rüffen jhn an in anfechtung/vmb hilff/Sie erkennen sein wort vnnd gnad/loben vnnd preysen jn/Da dagegen/wo dise predigt nicht ist/die hertzē von Got fliehen/für jm sich fürchten/vnd inn der not verzagen/So lerne nun dise predigt/das Gott vns lieb hat/vnnd seinen Sun vns schencket/vñ will das wir an jn glaubē/das ist/das wir vns sein sollen trösten/vnnd durch jn selig werdē. An diser kunst ligt es gar/Es ist aber nit ein mēschen kunst/der heylige Geyst muß hie meister sein/sonst ist es vngelernet. Nu haben wir aber die verheissung lauter vnnd klar/das Gott sein heyligen Geyst denen wolle geben/so jn darumb bitten. Darumb sollen wir mit dem Gebet anhalten/vnd vmb den glauben an dise verheissung bitten.

This is, as it were, a summary of the whole gospel, or of the preaching which the Holy Ghost carries on in the world and by which He changes hearts. For where this is certain in the heart, that God is not our enemy on account of our sins, but loves us and will save us through His Son, there our hearts are no longer afraid of God, but hold to Him, look to Him for help and comfort, and are willing to do all that they know pleases Him and that He would have. They call upon Him for help in temptation; they acknowledge His word and grace, and praise and glorify Him. Whereas, on the other hand, where this preaching is not, hearts flee from God, are afraid of Him, and despair in need. Learn, then, this preaching: that God loves us and gives us His Son, and wills that we believe in Him, that is, that we comfort ourselves with Him and are saved through Him. On this art everything depends. But it is not an art of men; the Holy Ghost must be the master here, or it remains unlearned. Now we have the promise, plain and clear, that God will give His Holy Ghost to those who ask Him for it. Therefore we are to persevere in prayer, and pray for faith in this promise.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 3:16–18 in King James wording. Dietrich's text lacks "For" at the start, "into the world" and "on him," and the translation follows him.
- **"Summary."** *summa*, the sum and substance.
- **The promise.** Luke 11:13: "how much more shall your heavenly Father give the Holy Spirit to them that ask him?"
- **The last lines** of the summary are set as a tapering block (p. 134), a common way of ending a section.

**Prayer (PDF p. 135)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/der du auß väterlicher lieb gegen vns arme sünder/deinen Sun vns geschencket hast/das wir an jn glauben/vñ durch den glauben sollen selig werden/Wir bitten dich/gib deinen heyligen Geyst in vnsere hertzen/das wir in solchem glauben/biß ans ende beharren/vnd selig werden/Durch Jesum Christum vnseren lieben Herren. AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who of Thy fatherly love toward us poor sinners hast given us Thy Son, that we should believe in Him, and through faith be saved: We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts, that we may continue steadfast in such faith unto the end, and be saved; through Jesus Christ, our dear Lord, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- Restored "toward us poor sinners" (*gegen vns arme sünder*), which TLHy omits.
- "that through faith in Him we may be saved" → "that we should believe in Him, and through faith be saved" (*das wir an jn glauben/vñ durch den glauben sollen selig werden*).
- "grant us Thy Holy Spirit in our hearts" → "give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts" (*gib deinen heyligen Geyst in vnsere hertzen*).
- "and thus obtain everlasting salvation" → "and be saved" (*vnd selig werden*).
- "through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord," → "through Jesus Christ, our dear Lord," which is Dietrich's own close (*Durch Jesum Christum vnseren lieben Herren*); the conclusion continues it (§2.4d).

#### [45] Festival of the Holy Trinity, first prayer

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 136):** *Vom Fest der heyligen Tryfeltigkeyt/Matth. 4.* [Of the Feast of the Holy Trinity. Matthew 4 [sic].] The text expounded is Matthew 3:16–17 ("Da Jesus getauffet war…"), so "4" is a misprint for "3."

**Verse (PDF pp. 136–137)**

> DA Jesus getauffet war/steyg er bald herauff auß dem wasser/Vnd sihe/da thet sich der himel auff vber jm/vnnd Johannes sahe den Geyst Gottes/gleych alß eine Taube berab [sic, for herab] faren/vnd vber jn kommen/vnnd sihe/eine stim̃ vom himel herab sprach: Diß ist mein lieber Sune [p. 137]/an welchem ich wolgefallen (das ist/alle frewde) habe.

When Jesus was baptized, he went up straightway out of the water: and, lo, the heavens were opened unto him, and John saw the Spirit of God descending like a dove, and lighting upon him: and lo a voice from heaven said, This is my beloved Son, in whom I am well pleased (that is, in whom I have all joy).

**Summary (PDF pp. 137–139)**

> DEn heutigē Sontag nennet man den tag der heyligen Trifeltigkeyt/auff dz alle Christen lernē/was sie von Gott glauben/vñ wie sie Gott recht erkennen sollen. Denn an disem stück fehlet es allen menschen auff erden. Weder Türcken/Juden noch Heyden wissen was Gott sey/Allein die Christen wissen es/das Got der ewige Vatter/in ewigkeyt einen Sun gezeuget/vnnd das der heylige Geyst vom Vatter vnnd dem Sune in ewigkeyt außgehe/Das also Gott heyst/vñ ist/Got Vatter/Gott Sun/Gott heyliger Geyst. Diser eynige ewige Got/aber drey vnterschiedne Personen/haben sich gegen vns menschen also offenbaret/das Got der Vatter/durch sein wort vns/vnnd alle Creatur erschaffen hat/vnd für vnnd für erhelt. Got der Sun/ist auß des Vaters willen mensch worden/vnnd hat [p. 138] vns mit seinem todt von sünden erlediget/vnnd mit Gott versünet. Gott der heylig Geyst/kompt durch die predig des Euangelij in vnsere hertzen/vnnd zündet den glauben in vns an/vnd heyliget vns/das wir durch den glauben vergebung der sünden habē/vnd der sünde widerstehen. Also sihest du/wie die gantze Gottheyt in gnaden gegen vns sich auffthun/vnd wir alles zeytliches vnd ewiges allein durch die gnade Gottes haben. Darumb sollen wir solchen gnedigen Gott loben vnd lieben/vnd gern alles thun/das wir wissen das er haben will. Da vnser lieber Herr Christus getauffet ist worden/hat sich Gott auff dise weyse offenbaret. Das Johannes den heyligen Geyst sihet/vber dem Herren Christo in gestalt einer Tauben. Got Vatter aber im Himel redet mit verstendlicher stimme/vnnd zeuget: Das ist mein Sun/Derhalben sollen wir an solchem glauben fest halten/Gott Vatter/Gott Sun/Gott heyliger Geyst/sey der eynige allmechtige [p. 139] ewige Gott/vnser aller schöpffer/erlöser vnd seligmacher/vñ sollen solchen Gott in allen nöten anruffen/vñ seiner gnad vnd hilff vns trösten.

Today's Sunday is called the day of the Holy Trinity, so that all Christians may learn what they are to believe of God, and how they are to know God rightly. For in this point all men on earth are lacking. Neither Turks, Jews nor heathen know what God is. Christians alone know it: that God the eternal Father has begotten a Son from eternity, and that the Holy Ghost proceeds from the Father and the Son from eternity; so that God is called, and is, God the Father, God the Son, God the Holy Ghost. This one eternal God, yet three distinct Persons, has revealed Himself toward us men in this way: that God the Father has created us and every creature through His word, and preserves them continually; God the Son became man by the Father's will, and has delivered us from sins by His death and reconciled us with God; God the Holy Ghost comes into our hearts through the preaching of the gospel, kindles faith in us, and sanctifies us, so that through faith we have forgiveness of sins and resist sin. So you see how the whole Godhead opens itself toward us in grace, and how we have everything temporal and eternal by the grace of God alone. Therefore we are to praise and love so gracious a God, and gladly do everything that we know He would have. When our dear Lord Christ was baptized, God revealed Himself in this way: John sees the Holy Ghost above the Lord Christ in the form of a dove, while God the Father in heaven speaks with an intelligible voice and testifies: "This is my Son." Therefore we are to hold fast to this faith: God the Father, God the Son, God the Holy Ghost is the one almighty, eternal God, the Creator, Redeemer and Savior of us all; and we are to call upon this God in every need, and comfort ourselves with His grace and help.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 3:16–17, as at the Sunday after New Year ([10]), in King James wording, with Dietrich's "John saw" and his gloss "(that is, all joy)" on the Father's good pleasure. The heading's "Matth. 4." is a misprint (Appendix D). In the print the first *herab* is set *berab*.
- **"Turks."** The Ottoman Turks, that is, Muslims.
- **"Proceeds from the Father and the Son."** The language of the Nicene Creed (*qui ex Patre Filioque procedit*).
- **"Has revealed Himself."** The print has a plural verb (*haben sich … offenbaret*) after "three distinct Persons."
- **"Opens itself."** *sich auffthun*, as printed (an infinitive where the finite *auffthut* would be expected).

**Assignment:** TLHy's Festival of the Holy Trinity, beside TLHy's present collect. That collect is Dietrich's *second* Trinity prayer [46], which comes from the separate section *An der heyligen Trifaltigkeyt tag/wie der Herr Nicodemum lehret* [On the Day of the Holy Trinity, how the Lord teaches Nicodemus], PDF pp. 140–143.

**Prayer (PDF p. 139)**

> ALmechtiger ewiger Gott/Gott Vatter/Gott Sun/Gott heyliger Geyst/der du dein gnad vns menschē in dem erzeygt/vnnd dein barmhertzigkeyt reychlich hast sehen lassen/das erstlich du ewiger Vater/vns zu menschen erschaffen/vnd du ewiger Sun Gottes/für vns mensch wordē/vñ für vnsere sünde am Creutz bezalet/vnd du ewiger Geyst/der du vom Vater vñ Sun in ewigkeyt außgehest/vns durch das heylige Euangelion glaubig gemacht/vnd geheyliget hast/Wir bitten dich/O eyniger/ewiger vnd allmechtiger Gott/Gott Vater/gott Sun/gott heyliger geyst erhalte vns in deiner gnad/biß an dz ende/vnd mache vns ewig selig/AMEN.

**New translation**

> Almighty and everlasting God, God the Father, God the Son, God the Holy Ghost, who hast shown Thy grace unto us men, and hast richly displayed Thy mercy, in that, first, Thou, eternal Father, didst create us men; and Thou, eternal Son of God, didst become man for us, and didst pay for our sins upon the cross; and Thou, eternal Spirit, who from eternity proceedest from the Father and the Son, hast made us believers through the holy gospel, and hast sanctified us: We beseech Thee, O one, eternal, and almighty God, God the Father, God the Son, God the Holy Ghost, keep us in Thy grace unto the end, and save us eternally; *who livest and reignest, one God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Rendering choices.**
  - *erstlich* opens a list ("first … and … and"); it does not mean "at the beginning."
  - *vns zu menschen erschaffen* means "created us human beings."
  - *Gott heyliger Geyst* is "God the Holy Ghost," as TLHy writes in Trinitarian formulas.
- **Ending.** The prayer is addressed to the Holy Trinity, so it takes the prayer-book ending for such a prayer, "who livest and reignest, one God, world without end" (§2.4f).

#### [46] Festival of the Holy Trinity, second prayer

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 140):** *An der heyligen Trifaltigkeyt tag/wie der Herr Nicodemum lehret/von der seligkeyt/wie wir inn himel mögen kommen/Joh. 3.* [On the Day of the Holy Trinity: how the Lord teaches Nicodemus concerning salvation, how we may come into heaven. John 3.]

**Verse (PDF p. 140)**

> ES sey denn/das yemand geborn werde auß dem wasser vñ geyst/so kan er nicht in das reych Gottes kommen/Was vom fleysch geboren wirdt/das ist fleisch/vnnd was vom Geyst geboren wirdt/das ist Geyst.

Except a man be born of water and of the Spirit, he cannot enter into the kingdom of God. That which is born of the flesh is flesh; and that which is born of the Spirit is spirit.

**Summary (PDF pp. 141–142)**

> VNser lieber Herr Christus lehret in disen wortē/wie wir müssen selig werden. Vernunfft gedēckt/wenn wir ein züchtigen wadel [sic, for wandel] füren/mit der hand nicht tödtē/nit stelē/noch anders thun das ergerlich sey so habe man gnug zum ewigen leben. Aber vnser lieber Herr Christus sagt/so wir selig werden sollen/müssen wir andere menschen/vñ von newem geborn werden/es sey nit genug das wir die hende/augen/zungen zemen/das sie nicht vnrecht thun/das hertz müsse gar anders werden. Solches geschehe aber/wenn wir im namen/vnnd in den todt Christi getauffet werden/durch solche tauff wölle der heylig Geyst in die hertzen kommen/den glauben drinn anzünden/vnd gar newe menschen auß vns machen. Denn durch den glauben werden wir von sünden reyn/vnnd der heylig Geyst hilfft wider die sünde vns streyten/vnnd weyset weytrer [sic, for weyter] vns zum gehorsam Gottes/Jst allso [p. 142] diß der eynige weg zur seligkeyt/an Christum glaubē/Solches können wir von vns selb nicht/es ist des heyligen geystes werck/der will durch das wort Gottes/vñ die heyligen Sacrament/den glauben in vns anzünden vnnd erhalten.

In these words our dear Lord Christ teaches how we must be saved. Reason thinks that if we lead a decent life, do not kill with our hand, do not steal, nor do anything else that gives offense, then we have enough for eternal life. But our dear Lord Christ says that if we are to be saved, we must become other men and be born anew. It is not enough that we tame our hands, eyes and tongues, so that they do no wrong; the heart must become wholly different. This happens, however, when we are baptized in the name and into the death of Christ. Through such baptism the Holy Ghost will come into the hearts, kindle faith in them, and make wholly new men of us. For through faith we are made clean from sins, and the Holy Ghost helps us to fight against sin, and further directs us to obedience to God. This, then, is the one way to salvation: to believe in Christ. This we cannot do of ourselves; it is the work of the Holy Ghost, who will kindle and preserve faith in us through the word of God and the holy sacraments.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 3:5–6 in King James wording.
- **"Decent life."** *züchtigen wandel*, an orderly, respectable conduct. *wadel* lacks its n (or the bar that stands for it).
- **"Born anew."** *von newem geborn*: John 3:3, 7 (King James "born again").
- **"Into the death of Christ."** Romans 6:3, as at Advent 4 ([4]).
- **Two Trinity sections.** This is the second section for Trinity Sunday, on the Nicodemus Gospel (John 3:1–15), the one TLHy uses; the first ([45]) treats Christ's baptism (Appendix D).

**Relation to TLHy.** This is the prayer TLHy uses for the Festival of the Holy Trinity. Dietrich's first Trinity prayer, from the section on Matthew 3, is [45].

**Prayer (PDF pp. 142–143)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/wir arme sünder bekennen/das nichts gutes an vns ist/vnd wir vnserthalben musten in sünden vnd ewigem todt verderben/Sintemal was vom fleysch geborn wirdt/ist fleysch/vnnd kan dein Reych nicht sehen/Wir bitten dich aber/sey gnedig vnd barmhertzig/vñ sende deinen heyligen Geyst/vmb deines Suns Christi Jesu willen in vnsere hertzen/vnd mache newe menschen auß vns/das wir vergebung der sünden durch Christum/wie vns in der Tauff zugesagt/gewiß glauben/vnd in der lieb gegen vnserm Nechstem/vnd allen andern Christlichen tugenden von tag zu tag zunemen/biß wir endlich selig werden. AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father: We poor sinners confess that there is no good thing in us, and that, left to ourselves, we must perish in sin and eternal death, since that which is born of the flesh is flesh and cannot see Thy kingdom. But we beseech Thee: Be gracious and merciful, and for the sake of Thy Son, Jesus Christ, send Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts, and make new men of us, that we may firmly believe the forgiveness of sins through Christ, according to Thy promise in baptism, and that we may daily increase in love toward our neighbor, and in all other Christian virtues, until at last we be saved; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "O Lord God" → "Lord God" (*HErr Gott*); the "O" is not in the German.
- "that in our flesh dwelleth no good thing" → "that there is no good thing in us" (*das nichts gutes an vns ist*).
- "we die and perish in sin" → "we must perish in sin and eternal death" (*musten in sünden vnd ewigem todt verderben*).
- "and cannot see the kingdom of God" → "and cannot see Thy kingdom" (*vnnd kan dein Reych nicht sehen*). Dietrich turns the words of John 3:3 into address to God.
- "Grant us Thy grace and mercy" → "Be gracious and merciful" (*sey gnedig vnd barmhertzig*).
- "that being regenerate, we may firmly believe the forgiveness of sins" → "and make new men of us, that we may firmly believe the forgiveness of sins through Christ" (*vnd mache newe menschen auß vns/das wir vergebung der sünden durch Christum ... gewiß glauben*).
- "daily increase in brotherly love, and in other good works" → "daily increase in love toward our neighbor, and in all other Christian virtues" (*in der lieb gegen vnserm Nechstem/vnd allen andern Christlichen tugenden von tag zu tag zunemen*).
- "until we at last obtain eternal salvation" → "until at last we be saved" (*biß wir endlich selig werden*). Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics (§2.4b).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

### The Trinity season

#### [47] First Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 143):** *Am ersten Sontag/vom reychen Mann/vnd armen Lazaro/Luce am 16.* [On the First Sunday: of the rich man and poor Lazarus. Luke 16.]

**Verse (PDF p. 144)**

> ABraham sprach zum Reychen Mann: Gedencke Sun/das du dein gutes empfangen hast in deinem leben/vnd Lazarus dagegen hat böses empfangen. Nun aber wirdt er getröstet/vnnd du wirdst gepeyniget.

Abraham said unto the rich man, Son, remember that thou in thy lifetime receivedst thy good things, and likewise Lazarus evil things: but now he is comforted, and thou art tormented.

**Summary (PDF pp. 144–145)**

> DJsen spruch sollē wir fleyssig mercken/Deñ weyl die Christen auff erden sich vil müssen leyden/vnd es dagegen den vnchristen vnd bösen buben/nach all jrem willen gehet/Haben die Christen allein disen trost der hie stehet. Nemlich/das es nach disem leben sich zu beden theylen werde vmbkeren/Welchen es hie auff erden wol gehet/vnd alles gnug haben/vnnd one anfechtung sind/vnnd derhalb alles thun was sie gelüstet/one alle schew/den selben soll es dort in ewigkeyt/wie dem Reychen Mann/vbel gehn/Widerum̃ aber/die sich hie [p. 145] auff erden vil müssen leyden/vñ doch im leyden gedultig sind/vnnd durch Christum auff den ewigen trost hoffen/die sollen mit dem armen Lazaro ewig selig werden. Derhalb erschrecke niemand für dem Creutz vnd leyden/weyl dise ewige hoffnūg dabey ist. Widerumb/vberhebe sich niemand gelts vnd guts/sonder fürchte Gott/vnd brauche seines segens/zu Gottes ehr/vnnd des negsten nutz/so soll es nicht not haben.

This saying we are to mark diligently. For since Christians on earth must suffer much, while unchristian men and wicked knaves have everything go just as they will, Christians have this comfort alone which stands here: namely, that after this life it will be reversed for both parties. Those with whom it goes well here on earth, who have enough of everything and are without temptation, and therefore do whatever they please without any scruple, it shall go ill with them yonder in eternity, as with the rich man. But on the other hand those who must suffer much here on earth, and yet are patient in suffering and hope through Christ for the eternal comfort, shall be blessed forever with poor Lazarus. Therefore let no one be terrified at the cross and suffering, since this eternal hope goes with it. On the other hand, let no one be puffed up over money and goods, but let him fear God, and use His blessing to God's honor and his neighbor's good; then there shall be no danger.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 16:25 in King James wording, introduced by "Abraham said unto the rich man."
- **"Without any scruple."** *one alle schew*, without any shyness or restraint (*Scheu*).
- **"There shall be no danger."** *so soll es nicht not haben*, an idiom: then there is nothing to fear.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 145–146)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest vnsere hertzē durch deinen heyligen Geyst/also füren vnd regieren/das wir dein liebes wort/nit/wie der reyche Man/one frucht hören/vnd mit dem zeytlichen gut nit also vmbgehn/das wir des ewigen dabey vergessen/sonder armen leuten gern vñ miltigklich nach vnserm vermögen helffen/vnd mit hoffart vnd vberfluß vns nit versündigen/auch wo wir mit dem Creutz vnnd vnglück bedranget werden/nicht verzagen/sonder alle hoffnung an deiner ewigen hilffe vnd genad haben/vnd in gedult alles vberwinden. Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we beseech Thee so to direct and govern our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may not, like the rich man, hear Thy dear word without fruit, nor so occupy ourselves with temporal goods as to forget things eternal; but that we readily and liberally, according to our ability, minister to such as are in need, and sin not through pride and excess; and that, when we are oppressed with cross and misfortune, we may not despair, but put all our hope in Thine eternal help and grace, and in patience overcome all things; *through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "so to rule and govern our hearts by Thy Holy Spirit" → "so to direct and govern our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost" (*vnsere hertzē durch deinen heyligen Geyst/also füren vnd regieren*).
- "hear Thy word in vain" → "hear Thy dear word without fruit" (*dein liebes wort ... one frucht hören*). TLHy has "in vain" here and "without fruit" at [48], where the German has the reverse.
- "and become so devoted to things temporal as to forget things eternal" → "nor so occupy ourselves with temporal goods as to forget things eternal" (*vnd mit dem zeytlichen gut nit also vmbgehn/das wir des ewigen dabey vergessen*).
- "readily and according to our ability" → "readily and liberally, according to our ability" (*gern vñ miltigklich nach vnserm vermögen*).
- "and not defile ourselves with surfeiting and pride" → "and sin not through pride and excess" (*vnd mit hoffart vnd vberfluß vns nit versündigen*).
- "in trial and misfortune keep us from despair" → "and that, when we are oppressed with cross and misfortune, we may not despair" (*auch wo wir mit dem Creutz vnnd vnglück bedranget werden/nicht verzagen*). Dietrich continues the petition; TLHy turns it into a new request.
- "and grant us to put our trust wholly in Thy fatherly help and grace" → "but put all our hope in Thine eternal help and grace" (*sonder alle hoffnung an deiner ewigen hilffe vnd genad haben*).
- "so that in faith and Christian patience we may overcome all things" → "and in patience overcome all things" (*vnd in gedult alles vberwinden*). "In faith and Christian" is not in the German. Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics.

#### [48] Second Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 146):** *Am anderē Sontag/nach Trinitatis/das gleychnuß vom grossen Abendmal/Luce. 14.* [On the Second Sunday after Trinity: the parable of the great supper. Luke 14.]

**Verse (PDF p. 147)**

> GEhe auß auff die landstrassen/vnd an die zeune/vnd nötige sie herein zukommen/auf das mein hause vol werde/Jch sage euch aber/das der Männer keyner/die geladen sind/mein Abendmal schmecken wirdt.

Go out into the highways and hedges, and compel them to come in, that my house may be filled. For I say unto you, That none of those men which were bidden shall taste of my supper.

**Summary (PDF pp. 147–148)**

> VNser lieber Herr Christus lehret in disem gleychnuß/vom Abendmal zwey ding/Erstlich/das Gott so gnedig vnnd barmhertzig ist/vñ gern yederman durch seinen Son Christum Jesum wolte selig machen/Denn er lest jhn nicht vmb des/oder yenes willen/mensch werden vnd leyden/Er soll das Lemlein sein/das der gantzen welt sünde wegnimbt/vnnd legts auff sich vnd bezalet dafür/vnd fehlet ferner an nichte/denn das alle arme sünder die sünde erkennen/vñ solche gnad vnd wolthat/des Sone Gottes annemen/vnd der selben sich trösten/das er vnser sünde von vns [p. 148] genommen/auff sich geladen vnd dafür bezalet hat/Das ist das erste.

Our dear Lord Christ teaches two things in this parable of the supper. First, that God is so gracious and merciful, and would gladly save everyone through His Son, Jesus Christ. For He lets Him become man and suffer not for the sake of this one or that one: He is to be the Lamb that takes away the sin of the whole world, and lays it upon Himself and pays for it. And nothing further is lacking but that all poor sinners acknowledge their sin, receive this grace and benefit of the Son of God, and comfort themselves with it, that He has taken our sin from us, laid it upon Himself, and paid for it. That is the first.

> Das ander ist/das leyder die welt so blind vnd ellend ist/vñ will solcher gnade nicht/Etliche halten sich für from̃/vnnd gedencken sie dürffen der gnade nicht/Etliche sind mechtig vñ reych/die verachten das Euangelion gar/Etliche gehn noch damit vm̃/das sie reych werden/vñ schlagen sich in mancherley hendel/die haben so vil weil nicht/das sie mit dem Euangelion köndten vmbgehen/Aber da folget ein schwerlich vrteil/Wer das Euangelion also verachtet/soll nimmermehr selig werden. Darum̃ sollen wir Gottes wort fleyssig hören/vnd es vnsern höchsten trost vnnd besten schatz lassen sein/so werden wir dadurch selig werden. Solchs aber thun nur die krüppel vnnd lamen/so für der welt ellend vnd arm sind/vnnd jre sünde vnd missethat erkennen.

The second is that, alas, the world is so blind and wretched, and will not have this grace. Some hold themselves to be godly, and think they have no need of grace. Some are mighty and rich; they despise the gospel altogether. Some are still busy getting rich, and involve themselves in all manner of business; they have not so much leisure that they could occupy themselves with the gospel. But there follows a grievous judgment: whoever thus despises the gospel shall never be saved. Therefore we are to hear God's word diligently, and let it be our highest comfort and best treasure; so shall we be saved by it. But this only the crippled and the lame do, who are wretched and poor before the world and acknowledge their sins and misdeeds.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 14:23–24 in King James wording; Dietrich omits "the lord said unto the servant."
- **"Not for the sake of this one or that one."** *nicht vmb des/oder yenes willen*: not for any one person only, but for the whole world.
- **"The Lamb."** John 1:29.
- **"The crippled and the lame."** Luke 14:21: "bring in hither the poor, and the maimed, and the halt, and the blind."
- **"Leisure."** *weil*, time to spare (*Weile*).

**Prayer (PDF p. 149)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/wir dancken dir/das du vns zu deiner Malzeit dem ewigen leben/durch dein liebes wort hast lassen laden/Vñ bitten dich/du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst vnsere hertzen erwecken/das wir dein wort nicht vergeblich hören/vns zu solcher Malzeyt recht schicken/vnd das zeytliche vns nit dran hindern lassen/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we give thanks unto Thee, that through Thy dear word Thou hast called us to Thy supper, even to eternal life, and we beseech Thee: Quicken our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may not hear Thy word in vain, but that we may prepare ourselves rightly for such a supper, and not suffer ourselves to be hindered therein by things temporal; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "through Thy holy word" → "through Thy dear word" (*durch dein liebes wort*).
- "to Thy great supper" → "to Thy supper, even to eternal life" (*zu deiner Malzeit dem ewigen leben*). "Great" comes from the Gospel (Luke 14:16), not the prayer; Dietrich names the supper as eternal life.
- "without fruit" → "in vain" (*vergeblich*); see [47].
- "for Thy kingdom" → "for such a supper" (*zu solcher Malzeyt*).
- "by any worldly care" → "therein by things temporal" (*vnd das zeytliche vns nit dran hindern lassen*).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit"; the OLM web text's "Son. Jesus Christ" corrected to "Son, Jesus Christ."

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [49] Third Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 149):** *Am dritten Sontag nach Trinitatis/von dem verlornen schefflein/welchem der Hirt nachgehet/vnd es suchet/Luc. 19. [sic, for Luke 15]* [On the Third Sunday after Trinity: of the lost sheep, which the shepherd goes after and seeks. Luke 15.]

**Verse (PDF p. 150)**

> ES wirdt frewde im Himel sein/vber ein sünder/der busse thut/für neun vnnd neuntzig gerechten/die der busse nit bedürffen.

Joy shall be in heaven over one sinner that repenteth, more than over ninety and nine just persons, which need no repentance.

**Summary (PDF pp. 150–152).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> DAs ist zu gleych ein trost/vnd ein lehr für alle arme sünder/die gern vom zorn Gottes wolten ledig sein/Denn vnser lieber Herre Christus sagt in disem gleichnuß/eim sünder sey eben/wie eim Schefflein/das in die jrre gerathen/vnd sich von [p. 151] seim Hirten verlassen hat/Er aber wölle die sünder nicht in der jrre lassen/sonder durch sein wort sie suchen/vnd wider zu recht bringen/Das ist doch ye ein grosser trost. Deñ wir gedencken/wenn wir vnser sünde gewar werden/Got sey vns feind/vnd wölle sich vnser nicht mehr annemen/so er doch wie ein Hyrt/dem jrrigen Schefflein nachgehen/vnd es wider suchen/vnnd inn sein stellin bringen will.

This is at once a comfort and a teaching for all poor sinners who would gladly be set free from the wrath of God. For our dear Lord Christ says in this parable that it is with a sinner just as with a little sheep that has gone astray and has strayed from its shepherd. But He will not leave sinners in their straying, but will seek them through His word and bring them back to the right way. That is surely a great comfort. For when we become aware of our sin, we think that God is our enemy and will no longer take care of us; whereas He will, like a shepherd, go after the straying little sheep, and seek it again, and bring it into His little fold.

> Dagegen aber sollen die Schefflein auff des Hirten stym̃ gute acht haben/vnd jnen nit vergebens lassen nachgehn/sonder sich zu jrem Hirten der sie suchet/wenden/das ist/die sünder sollen buß thun/vnd wenn Christus sie durch sein wort suchet/von sünden abfordert/straffet vñ vermanet/sollen sie hören/vñ in der jrre nit fort lauffen/sonder von der sünde ablassen/rew vnd leyd drüber haben/vnd sich des Herrn Christi vnd seiner gnade trösten/so wil Christus sie mit frewden annemen/vñ wie ein hirt jr warten [p. 152]/das sie nicht allein jhr futter haben/sonder auch inn ewigkeyt/wider den wolff den Teuffel sollen beschützet sein/Amen.

But on the other hand the little sheep are to give good heed to the shepherd's voice, and not let him go after them in vain, but turn to their shepherd who seeks them. That is: sinners are to repent; and when Christ seeks them through His word, calls them away from sins, rebukes and admonishes them, they are to listen, and not run on in their straying, but leave off sin, be sorry and grieved for it, and comfort themselves with the Lord Christ and His grace. Then Christ will receive them with joy, and tend them like a shepherd, so that they not only have their pasture, but are also protected forever against the wolf, the devil. Amen.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 15:7 in King James wording, without "I say unto you" and "likewise." The heading's "Luc. 19." is a misprint (Appendix D).
- **"Has strayed from."** *sich … verlassen hat*: *sich verlaufen* or *sich verlassen von* is to stray away from.
- **"Little fold."** *stellin*, a diminutive of *Stall*.
- **"Repent."** *buß thun*, as in the verse.
- **"Amen."** As at Epiphany ([12]), the summary itself ends with "Amen."

**Prayer (PDF p. 152)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/wir sind leyder wie die Schaffe in der jrre/vnnd haben vns durch den Sathan/vnnd vnser sündhafftes fleysch vom rechten wege lassen abfüren/wir bitten dich aber/sey gnedig/vnd vergib alle vnsere sünde/vmb deines Sones Christi Jesu willen/vnd erwecke vnsere hertzen durch deinen heyligen Geyst/das wir an deinem wort fest halten/vnnd im Schaffstal deyner Christlichen Kirchen/in rechter Buß vnd warem glauben/biß ans ende beharren/vnd selig werden/ANEN. [sic, for AMEN]

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we are, alas, as sheep going astray, having suffered ourselves to be led away from the right path by Satan and our own sinful flesh: But we beseech Thee graciously to forgive us all our sins for the sake of Thy Son, Jesus Christ; and quicken our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may hold fast to Thy word, and in true repentance and true faith continue unto the end in the sheepfold of Thy Christian Church, and be saved; *through our Lord Jesus Christ, Thy Son, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "we all like sheep have gone astray" → "we are, alas, as sheep going astray" (*wir sind leyder wie die Schaffe in der jrre*). TLHy quotes Isaiah 53:6 and drops "alas"; Dietrich's present tense is closer to 1 Peter 2:25 ("as sheep going astray").
- "We beseech Thee" → "But we beseech Thee" (*wir bitten dich aber*).
- "that we may abide in Thy word" → "that we may hold fast to Thy word" (*das wir an deinem wort fest halten*).
- "and in true repentance and a steadfast faith continue in Thy Church unto the end" → "and in true repentance and true faith continue unto the end in the sheepfold of Thy Christian Church" (*vnnd im Schaffstal deyner Christlichen Kirchen/in rechter Buß vnd warem glauben/biß ans ende beharren*).
- "and obtain eternal salvation" → "and be saved" (*vnd selig werden*). Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics.
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit"; the OLM web text's missing period before "Amen" supplied.

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

---

#### [50] Fourth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 153):** *Am vierdē Sontag nach Trinitatis/ein lehr/wie wir vntereinander freundlich vnd Christlich leben sollen/Luce am 6.* [On the Fourth Sunday after Trinity: a teaching on how we should live with one another in a friendly and Christian manner. Luke 6.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 153–154)**

> SEydt barmhertzig/wie ewer Vatter barmhertzig ist. Richtet nicht/so werdet jr nicht gerichtet. Verdammet nicht/so werdet jhr nicht verdambt. Vergebt/so wirdt euch vergeben. Gebet/so wirdt euch gegeben/Ein vol gedrückt/gerüttelt/ [p. 154] vnd vberflüssige maß/wirdt man in ewer schoß geben/Deñ eben mit dem maß jr messet/wirdt euch wider gemessen werden.

Be ye therefore merciful, as your Father also is merciful. Judge not, and ye shall not be judged: condemn not, and ye shall not be condemned: forgive, and ye shall be forgiven: give, and it shall be given unto you; good measure, pressed down, and shaken together, and running over, shall men give into your bosom. For with the same measure that ye mete withal it shall be measured to you again.

**Summary (PDF pp. 154–156)**

> ES hat vnser lieber Herr Gott/dreyerley stend auff erden verordnet/die richten vnnd straffen sollen/doch ein yeder nach seinem maß/Vatter vnd Mutter im hause/sollen richten vnd straffen jhre Kind vnd gesind/mit worten vnd schlegen/vnd ja nicht zusehen noch gedulden/das sie vnrecht thun/solches ist Gottes ernster befelh. Darnach ist ein höhers vnd weytleufftigers Regiment/Dem hat Gott das schwerdt geben/vnnd befolhen/alle offentliche ergernuß damit zu straffen/vnnd die leut also zur forcht vnd zucht halten/Solchen befelh hat weltliche Oberkeyt/vñ ist grosse sünde/wo sie gnedig sein/vnd das schwerdt nicht flugs will gehen lassen. Das dritte vnd höhest Regiment [p. 155]/ist das Kirchenampt/dem ist auffgelegt durch das wort/vnd nicht durchs schwerd oder gewalt alle menschen zu richten/vnnd alle sünde zu straffen. Jn disen dreien stenden/muß das gericht vnd die straff gehen/vnd soll gar nichts vergeben noch geschencket werden/Denn damit würde man die leut nur erger machen. Wo aber kein solcher befelh noch Regiment ist/dir ist weder das schwerdt/noch das wort/noch Haußregiment befolhen/Da sagt Christus/sollen wir vns des richtens vnd verdammens enthaltē/vnd yederman vergeben vnd geben/vnd verheisset/wer solches thut/dem wölle Gott gnedig sein/jn auch nicht richten noch verdammen/sonder vergeben vnd geben. So lerne nun yederman dem es nit befolhen ist/das er andere leut zufriden lasse/vnd nit richte/er richte aber sich selb/da wirdt er gnug zuschaffen finden/seim nechsten aber sey er freundlich vnd dienstlich/vnd lasse ja dem zorn den zaum nicht/deñ damit verdienet man Gottes vngnad [p. 156]. Denn mit dem maß wir andern messen/will Got vns auch messen.

Our dear Lord God has ordained three kinds of estate on earth which are to judge and punish, yet each according to its measure. Father and mother in the house are to judge and punish their children and servants with words and blows, and by no means look on or tolerate their doing wrong; this is God's earnest command. Next there is a higher and wider government. To it God has given the sword, and commanded it to punish all public offenses with it, and so to keep people in fear and discipline. This command the secular authority has, and it is a great sin if it would be merciful and not promptly let the sword go forth. The third and highest government is the office of the Church. On it is laid the charge to judge all men and to rebuke all sin, by the word, and not by the sword or by force. In these three estates judgment and punishment must take their course, and nothing at all is to be forgiven or remitted; for by that one would only make people worse. But where there is no such command or government, where neither the sword nor the word nor the government of a household is committed to you, there, says Christ, we are to refrain from judging and condemning, and forgive and give to everyone. And He promises that whoever does this, to him God will be gracious; He will not judge or condemn him either, but forgive and give. So let everyone to whom it has not been committed learn to leave other people in peace and not to judge. But let him judge himself; there he will find enough to do. And to his neighbor let him be kind and serviceable, and by no means give anger its head; for by that one earns God's displeasure. For with the measure with which we measure others, God will measure us too.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 6:36–38 in King James wording.
- **The three estates.** The household (*Haußregiment*), the secular government (*weltliche Oberkeyt*, "the sword," Romans 13:4) and the office of the Church (*Kirchenampt*, "the word"). These are the three orders of Lutheran social teaching; Dietrich ranks the Church's office highest.
- **"Rebuke."** *straffen* means both "punish" and "rebuke"; for the Church's office, which acts by the word, "rebuke" is used.
- **"Remitted."** *geschencket*, let off.
- **"Give anger its head."** *dem zorn den zaum … lassen*, literally "give anger the rein."

**Prayer (PDF p. 156)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du barmhertzig bist/vnd vns durch Christum zusagest/du wöllest nit richten noch verdammen/sonder genedigklich alle sünde vergeben/vnnd was wir bedürffen/reychlich geben/Wir bitten dich/mache solches vertrawen auff dein barmhertzigkeyt/durch deinē heiligen geyst in vnsern hertzen gewiß vnd fest/vnd lehre vns dergleychen gegen vnserm nechsten auch thun/das wir niemand richten noch verdammen/yederman gern vergeben/vnd geben/vnnd aber vns selb richten/vnd in deiner forcht seligklich leben/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who art merciful, and through Christ dost promise us that Thou wilt neither judge nor condemn us, but graciously forgive us all our sins, and abundantly give us all that we need: We pray Thee, that by Thy Holy Ghost Thou wilt make such confidence in Thy mercy sure and firm in our hearts, and teach us also to do likewise toward our neighbor, that we may not judge or condemn others, but willingly forgive and give to all men, and, judging only ourselves, lead blessed lives in Thy fear; *through Thy dear Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "didst promise us" → "dost promise us" (*zusagest*, present tense).
- "and abundantly provide for all our wants of body and soul" → "and abundantly give us all that we need" (*vnnd was wir bedürffen/reychlich geben*). "Of body and soul" is not in the German.
- "that by Thy Holy Spirit Thou wilt establish in our hearts a confident faith in Thy mercy" → "that by Thy Holy Ghost Thou wilt make such confidence in Thy mercy sure and firm in our hearts" (*mache solches vertrawen auff dein barmhertzigkeyt/durch deinē heiligen geyst in vnsern hertzen gewiß vnd fest*).
- "and teach us also to be merciful to our neighbor" → "and teach us also to do likewise toward our neighbor" (*vnd lehre vns dergleychen gegen vnserm nechsten auch thun*).
- "but willingly forgive all men" → "but willingly forgive and give to all men" (*yederman gern vergeben/vnd geben*); TLHy omits "and give."
- The OLM web text's "Judging" corrected to "judging." Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics.

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [51] Fifth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 157):** *Am fünfften Sontag/wie Petrus auff den befelh Christi/vil Fisch fahet/Luce am 5.* [On the Fifth Sunday: how Peter, at Christ's command, catches many fish. Luke 5.]

**Verse (PDF p. 157)**

> PEtrus sprach: Meyster/wir haben die gantze nacht gearbeytet/vnd nichts gefangen/Aber auff dem wort will ich das netz außwerffen. Vnd da sie das theten/fiengen sie ein grosse meng fisch/vnnd das netz zureyß.

Peter said, Master, we have toiled all the night, and have taken nothing: nevertheless at thy word I will let down the net. And when they had this done, they caught a great multitude of fishes: and the net brake.

**Summary (PDF pp. 158–159)**

> SOlche Histori lehret/wie wir vns sollen halten/weñ wir bergern [sic, for begern] glück vñ segen zu vnser arbeit vnd beruf. Denn da Petrus vergebens fischet/hat er Gottes wort nicht/vñ trawet bloß seiner kunst/alß könne es jm nit fehlen. Aber wenn wir allein auff vnser geschickligkeyt vñ arbeyt das vertrawen setzen/gibt Gott keinen segen dazu. Wer aber Gottes wort höret/vnd nimbts mit glauben an/vnnd ist bey seiner arbeyt fleyssig/vnnd gibt Gott nicht vrsach zur straffe/durch sündhafftes leben/dem wil Got glück vnd segen geben/Darumb lerne yederman sich fleyssig zu Gottes wort halten/vnnd für sünden sich hütten/Denn die sünde muß vnglück mit sich bringen/vñ sey darnach in der arbeit fleyssig/vnd trawe Got/der vns zur arbeyt beruffen hat/er werd auch seinen segen/wie Petro/geben/vnd vns gnedigklich bedencken/Arbeyten vnd from̃ sein/gehört zusam̃/Faulentzen vnd müssig gehn/oder vnfleyssig sein/ [p. 159] ist verbotten. Soll derhalb kein glück dabey sein/wie Salomo sagt: Arbeytsame hende machen reych/verstehe/das man doch gottselig dabey sey/vnd durch sünde zum fluch vñ vnglück nit vrsach gebe.

This history teaches how we are to conduct ourselves when we desire good fortune and blessing on our work and calling. For when Peter fishes in vain, he does not have God's word, and trusts merely in his own skill, as though he could not fail. But when we set our trust in our own skill and work alone, God gives no blessing to it. But whoever hears God's word and receives it with faith, and is diligent at his work, and gives God no occasion for punishment by a sinful life, to him God will give good fortune and blessing. Therefore let everyone learn to keep diligently to God's word and to guard himself against sins, for sin must bring misfortune with it; and then let him be diligent in his work, and trust God, who has called us to work, that He will give His blessing too, as He did to Peter, and be graciously mindful of us. Working and being godly belong together; idling and being idle, or being negligent, is forbidden, and so there shall be no good fortune in it. As Solomon says: "The hand of the diligent maketh rich"; understand, provided that one is godly in it, and gives no occasion through sin for the curse and misfortune.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 5:5–6 in King James wording, introduced by "Peter said" (King James "Simon answering said unto him"). Dietrich has "they caught" (*fiengen sie*) where Luther and the King James Version have "inclosed," and "the net" for "their net"; the translation follows him. In *fiengen* and *vnnd* the type is damaged.
- **"Calling."** *beruf*, one's vocation and station in life, as at the Lord's Supper ([32]).
- **"Be graciously mindful of us."** *vns gnedigklich bedencken*; compare *bedacht* in the table in §2.2.
- **Solomon's word.** Proverbs 10:4, which Dietrich quotes freely (*Arbeytsame hende machen reych*, "industrious hands make rich"); it is given in King James wording.
- **Misprint.** *bergern* for *begern* ("desire").

**Prayer (PDF p. 159)**

> O Jesu Christe/du Son des lebendigen Gottes/der du vnns dein liebes wort geben/vnd mit allerley leyblichem segē begnadet hast/Wir erkennen vnd bekennen/das wir zu solchem allem vnwirdig/vnnd wol ergers verdienet hetten/Vnnd bitten aber dich/du wöllest vns/wie Petro/vnsere sünde vergeben/vnd glück vnnd heyl zu vnserm beruff geben/auff das wir durch dich/zeytlich vnd ewig erhalten/vnd beschützet/Dich inn ewigkeyt loben vnd preysen/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> O Jesus Christ, Thou Son of the living God, who hast given us Thy dear word, and hast favored us with all manner of bodily blessing, we acknowledge and confess that we are unworthy of all these mercies, and that we have well deserved worse: But we beseech Thee, as Thou didst Peter, forgive us our sins, and prosper and bless us in our several callings, that through Thee we may be sustained and defended, both in time and in eternity, and so praise and glorify Thee eternally, *Thou who livest and reignest with the Father and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "Thy holy word" → "Thy dear word" (*dein liebes wort*).
- "and hast bountifully provided for all our temporal wants" → "and hast favored us with all manner of bodily blessing" (*vnd mit allerley leyblichem segē begnadet hast*).
- "we confess" → "we acknowledge and confess" (*Wir erkennen vnd bekennen*).
- "and that we have rather deserved punishment" → "and that we have well deserved worse" (*vnnd wol ergers verdienet hetten*).
- Restored "as Thou didst Peter" (*wie Petro*), which TLHy omits.
- "that by Thy strength we may be sustained and defended, now and forever" → "that through Thee we may be sustained and defended, both in time and in eternity" (*auff das wir durch dich/zeytlich vnd ewig erhalten/vnd beschützet*).
- Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending for a prayer to Christ is kept in italics.
