#### [52] Sixth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 160):** *Am sechsten Sontag nach Trinitatis/ein lehr/was Christliche gerechtigkeyt sey/Matth. 5.* [On the Sixth Sunday after Trinity: a teaching on what Christian righteousness is. Matthew 5.]

**Verse (PDF p. 160)**

> CHristus sprach: Es sey denn ewr gerechtigkeyt besser/denn der Schrifftgelerten vnd Phariseer/so werdet jr nicht in das Himelreych kommen.

Christ said: Except your righteousness shall exceed the righteousness of the scribes and Pharisees, ye shall in no case enter into the kingdom of heaven.

**Summary (PDF pp. 161–162).** The paragraph breaks follow the print.

> DJe Phariseer vñ Schrifftgelerten/hielten sich darumb für from̃ vñ gerecht/das sie eusserlicher zucht sich beflissen/vnnd für ergerlichem wandel sich hüteten/Das aber noch böse neygung vnd lüsten im hertzen waren/solches achteten sie für kein sünde.

The Pharisees and scribes held themselves godly and righteous because they applied themselves to outward discipline and guarded themselves against offensive conduct. But that there were still evil inclinations and lusts in their hearts, this they counted no sin.

> Darumb schleust vnser HERR Christus/wer nicht mehr denn solche eusserliche zucht habe/werde in hymel nicht kommen. Denn Gott gebeut in den zehen Gebotten nicht allein/das wir böse werck vnterlassen/vnnd gute werck thun sollen/sonder er wil ein reynes hertz haben/das der mensche nicht eusserlich allein/sonder auch jnnerlich/vnd nicht stückweyse/sonder gantz from̃ sey.

Therefore our Lord Christ concludes that whoever has no more than such outward discipline will not come into heaven. For in the Ten Commandments God commands not only that we are to leave off evil works and do good works; He will have a pure heart, so that a man is godly not outwardly alone but inwardly too, and not piecemeal but wholly.

> Wie können wir aber zu solchem hertzen kommen/das gantz vnd gar rein vnd one sünd sey? Also. Erstlich/weñ du sihest/ob gleich du dich für dem eusserlichen ergernuß hüttest/das dennoch dein hertz lust vñ liebe dazu hette [p. 162]/da hüte dich/vnd sey nicht sicher/alß wer solches kein sünd/sonder beichte es Gott/vnd bitt vmb vergebung/das er solche vnreynigkeyt/vnd böse neygung deines hertzens nicht wölle straffen/Darnach tröste dich des Herren Christi/vnd hoffe durch jn genad vnd barmhertzigkeyt/Also bist du gerecht vnd gefellest Gott wol/deñ erstlich hütest du dich für sünden/vnnd dem eusserlichen ergernuß/vnnd ob gleych böse neygung im hertzen sindt/so demütigest du dich für Gott/vnnd bittest vmb gnad/vñ hoffest darnach/Got werde solche böse neygung sampt andern sünden/vmb Christi willen vergeben/das heist alß denn die rechte gerechtigkeyt/so für Got gilt/vnd vns in das reych bringet. Jn summa/Buß thun/das ist/sich für einen sünder erkennen/vnd bessern/vnd vergebung der sünden glaubē durch Christum/solches ist der eynige gewise weg zum ewigen leben.

But how can we come to such a heart, one that is wholly and utterly pure and without sin? Thus. First, when you see that, although you guard yourself against outward offense, your heart nevertheless has delight and love for it, then be on your guard and do not be secure, as though this were no sin; but confess it to God, and pray for forgiveness, that He would not punish such impurity and evil inclination of your heart. Then comfort yourself with the Lord Christ, and hope for grace and mercy through Him. So you are righteous and well-pleasing to God. For, first, you guard yourself against sins and outward offense; and although there are evil inclinations in the heart, you humble yourself before God, pray for grace, and then hope that God will forgive such evil inclinations, together with other sins, for Christ's sake. That, then, is called the true righteousness which avails before God and brings us into the kingdom. In sum: to repent, that is, to acknowledge oneself a sinner and amend, and to believe the forgiveness of sins through Christ: this is the one sure way to eternal life.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 5:20 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ said." In *Schrifftgelerten* the t after ff is damaged and looks like an r.
- **"Lusts."** *lüsten*, evil desires.
- **"Confess it to God."** *beichte es Gott*.
- **"Avails before God."** *so für Got gilt*, Luther's phrase for the righteousness of faith (Romans 1:17 and 3:21 in his Bible: *die Gerechtigkeit, die für Gott gilt*).

**Prayer (PDF p. 163)**

> HERR Gott himlischer Vatter/wir bekennen das wir leyder arme/ellende sünder sint/da gar nichts gutes an ist/Deñ vnser hertz/fleysch vnd blut/ist durch die sünde dermassen vergifftet/das wir der bösen lüste nymmer können on sein. Darumb lieber Vatter/bitten wir dich/vergib solche sünde/vnnd reynige vnsere hertzen durch deinen heyligen Geyst/das wir zu deinem wort lust vñ lieb haben/vnd vns darnach halten/vnnd durch Christum in deiner genade ewig bleyben mögē/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we confess that we are, alas, poor, wretched sinners, and that there is no good in us at all, our hearts, flesh and blood being so poisoned by sin that we can never be without evil lusts; therefore we beseech Thee, dear Father, forgive us these sins, and let Thy Holy Ghost so cleanse our hearts that we may desire and love Thy word, abide by it, and remain forever in Thy grace through Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- Restored "alas" (*leyder*) and "at all" (*gar*).
- "so corrupted by sin" → "so poisoned by sin" (*durch die sünde dermassen vergifftet*).
- "that we never in this life can be without sinful lust and concupiscence" → "that we can never be without evil lusts" (*das wir der bösen lüste nymmer können on sein*). "In this life" is not in the German.
- "and thus by Thy grace be forever saved; through our Lord Jesus Christ, Thy Son," → "and remain forever in Thy grace through Christ," (*vnnd durch Christum in deiner genade ewig bleyben mögē*). Dietrich asks to remain in grace, not to be saved by it; his "through Christ" leads into the conclusion (§2.4c).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [53] Seventh Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 164):** *Am sibenden sontag nach Trinitatis/wie der Herr Jesus mit wenig brodt/vil menschen speyset/in der Wüsten/Mar. 8.* [On the Seventh Sunday after Trinity: how the Lord Jesus feeds many people with a little bread in the wilderness. Mark 8.]

**Verse (PDF p. 164)**

> CHristus spricht: Mich jammert des volcks/denn sie haben nun drey tag bey mir beharret/vnnd haben nichts zu essen.

Christ saith: I have compassion on the multitude, because they have now been with me three days, and have nothing to eat.

**Summary (PDF p. 165).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> HJe hörest vnnd sihest/das vnser lieber herr Christus auch sich darum̃ annimbt/das die/so sein wort hören vnd annemen/zu essen haben/vnd jammert jhn/das sie sollen mangel leyden/Es bleybet aber bey dem nicht/das er sich solches mangels halb kümmert/Da sonst nicht mehr/denn siben brodt/vnd ein wenig fischlein da sind/gibt er seinen segen dazu/das viertausent Man/gnug dran zu essen haben.

Here you hear and see that our dear Lord Christ also takes care that those who hear and receive His word have something to eat, and it moves Him with compassion that they should suffer want. But it does not stop at His being troubled over such want. Where there are no more than seven loaves and a few little fishes, He gives His blessing to them, so that four thousand men have enough to eat from them.

> Solches Exempel merck/wenn du wilt/das Gottes segen bey dir sey/halte dich zu dem Herrn Christo/das ist/höre sein wort/nim̃ es mit glauben vnd hertzen an/So alß deñ ein mangel fürfellet/schreye den Herrn Christum an vmb hilff so soll es dir widerfaren/Denn er kan niemand lassen/der jn anrüffet/er will helffen vnnd segnen/Wer aber des Herren Christi wort nit annimbt/der wirt nit allein zeytliches vnfalß/sonder auch des ewigen verdamniß sich besorgen müssen.

Mark this example. If you would have God's blessing with you, hold to the Lord Christ; that is, hear His word, and receive it with faith and with your heart. Then, if want befalls you, cry to the Lord Christ for help, and it shall come to you. For He cannot forsake anyone who calls upon Him; He will help and bless. But whoever does not receive the word of the Lord Christ will have to fear not only temporal misfortune but eternal damnation as well.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Mark 8:2 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ saith."
- **"Four thousand men."** Mark 8:9 has "about four thousand"; the prayer adds "not counting women and children," as at Lent 4 ([28]), whose prayer this one nearly repeats.
- **"Cannot forsake."** *kan niemand lassen*, cannot abandon anyone.

**Prayer (PDF p. 166)**

> HERR Gott himlischer Vatter/der du durch deinen Son in der wüsten viertausent Mañ/Weyb vnnd kind nicht mit gerechnet/nur mit siben broten vnd wenig fischen/reichlich gespeyset hast/Wir bitten dich/sey mit deinem segen auch gnedig bey vns/behüte für dem geytz/vnnd sorgen der narung/das wir dein Reych vnd dein gerechtigkeyt am ersten suchen/vnnd deine milte Vätterliche güte inn allem/was wir zu leyb vnd Seel bedürffen/gnedigklich spüren/durch Jhesum Christum vnsern Herrn/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who in the wilderness didst by Thy Son abundantly feed four thousand men besides women and children with only seven loaves and a few small fishes: We beseech Thee, graciously abide also among us with Thy blessing, and keep us from covetousness and the cares of this life, that we may seek first Thy kingdom and Thy righteousness, and in all things needful for body and soul graciously perceive Thy bountiful fatherly goodness; through Jesus Christ, our Lord, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "with seven loaves" → "with only seven loaves" (*nur mit siben broten*).
- "graciously abide among us" → "graciously abide also among us" (*auch gnedig bey vns*).
- "experience Thine ever-present help" → "graciously perceive Thy bountiful fatherly goodness" (*deine milte Vätterliche güte ... gnedigklich spüren*).
- "through Thy Son, our Lord Jesus Christ," → "through Jesus Christ, our Lord," which is Dietrich's own close (*durch Jhesum Christum vnsern Herrn*); the conclusion continues it (§2.4d).

**Note.** "Besides women and children" and "a few small fishes" are the King James wording of the feeding stories (Matthew 15:38; Mark 8:7), which Dietrich's German follows; they are kept.

#### [54] Eighth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 167):** *Am achten Sontag nach Trinitatis/warnung der falschen Propheten halb/Mathei am 7.* [On the Eighth Sunday after Trinity: a warning concerning false prophets. Matthew 7.]

**Verses (PDF pp. 167–168).** The two verses are printed as separate paragraphs.

> CHristus spricht: Sehet euch für/für den falschen Propheten/die in Schaffskleydern zu euch kommen [p. 168]/jnwendig aber sindts reyssende Wölff/An jren früchten solt jr sie erkennen.
>
> Es werden nicht alle die zu mir sagen: Herr Herr/in das Himelreych kommen/sonder die den willen thun meines Vatters im himel.

Christ saith: Beware of false prophets, which come to you in sheep's clothing, but inwardly they are ravening wolves. Ye shall know them by their fruits.

Not every one that saith unto me, Lord, Lord, shall enter into the kingdom of heaven; but they that do the will of my Father which is in heaven.

**Summary (PDF pp. 168–169).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> VNser Herr Jesus vermanet vnd warnet alle Christen/das sie sondern fleyß auff reyne Christliche lehre wenden wöllen/denn der Teuffel werde seine diener haben/die sich stellen/alß seyen sie rechtschaffene Prediger/aber es werden eytel schedliche/verfürer vnd Seelmörder sein. Darumb soll ein yeder Christ nit auff dē schein/sonder auff die rechte frucht sehen/die heist den willen des Vaters im himel thun/Solchs thut ein prediger/wenn er seine zuhörer allein auff die gnade Gottes/vnd den tod vñ die aufferstehung vnsers Herren Christi [p. 169] weyset/das sie damit wider die sünd/vnd den todt/vnd alles vnglück sich trösten/Solchs thut ein yeder Christ/ob er gleich kein prediger ist/wenn er durch Christum vergebung seiner sünden glaubt/vnd zu Gott sich aller genaden versihet/Auff solche frucht folget gewiß das ewig leben.

Our Lord Jesus admonishes and warns all Christians to apply special diligence to pure Christian doctrine; for the devil will have his servants, who make out that they are upright preachers, but they will be nothing but harmful seducers and murderers of souls. Therefore every Christian is to look not at the appearance but at the true fruit, which is called doing the will of the Father in heaven. A preacher does this when he points his hearers to the grace of God alone, and to the death and resurrection of our Lord Christ, so that they comfort themselves with these against sin, death and every misfortune. Every Christian does this, even if he is no preacher, when he believes the forgiveness of his sins through Christ and looks to God for every grace. Upon such fruit eternal life surely follows.

> Wo aber dise frucht nicht ist/ob gleich daselb Moses Gesetz/vnd allerley tugend vnd Gottesdienst sind/so kan man doch zum Reych Gottes nit kommen/noch dadurch selig werden.

But where this fruit is not, even though Moses' law and all manner of virtue and worship are there, one cannot come to the kingdom of God, nor be saved by them.

**Notes on the verses and summary**
- **The verses** are Matthew 7:15–16 and 7:21 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ saith." Dietrich, with Luther, has the plural "they that do the will" (*die den willen thun*) for the King James "he that doeth."
- **The woodcut** (p. 167) shows the false prophets as wolves in clerical dress, in bishops' mitres and a papal tiara, with a winged devil above them.
- **"Special diligence."** *sondern fleyß*: *sonder* is the adjective "particular, special."
- **"Murderers of souls."** *Seelmörder*.
- **"Looks to God for every grace."** *zu Gott sich aller genaden versihet*, as at Palm Sunday ([30]).
- **"Worship."** *Gottesdienst*, as at Septuagesima ([21]).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 169–170)**

> HERR Gott hymelischer Vatter/Wir dancken dir von hertzen/das du vns hast lassen zum erkentnuß deines lieben worts kommen/Wir bitten dich aber auch/du wöllest vns dabey gnedig erhalten/vnd seligklich drinn sterben lassen/wöllest auch alle from̃e prediger/so dein wort trewlich fürtragen/für allem ergernuß vnd anderm vnglück/gnedig behüten/vnd jnen jr leben fristen. Die andern aber so vntrewlich mit deinem wort vmbgehen/vnd sich alß from̃e scheflin lassen sehē/sind aber reyssende Wölff/die wöllest jrem verdienst nach/straffen/vñ jnen wehren/Vnd dein arme Christenheyt gnedig für jnen behüten/durch Christum Jesum deinen Son/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we most heartily thank Thee that Thou hast caused us to come to the knowledge of Thy dear word. We pray Thee also: graciously keep us steadfast therein, and let us die blessedly therein; graciously defend also all pious preachers, who faithfully preach Thy word, from all offense and other misfortune, and grant them long life. But the others, who deal unfaithfully with Thy word, and show themselves as pious sheep, but are ravening wolves, do Thou punish according to their desert, and restrain them, and graciously defend Thy poor Christendom from them; through Jesus Christ, Thy Son, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy.** TLHy recasts the second half of this prayer freely.
- "the knowledge of Thy word" → "the knowledge of Thy dear word" (*zum erkentnuß deines lieben worts*).
- "We pray Thee: graciously keep us steadfast in this knowledge unto death, that we may obtain eternal life" → "We pray Thee also: graciously keep us steadfast therein, and let us die blessedly therein" (*Wir bitten dich aber auch/du wöllest vns dabey gnedig erhalten/vnd seligklich drinn sterben lassen*), as at [13].
- "send us now and ever pious pastors, who faithfully preach Thy word, without offense or false doctrine, and grant them long life" → "graciously defend also all pious preachers, who faithfully preach Thy word, from all offense and other misfortune, and grant them long life" (*wöllest auch alle from̃e prediger/so dein wort trewlich fürtragen/für allem ergernuß vnd anderm vnglück/gnedig behüten/vnd jnen jr leben fristen*). Dietrich asks protection for the preachers the Church already has; TLHy asks for preachers to be sent.
- "Defend us from all false teachings, and frustrate Thou the counsels of all such as pervert Thy word, who come to us in sheep's clothing, but are inwardly ravening wolves" → "But the others, who deal unfaithfully with Thy word, and show themselves as pious sheep, but are ravening wolves, do Thou punish according to their desert, and restrain them" (*Die andern aber so vntrewlich mit deinem wort vmbgehen/vnd sich alß from̃e scheflin lassen sehē/sind aber reyssende Wölff/die wöllest jrem verdienst nach/straffen/vñ jnen wehren*). "Ravening wolves" is kept from TLHy (Matthew 7:15).
- "that Thy true Church may evermore be established among us, and be defended and preserved from such false teachers" → "and graciously defend Thy poor Christendom from them" (*Vnd dein arme Christenheyt gnedig für jnen behüten*).
- "Through Jesus Christ, Thy Son" is Dietrich's own close (*durch Christum Jesum deinen Son*, rendered "Jesus Christ" as in §2.2); TLHy already has it, and the conclusion continues it (§2.4d).

#### [55] Ninth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 170):** *Am neundē Sontag/vom vnrechten Haußhalten/Luc. 16.* [On the Ninth Sunday: of unjust stewardship. Luke 16.]

**Verse (PDF p. 171)**

> CHristus spricht: Jch sage euch/machet euch freund mit dem vnrechten Mammon/auff das/wenn jr nun darbet/sie euch auffnemen in die ewigen hütten.

Christ saith: I say unto you, Make to yourselves friends of the mammon of unrighteousness; that, when ye fail, they may receive you into everlasting habitations.

**Summary (PDF pp. 171–172).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> VNser Herr Jesus Christus warnet mit disem spruch/alle reyche leut/das sie jhr gelt vnd gut nach Gottes willen recht/vñ wol brauchen sollen/das ist/den armen leuten sollen sie helffen so not leyden/vnd jr narung entweder kranckheyt/oder anders vnfals halben/nicht mehr gewinnen können/vnnd verheysset er wölle solche hilff/so armen leuten hie auff erden geschicht/inn yenem leben reychlich bezalen.

With this saying our Lord Jesus Christ warns all rich people that they are to use their money and goods rightly and well, according to God's will; that is, they are to help poor people who suffer need and can no longer earn their living, whether because of sickness or of some other mishap. And He promises that such help as is given to poor people here on earth He will repay richly in the life beyond.

> Solche verheissung bringet diß vrtheyl klar mit sich/das alle/so armen leuten wol köndten helffen/wöllens aber vmb des guts willen nit thun/oder thun es kerglich/oder noch auch den leuten vmb des geytzes willen vnrecht [p. 172] thun/sie vbersetzen/oder sonst vberfortheylen/das sie Gott in yener welt auch wölle lassen mangel leyden/vnd vmb solcher vntrew willen ewig verdammen. Darumb sollen alle reyche diser lehre gut acht nemen/vnnd darnach sich haltē/das sie jnen freunde mit dem vnrechten Mammon machen/das ist/mit gelt vnd gut/welchs der Herr darumb vnrecht heyst/das schier niemandt sein recht vnnd wol brauchet/sintemal yederman sich lest bedunckē/er sey eygen Herr vber das seine/so wir doch alle nur Schaffner vnd verwalter drüber sind/vnd müssen Gott am Jüngsten tag rechenschafft drüber geben/wie wirs gebrauchet haben.

This promise clearly carries this judgment with it: all who could well help poor people, but will not do it for the sake of their goods, or do it stingily, or even wrong people out of covetousness, overcharge them or otherwise take advantage of them, God will let them too suffer want in the world beyond, and condemn them eternally for such unfaithfulness. Therefore all rich people are to pay good heed to this teaching, and conduct themselves by it, so that they make themselves friends with the unrighteous mammon, that is, with money and goods. These the Lord calls unrighteous because scarcely anyone uses them rightly and well; for everyone imagines that he is lord in his own right over what is his, whereas we are all only stewards and managers of it, and must give account to God on the Last Day of how we have used it.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 16:9 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ saith." *wenn jr nun darbet* ("when you are now in want") is the King James "when ye fail."
- **"Overcharge … take advantage of."** *vbersetzen* is to charge too much, *vberfortheylen* to take unfair advantage of.
- **"Lord in his own right."** *eygen Herr*, owner outright, as against a steward.
- **"Stewards and managers."** *Schaffner vnd verwalter*: the rich are what the steward of the parable is (Luke 16:1–2, "give an account of thy stewardship").

**Prayer (PDF pp. 172–173)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du vnns reychen segen/vnnd das tegliche brod vberflüssig hast gebē/Wir bitten dich/behüte für geytz/vñ erwecke vnsere hertzen/das wir armen leuten gern vnd miltigklich von solchem segen mitteylen/auff das wir trewe haußhalter vber deine gütter erfunden werden/vnnd nicht müssen inn ewigkeyt darben/wenn wir von solcher haußhaltung abgefordert/vñ für dein gericht kommen werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who hast bountifully given us rich blessing and our daily bread: We beseech Thee, preserve us from covetousness, and so quicken our hearts that we willingly and liberally share such blessing with the poor; that we may be found faithful stewards of Thy goods, and may not suffer want in eternity, when we shall be removed from our stewardship, and shall come before Thy judgment; *through our Lord Jesus Christ, Thy Son, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "Thy blessing" → "rich blessing" (*reychen segen*).
- "that we willingly share Thy blessed gifts with our needy brethren" → "that we willingly and liberally share such blessing with the poor" (*das wir armen leuten gern vnd miltigklich von solchem segen mitteylen*).
- "faithful stewards of Thy gifts" → "faithful stewards of Thy goods" (*trewe haußhalter vber deine gütter*), the word of the parable (Luke 16:1).
- "and abide in Thy grace" → "and may not suffer want in eternity" (*vnnd nicht müssen inn ewigkeyt darben*).
- Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics.

#### [56] Tenth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 173):** *Am zehenden Sontag von der zerstörung Jerusalem/Luce am 19.* [On the Tenth Sunday: of the destruction of Jerusalem. Luke 19.]

**Verse (PDF p. 174)**

> CHristus sprach: Jerusalem/deine Feynde werden dich schleyffen/vnnd keinen steyn auff dem andern lassen/Darumb das du nicht erkennet hast die zeyt/darinn du heymgesuchet bist.

Christ said: Jerusalem, thine enemies shall lay thee even with the ground, and they shall not leave one stone upon another; because thou knewest not the time of thy visitation.

**Summary (PDF pp. 174–175).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> DJe herrliche Stadt Jerusalem/stehet zum ewigem Exempel da/das wir zwey ding lernen sollen. Das erste/wenn Gott sein liebes wort der welt offenbaret/das yederman solches für die höchste gnad/vnd den besten schatz achten/es mit frewden annemen/darauß sich besseren/vnd frömmer soll werden. Denn diser vrsach halb schicket Got sein wort/das wir jn dadurch erkennen/seynen willen darauß lernen vnd thun/vnd durch den glauben an Christum sollen endlich selig werden.

The glorious city of Jerusalem stands there as an everlasting example, from which we are to learn two things. The first: when God reveals His dear word to the world, everyone is to esteem it as the highest grace and the best treasure, receive it with joy, amend his life by it, and become more godly. For God sends His word for this reason: that by it we may know Him, learn His will from it and do it, and at last be saved through faith in Christ.

> Das ander/wer aber solche gnad nicht mit hertzen annemen/Gelt/gut/ [p. 175] macht/ehr/vnd anders jm mehe [sic, for mehr]/deñ das wort will lieben lassen/Er hörets wol/aber bessert sich nicht/vnd bleybt heut wie gester/hewer wie fernet/da soll das vrtheyl folgen/wie vber Jerusalem/das Gott die verachtung seines worts/mit zeytlichem vnfall vnd jammer/vñ letzlich mit dem hellischen fewer straffen wil. Darumb gewehne sich yederman dahin/das er Gottes wort fleyssig höre vnd wol lerne/vnd sein leben darnach anrichte/vnd bessere/so will Gott zu allem thun/glück vnd heyl geben/vnnd dazu ewig selig machen/Amen.

The second: whoever will not receive such grace with his heart, but lets money, goods, power, honor and other things be dearer to him than the word; who hears it well enough but does not amend, and stays today as he was yesterday, this year as he was last year: upon him the judgment shall follow, as upon Jerusalem, that God will punish the contempt of His word with temporal mishap and misery, and at last with the fire of hell. Therefore let everyone accustom himself to hear God's word diligently and learn it well, and to order his life by it and amend. Then God will give good fortune and well-being in all that he does, and save him eternally besides. Amen.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 19:43–44, condensed by Dietrich and introduced by "Christ said," in King James wording. *schleyffen*, "raze," is the King James "lay thee even with the ground."
- **"An everlasting example."** The city's destruction by the Romans in A.D. 70.
- **"Today as he was yesterday, this year as he was last year."** *heut wie gester/hewer wie fernet*, a proverbial pairing (*heuer*, this year; *fernd*, last year).
- **"Amen."** As at Epiphany ([12]) and Trinity 3 ([49]), the summary itself ends with "Amen."

**Prayer (PDF pp. 175–176)**

> ALmechtiger ewiger Got/der du dein wort von deinem Son Christo Jesu/durch dein heiligen Geyst vns offenbaret/vnd kundt hast gethon/Wir bitē dich/erwecke vnsere hertzen/das wirs mit ernst añemen/vñ ja nit in wind schlagen/oder vnfleyssig hören/wie dein volck/die vnglaubigen Juden/Auff das wir in deiner forcht/vnd rechtem vertrawen auff deine Barmhertzigkeyt leben vnd teglich zunemen/Vnd endlich durch deinē Son/Christum Jesum/ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Almighty and everlasting God, who by Thy Holy Ghost hast revealed and made known unto us Thy word concerning Thy Son, Jesus Christ: We beseech Thee so to quicken our hearts that we may earnestly receive Thy word, and by no means make light of it, or hear it negligently, as did Thy people, the unbelieving Jews; that we may live in Thy fear and in a right confidence in Thy mercy, and daily increase therein, and finally obtain eternal salvation through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "hast revealed unto us the gospel of Thy Son, Jesus Christ" → "hast revealed and made known unto us Thy word concerning Thy Son, Jesus Christ" (*dein wort von deinem Son Christo Jesu ... vns offenbaret/vnd kundt hast gethon*).
- "sincerely receive" → "earnestly receive" (*mit ernst añemen*).
- "and not make light of it, or hear it without fruit" → "and by no means make light of it, or hear it negligently" (*vñ ja nit in wind schlagen/oder vnfleyssig hören*).
- "but that we may fear Thee and daily grow in faith in Thy mercy" → "that we may live in Thy fear and in a right confidence in Thy mercy, and daily increase therein" (*Auff das wir in deiner forcht/vnd rechtem vertrawen auff deine Barmhertzigkeyt leben vnd teglich zunemen*).
- "and finally obtain eternal salvation, through Thy Son, Jesus Christ our Lord," → "and finally obtain eternal salvation through Thy Son, Jesus Christ," which is Dietrich's phrase (*Vnd endlich durch deinē Son/Christum Jesum/ewig selig werden*); the conclusion follows (§2.4c).

#### [57] Eleventh Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 176):** *Am eylfften sontag/vom Phariseer vnd Zölner/wie sie bede im Tempel betten/Luc. 18.* [On the Eleventh Sunday: of the Pharisee and the publican, how they both pray in the Temple. Luke 18.]

**Verse (PDF p. 177)**

> DER Zölner stundt von ferne/wolte auch seine augen nit auffheben gen Himel/sonder schlug an sein brust/vnd sprach: Got sey mir sünder gnedig/Diser gieng hinab gerechtfertiget in sein hauß. Denn wer sich selb erhöhet/der wirt ernidriget/Vnd wer sich selb ernidriget/der wirt erhöhet werden.

The publican, standing afar off, would not lift up so much as his eyes unto heaven, but smote upon his breast, saying, God be merciful to me a sinner. This man went down to his house justified: for every one that exalteth himself shall be abased; and he that humbleth himself shall be exalted.

**Summary (PDF pp. 177–178).** The paragraph breaks follow the print.

> DJß ist ein schönes vnd nützes exempel/auß welchem wir lernen/wie wir zu vergebung der sünden sollen kommen. Diser Zölner bekennets/er sey ein armer sünder/vñ erschrickt für seinen sündē dermassen/das er so kleynmütig vnd engstig drüber wirdt/das er nicht vnter die leut/noch seine augen gen himel darff auffheben/vnd dennoch bittet er vmb vergebung.

This is a beautiful and profitable example, from which we learn how we are to come to the forgiveness of sins. This publican confesses that he is a poor sinner, and is so terrified at his sins, and becomes so fainthearted and anxious over them, that he dares not go among people nor lift up his eyes to heaven; and yet he asks for forgiveness.

> Also sollen wir auch thun/vnsere sünde sollen wir erkennen/vnd bekennen [p. 178]/vnd vns von hertzen lassen leyd sein/das wir dem Teuffel so lang gefolget/vnd Gottes wort vnd willen/so leychtfertigklich verachtet haben/Da vmb gelts/dort vmb ehr/da vmb gutes lebens willen ꝛc. Wenn nun die sünde dir also hertzlich leyd ist/deñ du sihest/was für ein straff Gott drauff geschlagen hat/So sihe alß deñ den Son Gottes am creutz an/tröste dich seines sterbens/damit er für der gantzen welt sünde bezalet hat/vnd rüffe zu Gott/vnd bitte vmb gnad/vnnd zweiffel nicht/wie du bittest/Got wölle vmb seines Sons willen dir gnedig sein/vnd sünde vergeben/so wirdts dir gewißlich widerfaren.

So we too are to do. We are to acknowledge and confess our sins, and let it grieve us from our hearts that we have followed the devil so long and have despised God's word and will so lightly, here for money's sake, there for honor's, there for the sake of good living, etc. Now when sin grieves you thus heartily, since you see what punishment God has laid upon it, then look upon the Son of God on the cross, comfort yourself with His dying, by which He has paid for the sin of the whole world, and cry to God and pray for grace; and do not doubt, as you pray, that God will be gracious to you for His Son's sake and forgive sin. Then it will surely be done for you.

> Wir sollen aber auch für vnd für/des willen Gottes vns fleyssigen/vnd für sünden vns hütten/Denn sonst ist es nur Heucheley/wenn du in sünden ligen/vnd nicht teglich dich woltest bessern.

But we are also continually to apply ourselves to God's will and guard ourselves against sins. For otherwise it is mere hypocrisy, if you would lie in sins and not amend yourself daily.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 18:13–14 in King James wording, introduced by "The publican"; Dietrich lacks "I tell you" and "rather than the other."
- **"Dares not go among people."** *das er nicht vnter die leut … darff*: the publican "stood afar off."
- **"ꝛc."** The sign for *et cetera*, kept as printed.
- **"Apply ourselves to God's will."** *des willen Gottes vns fleyssigen*, as at New Year ([8]).

**Prayer (PDF p. 179)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/Jch bitte dich/du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst/mich also leyten vñ füren/das ich erstlich meiner sünde nicht vergesse/noch sicher werde/sonder in steter busse stehe/vnd mich von tag zu tag bessere/vnd darnach allein mich dessen tröste/das du vmb deines Suns Christi Jhesu willen/mir genedig sein/alle sünde vergeben/vnd selig wilt machen/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, I beseech Thee so to guide and direct me by Thy Holy Ghost, that, first, I may not forget my sin, nor grow secure, but continue in constant repentance, and amend my life from day to day; and then comfort myself with this alone, that for the sake of Thy Son, Jesus Christ, Thou wilt be gracious unto me, forgive me all my sins, and save me; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Congregational form (TLHy practice)**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we beseech Thee so to guide and direct us by Thy Holy Ghost, that, first, we may not forget our sin, nor grow secure, but continue in constant repentance, and amend our lives from day to day; and then comfort ourselves with this alone, that for the sake of Thy Son, Jesus Christ, Thou wilt be gracious unto us, forgive us all our sins, and save us; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- Person: Dietrich's prayer is in the first person singular ("I"); TLHy makes it plural. The revised text follows Dietrich, and a congregational form is supplied (§2.2).
- "that we may not forget our sins and be filled with pride" → "that, first, I may not forget my sin, nor grow secure" (*das ich erstlich meiner sünde nicht vergesse/noch sicher werde*). Dietrich's word is carnal security, not pride.
- "but continue in daily repentance and renewal" → "but continue in constant repentance, and amend my life from day to day" (*sonder in steter busse stehe/vnd mich von tag zu tag bessere*).
- "seeking our comfort only in the blessed knowledge that Thou wilt be merciful unto us" → "and then comfort myself with this alone, that for the sake of Thy Son, Jesus Christ, Thou wilt be gracious unto me" (*vnd darnach allein mich dessen tröste/das du vmb deines Suns Christi Jhesu willen/mir genedig sein*). TLHy omits "for the sake of Thy Son."
- "forgive us our sins, and grant us eternal life" → "forgive me all my sins, and save me" (*alle sünde vergeben/vnd selig wilt machen*). Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics.
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [58] Twelfth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 180):** *Am zwölfftē Sontag/wie vnser Herr Jesus einen stummen/vnd tauben gesund machet/Marci am 7.* [On the Twelfth Sunday: how our Lord Jesus makes a dumb and deaf man whole. Mark 7.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 180–181)**

> VNser Herr Jesus nam den stummen/vnd tauben menschen/füret jn besonders vom volck/vñ leget jm die finger in die ohren/vnnd spürzet/vnnd rüret seine zunge/vnd sahe auff gen Himel/seufftzet vnnd [p. 181] sprach: Hephata/das ist/thu dich auf/Vnnd alß bald theten sich seine ohren auff/vnd das band seiner zunge war ledig/vnd redet recht.

Our Lord Jesus took the dumb and deaf man aside from the multitude, and put his fingers into his ears, and spit, and touched his tongue; and looking up to heaven, he sighed, and said, Ephphatha, that is, Be opened. And straightway his ears were opened, and the string of his tongue was loosed, and he spake plain.

**Summary (PDF pp. 181–182)**

> DJß wunderwerck/will vnser lieber Herr Christus/für vnnd für in seiner kirchen üben/vñ sehen lassen. Denn ob wol wir leyblich nicht stum̃ noch taub sind/so sind aber vnsere hertzen durch die sünde dermassen vergifftet/das wir Gottes wort nicht hören/noch reden wöllen/lieber hören vnnd reden wir von ergerlichen/leychtfertigen/vnnützen dingen/Da hilfft denn der böse feind auch zu/wie wir sehen/das das junge volck fluchet/vnd Gott lestert/vnnd wenig andacht noch lust zur predigt hat/Solches sind taube ohren/vnnd gebundene stum̃e zungen. Disem jammer nun/wehret vnser lieber Herr Christus/durch seinen finger/das ist/durch den heyligen Geyst/damit er [p. 182] die ohren öffnet/vnnd durch seinen speychel/das ist/durch sein wort/damit er die zunge löset. Denn wer Gottes wort fleyssig höret/der empfehet dadurch den heyligen Geyst/vnd folget ferner/das die hertzen Gottes wort annemen/vnd gern hören/vnd der munde den glauben bekennet/vñ Gott vnd sein gnad rhümet vñ preyset. Solches ist vnsers lieben Herren Christi werck vnd kunst/welche er hie leyblich an disem armen menschen bewisen hat/An vns aber will ers geistlich beweysen/sd [sic, for so] nur wir vns fleyssig zu seim wort halten/Amen.

This miracle our dear Lord Christ will continually perform and show in His Church. For although bodily we are neither dumb nor deaf, yet our hearts are so poisoned by sin that we will neither hear nor speak God's word; we would rather hear and speak of offensive, frivolous and unprofitable things. And the wicked enemy helps in this too, as we see that the young people curse and blaspheme God, and have little devotion or desire for the sermon. These are deaf ears and bound, dumb tongues. This misery our dear Lord Christ now remedies by His finger, that is, by the Holy Ghost, with which He opens the ears, and by His spittle, that is, by His word, with which He looses the tongue. For whoever hears God's word diligently receives the Holy Ghost through it; and it follows further that hearts receive God's word and gladly hear it, and that the mouth confesses the faith, and praises and extols God and His grace. This is the work and art of our dear Lord Christ, which He showed here bodily on this poor man; but on us He will show it spiritually, if only we keep diligently to His word. Amen.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Mark 7:33–35 in King James wording, introduced by "Our Lord Jesus took." Dietrich has "the dumb and deaf man" for the King James "him." *spürzet* is an old form of "spat."
- **"His finger, that is, the Holy Ghost."** Compare Luke 11:20, "the finger of God," with Matthew 12:28, "the Spirit of God" (Lent 3, [27]).
- **Misprint.** *sd* has d for o. In *taub* the printer uses a b with a closed, looped ascender, which looks like ß but sits on the line; the same b occurs in *lieben* on p. 184.
- **"Amen."** The summary ends with "Amen," as at Trinity 3 and 10.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 182–183)**

> ALmechtiger ewiger Got/du gnediger schöpffer aller ding/Jch dancke dir/das du mir gesunden leyb geben/vnnd meine zungen/ohren/vnd andere glidmaß/genedigklich für dem bösen feind behütet hast/Vnd bitte dich/gibe mir dein gnad/das ich sonderlich meine ohren vñ zunge/recht brauche/Mit meinen ohren dein wort fleyssig hören/vñ wol mercken/vnnd mit meiner zungen/dein gnad preysen vñ rhümen möge/auff das niemandt durch mein zunge geergert/sonder yederman dadurch gebessert werde/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Almighty and everlasting God, Thou gracious Creator of all things, I thank Thee that Thou hast given me a sound body, and hast graciously preserved my tongue, ears, and other members from the evil foe; and I beseech Thee, grant me Thy grace, that I may rightly use especially my ears and tongue: with my ears diligently hear Thy word and mark it well, and with my tongue praise and magnify Thy grace, that no one may be offended by my tongue, but that all may be edified thereby; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Congregational form (TLHy practice)**

> Almighty and everlasting God, Thou gracious Creator of all things, we thank Thee that Thou hast given us sound bodies, and hast graciously preserved our tongues, ears, and other members from the evil foe; and we beseech Thee, grant us Thy grace, that we may rightly use especially our ears and tongues: with our ears diligently hear Thy word and mark it well, and with our tongues praise and magnify Thy grace, that no one may be offended by our tongues, but that all may be edified thereby; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- Person: Dietrich's prayer is in the first person singular ("I"); TLHy makes it plural. The revised text follows Dietrich, and a congregational form is supplied (§2.2).
- "who hast created all things" → "Thou gracious Creator of all things" (*du gnediger schöpffer aller ding*).
- "our tongues and other members" → "my tongue, ears, and other members" (*meine zungen/ohren/vnd andere glidmaß*); TLHy omits "ears."
- "from the power of the adversary" → "from the evil foe" (*für dem bösen feind*).
- "that we may rightly use our ears and tongues" → "that I may rightly use especially my ears and tongue" (*das ich sonderlich meine ohren vñ zunge/recht brauche*).
- "help us to hear Thy word diligently and devoutly, and with our tongues so to praise and magnify Thy grace" → "with my ears diligently hear Thy word and mark it well, and with my tongue praise and magnify Thy grace" (*Mit meinen ohren dein wort fleyssig hören/vñ wol mercken/vnnd mit meiner zungen/dein gnad preysen vñ rhümen möge*).
- "offended by our words" → "offended by my tongue" (*durch mein zunge geergert*). Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics.

#### [59] Thirteenth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 183):** *Am dreyzehendē Sontag von der schrifftgelerten frag/was man thun soll/das man selig werde/Luce am 10.* [On the Thirteenth Sunday: of the lawyer's question, what one must do to be saved. Luke 10.]

**Verse (PDF p. 184)**

> CHristus wandte sich zu seinen Jüngern/vnnd sprach in sonderheyt: Selig sind die augen/die da sehen das jr sehet/Denn ich sage euch: Vil König vnnd Propheten/wolten sehen das jr sehet/vnd habens nit gesehen/vnd hören das jr höret/vnd habens nicht gehöret.

Christ turned him unto his disciples, and said privately, Blessed are the eyes which see the things that ye see: for I tell you, that many kings and prophets have desired to see those things which ye see, and have not seen them; and to hear those things which ye hear, and have not heard them.

**Summary (PDF pp. 184–185)**

> ES preyset vnser lieber Herre Christus/mit disen worten die lehr des heyligē Euangelij/das es der höchste/beste/vnd edleste schatz sey/sintemal wir allein durch das Euangelion/zum rechtē erkentnuß Gottes/zu vergebung der sünde/vñ dem ewigen leben komen. Das Gesetz/wie der Schrifftgelerter hie drauff dringet/ist wol auch ein göttliche rechtgeschaffne lehr/Denn was kan man bessers heyssen/denn das wir Gott von gantzem hertzen/vnd vber alles/vnd den nechsten alß vns selb lieben sollen? [p. 185] Aber es ist nit ein selige lehr/dadurch wir köndten selig werden. Denn wir können solchem befelh nicht erfüllen/vnsere hertzen sindt durch die sünde dermassen verderbt/das wir weder Gott/noch vnsern nechsten können lieben wie wir solten. Jst also der fehle nicht an der lehr/sonder an vns/Das Euangelion aber ist die rechte selige lehr/die selige ohren vñ augen macht/denn es bringt vns den heyligen geyst der zündet in den hertzen den glauben an/das die sünde vns vergeben werden/vnd wir newe hertzen vberkommen/vnd Gott vnd vnsern nechsten anfahen zu lieben. Ob aber gleich die lieb so gar volkommen nicht ist/wie sie solte sein/so wirdt doch was noch daran mangelt/durch den glauben an Christum vergeben.

With these words our dear Lord Christ praises the doctrine of the holy gospel: that it is the highest, best and noblest treasure, since it is through the gospel alone that we come to the true knowledge of God, to the forgiveness of sins, and to eternal life. The law, on which the scribe here insists, is indeed also a divine and upright doctrine; for what better could one command than that we should love God with all our heart and above all things, and our neighbor as ourselves? But it is not a saving doctrine, by which we could be saved. For we cannot fulfill that command; our hearts are so corrupted by sin that we can love neither God nor our neighbor as we should. So the fault is not in the doctrine but in us. But the gospel is the true saving doctrine, which makes blessed ears and eyes; for it brings us the Holy Ghost, who kindles faith in the heart, that our sins are forgiven us, and we receive new hearts and begin to love God and our neighbor. And although love is not so wholly perfect as it should be, yet whatever is still lacking in it is forgiven through faith in Christ.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 10:23–24 in King James wording, with "Christ" for "he." Dietrich has "kings and prophets" for the King James "prophets and kings."
- **"Saving … blessed."** *selig* is both "saved" and "blessed" (see [3]); Dietrich plays on the verse's "Blessed are the eyes": the law is not a *selige lehr*, but the gospel is the *selige lehr* that makes *selige ohren vñ augen*.
- **The scribe's question.** Luke 10:25–27: "what shall I do to inherit eternal life?" The heading names it (*von der schrifftgelerten frag*).
- **"Command."** *heyssen*, to bid.

**Prayer (PDF p. 186)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/Wir dancken dir von grund vnsers hertzens/dz du vns dise selige zeyt hast erleben lassen/das wir die selige lehr/dein heyliges Euangelion hören/deinen willen vnd Väterliches hertz dadurch erkennē/vñ deinen sun Christum Jesum sehen können/Wir bitten dein grundtlose barmhertzigkeyt/du wöllest solches seliges liecht deines worts/vns gnedig erhalten/vnnd durch deinen heyligen Geyst/vnsere hertzen also leyten vnd füren/das wir nimmermehr dauon abweychen/sonder fest dran halten/ehe alles verlassen/vnd endlich dadurch selig werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we most heartily thank Thee that Thou hast granted us to live in this blessed time, when we may hear the blessed doctrine, Thy holy gospel, know thereby Thy will and fatherly heart, and behold Thy Son, Jesus Christ! We beseech Thy fathomless mercy: Graciously preserve unto us this blessed light of Thy word, and so guide and direct our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may never depart from it, but hold fast to it, and sooner forsake all things, and at last be saved thereby; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "this accepted time" → "this blessed time" (*dise selige zeyt*). TLHy's phrase comes from 2 Corinthians 6:2; Dietrich's "blessed" answers the Gospel of the day, "Blessed are the eyes which see the things that ye see" (Luke 10:23).
- "when we may hear Thy holy gospel, know Thy fatherly will" → "when we may hear the blessed doctrine, Thy holy gospel, know thereby Thy will and fatherly heart" (*das wir die selige lehr/dein heyliges Euangelion hören/deinen willen vnd Väterliches hertz dadurch erkennē*).
- "We pray Thee, most merciful Father: Let the light of Thy holy word remain with us" → "We beseech Thy fathomless mercy: Graciously preserve unto us this blessed light of Thy word" (*Wir bitten dein grundtlose barmhertzigkeyt/du wöllest solches seliges liecht deines worts/vns gnedig erhalten*).
- "and so govern our hearts" → "and so guide and direct our hearts" (*vnsere hertzen also leyten vnd füren*), as at [57].
- "that we may never forsake Thy word, but remain steadfast in it" → "that we may never depart from it, but hold fast to it, and sooner forsake all things" (*das wir nimmermehr dauon abweychen/sonder fest dran halten/ehe alles verlassen*). TLHy omits the last clause.
- "and finally obtain eternal salvation" → "and at last be saved thereby" (*vnd endlich dadurch selig werden*). Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics.
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [60] Fourteenth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 187):** *Am vierzehenden Sontag von den zehen Aussetzigen/Luce am 17.* [On the Fourteenth Sunday: of the ten lepers. Luke 17.]

**Verse (PDF p. 187)**

> JEsus sprach zu dem Samaritten: Sind jr nit zehen reyn worden? Wo sind aber die neune? Hat sich sonst keiner funden/der widerumb keret/vnd Got die ehre gebe denn diser frembdlinger/vñ er sprach zu jm: Stehe auff dein glaub hat dir geholffen.

Jesus said unto the Samaritan: Were there not ten cleansed? but where are the nine? There are not found that returned to give glory to God, save this stranger. And he said unto him, Arise, thy faith hath helped thee.

**Summary (PDF p. 188)**

> VNser lieber Herr Christus beweyset sein güte/vnd mitleydentes hertz hie widerum̃/das er den armen aussetzigen/bald sie jn vmb hilff anschreyen/so gnedigklich hilfft. Aber es wird jm vbel gedanckt/vnter den zehen ist nur einer/der ein Heyd war/der wider kombt/vñ solcher gnedigen grossen hilff jm danckt/die andern neun bl[i]ben aussen/vnd lassen sich bereden/Christus habe sie nicht gesund gemachet/es sey sonst an der zeyt also gewest/Aber den Herren verdreust solcher vndanck. Derhalb lerne yederman/Gottes gnad vñ segen erkennē/vnd darnach auch dafür dancken/vñ bitten/das Gott seinen segen von vns nicht nemen/sonder teglich mehrē wölle. Der spruch: Dein glaub hat dir geholffen/zeuget/weñ wir Gott anrüffen/vñ etwas von jm begern wöllen/das wir allein auff sein gnad vñ barmhertzigkeyt sehē/vñ der selben vns trösten sollen/so soll hilff vns widerfaren.

Our dear Lord Christ here shows His goodness and compassionate heart again, in that He so graciously helps the poor lepers as soon as they cry to Him for help. But He is ill thanked. Among the ten there is only one, who was a heathen, who comes back and thanks Him for such gracious and great help. The other nine stayed away, and let themselves be persuaded that Christ had not made them whole, but that it was simply the time for it anyway. But such ingratitude grieves the Lord. Therefore let everyone learn to acknowledge God's grace and blessing, and then also to give thanks for it, and to pray that God would not take His blessing from us, but increase it daily. The saying, "Thy faith hath helped thee," testifies that when we call upon God and would ask something of Him, we are to look to His grace and mercy alone and comfort ourselves with it; then help shall come to us.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 17:17–19 in King James wording, introduced by "Jesus said unto the Samaritan," with "helped thee" for the King James "made thee whole," as at Quinquagesima ([23]): Dietrich expounds the saying as a promise of help. *Sind jr nit zehen reyn worden* is "Are not ten of them made clean?" (*jr*, genitive "of them"), the King James "Were there not ten cleansed?"
- **"A heathen."** Dietrich calls the Samaritan *ein Heyd*, the Gospel's "stranger" (*frembdlinger*).
- **"Stayed away."** *bl[i]ben aussen*: one narrow letter has failed to print, leaving a gap; *bliben* (*blieben*) is the likely reading.
- **"Simply the time for it anyway."** *es sey sonst an der zeyt also gewest*: they would have been healed in any case, in due course.

**Prayer (PDF p. 189)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/du hast durch deinen sun/Christum Jesum vns glaubigen allen/von dem grewlichen Aussatz der sünde/durch dein wort vnd heylige Tauff gnedigklich geholffen/vnd beweysest noch tegliche gnedige hilff/in allē was wir bedürffen. Wir bitten dich/erwecke vnsere hertzen/durch deinen heyligen Geyst/das wir solcher wolthat nimmer mehr vergessen/sonder in deiner forcht vnd hertzlichem vertrawen auff deine Barmhertzigkeyt für vnnd für leben/vnd mit frölichem hertzen dir jmmerdar dancken/vnnd dich loben/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, by Thy word and Thy holy baptism hast graciously delivered all us who believe from the fearful leprosy of sin, and still dost daily grant us Thy gracious help in all our need: We beseech Thee so to quicken our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may never forget these Thy blessings, but ever live in Thy fear and in hearty confidence in Thy mercy, and with joyful hearts continually thank and praise Thee; *through Thy Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- Restored "through Thy Son, Jesus Christ" (*durch deinen sun/Christum Jesum*), which TLHy omits.
- "by Thy blessed word" → "by Thy word" (*durch dein wort*).
- "hast mercifully cleansed all who believe" → "hast graciously delivered all us who believe" (*vns glaubigen allen ... gnedigklich geholffen*).
- "and daily dost grant us" → "and still dost daily grant us" (*vnd beweysest noch tegliche gnedige hilff*).
- "so to enlighten our hearts" → "so to quicken our hearts" (*erwecke vnsere hertzen*).
- "but ever live in Thy fear, and, trusting fully in Thy grace, with thankful hearts continually praise and glorify Thee" → "but ever live in Thy fear and in hearty confidence in Thy mercy, and with joyful hearts continually thank and praise Thee" (*sonder in deiner forcht vnd hertzlichem vertrawen auff deine Barmhertzigkeyt für vnnd für leben/vnd mit frölichem hertzen dir jmmerdar dancken/vnnd dich loben*).
- Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics. "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [61] Fifteenth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 190):** *Am fünffzehendē Sontag Matthei am 6.* [On the Fifteenth Sunday. Matthew 6.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 190–191)**

> CHristus spricht: Jr solt nicht sorgen/vnnd sagen: Was werden wir essen? Was werdē wir drincken? Womit werden wir vns kleyden? Nach solchem allem trachten die Heyden/Denn ewer himlischer Vatter/weyß das jr das alles bedürffet. Trachtet am ersten nach dem Reych [p. 191] Gottes/vnnd nach seiner gerechtigkeyt/so wirdt euch solches alles zufallen.

Christ saith: Take no thought, saying, What shall we eat? What shall we drink? Wherewithal shall we be clothed? For after all these things do the Gentiles seek: for your heavenly Father knoweth that ye have need of all these things. Seek ye first the kingdom of God, and his righteousness; and all these things shall be added unto you.

**Summary (PDF pp. 191–192)**

> DJß ist ein nötige warnung/das wir Christen/vnsers beruffs vnd arbeyt trewlich warten/vñ aller zeitlicher sorg vns entschlagen/Got aber im himel drumb trawen sollen/wie er durch seinen segen vns erhalten/vnd was wir bedürffen/schaffen werde. Dahin aber sollen wir alle sorg vnnd fleyß wendē/das wir zu Gottes reich/das ist/zu Gottes wort/vnnd seiner gerechtigkeyt/das ist/zu vergebung der sünden kommen. Wenn wir das haben/so hat es nicht not/Denn wir haben einen Vater im himel/der vns seine kindlein nicht wirdt lassen not leyden/er weyß was wir bedürffen/will es auch nach notturfft vns widerfaren lassen. Solches ist ein nötige lehr/aber leyder der weniger teyl folget/Des sorgens köñē wir nit lassen/ [p. 192] vnnd wöllen mit dem glauben nit hinan/Nun hengen aber dise zwey grosse vnglück an der sorg/das wir erstlich nichts damit außrichten. Denn es ist alles allein an Gottes segen gelegen. Zum andern/ists auch ein grosse sünd/die vns vom Gottesdienst gar abzeucht/vñ macht Mammons knechte auß vns. Darumb lerne yederman für der sorge sich hüten/Nemme sich aber mit hertzen vnd ernst vmb Gottes wort an/vnnd sey in seim beruff/vnd in der arbeyt fleyssig/vnnd lasse Gott sorgen/wie wir ernehret werden.

This is a necessary warning: that we Christians are to attend faithfully to our calling and work and put away all temporal care, but trust God in heaven for this, that He will sustain us by His blessing and provide what we need. But all our care and diligence we are to turn to this, that we come to God's kingdom, that is, to God's word, and to His righteousness, that is, to the forgiveness of sins. When we have that, there is no danger. For we have a Father in heaven who will not let us, His little children, suffer want; He knows what we need, and will let it come to us as our need requires. This is a necessary teaching, but alas, the smaller part follows it. We cannot leave off worrying, and will not rise to faith. But two great misfortunes hang on worry. First, that we accomplish nothing by it, for everything depends on God's blessing alone. Secondly, it is also a great sin, which draws us away from the service of God altogether and makes servants of Mammon of us. Therefore let everyone learn to guard himself against worry, but to concern himself heartily and earnestly with God's word, and be diligent in his calling and in his work, and let God take care how we are to be fed.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 6:31–33 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ saith." *sorgen* is the King James "take thought," that is, be anxious; in the summary it is "care" and "worry."
- **"As our need requires."** *nach notturfft*.
- **"Will not rise to faith."** *wöllen mit dem glauben nit hinan*, will not go up (*hinan*) with faith.
- **"Servants of Mammon."** Matthew 6:24, "Ye cannot serve God and mammon," from the same Gospel. *Gottesdienst* is here the service of God.
- **The woodcut** (p. 190) shows a man kneeling in church between Christ and a horned devil, who tempts him with a box.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 192–193)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/Wir dancken dir für alle deine wolthat/das du leyb vnd leben geben/vnd bißher vns gnedigklich erhalten hast/Vnnd bitten dich/nimme deinen segen nicht von vns/sonder behüte für dem geytz/auff das wir dir allein dienen/dich lieben/vnd dir anhangen/vnd vns nit mit Abgötterey vñ dem schedlichen Mammonßdienst versündigen/sonder alle hoffnung/trost vnnd zuuersicht/allein auff dein güte vnd gnad setzen/durch Christum deinen Sun/vnd vnsern Herren/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee for all Thy benefits: that Thou hast given us body and life, and graciously sustained us unto this day: We beseech Thee, take not Thy blessing from us, but preserve us from covetousness, that we may serve Thee only, love Thee, and cleave unto Thee, and not sin through idolatry and the hurtful service of mammon, but set all our hope, comfort, and confidence on Thy goodness and grace alone; through Christ, Thy Son and our Lord, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "given us life" → "given us body and life" (*leyb vnd leben*).
- "preserve us from covetousness" → "but preserve us from covetousness" (*sonder behüte für dem geytz*).
- "love and abide in Thee" → "love Thee, and cleave unto Thee" (*dich lieben/vnd dir anhangen*), the wording of Deuteronomy 13:4.
- "and not defile ourselves by idolatrous love of mammon" → "and not sin through idolatry and the hurtful service of mammon" (*vnd vns nit mit Abgötterey vñ dem schedlichen Mammonßdienst versündigen*).
- "but hope and trust only in Thy grace" → "but set all our hope, comfort, and confidence on Thy goodness and grace alone" (*sonder alle hoffnung/trost vnnd zuuersicht/allein auff dein güte vnd gnad setzen*).
- "through Jesus Christ our Lord," → "through Christ, Thy Son and our Lord," which is Dietrich's own close (*durch Christum deinen Sun/vnd vnsern Herren*); the conclusion continues it (§2.4d).

#### [62] Sixteenth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 193):** *Am sechzehendē Sontag/wie Christus den todten Sun/einer Wittib auffwecket/Luc. 7.* [On the Sixteenth Sunday: how Christ raises a widow's dead son. Luke 7.]

**Verse (PDF p. 194)**

> DER Herr Jesus trat hinzu/vñ rüret den Starck [sic, for Sarck] an/vñ die treger stunden/vñ er sprach: Jüngling ich sage dir/stehe auff/vnnd der todte richtet sich auff/vnnd fieng an zu reden/vnd er gab jn seiner Mutter.

The Lord Jesus came and touched the bier: and they that bare him stood still. And he said, Young man, I say unto thee, Arise. And he that was dead sat up, and began to speak. And he delivered him to his mother.

**Summary (PDF pp. 194–195)**

> DJß herrlich wunderwerck ist zum sondern trost allen Christen geschriben/auff das wir lernen/durch den Herrn Christum vns wider den todt trösten. Denn sihe/da sonst alle welt vber einen todten menschen stunde/schrie/rüttele/vnd thete was zu thun müglich ist/so wer es doch alles vmb sonst. Aber vnser lieber Herr Christus spricht nur ein eyniges wort: Jüngling ich sage dir/stehe auff/Solches wort ist so krefftig das es der verstorbne jüngling höret/vnnd bald solchem wort nach/auffstehet vñ wider lebet. Also sihest wie des [p. 195] Herrn Christi wort allein/die rechte ärtzney wider den tod ist/Derhalb so du wilt/das dir vom tod auch geholffen werde/vnnd du zum rechten lebē kommen mögest/halte dich zu Christo/der ist der Herr vber den tod/der tod ist jm nur ein schlaff/mit eim wort kan er dir helffen/da sonst alle hilff vmb sonst vnd vergebens ist.

This glorious miracle is written for the special comfort of all Christians, so that we may learn to comfort ourselves through the Lord Christ against death. For see: though all the world otherwise stood over a dead man, cried out, shook him, and did whatever is possible to do, yet it would all be in vain. But our dear Lord Christ speaks only a single word: "Young man, I say unto thee, Arise." This word is so mighty that the dead young man hears it, and at once, at this word, rises up and lives again. So you see that the word of the Lord Christ alone is the true medicine against death. Therefore, if you would be helped from death too, and come to the true life, hold to Christ. He is the Lord over death; death is to Him only a sleep. With a word He can help you, where all other help is in vain and to no purpose.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 7:14–15 in King James wording, introduced by "The Lord Jesus." The print's *Starck* ("strong") is a misprint for *Sarck* (*Sarg*, "bier"), Luther's word here.
- **"Death is to Him only a sleep."** Compare Luke 8:52, "she is not dead, but sleepeth," and John 11:11.
- **"Shook him."** *rüttele*, as of someone trying to wake a sleeper.

**Prayer (PDF p. 195)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/der du deinen Sun hast lassen mensch werdē/das er erstlich mit seinem todt für vnsere sünde bezalen/vnd darnach vns vom ewigen todt helffen soll/Wir bitten dich erhalte vns in solcher hoffnung/auff das wir ja nicht dran zweyffeln/wie vnser lieber Herr Christus der Witwen sun/durch sein wort hat aufferwecket/das er der gleichen vns am Jüngsten tag auch aufferwecken/vnnd ewig werde selig machen/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who didst suffer Thy Son to become man, that He should first by His death pay for our sins, and then deliver us from eternal death: We pray Thee, keep us in this hope, that we may in no wise doubt that, as our dear Lord Christ by His word raised the widow's son, so He will in like manner raise us also on the last day, and save us eternally; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "who didst send Thy Son to be made flesh" → "who didst suffer Thy Son to become man" (*der du deinen Sun hast lassen mensch werdē*), TLHy's own wording for this phrase at [3]. "Send" is not in the German.
- "that by His death He might atone for our sins and deliver us from eternal death" → "that He should first by His death pay for our sins, and then deliver us from eternal death" (*das er erstlich mit seinem todt für vnsere sünde bezalen/vnd darnach vns vom ewigen todt helffen soll*).
- "confirm in our hearts the hope that" → "keep us in this hope, that we may in no wise doubt that" (*erhalte vns in solcher hoffnung/auff das wir ja nicht dran zweyffeln*).
- "our Lord Jesus Christ, who with but a word raised the widow's son, in like manner will raise us on the last day, and grant us eternal life" → "as our dear Lord Christ by His word raised the widow's son, so He will in like manner raise us also on the last day, and save us eternally" (*wie vnser lieber Herr Christus der Witwen sun/durch sein wort hat aufferwecket/das er der gleichen vns am Jüngsten tag auch aufferwecken/vnnd ewig werde selig machen*).
- Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics; the OLM web text's colon before it is made a semicolon.

#### [63] Seventeenth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 196):** *Am sibenzehenden Sontag/wie der Herr Christus inn eins Phariseers hause/am Sabbath einen Wassersüchtigen gesund machet/Luce am 14.* [On the Seventeenth Sunday: how the Lord Christ, in a Pharisee's house on the Sabbath, makes a man with dropsy whole. Luke 14.]

**Verse (PDF p. 196)**

> WER sich selb erhöhet/soll ernidriget werden/vnnd wer sich selb ernidriget/der soll erhöhet werden.

Whosoever exalteth himself shall be abased; and he that humbleth himself shall be exalted.

**Summary (PDF pp. 197–198)**

> CHristus beweyset sich hie wider/das er sich vnsers ellends vnd jammers annemen/vnd vns gern wil helffen/in allerley nöten. Derhalben sollen wir vns zu jm haltē/vnd durch glaubiges gebet solche hilff bey jm suchen. Es ergert aber die Phariseer/das er solches gutes werck auff einen Sabbath thut/Darum̃ verantwortet sich der Herr/vnd lehret man soll sonderlich auff den Sabbath sich fleyssigen/das man armen leuten helffe. Deñ Gottes gebot heyst nicht/das man auff dem Sabbath gar müssig gehen/vñ nichts thun soll/sonder das man jn heyligen/das ist/mit heyligen wercken sol zu bringen. Nu sind aber das heylige werck/Gottes wort hören/andere in Gottes wort vnterrichten/Zum gebet vnd Gottesdienst bey andern Christen sich samlen/Almosen geben/vnnd in andere wege armen dürfftigen leuten dienen/Mit solchen wercken wirdt der Sabbath nicht gebrochen [p. 198]/sonder nach Gottes willen geheyliget. Das westen die Phariseer nicht/die hoffertigen heyligen/Legtens derhalb dem Herren vbel auß/Aber er sagt/weil sie hoffertig sindt/werde sie Got demütigen.

Here again Christ shows that He will take our wretchedness and misery to heart and gladly help us in every kind of need. Therefore we are to hold to Him, and seek such help from Him by believing prayer. But it offends the Pharisees that He does such a good work on a Sabbath. Therefore the Lord answers for Himself, and teaches that on the Sabbath especially one is to take pains to help poor people. For God's commandment does not mean that on the Sabbath one is to be wholly idle and do nothing, but that one is to hallow it, that is, to spend it in holy works. Now these are holy works: to hear God's word; to instruct others in God's word; to gather with other Christians for prayer and divine service; to give alms; and to serve poor and needy people in other ways. By such works the Sabbath is not broken, but hallowed according to God's will. This the Pharisees, those proud saints, did not know, and so they took it ill of the Lord. But He says that, because they are proud, God will humble them.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 14:11 in King James wording, without its opening "For." The summary treats chiefly the healing of the man with dropsy on the Sabbath, the first part of the Gospel (Luke 14:1–6).
- **"Hallow it."** The Third Commandment, "Remember the sabbath day, to keep it holy" (Exodus 20:8; in Luther's catechism, *Du solt den Feiertag heiligen*).
- **"Divine service."** *Gottesdienst*, here public worship.
- **"Took it ill of the Lord."** *Legtens … dem Herren vbel auß*, they put a bad construction on what He did.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 198–199)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest durch deinē heyligen Gerst [sic, for Geyst]/vns also regieren vnnd füren/das wir in deiner forcht vns halten/vnd nicht hoffertig werden/sonder mit gantzem hertzen dein wort hören/vñ annemen/vñ den Sabbath recht heyligen/auff das durch dein wort wir auch geheyliget werden/erstlich also/das wir auff deinen Sun Jesum Christum/all vnser vertrawen vnd hoffnung setzen/das er allein sey vnser gerechtigkeyt vnd erlösung/vñ darnach/das wir vnser leben nach deinem wort auch bessern/vnd für allem ergernuß vnns hüten biß wir durch dein gnad in Christo ewig selig werdē/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father: We beseech Thee so to govern and direct us by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may keep ourselves in Thy fear, and not become proud, but with our whole hearts hear and receive Thy word, and rightly hallow the Sabbath, that we also may be hallowed by Thy word: first, that we may place all our trust and hope in Thy Son, Jesus Christ, who alone is our righteousness and redemption; and then, that we may also amend and better our lives in accordance with Thy word, and avoid all offenses, until we obtain eternal salvation through Thy grace in Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "so to guide and direct us" → "so to govern and direct us" (*vns also regieren vnnd füren*), as at [34] and [43].
- "that we may not exalt ourselves, but humbly fear Thee" → "that we may keep ourselves in Thy fear, and not become proud" (*das wir in deiner forcht vns halten/vnd nicht hoffertig werden*).
- "hear and keep Thy word" → "hear and receive Thy word" (*dein wort hören/vñ annemen*).
- "and hallow the Lord's day" → "and rightly hallow the Sabbath" (*vñ den Sabbath recht heyligen*), the day of the Gospel (Luke 14:1).
- "help us, first, to place our hope and confidence in Thy Son, Jesus Christ, who alone is our righteousness and Redeemer" → "first, that we may place all our trust and hope in Thy Son, Jesus Christ, who alone is our righteousness and redemption" (*erstlich also/das wir auff deinen Sun Jesum Christum/all vnser vertrawen vnd hoffnung setzen/das er allein sey vnser gerechtigkeyt vnd erlösung*). "Righteousness and redemption" are the words of 1 Corinthians 1:30.
- "and, then, so to amend and better our lives in accordance with Thy word, that we may avoid all offenses and finally obtain eternal salvation" → "and then, that we may also amend and better our lives in accordance with Thy word, and avoid all offenses, until we obtain eternal salvation" (*vñ darnach/das wir vnser leben nach deinem wort auch bessern/vnd für allem ergernuß vnns hüten biß wir ... ewig selig werdē*).
- "Through Thy grace in Christ" is Dietrich's phrase (*durch dein gnad in Christo*), as in TLHy, and the conclusion follows it (§2.4c).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit"; the OLM web text's "one true God. world without end" corrected.

#### [64] Eighteenth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 199):** *Am achzehenden Sontag wie der Schrifftgelerte erstlich Christum fraget/von dem fürnemesten gebot im Gesetz. Christus aber die Phariseer widerfraget/wens Sun Christus sey/Matth. 22.* [On the Eighteenth Sunday: how the lawyer first asks Christ about the chief commandment in the Law, but Christ asks the Pharisees in return whose Son Christ is. Matthew 22.]

**Verse (PDF p. 200)**

> DV solt Gott lieben von gantzem hertzen/von gantzer seel/vñ von gantzem gemüt/Diß ist das fürnemest/vnd das gröste Gebot. Das ander aber ist dem gleych/Du solt deinen nechsten lieben alß dich selbs/Jn disen zweyen gebotten/Hanget das gantze gesetz vnd die Propheten.

Thou shalt love God with all thy heart, and with all thy soul, and with all thy mind. This is the first and great commandment. And the second is like unto it, Thou shalt love thy neighbour as thyself. On these two commandments hang all the law and the prophets.

**Summary (PDF pp. 200–202)**

> DJse zwo fragen schickē sich sehr fein auff einander/vñ ist gleych alß in einer summa/drinn angezeyt/was ein yeder Christ wissen soll. Ein yeder Christ soll erstlich das Gesetz wissen/in welchem Gott vns lehret/was er von vns wölle haben/das wir thun sollen. Solches stehet auff disen zweyen haubtpuncten/das wir Gott vnnd vnsern nechsten von hertzen sollen lieben. Diß ist der höchste/vñ beste Gottesdienst/den wir durch vnser gantzes leben leysten/vnnd dawider nichts thun sollen. Aber da findet [p. 201] sich ein grewlicher mangel an vns menschen allen/das vnsere hertzen durch die sünde verderbet sind/vnnd vns vnmüglich ist/solche lieb reyn vñ volkommen leysten/Darumb müssen wir/neben dem Gesetz auch das Euangelion haben vnd wissen/das lehret vns/wens Sun Christus sey/Ein mensch ist er/vnd kombt von Dauids same her/Vnnd dennoch heyst jn Dauid seinen Herrn/vnnd sagt: Gott heysse jn zu seiner rechten sitzen/Solches zwinget/das/Christus nicht allein ein mensch/sonder auch Gott ist/Da folget nu ferner/das du lernest/warumb er am Creutz stirbt/warum̃ er von todten wider aufferstehet ꝛc. Es sagt aber das Euangelion/es sey geschehē vmb vnser sünde willen/das die selben versöhnet würden/vñ vmb vnser gerechtigkeyt willen/das wir in Gottes gnade ewig bliben. Durch solche predigt wirdt der heylig Geyst vns mitgetheylet/der macht newe hertzen in vns. Also wirdt vns durch den glauben an Christum vergeben/ [p. 202] was wir wider Gottes Gesetz gesündiget haben/vnnd der heylige Geyst hilfft vns/das wir doch etwas vom Gesetz thun. Wer solches von Christo nicht weyß/wie die Phariseer hie/dem ist das gesetz nur ein last/vnd fluch/vñ hilfft zur verzweyfflung vnd tod.

These two questions fit very finely together, and in them, as in a summary, is shown what every Christian should know. Every Christian should first know the law, in which God teaches us what He would have of us, what we are to do. This rests on these two chief points: that we are to love God and our neighbor from the heart. This is the highest and best service of God, which we are to render through our whole life, and against which we are to do nothing. But here a dreadful lack is found in all of us men: our hearts are corrupted by sin, and it is impossible for us to render such love pure and perfect. Therefore, besides the law, we must also have and know the gospel. This teaches us whose son Christ is. He is a man, and comes of David's seed; and yet David calls Him his Lord, and says that God bids Him sit at His right hand. This compels the conclusion that Christ is not only a man but also God. Then it follows further that you learn why He dies on the cross, why He rises again from the dead, etc. And the gospel says that this happened for our sins, that they might be atoned for, and for our righteousness, that we might remain forever in God's grace. Through such preaching the Holy Ghost is imparted to us, and He makes new hearts in us. So through faith in Christ we are forgiven whatever we have sinned against God's law, and the Holy Ghost helps us to do at least something of the law. Whoever does not know this of Christ, as the Pharisees here do not, to him the law is only a burden and a curse, and helps him to despair and death.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 22:37–40 in King James wording. Dietrich has "Thou shalt love God" without Luther's "thy Lord" (*deinen HErrn*); *das fürnemest/vnd das gröste* is the King James "the first and great."
- **The two questions.** The scribe's question about the great commandment (Matthew 22:35–40) and Christ's question to the Pharisees, "What think ye of Christ? whose son is he?" (22:41–46), answered from Psalm 110:1, "The LORD said unto my Lord, Sit thou at my right hand."
- **"Service of God."** *Gottesdienst*.
- **"For our sins … for our righteousness."** Romans 4:25, the text of Easter Day ([34]).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 202–203)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/Wir sind ja arme ellende sünder/Deinen willen wissen wir/aber wir sind zu schwach/wir können jn nicht erfüllen/fleysch vnd blut ist in vns zu starck/So lest der leydige feind der Teuffel/vnns auch nicht zu friden/Darumb bitten wir dich/du wöllest deinen heyligen Geyst inn vnsere hertzē außgiessen/das wir mit festem glauben/vns an deinen Sun Christum Jesum halten/seines todes vnd sterbens vns trösten/vnnd volle vergebung aller sünden/durch jhn glauben/Vnnd also hie auff erden in deinem willen vnnd gehorsam heyligklich leben/vnd auff dein gnad/durch Christum Jesum seliglich sterben mögen/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father: We are indeed poor, miserable sinners; we know Thy will, but we are too weak to fulfill it, for flesh and blood is too strong in us; moreover, the hateful enemy, the devil, will not leave us in peace. Therefore we beseech Thee, shed Thy Holy Ghost in our hearts, that, in steadfast faith, we may cling to Thy Son, Jesus Christ, find comfort in His death and dying, and believe the full forgiveness of all sins through Him; and so here on earth lead holy lives in Thy will and obedience, and, trusting in Thy grace, die blessedly through Jesus Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "We are poor, miserable sinners" → "We are indeed poor, miserable sinners" (*Wir sind ja arme ellende sünder*).
- "but cannot fulfill it because of the weakness of our flesh and blood" → "but we are too weak to fulfill it, for flesh and blood is too strong in us" (*aber wir sind zu schwach/wir können jn nicht erfüllen/fleysch vnd blut ist in vns zu starck*). TLHy reverses Dietrich's point: the weakness is ours, and flesh and blood are too strong.
- "and because our enemy, the devil, will not leave us in peace" → "moreover, the hateful enemy, the devil, will not leave us in peace" (*So lest der leydige feind der Teuffel/vnns auch nicht zu friden*).
- "find comfort in His passion and death" → "find comfort in His death and dying" (*seines todes vnd sterbens vns trösten*).
- "believe the forgiveness of sin through Him" → "believe the full forgiveness of all sins through Him" (*volle vergebung aller sünden/durch jhn glauben*).
- "and in willing obedience to Thy will lead holy lives on earth" → "and so here on earth lead holy lives in Thy will and obedience" (*Vnnd also hie auff erden in deinem willen vnnd gehorsam heyligklich leben*).
- "until by Thy grace, through a blessed death, we depart from this world of sorrow, and obtain eternal life, through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord," → "and, trusting in Thy grace, die blessedly through Jesus Christ," (*vnd auff dein gnad/durch Christum Jesum seliglich sterben mögen*), as at [13]. "This world of sorrow" and "obtain eternal life" are not in the German; Dietrich's "through Jesus Christ" leads into the conclusion (§2.4c).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."
