#### [65] Nineteenth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 203):** *Am neunzehendē Sontag wie Christus einem Gichtbrüchtigen menschen seine sünde vergibt/vnd jn gesund machet/Matth. 9.* [On the Nineteenth Sunday: how Christ forgives a man sick of the palsy his sins, and makes him whole. Matthew 9.]

**Verse (PDF p. 204)**

> CHristus sprach: Auff das jr wisset das des menschen Sun macht habe/die sünde zu vergeben auff erden/sprach er zu dem Gichtbrüchtigen: Stehe auff/heb dein bette auff/vnd gehe heym/vnnd er stund auff/vnd gieng heym.

Christ said: That ye may know that the Son of man hath power on earth to forgive sins, (then saith he to the sick of the palsy,) Arise, take up thy bed, and go unto thine house. And he arose, and departed to his house.

**Summary (PDF pp. 204–205).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> ERstlich lernen wir im heutigen Euangelio/wie kranckheyt durch sünde verursachet werde/Sonst solte der Herr dem Gichtbrüchtigen nicht ehe vergebung der sünden haben zugesagt/denn jn geheylet. Derhalb weil doch yedermā kranckheyt scheuhet/vnd gern gesunden leyb behalten wolt/soll sonderlich das junge volck sich für sünden hüten/vnd in Gottes forcht wandeln/Vnd wen Got angriffen/vñ mit kranckheit beladen hat/der versöne sich am aller ersten mit Gott/vnd sehe wie er von sünden ledig wer/so soll es mit der kranckheyt sich auch bald bessern.

First, we learn in today's Gospel how sickness is caused by sin; otherwise the Lord would not have promised the paralytic forgiveness of sins before He healed him. Therefore, since everyone shrinks from sickness and would gladly keep a healthy body, the young people especially are to guard themselves against sins and walk in the fear of God. And whomever God has laid hold of and burdened with sickness, let him first of all be reconciled with God, and see to it that he is rid of his sins; then it shall soon be better with the sickness too.

> [p. 205] Zum andern lernen wir hie/wo wir vergebung der sünden sollen suchen/Nemlich/anderswo niergend/denn bey dem Herrn Christo/Der hat das predigampt/vnd die heyligen Sacrament/hinder sich auff erden gelassen/vnd befolhen in seinem name Buß vñ vergebung der sünden zu predigen/Derhalb so du vergebung deiner sünden begerest/suche es in dem wort vñ bey den Sacramenten/vnd was das wort dir durch Christum zusagt/das nimm mit vnzweyffenlichem glauben an/so soll es dir also widerfaren.

Secondly, we learn here where we are to seek the forgiveness of sins: namely, nowhere else than with the Lord Christ. He has left the office of preaching and the holy sacraments behind Him on earth, and has commanded that repentance and forgiveness of sins be preached in His name. Therefore, if you desire the forgiveness of your sins, seek it in the word and in the sacraments; and what the word promises you through Christ, receive with undoubting faith. Then it shall be done to you accordingly.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 9:6–7 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ said."
- **Sickness and sin.** Compare John 5:14: "sin no more, lest a worse thing come unto thee." *wen Got angriffen … hat*: whom God has seized, afflicted.
- **"Repentance and forgiveness of sins … in His name."** Luke 24:47. *in seinem name* is printed without a final n.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 205–206)**

> ALmechtiger Ewiger Gott/der du durch deinen Sun Christum Jesum dem Gichtbrüchtigen menschen an leib vnd seel/hast gnedigklich helffen lassen/Jch bit dein grundlose barmhertzigkeyt/sey mir auch genedig/vnnd vergib alle meine sünde/stercke meinen glauben/durch dein wort vnd heyligen Geyst/vnnd leyte mich also/das ich nit durch sünde zur kranckheyt/vñ anderm vnglück/vrsach gebe/sonder in deiner forcht mich halte/vnd also vom zeytlichen vnnd ewigem zorn vñ straff erledigt werde/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Almighty and everlasting God, who by Thy Son, Jesus Christ, didst graciously help the palsied man both in body and soul: I beseech Thy fathomless mercy: Be gracious also unto me; forgive me all my sins; strengthen my faith by Thy word and Thy Holy Ghost, and so lead me, that I may not by sin give occasion to sickness and other misfortune, but keep myself in Thy fear, and so be delivered from temporal and eternal wrath and punishment; *through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Congregational form (TLHy practice)**

> Almighty and everlasting God, who by Thy Son, Jesus Christ, didst graciously help the palsied man both in body and soul: We beseech Thy fathomless mercy: Be gracious also unto us; forgive us all our sins; strengthen our faith by Thy word and Thy Holy Ghost, and so lead us, that we may not by sin give occasion to sickness and other misfortune, but keep ourselves in Thy fear, and so be delivered from temporal and eternal wrath and punishment; *through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- Person: Dietrich's prayer is in the first person singular ("I"); TLHy makes it plural. The revised text follows Dietrich, and a congregational form is supplied (§2.2).
- "O mighty and everlasting God" → "Almighty and everlasting God" (*ALmechtiger Ewiger Gott*).
- "mercifully help" → "graciously help" (*gnedigklich helffen lassen*).
- "We beseech Thee, for the sake of Thy great mercy" → "I beseech Thy fathomless mercy" (*Jch bit dein grundlose barmhertzigkeyt*), as at [59].
- Restored "strengthen my faith by Thy word and Thy Holy Ghost" (*stercke meinen glauben/durch dein wort vnd heyligen Geyst*), which TLHy omits.
- "and so govern us by Thy Holy Spirit" → "and so lead me" (*vnnd leyte mich also*).
- "that we may not ourselves be the cause of sickness and other afflictions" → "that I may not by sin give occasion to sickness and other misfortune" (*das ich nit durch sünde zur kranckheyt/vñ anderm vnglück/vrsach gebe*).
- "keep us in Thy fear, and strengthen us by Thy grace that we may escape temporal and eternal wrath and punishment" → "but keep myself in Thy fear, and so be delivered from temporal and eternal wrath and punishment" (*sonder in deiner forcht mich halte/vnd also vom zeytlichen vnnd ewigem zorn vñ straff erledigt werde*).
- Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics.

#### [66] Twentieth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 206):** *Am zwentzigstē Sontag/Das gleychnuß von der Hochzeyt/da etliche gest nit auff wöllen/etliche aber kommen/vnd haben kein hochzeytlich kleyd an/die werden wider hinauß gestossen/Matth. 22.* [On the Twentieth Sunday: the parable of the wedding, where some guests will not come, while others come but have no wedding garment on, and are thrust out again. Matthew 22.]

**Verse (PDF p. 207)**

> DER König gieng hinein die geste zubesehen/Vnnd sahe alda einen menschen/der het kein hochzeytliches kleyd an/vnd sprach zu jm: Freund/wie bist du herein kommen/vnd hast kein hochzeytlich kleydt an? Er aber verstummet. Da sprach der König zu seinen knechten/Bindet jm hende vnnd füsse/vnd werffet jhn in das finsternuß hinauß/da wirdt sein heulen vnnd zeenklappen/Denn vil sind beruffen/aber wenig außerwelet.

The king came in to see the guests, and he saw there a man which had not on a wedding garment: and he said unto him, Friend, how camest thou in hither not having a wedding garment? And he was speechless. Then said the king to his servants, Bind him hand and foot, and cast him into outer darkness; there shall be weeping and gnashing of teeth. For many are called, but few are chosen.

**Summary (PDF pp. 207–209)**

> DJse Hochzeyt ist nichts anders/denn die predigt des heyligē Euangelij/in welcher vns Got durch seinen Sun anbeutet/vergebūg der sünden vñ ewiges leben/Solcher predigt wolten die Juden nicht/Deñ sie gedachten/sie hetten an Mose vnd dem Gesetz gnug zur seligkeyt/vñ bedörfften [p. 208] des Herrn Christi/vnd seines worts nichts dazu. Etliche/wie noch heuttigs tages geschicht/machten jhn mit dem zeytlichen so vil zu schaffen/das sie nicht weyl hetten/mit dem Euangelio vmbzugehen/Alß nun Gott die Juden liesse faren/vmb jres vnglaubens willen/vnnd beruffet zu diser Hochzeyt die Heyden/kamen sie mit grossem hauffen. Aber da finden sich etliche die haben kein hochzeytliches kleyd an/Jn der heyligen Tauff wirt vns ein reynes schönes kleyd angezogen/Nemlich vergebung der sünde durch Christum vnd gerechtigkeyt. Solches kleyd sollen wir reyn vñ sauber behalten/im glauben teglich wachsen vnd zunemen/vnnd für sünden vns hüten/Ob wir aber fallen/vnd durch sünde solches kleyd wider besudlen/sollen wir Buß thun/das ist/von den sünden ablassen/vnnd vmb gnad bitten. Aber der meyste theyl gehet in sünden on alle bußfertigkeyt hin/Dise haben kein hochzeytlich kleyd/vnd ob sie wol hie auff erden sich bergen [p. 209] können/sollen sie doch an yenem tag/außgestossen/vnnd vmb jrer sünden willen/mit der ewigen flammen gestraffet werdē. Jst derhalb diß Euangelion ein warnung vñ vermanung/das wir Gottee [sic, for Gottes] wort fleyssig hören/vnd vns darauß bessern/oder des ewigen zorns gewarten sollen.

This wedding is nothing else than the preaching of the holy gospel, in which God offers us, through His Son, the forgiveness of sins and eternal life. The Jews would not have this preaching; for they thought they had enough for salvation in Moses and the law, and had no need of the Lord Christ and His word besides. Some, as still happens today, made so much business for themselves with temporal things that they had no leisure to occupy themselves with the gospel. Now when God let the Jews go because of their unbelief, and called the heathen to this wedding, they came in great crowds. But here some are found who have not on a wedding garment. In holy baptism a pure and beautiful garment is put on us: namely, the forgiveness of sins through Christ, and righteousness. This garment we are to keep pure and clean, to grow and increase daily in faith, and to guard ourselves against sins. But if we fall, and soil this garment again by sin, we are to repent, that is, leave off our sins and pray for grace. But the greater part goes on in sins without any penitence. These have no wedding garment; and although they may hide themselves here on earth, yet on that day they shall be cast out and punished for their sins with the eternal flame. This Gospel, therefore, is a warning and an admonition that we are to hear God's word diligently and amend ourselves by it, or else look for the eternal wrath.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 22:11–14 in King James wording; Dietrich has "said" (*sprach*) for "saith," "his servants" for "the servants," and lacks "and take him away"; the translation follows him. *in das finsternuß hinauß*, "out into the darkness," is the King James "into outer darkness."
- **The baptismal garment.** Galatians 3:27, "as many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ"; compare Isaiah 61:10, "the robe of righteousness."
- **"Let the Jews go."** *liesse faren*, let them go their way, gave them up.
- **"Penitence."** *bußfertigkeyt*.
- **Misprint.** *Gottee* for *Gottes*.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 209–210)**

> HERR Gott himlischer Vatter/Jch dancke dir für dein grosse barmhertzigkeyt/vnnd gütigkeyt/das du mich zu deinem lieben wort auff die fröliche hochzeyt/kommen lassen/vnd durch deinen Sun/mir all meine sünde vergeben hast. Weil aber der teglichen anfechtung/ergernuß vnnd fahr/so vil ist/vñ wir für vns selb sehr schwach/vñ zur sünden hefftig geneyget sind/Bitt ich dich/du wöllest durch deinē heyligen Geyst/mich für allē fall gnedig behüten/Vnd aber wo ich falle/vnnd also mein kleyd/welches mir dein lieber Sun Christus Jesus angezogen hat/besudle/gnedig wider auff helffen/vnd mich zur rechten buß/vñ warem glauben/auff dein barmhertzigkeyt leyten/vnd in ewigkeyt nicht sincken lassen/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, I thank Thee for Thy great mercy and kindness, that Thou hast caused me to come to Thy dear word, unto the joyful marriage-feast, and through Thy Son hast forgiven me all my sins; but, since daily temptation, offense, and danger are so many, and we are very weak in ourselves and strongly inclined to sin, I beseech Thee graciously to protect me by Thy Holy Ghost from every fall; and if I fall, and so defile my wedding-garment, with which Thy dear Son, Jesus Christ, hath clothed me, graciously help me up again, and lead me to true repentance and to true faith in Thy mercy, and let me not sink forever; *through our Lord Jesus Christ, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Congregational form (TLHy practice)**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee for Thy great mercy and kindness, that Thou hast caused us to come to Thy dear word, unto the joyful marriage-feast, and through Thy Son hast forgiven us all our sins; but, since daily temptation, offense, and danger are so many, and we are very weak in ourselves and strongly inclined to sin, we beseech Thee graciously to protect us by Thy Holy Ghost from every fall; and if we fall, and so defile our wedding-garment, with which Thy dear Son, Jesus Christ, hath clothed us, graciously help us up again, and lead us to true repentance and to true faith in Thy mercy, and let us not sink forever; *through our Lord Jesus Christ, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- Person: Dietrich's prayer is in the first person singular ("I"), turning to "we" for the weakness common to all; TLHy makes it plural throughout. The revised text follows Dietrich, and a congregational form is supplied (§2.2).
- "that of Thy great mercy Thou hast called us by Thy holy word to the blessed marriage-feast of Thy Son" → "for Thy great mercy and kindness, that Thou hast caused me to come to Thy dear word, unto the joyful marriage-feast" (*für dein grosse barmhertzigkeyt/vnnd gütigkeyt/das du mich zu deinem lieben wort auff die fröliche hochzeyt/kommen lassen*). For Dietrich, coming to the word is coming to the wedding.
- "and through Him dost forgive us all our sins" → "and through Thy Son hast forgiven me all my sins" (*vnd durch deinen Sun/mir all meine sünde vergeben hast*).
- "being daily beset by temptation, offense, and danger, and being weak in ourselves and given to sin" → "since daily temptation, offense, and danger are so many, and we are very weak in ourselves and strongly inclined to sin" (*Weil aber der teglichen anfechtung/ergernuß vnnd fahr/so vil ist/vñ wir für vns selb sehr schwach/vñ zur sünden hefftig geneyget sind*).
- "that we fall not" → "from every fall" (*für allē fall*).
- "with which Thy Son hath clothed us" → "with which Thy dear Son, Jesus Christ, hath clothed me" (*welches mir dein lieber Sun Christus Jesus angezogen hat*).
- "graciously help us again and lead us to repentance, that we fall not forever; preserve in us a constant faith in Thy grace" → "graciously help me up again, and lead me to true repentance and to true faith in Thy mercy, and let me not sink forever" (*gnedig wider auff helffen/vnd mich zur rechten buß/vñ warem glauben/auff dein barmhertzigkeyt leyten/vnd in ewigkeyt nicht sincken lassen*).
- Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics. "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [67] Twenty-first Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 210):** *Am ein vnd zwentzigisten Sontag. wie der herr Jesus des Königischen Sun gesund macht/Jo. 4.* [On the Twenty-first Sunday: how the Lord Jesus makes the nobleman's son whole. John 4.]

**Verse (PDF p. 211)**

> DER Königische sprach zu Jesu: Herr/komme hinab ee deñ mein kind stirbet. Jesus spricht zu jm: Gehe hin/dein Son lebet/Der mensch glaubet dem wort/das Jesus zu jhm saget/vnd gieng hin.

The nobleman said unto Jesus, Sir, come down ere my child die. Jesus saith unto him, Go thy way; thy son liveth. And the man believed the word that Jesus had spoken unto him, and he went his way.

**Summary (PDF pp. 211–213).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> DAs ist ein sehr feine Histori/da wir erstlich jnne sehen/wie vnser lieber HERR Christus so gern vnnd willig hilfft/weñ wir nur vmb hilff bey jm ansuchen/vnnd das vertrawen zu jm haben.

This is a very fine history, in which we see, first, how gladly and willingly our dear Lord Christ helps, if only we seek help from Him and have confidence in Him.

> Zum andern/lernen wir an dem Königischen/was ein rechter glaube sey/vnd wie er für vnnd für wachsse vnd zuneme/Er hette das vertrawen zu Christo/er würde seinem krancken Son helffen/Aber solches vertrawen war noch gering vñ schwach. Denn er gedacht/wenn der Herr nit gegenwertig wer/würd er nicht können helffen/Darumb straffet jhn der Herr/Wenn jr nit zeychen vnd wunder [p. 212] sehet/spricht er/so glaubt jr nicht/Das ist/jr wölt den glauben inn den henden haben/vnnd nichts glauben was jr nit sehet. Aber glauben heyst/sich an das wort halten/vnd das vrtheyl nicht stellen nach dem die augen sehen/sonder nach dem die ohren hören. Derhalb da der Königische ferner vmb hilf anhelt/gibt der Herr jm das blosse wort/das er spricht: Gehe hyn/dein Son lebet/Solches wort glaubt der Königisch/vnd gehet hyn in vollem vertrawen/wie der Herr gesagt hab/also sey es/vnd findet sich auch also in der erfarung. Dahin soll vnd muß es mit vnns auch kommen/soll vns geholffen werdē. Vergebung der sünde/Gottes gnad/vnd das ewige leben/sehen wir mit augen nicht/wir hörens aber im wort/Wer nu das wort mit glauben annimbt/vñ zweiffel[et] nit/dem sol geschehē wie er glaubet/Vnd ob er gleich noch sünde fület/sollen doch die sünde durch Christum jm vergeben sein/Ob er gleych nichts denn den todt sihet vnnd fület/soll [p. 213] doch das ewige leben gewiß folgen/Wie der Herr etlich mal sagt: Dir geschehe wie du glaubest. Weyl aber der glaub nicht ein werck ist/das wir von vns selb vermögen/der heilige Geyst muß es geben/Derhalb soll ein yeder Christ Got ernstlich drum̃ bitten/vnd fleyssig sich zu Gottes wort halten.

Secondly, we learn from the nobleman what true faith is, and how it continually grows and increases. He had the confidence in Christ that He would help his sick son; but this confidence was still slight and weak. For he thought that if the Lord were not present, He would not be able to help. Therefore the Lord rebukes him: "Except ye see signs and wonders," He says, "ye will not believe"; that is, you would have faith in your hands, and believe nothing that you do not see. But to believe means to hold to the word, and to form one's judgment not by what the eyes see but by what the ears hear. Therefore, when the nobleman persists further in asking for help, the Lord gives him the bare word, saying: "Go thy way; thy son liveth." This word the nobleman believes, and goes his way in full confidence that as the Lord has said, so it is; and so he also finds it in experience. To this it shall and must come with us too, if we are to be helped. The forgiveness of sins, God's grace and eternal life we do not see with our eyes, but we hear them in the word. Whoever now receives the word with faith and does not doubt it, to him it shall be done as he believes. And although he still feels sin, yet his sins shall be forgiven him through Christ; although he sees and feels nothing but death, yet eternal life shall surely follow. As the Lord says more than once: "As thou hast believed, so be it done unto thee." But since faith is not a work that we are able to do of ourselves, but the Holy Ghost must give it, every Christian is to pray God earnestly for it and keep diligently to God's word.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 4:49–50 in King James wording, with Dietrich's "said unto Jesus" for "saith unto him." *der Königische* (Luther's word, "the king's man," a royal official) is the King James "nobleman."
- **"Except ye see signs and wonders."** John 4:48 in King James wording.
- **"Faith in your hands."** *den glauben inn den henden haben*: to want faith to be something one can hold and see.
- **"As thou hast believed."** Matthew 8:13 in King James wording; compare Matthew 9:29 and 15:28. *etlich mal*, "several times."
- **"Not a work."** Compare Ephesians 2:8, "it is the gift of God."
- **Damaged type.** In *zweiffel[et]* the letters after *fel* are smudged; *zweiffelet* is the likely reading.

**Prayer (PDF p. 213)**

> ALmechtiger ewiger got/der du durch deinen son/vergebung vnser sünde/gerechtigkeyt vnd ewiges leben/vns hast verheyssen/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst/vnsere hertzen also füren vnd erwecken/das wir solche hilff durch tegliches gebet/vnd sonderlich inn allen anfechtung bey jm suchen/vnd durch ein rechten festen glauben/auff sein zusagung vñ wort/gewiß finden vñ erlangen/Vnd endlich durch jn ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Almighty and everlasting God, who by Thy Son hast promised us the forgiveness of our sins, righteousness, and everlasting life: We beseech Thee, do Thou by Thy Holy Ghost so lead and quicken our hearts, that we may seek such help from Him in daily prayer, and especially in all temptations, and through a right and firm faith in His promise and word surely find and attain it, and at last obtain eternal salvation through Him, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "the forgiveness of sins" → "the forgiveness of our sins" (*vergebung vnser sünde*).
- "so quicken our hearts" → "so lead and quicken our hearts" (*vnsere hertzen also füren vnd erwecken*).
- "that we in daily prayer may seek our help in Christ against all temptations" → "that we may seek such help from Him in daily prayer, and especially in all temptations" (*das wir solche hilff durch tegliches gebet/vnd sonderlich inn allen anfechtung bey jm suchen*).
- "and, constantly believing His promise, obtain that for which we pray" → "and through a right and firm faith in His promise and word surely find and attain it" (*vnd durch ein rechten festen glauben/auff sein zusagung vñ wort/gewiß finden vñ erlangen*).
- "and at last be saved, through Thy Son Jesus Christ," → "and at last obtain eternal salvation through Him," which is Dietrich's phrase (*Vnd endlich durch jn ewig selig werden*); the conclusion follows (§2.4c).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [68] Twenty-second Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 214):** *Am zwey vñ zwentzigsten Sontag/Das gleychnuß vom knecht der seym Herrn zehen tausent pfund schuldig war/vnnd hette nicht zubezalen/Matth. 18.* [On the Twenty-second Sunday: the parable of the servant who owed his lord ten thousand pounds and had nothing to pay. Matthew 18.]

**Verse (PDF p. 214)**

> PEtrus sprach zum Herrn Jesu: Wie offt muß ich denn meinem Bruder vergeben/der an mit [sic, for mir] [s]ündiget? Jsts gnug siben mal? Jesus spricht zu jm: Jch sage dir nicht siben mal/sonder sibentzig mal siben mal.

Peter said unto the Lord Jesus, How oft shall my brother sin against me, and I forgive him? till seven times? Jesus saith unto him, I say not unto thee, Until seven times: but, Until seventy times seven.

**Summary (PDF pp. 215–216).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> DAs gleichnuß von dē knechte/der seinem Herrn schuldig war/vnd nicht kondt bezalen/lehret vnns wie Gott sich gegen vns wölle halten/vnd wir vns gegen den leuten halten sollen. Alle menschen auff erden sind Gott schuldig/vnnd ist nicht müglich/das sie können bezalen/Deñ in sünden sindt wir geboren/vnnd ist vns vnmüglich/das wir vns selb von sünden helffen/vnd die verderbte natur köndten endern. Darumb gehet des gesetzes sententz vnd vrtheyl vber vns/das wir den todt verschuldet haben. Wer nu solchen jammer sihet vñ weyß/vnd lest jm sein sünde leyd sein/vnd gedencket der schulde nicht mehr zu machen/vnd bittet alß denn Gott vmb gedult vnd vergebung/vnd hoffet durch Christum vergebung/Dem will Gott alle schuld nachlassen/vnd schencken frey lauter vmb sonst/vmb seines Sons Christi Jesu willen.

The parable of the servant who owed his lord and could not pay teaches us how God will deal with us, and how we are to deal with people. All men on earth are debtors to God, and it is not possible for them to pay. For we are born in sins, and it is impossible for us to help ourselves out of sins and to change our corrupt nature. Therefore the sentence and judgment of the law goes forth over us, that we have deserved death. Whoever now sees and knows this misery, and is sorry for his sin, and means to run up the debt no more, and then asks God for patience and forgiveness, and hopes for forgiveness through Christ, to him God will remit all the debt, and forgive it freely, purely for nothing, for the sake of His Son, Jesus Christ.

> Da soll aber ferner folgen/gleych wie Gott vns gnedig ist/vnd gern alles [p. 216] vergibt/wenn wir jhn drumb bitten/das wir der gleichen mit vnserm nechsten auch thun/gern alles vergeben/vnd vns nicht rechen sollen/Wer es aber nicht thun/mit den leuten vnfreundlich sein/vnd nichts will nachlassen/Dem will Got auch also thun/vnd nit das wenigste nachlassen. So last nun vns Gottes genad/so durch Christum vns widerfaren ist/erkennen/vnd jm drumb dancken/vnd gegen vnserm nechsten auch alle freundligkeyt vnd dienst beweysen/vnd allen zorn faren lassen.

But it is further to follow that, as God is gracious to us and gladly forgives everything when we ask Him, so we are to do the like with our neighbor, gladly forgive everything, and not avenge ourselves. But whoever will not do this, but will be unfriendly with people and let nothing go, to him God will do the same, and not remit the least thing. So let us, then, acknowledge God's grace, which has come to us through Christ, and thank Him for it, and show our neighbor too every kindness and service, and let all anger go.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 18:21–22 in King James wording, introduced by "Peter said unto the Lord Jesus." Dietrich's "Is it enough seven times?" (*Jsts gnug siben mal*, as in Luther) is the King James "till seven times?" In the print *mit* stands for *mir*, and the s of *sündiget*, at the start of the line, has not printed.
- **"Ten thousand pounds."** The heading follows Luther's *zehen tausent pfund* (King James "ten thousand talents").
- **"Patience."** The servant's plea, "Lord, have patience with me" (Matthew 18:26).
- **"Sentence."** *sententz*, from Latin *sententia*, a judicial verdict.
- **"Run up the debt."** *der schulde … zu machen*, to incur debt.
- **"Avenge ourselves."** *vns … rechen* (*rächen*).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 216–217)**

> ALmechtiger ewiger Got/wir erkennen/das wir arme sünder sind/vnnd tieff in deinem regiester stehen/Wir dancken dir aber von hertzen/das du solche schulde von vns gekommen [sic, for genommen]/auff deinen lieben Son/Christum Jesum geleget/vnnd jn für vns hast bezalen lassen. Vnnd bitten dich auch/du wöllest in solchem glauben vns gnedig erhalten/vnd durch deinen heyligen Geyst/hie auff erden also leyten vnd füren/das wir deinem willen nach leben/vnd vnserm nechsten alle lieb/dienst vnnd hilffe gern beweysen. Widerumb/allen zorn/vngedult vnnd rachgirigkeyt fallen lassen/auff das wir deinen zoren wider vns nicht erregen/sonder jmmerdar einen genedigen Vatter durch Christum Jesum an dir habē. Der mit dir vnd dem heiligen Geyst/lebet vnd regieret/von ewigkeyt zu ewigkeyt/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Almighty, eternal God: We acknowledge that we are poor sinners, and deep in Thy debt; but with all our hearts we thank Thee, that Thou hast taken this debt from us and laid it upon Thy dear Son, Jesus Christ, and made Him pay it for us: And we pray Thee also graciously to keep us in this faith, and by Thy Holy Ghost so to guide and direct us here on earth, that we may live according to Thy will, and gladly show our neighbor all love, service, and help; and, on the other hand, put away all anger, impatience, and vindictiveness, that we may not provoke Thy wrath against us, but always find in Thee a gracious Father through Jesus Christ, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, world without end. Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "O almighty, eternal God" → "Almighty, eternal God" (*ALmechtiger ewiger Got*).
- "We confess that we are poor sinners and cannot answer one of a thousand, when Thou contendest with us" → "We acknowledge that we are poor sinners, and deep in Thy debt" (*wir erkennen/das wir arme sünder sind/vnnd tieff in deinem regiester stehen*, literally "stand deep in Thy register"). TLHy substitutes Job 9:3 for Dietrich's image of the debt book, which belongs to the parable of the day.
- "all our guilt" → "this debt" (*solche schulde*).
- "and made Him to atone for it" → "and made Him pay it for us" (*vnnd jn für vns hast bezalen lassen*).
- "We pray Thee graciously to sustain us in faith" → "And we pray Thee also graciously to keep us in this faith" (*Vnnd bitten dich auch/du wöllest in solchem glauben vns gnedig erhalten*).
- "and so to govern us by Thy Holy Spirit" → "and by Thy Holy Ghost so to guide and direct us here on earth" (*vnd durch deinen heyligen Geyst/hie auff erden also leyten vnd füren*).
- "in neighborly love, service, and helpfulness" → "and gladly show our neighbor all love, service, and help" (*vnd vnserm nechsten alle lieb/dienst vnnd hilffe gern beweysen*).
- "and not give way to wrath or revenge" → "and, on the other hand, put away all anger, impatience, and vindictiveness" (*Widerumb/allen zorn/vngedult vnnd rachgirigkeyt fallen lassen*).
- "that we may not incur Thy wrath" → "that we may not provoke Thy wrath against us" (*auff das wir deinen zoren wider vns nicht erregen*).
- The conclusion is Dietrich's own (*durch Christum Jesum ... Der mit dir vnd dem heiligen Geyst/lebet vnd regieret/von ewigkeyt zu ewigkeyt*). TLHy's "our Lord" and "one true God" are not in it and are dropped; nothing is added (§2.4g).
- "Holy Ghost" for "Holy Spirit."

#### [69] Twenty-third Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 217):** *Am drey vnd zwentzigsten Sontag/wie die Phariseer Christum versuchen/ob man dem Keyser soll den zinß geben/Matth. 22.* [On the Twenty-third Sunday: how the Pharisees tempt Christ, whether one should give tribute to Caesar. Matthew 22.]

**Verse (PDF p. 218)**

> CHristus spricht: Gebt dem Keyser/was des Keysers ist/vñ Got was Gottes ist.

Christ saith: Render unto Caesar the things which are Caesar's; and unto God the things that are God's.

**Summary (PDF pp. 218–220)**

> DJe welt hat die vnart/das sie in keinem ding maß helt. Weñ sie anfahet geistlich zu werdē/so wil sie gar geistlich sein/wie die München vnnd Pfaffen/sich allem weltlichem gehorsam entzogen haben/vnter dem schein/das sie Got möchten recht dienē. Also wo die leut in die welt hendel [p. 219] gerathen/fragen sie lauter nichts nach Gott/nach seim wort/nach dem gewissen/nach dem ewigen. Also liessen sich die Juden auch bedüncken/Sie köndten nicht zu gleich dem Keyser zu Rom/vnd Gott dienen/Aber der Herre straffet solche meynung/Vnd sagt/es soll beides beysam̃ bleibē/das man dem Keyser gebe/was des Keysers ist/vnd Got gebe/was Gottes ist/Das ist/Gott will nicht alles von vns haben/Weñ wir jm mit dem hertzen/in rechtem glauben/forcht vñ anrüffung dienen/vnd vnsern nechsten lieben/da will er mit zu friden sein. Widerumb sollen wir aber dem Keyser auch nicht alles geben/Der hat vber gelt vnd gut zugebieten/vñ vber den leyb/da sollen wir jhm mit dienen/das will Gott haben/das wir also in disen zweyen diensten/vnsern gehorsam gegen Gott/vnd weltlicher Oberkeyt beweysen sollen/Wo aber der gehorsam sich stossen/vnd wekliche [sic, for weltliche] Oberkeyt Gott das seine nemen wolt/da soll man nicht den menschen/ [p. 220] sonder Gott gehorchen/vnnd drüber leyden/was zu leyden vns fürfelt.

The world has this perversity, that it keeps measure in nothing. When it begins to become spiritual, it will be wholly spiritual, as the monks and priests have withdrawn themselves from all worldly obedience, under the pretext that they might serve God rightly. Likewise, where people get into worldly business, they care nothing at all for God, for His word, for conscience, for the eternal. So the Jews too imagined that they could not serve both the emperor at Rome and God. But the Lord rebukes this opinion, and says that both shall stand together: that one give to Caesar what is Caesar's, and to God what is God's. That is: God does not want everything from us. If we serve Him with the heart, in true faith, fear and calling upon Him, and love our neighbor, with that He will be content. On the other hand, we are not to give Caesar everything either. He has authority to command over money and goods, and over the body; with these we are to serve him. This is what God would have: that in these two services we show our obedience toward God and toward the secular authority. But where the obedience would collide, and the secular authority would take from God what is His, there one is to obey not men but God, and suffer whatever there is to suffer for it.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 22:21 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ saith"; Dietrich lacks "therefore."
- **"Caesar."** *Keyser* is both the King James "Caesar" and the ordinary word for the emperor; it is rendered "Caesar" where the verse is quoted or echoed, and "the emperor at Rome" for *dem Keyser zu Rom*.
- **"Perversity."** *vnart*, as at Pentecost ([43]).
- **"Monks and priests."** *München vnnd Pfaffen*; *Pfaffen* is disparaging, as at Exaudi ([42]).
- **"Obey not men but God."** Acts 5:29, "We ought to obey God rather than men."
- **Misprint.** *wekliche* for *weltliche*.

**Prayer (PDF p. 220)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/Wir dancken dir/das du bißher genedigen frid geben/vnd für krieg vnd frembder Herrschafft vns Vätterlich behütet hast/Wir bitten dich/gib dein gnad ferner/das wir in deiner forcht/vnnd nach deim willen leben/vnnd durch sünde nicht vrsach zu krieg/vnnd andern der gleychen straffen geben. Regiere vnd füre vnser Oberkeit also/das sie deinen gehorsam nit hindere/sonder fürdere/vnd wir dest mehr segens vnd glücks/vnter jrem Regiment haben mögen/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father: we thank Thee that Thou hast hitherto granted us gracious peace, and as a father hast protected us from war and foreign dominion: We pray Thee, give us Thy grace henceforth, that we may live in Thy fear and according to Thy will, and by sin give no cause for war and other like punishments; govern and direct our magistrates, that they may not hinder the obedience due to Thee, but further it, and that we may enjoy the more happiness and blessing under their government; *through our Lord Jesus Christ, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "granted us peace and graciously spared us" → "granted us gracious peace, and as a father hast protected us" (*genedigen frid geben/vnd ... vns Vätterlich behütet hast*).
- "graciously let us continue to live in Thy fear according to Thy will" → "give us Thy grace henceforth, that we may live in Thy fear and according to Thy will" (*gib dein gnad ferner/das wir in deiner forcht/vnnd nach deim willen leben*).
- "giving no cause for wars or other punishment" → "and by sin give no cause for war and other like punishments" (*vnnd durch sünde nicht vrsach zu krieg/vnnd andern der gleychen straffen geben*).
- "but maintain righteousness" → "but further it" (*sonder fürdere*), that is, the obedience due to God.
- "that we may enjoy happiness and blessing" → "and that we may enjoy the more happiness and blessing" (*vnd wir dest mehr segens vnd glücks ... haben mögen*).
- Dietrich gives no conclusion, so TLHy's ending is kept in italics.

#### [70] Twenty-fourth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 221):** *Am vier vnd zwentzigsten Sontag/wie vnser lieber Herr/des Obersten Dochter vom todt aufferwecket/vnnd vnter wegens dem krancken Weyblein hilfft/Matthei am 9.* [On the Twenty-fourth Sunday: how our dear Lord raises the ruler's daughter from death, and on the way helps the sick woman. Matthew 9.]

**Verses (PDF pp. 221–222).** The two verses are printed as separate paragraphs.

> DAs Weyblin sprach bey jhr selb: Möchte ich nur sein kleyd anrüren/so würde ich gesundt/Da wendet sich Jesus vmb/vnd sahe sie/ [p. 222] vnd sprach: Sey getröst mein Dochter/dein glaub hat dir geholffen/Vnd das Weyb war gesundt/zur selben stundt.
>
> Alß Jesus in des Obersten hauß kam/vnd sahe/die Pfeyffer vnd das getum̃el des volcks/sprach er zu jnen: Weychet/denn das Medlin ist nicht todt/sonder es schlefft.

The woman said within herself, If I may but touch his garment, I shall be whole. But Jesus turned him about, and when he saw her, he said, Daughter, be of good comfort; thy faith hath made thee whole. And the woman was made whole from that hour.

And when Jesus came into the ruler's house, and saw the minstrels and the people making a noise, he said unto them, Give place: for the maid is not dead, but sleepeth.

**Summary (PDF pp. 222–224).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> DAS sindt zwo treffenliche Histori/in der ersten sehen wir/wie ein so gewaltiges mechtigs ding es vmb den glauben sey/wo man alles gutes sich zum Herrn Christo versihet/vnd auff sein hilff hoffet. Denn da disem weyblin zwölff gantze Jar/kein Artzt noch ärtzney kondte helffen/wirdt sie yetzund in eim augenblick gesund/da sie des Herren Christi kleyd anrüret. Solche krafft ist nicht im kleid Christi gestecket/Denn andere leut haben es auch angerüret/aber dadurch ist jnen [p. 223] nicht geholffen/Allein der glaub vnd das vertrawen/auff den Herrn Christum hat es thun/das sie den Herren Christum dafür hielte/er würde jhr helffen/wenn sie nur sein kleyd köndte anrüren. Solchem Exempel sollen wir folgen/vñ Gottes wort mit fleyß mercken/vnnd mit gantzem hertzen dran hangen/was es vns durch Christum zusaget/das es gewiß muß geschehen/es scheyne gleych so vnmüglich für der vernunfft alß es ymmer könne.

These are two excellent histories. In the first we see what a powerful and mighty thing faith is, where one looks to the Lord Christ for every good thing and hopes for His help. For although for twelve whole years no physician or medicine could help this woman, she now becomes whole in an instant when she touches the Lord Christ's garment. This power was not hidden in Christ's garment; for other people touched it too, but they were not helped by it. Faith and trust in the Lord Christ alone did it: that she believed of the Lord Christ that He would help her if she could but touch His garment. This example we are to follow: to mark God's word diligently, and to cling with our whole heart to what it promises us through Christ, that it must surely come to pass, however impossible it may seem to reason.

> Die ander Histori/vom verstorbnen Meydlin ist auch tröstlich. Denn Christus gehet nicht anders mit jhm vmb/denn alß schlieffe es/Er nimbts bey der hand/wie man ein schlaffendes mensch auffmundert/bald stehet das Meydlin auf/vnd lebet. Derhalb sollen wir den todt ansehen/nicht wie er gegen vns ist/Denn wir sind jm zu schwach/er tödet vns alle/sonder wie er gegen dem Herren Christo ist/da ist er nicht ein todt/sonder ein schlaff/Vnnd ist gewiß/alle so an Christum [p. 224] glauben/sterben nicht/sonder entschlaffen/Vnd ist dem Herrn Christo nur vmb ein wort zu thun am Jüngsten tag/so werden sie auß dem schlaff auffwachen zum ewigen leben.

The other history, of the little girl who had died, is comforting too. For Christ deals with her no otherwise than as if she were asleep. He takes her by the hand, as one rouses a sleeping person; at once the girl gets up and lives. Therefore we are to look at death not as it is toward us, for we are too weak for it, and it kills us all, but as it is toward the Lord Christ: there it is not a death but a sleep. And it is certain that all who believe in Christ do not die, but fall asleep; and on the Last Day the Lord Christ has but to speak a word, and they will wake out of sleep to eternal life.

**Notes on the verses and summary**
- **The verses** are Matthew 9:21–22 and 9:23–24 in King James wording. Dietrich begins "The woman said within herself" (King James "For she said within herself"). Luther's *dein glaub hat dir geholffen* ("thy faith hath helped thee") is given here in the King James form, "made thee whole," since the summary speaks of her becoming whole (*gesund*).
- **"Twelve whole years … no physician."** Matthew 9:20 has the twelve years; that no physician could help her is from Mark 5:26 and Luke 8:43.
- **"Other people touched it too."** Compare Luke 8:45, "the multitude throng thee and press thee."
- **"He takes her by the hand."** Matthew 9:25.
- **"Has but to speak a word."** *ist dem Herrn Christo nur vmb ein wort zu thun*; compare Epiphany 2 ([15]), "it costs Him no more than a word."
- **Spelling.** The girl is *Medlin* in the verse and *Meydlin* in the summary; *getum̃el* is *getümmel*.

**Prayer (PDF p. 224)**

> ALmechtiger ewiger Gott/der du durch deinen Son/vergebung der sünden/vñ rettung wider den ewigen todt/vns hast zugesaget/Wir bitten dich/stercke vns durch deinen heyligen Geyst/das wir inn solchem vertrawen auf dein gnad durch Christum/teglich zunemen/vnnd die hoffnung fest vnnd gewiß behalten/das wir nicht sterben/sonder sanfft entschlaffen/vnnd durch deinen Son Christum Jesum am Jüngsten tag/zum ewigen leben wider sollen aufferwecket werden/AMEN.

**Revised TLHy**

> Almighty and everlasting God, who by Thy Son hast promised us forgiveness of sins and deliverance from eternal death: We pray Thee, strengthen us by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may daily increase in such confidence in Thy grace through Christ, and hold fast and sure the hope that we shall not die, but peacefully fall asleep, and be raised again on the last day to eternal life through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "O almighty and everlasting God" → "Almighty and everlasting God" (*ALmechtiger ewiger Gott*).
- "forgiveness of our sins" → "forgiveness of sins" (*vergebung der sünden*).
- "We pray that by Thy Holy Spirit Thou wilt daily increase our faith in Thy grace through Christ" → "We pray Thee, strengthen us by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may daily increase in such confidence in Thy grace through Christ" (*stercke vns durch deinen heyligen Geyst/das wir inn solchem vertrawen auf dein gnad durch Christum/teglich zunemen*).
- "and establish us in the certain hope" → "and hold fast and sure the hope" (*vnnd die hoffnung fest vnnd gewiß behalten*).
- "but peacefully sleep" → "but peacefully fall asleep" (*sonder sanfft entschlaffen*).
- "and be raised again on the last day to eternal life and salvation; through our Lord, Jesus Christ, Thy Son," → "and be raised again on the last day to eternal life through Thy Son, Jesus Christ," (*vnnd durch deinen Son Christum Jesum am Jüngsten tag/zum ewigen leben wider sollen aufferwecket werden*). "And salvation" is not in the German; Dietrich's "through Thy Son" leads into the conclusion (§2.4c).

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [71] Twenty-fifth Sunday after Trinity

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 225):** *Am fünff vnd zweintzigstē Sontag/vom grewel der verwüstung/Matth. 24.* [On the Twenty-fifth Sunday: of the abomination of desolation. Matthew 24.]

**Verse (PDF p. 225)**

> WEnn jhr sehen werdet den grewel der verwüstung/das er stehet an der heiligen stett/Alß denn fluhe auff die berge/wer im Jüdischen Land ist. Bittet aber das ewre flucht nit gescheh im winter/noch am Sabbath.

When ye shall see the abomination of desolation stand in the holy place, then let them which be in Judaea flee into the mountains. But pray ye that your flight be not in the winter, neither on the sabbath day.

**Summary (PDF pp. 226–227).** The paragraph breaks follow the print.

> DAs ist ein warnung für die Christen/das sie der straffe/so vber die vnglaubigen Juden ergehn must/entgehen solten. Denn diß hat Gott ye vnd ye gethon. Gleich wie er den rohen Gottlosen hauffen/endlich heimsuchet vnd straffet/Also errettet er die/so jn fürchten/vñ sein wort vor augen haben. Wie Petrus spricht: 1. Pet. 2. [sic, for 2. Pet. 2.] Der Herr weyß die gottseligen auß der versuchung zuerlösen/Die vngerechten aber/behalten zum tag des gerichts zum peynigen.

This is a warning to Christians, that they should escape the punishment which had to come upon the unbelieving Jews. For God has always done this: as He at last visits and punishes the coarse, godless crowd, so He delivers those who fear Him and keep His word before their eyes. As Peter says, 2 Peter 2: "The Lord knoweth how to deliver the godly out of temptations, and to reserve the unjust unto the day of judgment to be punished."

> Gleych aber/wie bey den Juden/die verwüstung muste folgen/da der grewel an der heyligen Stedte stunde/das ist/da an stadt des wort Gottes/die lügen vnd lesterung/vnd an stadt des heyligen Gottesdiensts/die Abgötterey mit gewalt gienge/Also gehet es noch heutiges tages/wo man wider Gottes wort tobet/es verfolget/Abgötterey verteydigt/vnd der gleychen thut/da hoffe niemandt bessers/es muß mit solchen leuten zu boden [p. 227] gehen/wenn sie gleych die mechtigsten auff erden weren.

But just as with the Jews the desolation had to follow when the abomination stood in the holy place, that is, when in place of the word of God lies and blasphemy held sway by force, and idolatry in place of the holy service of God, so it still goes today. Where men rage against God's word, persecute it, defend idolatry and do the like, let no one hope for anything better: things must go to ruin with such people, even though they were the mightiest on earth.

> Es ist aber tröstlich für die Christen/das der Herr sie heyst betten/das jr flucht nicht geschehe im winter/da das reysen schwer ist/oder am Sabbath/da es den Juden verbottē war/Denn damit lehret er/es wölle Gott das gebet der seinen erhören/vnd die straff vmb jres gebets willen lindern/Derhalb muß es bedes beysam̃ sein/das wir Gottes wort fleyssig hören/vnd es mit hertzen annemen/vñ vns drauß bessern/vnnd das wir mit dem gebet stetigs anhalten/das Gott für vnglück behütten/sünde vergeben/vnd vmb Christus willen vnser verschonen wölle/Amen.

But it is a comfort for Christians that the Lord bids them pray that their flight be not in the winter, when traveling is hard, or on the Sabbath, when it was forbidden to the Jews. For by this He teaches that God will hear the prayer of His own, and soften the punishment for the sake of their prayer. Therefore both must go together: that we hear God's word diligently, receive it with our hearts and amend ourselves by it, and that we continue steadfastly in prayer, that God would keep us from misfortune, forgive sin, and spare us for Christ's sake. Amen.

**Rubric (PDF p. 227).** Between the summary and the prayer, which follows on p. 228:

> Ende der Sontags Euangelien.

End of the Sunday Gospels.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 24:15–16 and 24:20 in King James wording, without "spoken of by Daniel the prophet" and "(whoso readeth, let him understand)," which Dietrich omits. *wer im Jüdischen Land ist* is the King James "them which be in Judaea"; *fluhe* is an old form of *fliehe*.
- **Peter's word** is 2 Peter 2:9, in King James wording; the print's "1. Pet. 2." is a misprint.
- **The abomination.** Dietrich explains it as lies and blasphemy in place of God's word, and idolatry in place of true worship (compare Daniel 9:27 and 11:31).
- **"Coarse, godless crowd."** *den rohen Gottlosen hauffen*.
- **"Go to ruin."** *zu boden gehen*, to go to the ground.
- **Order.** The rubric "End of the Sunday Gospels" stands after this summary and before its prayer; the sanctorale begins after the prayer, on p. 229.

**Prayer (PDF p. 228)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/wir dancken dir hertzlich/das du vns auß dem schedlichen jrrthumb des Bapstumbs/durch dein wort gefüret/vnd zum rechten liecht deiner gnaden gebracht hast/Vnd bitten dich/du wöllest inn solchem liecht vns gnedig erhalten/für allem jrrthumb vnd ketzerey bewaren/vnd ja dafür behüten/das wir nicht wie die Juden dir vndanckbar werden/dein wort verachten oder verfolgen/sonder/das wirs mit gantzem hertzen annemen/vnser leben darnach bessern/vnd alles vertrawen allein auff dein gnad vnnd verdienst deines lieben sons Christi Jesu setzen/Der mit dir vnd dem heyligen Geyst/lebt vnd regieret/von ewigkeyt zu ewigkeyt/Amen.

**Revised TLHy**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we most heartily thank Thee that by Thy word Thou hast led us out of the harmful error of the Papacy, and brought us to the true light of Thy grace: We beseech Thee, graciously keep us in that light, preserve us from all error and heresy, and by all means guard us, that we may not, as the Jews, become unthankful unto Thee, and despise or persecute Thy word, but receive it with all our heart, amend our lives according to it, and put all our trust in Thy grace alone and in the merit of Thy dear Son, Jesus Christ, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, world without end. Amen.

**Changes from TLHy**
- "hast brought us out of the darkness of Papacy into the light of Thy grace" → "hast led us out of the harmful error of the Papacy, and brought us to the true light of Thy grace" (*auß dem schedlichen jrrthumb des Bapstumbs/durch dein wort gefüret/vnd zum rechten liecht deiner gnaden gebracht hast*).
- "mercifully help us to walk in that light" → "graciously keep us in that light" (*du wöllest inn solchem liecht vns gnedig erhalten*).
- "guard us from all error and false doctrine" → "preserve us from all error and heresy" (*für allem jrrthumb vnd ketzerey bewaren*).
- "and grant that we may not, as the Jews, become ungrateful and despise and persecute Thy word" → "and by all means guard us, that we may not, as the Jews, become unthankful unto Thee, and despise or persecute Thy word" (*vnd ja dafür behüten/das wir nicht wie die Juden dir vndanckbar werden/dein wort verachten oder verfolgen*).
- "govern our lives according to it" → "amend our lives according to it" (*vnser leben darnach bessern*).
- "and put all our trust in Thy grace through the merit of Thy dear Son, our Lord Jesus Christ" → "and put all our trust in Thy grace alone and in the merit of Thy dear Son, Jesus Christ" (*vnd alles vertrawen allein auff dein gnad vnnd verdienst deines lieben sons Christi Jesu setzen*).
- The conclusion is Dietrich's own (*Der mit dir vnd dem heyligen Geyst/lebt vnd regieret/von ewigkeyt zu ewigkeyt*). TLHy's "one true God" is not in it and is dropped; nothing is added (§2.4g).

## PART II. SANCTORALE

**Section title (PDF p. 229).** The sanctorale opens with a title page of its own, above a woodcut of Christ on the shore calling fishermen from their boats and nets:

> Sum̃aria Christlicher ler auß den Euangelien der fürnembsten Fest durchs Jar.

Summaries of Christian doctrine from the Gospels of the principal feasts through the year.

### November and December

#### [79] St. Andrew, Apostle (November 30)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 230):** *An Sanct Andreas tag/Wie er zum Herren Christo komen/vnnd zum Apostelampt beruffen sey/Johannis am 1.* [On St. Andrew's Day: how he came to the Lord Christ and was called to the apostolic office. John 1.]

**Verse (PDF p. 230)**

> DEs andern tages stundt abermal Johannes/vnd zween seyner Jünger/Vnd alß er sahe Jesum wandeln/sprach er: Sihe/das ist Gottes Lamb/Vnnd zween seyner Jünger höreten jn reden/vnd folgeten Jesu nach. Der eine aber auß den zweyen/war Andreas Simonis Bruder.

Again the next day after John stood, and two of his disciples; and looking upon Jesus as he walked, he saith, Behold the Lamb of God! And the two disciples heard him speak, and they followed Jesus. One of the two was Andrew, Simon's brother.

**Summary (PDF pp. 230–232)**

> DEr Euangelist zeygt klar an/wie der heylige Andreas durch die predig Johannis des tauffers verursacht sey/das er anfencklich zum Herren Christo kommen ist. Derhalb ist an solcher predig am meysten gelegen/Vnd ist noch die hoffnung gewiß/ [p. 231] wo mans fleyssig den leuten fürhelt/das sie/wie Andreas/auch zum Herren Christo dadurch geleytet werden. Da vnser lieber Herr Christus auff erden ist vmbgangen/hat er kein sonders ansehen gehabt/wie Johannes der Tauffer zu den Juden sagt: Er ist mitten vnter euch getretten/vnd jr kennet jn nit/Alß es sich aber auff einen tag begibt/das der Herr am Jordan gehet/deutet Johannes auff jn/vnd spricht: Diß ist Gottes lamb/Bald Andreas vnd sein gesell solches hören/folgen sie dem Herren Christo nach/Deñ sie verstehn/was Johannes mit disem wort meynet/das er jn Gottes lamb heisset. Der Prophet Esaias hette lang zuuor gesaget/der Messias würde wie ein lemblein/das zur schlachtbanck gefüret wirdt/seynen mundt nicht auffthun. Das aber Johannes jn nicht schlecht ein lamb/sonder Gottes lamb nennet/deutten sie dahyn/das Gott disen Jesum/seynen Son/dazu verordnet habe/das [p. 232] er soll ein opffer sein/vñ für der gantzen welt sünde mit seinem blut bezalen. Derhalb wöllen sie/bey disem Lemlin bleiben/Gottes willen von jm lernen vnd hören/vnd seines opffers sich trösten. Das sollen wir auch thun/vñ allein vns an disen menschen halten/der für vns gestorben ist/Andere heiligen sind für vns nit gestorben/können vns auch nicht helffen. Darumb sollen wir sie weder anrüffen/noch auff sie vertrawē/An dem Sone Gottes aber/der für vnns gestorben ist/können wir nicht fehlen wie die verheyssung lautter ist.

The evangelist shows clearly how holy Andrew was moved by the preaching of John the Baptist to come to the Lord Christ in the first place. Therefore the most depends on such preaching; and there is still sure hope that, where it is diligently set before people, they too, like Andrew, will be led by it to the Lord Christ. When our dear Lord Christ went about on earth, He had no outward distinction, as John the Baptist says to the Jews: "He standeth among you, and ye know Him not." But when it happens one day that the Lord walks by the Jordan, John points to Him and says: "This is the Lamb of God." As soon as Andrew and his companion hear this, they follow the Lord Christ; for they understand what John means by this word, in calling Him the Lamb of God. The prophet Isaiah had said long before that the Messiah would be like a lamb that is led to the slaughter, and would not open His mouth. But that John calls Him not simply a lamb but the Lamb of God, they take to mean that God has ordained this Jesus, His Son, to be a sacrifice, and to pay with His blood for the sin of the whole world. Therefore they will abide with this little Lamb, learn and hear God's will from Him, and comfort themselves with His sacrifice. This we too are to do, and hold to this man alone, who died for us. Other saints did not die for us, and cannot help us either. Therefore we are neither to call upon them nor trust in them. But in the Son of God, who died for us, we cannot go wrong, as the promise is clear.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 1:35–37 and 1:40 in King James wording, with Dietrich's "Simon's brother" (King James "Simon Peter's brother") and without "which heard John speak, and followed him."
- **"No outward distinction."** *kein sonders ansehen*, no special standing or appearance. John's word is John 1:26, the verse of Advent 4 ([4]).
- **Isaiah.** Isaiah 53:7, "he is brought as a lamb to the slaughter … so he openeth not his mouth."
- **"His companion."** *sein gesell*, the other of the two disciples, whom the Gospel does not name.
- **The saints.** Dietrich applies the feast to the Lutheran rejection of the invocation of saints (compare Augsburg Confession, Article XXI).
- **"Go wrong."** *fehlen*, to miss the mark.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 232–233)**

> HERR Gott himelischer Vatter/der du durch den heyligen Johannem am Jordan/deinen son Christum Jesum inn der welt hast offenbar gemacht/Wir bittē dich/du wöllest auch vnsere hertzen durch deinen heyligen geyst rüren/das wir mit dem heyligē Andrea/vns solcher predig von deynem Son hertzlich annemen/jhn für vnsern Seligmacher erkennen/vnd jm nachfolgen/vñ ja kein verfolgung vns von jhm lassen abweysen/biß wir durch jn gar selig werden/AMEN.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who by Saint John at the Jordan didst make Thy Son, Jesus Christ, manifest in the world: We beseech Thee, move our hearts also by Thy Holy Ghost, that with Saint Andrew we may heartily embrace such preaching of Thy Son, acknowledge Him as our Savior, and follow Him, and by no means suffer any persecution to turn us away from Him, until we be wholly saved through Him, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Rendering choices.** *rüren* means touch or move. *vns … annemen* (with genitive) means to embrace or take to heart. *gar selig* means wholly saved.
- **Ending.** Dietrich's closing "through Him" is moved to the end of its clause so that TLHy's "who liveth and reigneth…" can follow it; only the word order changes (§2.4c).

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [80] St. Thomas, Apostle (December 21)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 233):** *An Sanct Thomas tag/Johannis am 20.* [On St. Thomas's Day. John 20.]

**Verse (PDF p. 234)**

> CHristus spricht zu Thoma: Dieweyl du mich gesehen hast Thoma/so glaubest du/Selig sind/die nicht sehen/vnnd doch glauben. Dise zeychen sind geschriben/das jr glaubt/Jesus sey Christ/der Son Gottes/vnnd das jr durch den glauben/das leben habet in seinem namen.

Christ saith unto Thomas: Thomas, because thou hast seen me, thou hast believed: blessed are they that have not seen, and yet have believed. These signs are written, that ye might believe that Jesus is the Christ, the Son of God; and that believing ye might have life through his name.

**Summary (PDF pp. 234–236)**

> DAs Exempel mit Thoma/ist geschriben zum sonderen trost/den schwachglaubigē Christen/Denn weyl wir wissen/das der glaub allein vns selig machet/vnd aber befinden wie wir im glauben so gar schwach sind/vnd bald auch in geringer anfechtung vnnd not/alle hoffnung fallen lassen/daher förchten wir vns/vnnd besorgen/Gott wölle vnser nicht. Aber da nimb des Apostels Thome exempel für dich/vñ sihe erstlich wie so inn eim grossen vnglauben er stecket/Er glaubt von dem Herrn [p. 235] Christo mer nit/deñ von eim andern menschen/Vñ da die weyber/Petrus die zween jünger von Emahus/vnd hernach die zehen Apostel vnd andere jünger sagen/Christus sey von todten aufferstanden/müssen sie alle jhm liegen/er wil allein recht haben/Christus sey nit erstanden. Darnach sihe auch wie Christus mit so eim vnglaubigen schüler vmbgehet/Nicht ein böses wort gibt er jm/sonder eben durch diß mittel hilfft er jm vom vnglauben zum glauben/welches Thomas selb fürstellet/vnd lest sich nit allein/wie die andern/sehen/sonder auch greyffen. Darumb lerne es/Es will vnser Herr Got auch die schwachglaubigen duldē/Eben wie ein reycher Man nit allein starckes gesundes gsind/sonder auch kleyne gebrechliche kind/in seim hause hat/die on hilff vnd wart nicht können erzogen werden. Doch soll es folgen/das wir nicht ymmer schwach bleyben/sonder durchs wort/von tag zu tag im glauben zunemen/Du glaubest aber vhest oder schwach/so ist diß [p. 236] war/das die selig sindt die glauben/das Jhesus der Christ/das ist/vnser Seligmacher sey/vnnd durch solchen glauben (nicht durch verdienst oder eygne werck wie die Papisten lehren) sollen wir das ewige leben haben/Wie der Euangelist Johannes selb dise Histori beschleust.

The example of Thomas is written for the special comfort of Christians who are weak in faith. For we know that faith alone saves us, but we find that we are so very weak in faith, and soon, even in slight temptation and need, let all hope fall; and so we are afraid, and fear that God will not have us. But here take the example of the apostle Thomas before you, and see, first, in how great an unbelief he is stuck. He believes no more of the Lord Christ than of any other man. And when the women, Peter, the two disciples from Emmaus, and afterward the ten apostles and other disciples say that Christ is risen from the dead, they must all be liars to him; he alone will be right, that Christ is not risen. Next, see also how Christ deals with so unbelieving a pupil. Not one harsh word does He give him, but by the very means that Thomas himself proposes He helps him from unbelief to faith, and lets Thomas not only see Him, as the others did, but also touch Him. Learn from this, then, that our Lord God will bear with the weak in faith too, just as a rich man has in his house not only strong, healthy servants but also small, sickly children, who cannot be brought up without help and care. Yet it is to follow that we do not remain weak forever, but grow in faith from day to day through the word. But whether you believe firmly or weakly, this is true: that they are blessed who believe that Jesus is the Christ, that is, our Savior, and that through such faith (not through merit or our own works, as the papists teach) we are to have eternal life, as the evangelist John himself concludes this history.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 20:29 and 20:31 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ saith unto Thomas." Dietrich's "These signs are written" (*Dise zeychen*) takes "signs" from verse 30.
- **The witnesses.** The women, Peter, and the two disciples from Emmaus (Luke 24), and the ten apostles (John 20:19–25). "Must all be liars to him": *müssen sie alle jhm liegen* (*lügen*).
- **"The very means that Thomas himself proposes."** John 20:25, "Except I shall see in his hands the print of the nails … and thrust my hand into his side, I will not believe"; compare 20:27.
- **"Will not have us."** *Gott wölle vnser nicht*, will have nothing to do with us.
- **"Help and care."** *hilff vnd wart* (*Wartung*), tending.
- **"Firmly."** *vhest* is *fest*.

**Prayer (PDF p. 236)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du durch deynen Son vns armen sündern rath vnd hilf hast schaffen wöllen/Sey mir gnedig/vnnd hilffe/das ich des vnglaubens mich erwehren/vnd mit gantzem vertrawen auff dein wort begeben möge/Vnd O du Son Gottes/der du den vnglaubigen Thomas in solcher sünde nit stecken lassen/sonder jm gnedigklich geholffen hast/Gibe dein heyligen Geyst auch in mein hertz/das ich an deiner gnad nymmer mehr zweiffle/sonder durch vesten glauben auff deine barmhertzigkeit ewig selig werde/Amen.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who through Thy Son hast been pleased to provide counsel and help for us poor sinners: Be gracious unto me, and help me, that I may resist unbelief, and with entire trust commit myself to Thy word. And O Thou Son of God, who didst not leave unbelieving Thomas stuck fast in such sin, but didst graciously help him: Give Thy Holy Ghost into my heart also, that I may nevermore doubt Thy grace, but through firm faith in Thy mercy obtain eternal salvation, *Thou who livest and reignest with the Father and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Congregational form (TLHy practice)**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who through Thy Son hast been pleased to provide counsel and help for us poor sinners: Be gracious unto us, and help us, that we may resist unbelief, and with entire trust commit ourselves to Thy word. And O Thou Son of God, who didst not leave unbelieving Thomas stuck fast in such sin, but didst graciously help him: Give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts also, that we may nevermore doubt Thy grace, but through firm faith in Thy mercy obtain eternal salvation, *Thou who livest and reignest with the Father and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Two addresses.** The prayer is in the first person singular and is addressed first to the Father and then to the Son. Because its last petition is to the Son, it takes TLHy's ending for a prayer to Christ (§2.4e).
- **Rendering choices.** *rath vnd hilf* means counsel (remedy) and help. *sich … erwehren* (with genitive) means to fend off or resist. *sich … begeben auff* means to commit oneself to.

#### [81] St. Stephen, Martyr (December 26)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 30):** *An Sanct Steffans tag/in den geschichten/wie er vmb des Herrn Christi willen/von Juden ist gesteyniget worden/Acto. 7.* [On St. Stephen's Day, from the Acts: how he was stoned by the Jews for the sake of the Lord Christ. Acts 7.] In the 1548 book this section stands in the Christmas sequence, between Christmas and the Sunday after Christmas.

**Verse (PDF pp. 30–31)**

> DJe Juden steynigeten Steffanum/Er aber betet vnd sprach: Herr Jesu/nim̃ meinen Geyst [p. 31] auff/er knyet aber nider/vnnd schrey laut/Herr behalt jn dise sünde nicht/vnd alß er das gesagt/entschlieff er.

The Jews stoned Stephen; but he prayed and said, Lord Jesus, receive my spirit. And he kneeled down, and cried with a loud voice, Lord, lay not this sin to their charge. And when he had said this, he fell asleep.

**Summary (PDF pp. 31–32)**

> WJR haben inn ettlichen Predigten nacheinander gehört/vom ampt vnsers Herren Christi/vnnd wie wir sein sollen geniessen/Nemlich/das wir durch jn vergebung der sünden/den heyligen Geyst/vnd ewiges leben sollen haben. Jn diser Histori aber/vom heyligen Steffano/sehen wir ein Exempel/wie es vns inn der welt gehen soll/wenn wir an Christum recht glauben vnd jn bekennen/Nemlich/das wir vns leyden/vnnd allerley vnglück gewarten müssen/Denn die welt/vnd jr Herr der Teuffel/kan solchen glauben an Christum nicht leyden/setzt derhalb den Christen zu/biß er sie gar vmbbringet/Da sollen wir vns des heyligen Steffans Exempel trösten/vnnd dran lernen/das wir/wo es vns der gleychen begegnet/alles gern leyden/vnd yederman [p. 32] von hertzen vergeben sollen/Deñ das ein Christ stirbet ist nichts böses/weyl vnsers Herren Christi wort da stehet/vnd spricht: Wer an mich glaubet/wirdt den todt nicht sehen ewigklich/Vnd ob er schon stirbet/wirdt er doch wider leben. Derhalb ist Steffanus seiner person halb wol zu friden/er befilhet seinen Geyst dem Herren Christo/vnd weyß das er in ewigkeyt bewaret ist/Allein ist es jhm zuthun/vmb die blinden verfolger/die wolt er auch gern from̃ vñ selig sehen/Solchs sollen wir lernen/vnnd in der anfechtung auch so fein gedultig sein/glauben vnd lieben.

In several sermons one after another we have heard of the office of our Lord Christ, and how we are to have the benefit of Him: namely, that through Him we are to have the forgiveness of sins, the Holy Ghost, and eternal life. But in this history of holy Stephen we see an example of how it shall go with us in the world when we rightly believe in Christ and confess Him: namely, that we must bear and look for all manner of misfortune. For the world and its lord the devil cannot bear such faith in Christ, and so he presses hard upon Christians until he kills them outright. Here we are to comfort ourselves with the example of holy Stephen, and learn from it that, wherever the like befalls us, we are to suffer all things gladly and forgive everyone from our hearts. For that a Christian dies is nothing evil, since the word of our Lord Christ stands there and says: "He that believeth in me shall never see death; and though he die, yet shall he live." Therefore Stephen, as concerns his own person, is well content. He commends his spirit to the Lord Christ, and knows that it is kept safe for ever. His only concern is for the blind persecutors; them too he would gladly see godly and saved. This we are to learn, and in temptation to be as patient, and to believe and love as finely, as he.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Acts 7:59–60 in King James wording, introduced by "The Jews stoned Stephen."
- **"We have heard in several sermons."** Dietrich refers to the preceding sections, Advent 1 to Christmas, all of which treat Christ's office.
- **"Bear and look for."** *das wir vns leyden … gewarten müssen*: *sich leiden* is to endure patiently, *gewarten* to expect.
- **Christ's word** combines John 8:51 ("he shall never see death") and John 11:25 ("though he were dead, yet shall he live"); it is given in King James wording.
- **"It is kept safe."** *das er in ewigkeyt bewaret ist*; *er* may be his spirit or Stephen himself.

**Assignment:** St. Stephen's Day, beside TLHy's present collect, which is not Dietrich's ("O eternal and merciful God, our heavenly Father, who didst give such grace to Saint Stephen…"; Appendix A, [82]). TLHy also has a second collect for the day, a Norwegian prayer of 1887 for use with its second and third series of texts, which give Christmas readings (Appendix A).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 32–33)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/der du den heiligen Steffanum seins glaubens halb/leyden vñ steynigen hast lassen/vns damit anzuzeigen das dein reich nit von diser welt sey/sonder in ein anders leben gehöre/Wir bitē dich/wo wir vmb deines worts vñ namens willē auch müssen leidē/sterck vnser hertzē durch deinen heiligē geist/das wir fest bestehn/vñ vns mit deiner gnad/vnd den ewigen gütern/anstat der zeitlichen trösten mögen/Amen.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who didst permit Saint Stephen to suffer and to be stoned for his faith's sake, thereby to show us that Thy kingdom is not of this world, but belongeth to another life: We beseech Thee, if we also must suffer for the sake of Thy word and name, strengthen our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may stand fast, and comfort ourselves with Thy grace and with the eternal goods in place of the temporal; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **God's purpose.** *vns damit anzuzeigen* states what God intended, "thereby to show us." It is not something Stephen showed.

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

---

#### [83] The Holy Innocents (December 28)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 237):** *An der vnschuldigen kindlein tag/Matthei am 2.* [On the Day of the Innocent Children. Matthew 2.]

**Verse (PDF p. 237)**

> HErodes schicket auß/vnnd ließ alle Kinder zu Bethlehem tödten/die da zweyjerig vnd drunter waren.

Herod sent forth, and slew all the children that were in Bethlehem, from two years old and under.

**Summary (PDF pp. 238–239)**

> DAs ist ein sonder grosser jammer/vnd vnerhörete Tyranney gewest/das so ein grosser hauffe junger kindlin sind erwürget worden. Aber es hat der leydige Sathan bald sich müssen lassen sehen/wie er gegen dem kindlin Jesu/vnd seim reych gesinnet sey/das er mit würgen/oder sonst in andere wege/es gedenck wüst zumachen/vnd zu tilgen. Aber da ist der trost fest vnd gewiß/weder Teuffel noch Tyrannen sollen es enden/Ob sie gleich etliche Christen hin reyssen/damit geschicht jnē nichts arges/Denn sie werden durch den todt von disem argen zeytlichen leben/gefürdert zum ewigen. Christus aber/vnd sein Reych wirdt nur ye lenger ye grösser/wie yener lehrer spricht: Es sey der Christen blut in der kirch/wie ein mist auf dem acker/der macht das feld fett vnd fruchtbar. Denn Herodes verursachet durch sein Tiranney/das das kindlin Jesus auch in Egypten/vnter den Heyden bekand wirt. [p. 239] Zum dritten/gibt es die erfarung/dz die Tyrannen/durch solches fürnemē jr zeytliches vnnd ewiges verderben fürdern/Herodes hat müssen mit der haut bezalen/das bey lebendigem leib würm in jm gewachssen/vnd er dermassen gestuncken hat/das kein mensche vmb jn hat können bleyben/vnd ist nach jhm sein gantzes geschlecht zu boden gangen/vnnd schendtlich verdorben vnd vmbkomen. Das heist wie Christus zu Paulo sagt/mit dem fuß in ein stachel schlagen/vnd jm selb alles vnglück zurichten/Das sol den Tyrannen/so die vnschuldigen Christen verfolgen/endlicher lohn sein.

This was an especially great misery and an unheard-of tyranny, that so great a crowd of young children was slaughtered. But the wretched Satan had to show at once how he is minded toward the child Jesus and His kingdom: that he means to lay it waste and destroy it, by slaughter or in other ways. But here the comfort is firm and sure: neither devil nor tyrants shall accomplish it. Even if they carry off some Christians, no harm is done to them by it; for through death they are sped from this evil temporal life to the eternal one. But Christ and His kingdom only grow ever greater, as that teacher says: the blood of Christians is in the Church like dung on the field, which makes the field fat and fruitful. For by his tyranny Herod brings it about that the child Jesus becomes known in Egypt too, among the heathen. Thirdly, experience shows that the tyrants hasten their own temporal and eternal ruin by such undertakings. Herod had to pay with his own skin: worms grew in his body while he was still alive, and he stank so that no one could stay near him; and after him his whole house went to ruin, and perished and was destroyed in disgrace. That is what Christ, speaking to Paul, calls kicking against a goad, and bringing every misfortune upon oneself. This shall be the final reward of the tyrants who persecute innocent Christians.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 2:16, shortened, in King James wording.
- **"Lay it waste."** *es … wüst zumachen*.
- **"That teacher."** Tertullian, whose saying is "the blood of Christians is seed" (*Apology* 50); Dietrich gives it with the image of dung on a field.
- **Egypt.** The flight into Egypt, Matthew 2:13–15.
- **Herod's death.** The worms and the stench are from Josephus's account of the death of Herod the Great (*Antiquities* 17.6.5). *mit der haut bezalen*, "to pay with one's skin," is to pay in one's own person.
- **"Kicking against a goad."** Acts 9:5 and 26:14, "it is hard for thee to kick against the pricks."
- **Points.** The summary has no "first" and "second"; "Thirdly" follows the two points introduced by "But."

**Prayer (PDF pp. 239–240)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du deinen Son Christum Jhesum/durch dein wort vns offenbaret hast/du sihest/wie noch heutiges tages der Teuffel seine Herodes hat/vnd wider das wort reytzet vnd treibet/mit allerley verfolgung/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest den schedlichen Tyrannen wehren/alles vnglück/das sie vber deine Christliche Kirch gedencken/auff jren kopf schütten/vnd vns frid vnd ruhe genedigklich schaffen/auff das dein wort ye lenger ye weytter außbreytet/vñ wir deinen gnedigen schutz vnd hilff von tag zu tag/ye lenger ye mehr erkennen vnnd preysen mögen/AMEN.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who hast revealed unto us Thy Son, Jesus Christ, through Thy word: Thou seest how even unto this day the devil hath his Herods, and inciteth and driveth on against the word with all manner of persecution. We beseech Thee, restrain the harmful tyrants; pour out upon their own heads all the mischief which they devise against Thy Christian Church; and graciously grant us peace and quiet, that Thy word may spread abroad ever farther and farther, and that from day to day we may ever more and more know and praise Thy gracious protection and help; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Rendering choices.** *reytzet vnd treibet* has no object expressed in the German, so the English leaves none. *auff jren kopf schütten* echoes Psalm 7:16. *ye lenger ye weytter/ye mehr* means "ever farther / ever more."
