### January to March

#### [84] The Conversion of St. Paul (January 25)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 240):** *An Sanct Paulus bekerung/1. Timoth. 1.* [On the Conversion of St. Paul. 1 Timothy 1.]

**Verse (PDF p. 241).** The heading stands alone on p. 240; a woodcut of Saul struck down from his horse heads p. 241.

> DAs ist gewißlich war/vnnd ein tewres werdes wort/das Christus Jesus kommen ist inn die welt/die sünder selig zu machen/vnter welchen ich der fürnemest byn/Aber darumb ist mir barmhertzigkeit widerfaren/auff das an mir fürnemlich/Jesus Christus erzeygte alle gedult/zum Exempel denen/die an jhn glauben solten zum ewigen leben.

This is a faithful saying, and worthy of all acceptation, that Christ Jesus came into the world to save sinners; of whom I am chief. Howbeit for this cause I obtained mercy, that in me first Jesus Christ might shew forth all longsuffering, for a pattern to them which should hereafter believe on him to life everlasting.

**Summary (PDF pp. 242–243).** The paragraph break follows the print.

> WJE Paulus/der ein hefftiger verfolger der Christen war/bekeret sey worden/da er auff dem wege gen Damasco war/zeyget die Histori fein an/in den geschichten der Apostel am 9. vnd 25. [sic] Cap. Wozu aber solche Histori diene/vñ was wir drauß sollen lernen/hören wir hie von Paulo selb/Nemlich/das wir erstlich fest sollen glauben/Gott habe nit lust an des sünders todt/wölle auch nit/wie 2. Pet. 3. stehet/das yemandt verlorn werde/sonder das yederman sich zur buß kere/vñ selig werde. Darumb lest vnser lieber Herr Christus Paulum nit also in der verfolgung fort faren/er fordert jn dauon ab/vnd Paulus folget/Vnd noch für vnd für gehet vnser lieber Herr Christus vnns sündern mit seinem wort nach/rüffet vns/das wir von dem jrrweg ablassen/vnd vns bessern sollen/Denn dahin ist alle predig gerichtet/das man im namen Jesu Buß vnd vergebung der sünden soll predigen.

How Paul, who was a fierce persecutor of the Christians, was converted when he was on the road to Damascus, the history in the Acts of the Apostles, chapters 9 and 25, shows well. But what this history serves for, and what we are to learn from it, we hear here from Paul himself: namely, first, that we are to believe firmly that God has no pleasure in the death of the sinner, and also wills, as it stands in 2 Peter 3, that no one be lost, but that everyone turn to repentance and be saved. Therefore our dear Lord Christ does not let Paul go on so in persecution; He calls him away from it, and Paul obeys. And still our dear Lord Christ continually goes after us sinners with His word, and calls us to leave off the wrong way and amend ourselves. For all preaching is directed to this, that repentance and forgiveness of sins are to be preached in the name of Jesus.

> Zum andern sollē wir auch dem exempel Pauli folgen/vnd der himlischē offenbarung oder erscheynung nit vnglaubig sein/Bald Christus im gesicht jm anzeygt/er thue vnrecht/ist es jm leid/vnnd spricht mit zittern/Herr/was wilt du das ich thun soll? Also sollē wir auch thun/vns nicht vmb sonst lassen predigē/sonder erstlich von sünden ablassen/vñ vns bessern/vñ darnach durch den Herrn Christum Gottes gnad vnd barmhertzigkeyt gewiß hoffen/so soll das ewige leben folgen.

Secondly, we too are to follow the example of Paul, and not be disobedient to the heavenly revelation or vision. As soon as Christ shows him in the vision that he is doing wrong, he is sorry for it, and says with trembling: "Lord, what wilt thou have me to do?" So we too are to do: not let ourselves be preached to in vain, but first leave off sins and amend ourselves, and then hope surely for God's grace and mercy through the Lord Christ; so shall eternal life follow.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is 1 Timothy 1:15–16 in King James wording. The German "This is certainly true, and a precious, worthy word" (*gewißlich war/vnnd ein tewres werdes wort*) is the King James "a faithful saying, and worthy of all acceptation."
- **"Chapters 9 and 25."** Acts 25 contains no account of the conversion. Paul tells it himself in Acts 22 and 26, and the summary's second paragraph quotes Acts 26:19 (Luther: *war ich der himlischen erscheinung nicht vngleubig*, King James "I was not disobedient unto the heavenly vision"), so "25" is probably a misprint for 26.
- **"No pleasure in the death of the sinner."** Ezekiel 33:11 (compare 18:23). "That no one be lost" is 2 Peter 3:9, "not willing that any should perish, but that all should come to repentance."
- **"Repentance and forgiveness of sins … in His name."** Luke 24:47, as at Trinity 19 ([65]).
- **"Lord, what wilt thou have me to do?"** Acts 9:6 in King James wording, where Saul speaks "trembling and astonished."

**Prayer (PDF p. 243)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/der du den heyligen Paulū so gnedig beruffen/von sünden abgewendet/vñ auß eim verfolger einē erweletē rüstzeuge gemachet hast/Jch bitt dich/gibe mir dein gnad/das ich dein wort vñ warnung auch mit ernst anneme/vber meine sünde hertzliche rew habe/dauon ablasse/vnd mich allein deiner gnad vñ barmhertzigkeyt durch Christū tröste/vñ durch jn ewig selig werde/Amē.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who didst so graciously call Saint Paul, turn him from sin, and make of a persecutor a chosen vessel: I beseech Thee, give me Thy grace, that I also may earnestly receive Thy word and warning, have hearty contrition for my sins, forsake them, and comfort myself only with Thy grace and mercy through Christ, and obtain eternal salvation through Him, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Congregational form (TLHy practice)**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who didst so graciously call Saint Paul, turn him from sin, and make of a persecutor a chosen vessel: We beseech Thee, give us Thy grace, that we also may earnestly receive Thy word and warning, have hearty contrition for our sins, forsake them, and comfort ourselves only with Thy grace and mercy through Christ, and obtain eternal salvation through Him, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Rendering choices.** The German is in the first person singular. *erweletē rüstzeuge* is "a chosen vessel" (Acts 9:15).
- **Ending.** Dietrich's closing "through Him" is moved to the end of its clause so that TLHy's "who liveth and reigneth…" can follow it; only the word order changes (§2.4c).

#### [85] The Purification of Mary / Presentation of Our Lord (February 2)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 244):** *Am tag der Reynigung/da das kindlin Jesus/nach den sechs wochen/wirdt in den Tempel getragen/Luce am 2.* [On the Day of Purification, when the child Jesus, after the six weeks, is carried into the Temple. Luke 2.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 244–245)**

> DER alte Simeon sprach: Herr nun lessest du deinen Diener im fride faren/wie du gesagt hast/Denn meine augen haben deinen heyland gesehen/welchen du bereyttet hast für allen völckern. Ein liecht zu [p. 245] erleuchten die Heyden/vñ zum preyß deinem volck Jsrael.

Old Simeon said: Lord, now lettest thou thy servant depart in peace, according to thy word: for mine eyes have seen thy Saviour, which thou hast prepared before the face of all people; a light to lighten the Gentiles, and the glory of thy people Israel.

**Summary (PDF pp. 245–246)**

> DJß ist ein kurtze/aber seer treffentliche predigt von dem Herren Christo/was sein ambt sey/vnnd warzu wir sein geniessen sollen. Der alte Simeon heyst jn einen Heyland Gottes/das ist/ein hilf/welche Gott selb vns menschen geordnet hat/das vns wider die sünde/den tod/vnd den Sathan soll geholffen werden. Darumb sollen wir von Got im hymel anders nit gedencken/noch glauben/deñ das er gnedig vnd barmhertzig sey/vnd vns/ob wir gieych [sic, for gleych] arme sünder sind/hertzlich lieb hab/sintemal er vns verlornen/verdambten menschen selb hilff vnnd rettung wider die sünd vnd den todt schaffet. Auff das aber nicht yemandt gedencke/solche hilff sey allein den Juden vermeyndt/sagt Simeon/diser heyland Gottes sey bereytet/das ist/geordnet vñ vest gesetzt allē völckern/ [p. 246] den Heyden gleych so wol als den Juden/Die Heyden soll er erleuchten/das ist/Gottes wort vnd erkendtnuß vnter sie bringen/das sie auch selig werden/vnnd den Juden soll er ein preyß sein/das sie zu ehren kommen/vnd die sünde vñ der tod sie nit schenden möge noch vberweltigen. Derhalb soll ferner dise frucht bey vns folgen/welche wir an dem alten Simeon sehen. Er förchtet sich für dem tod nicht/sonder sagt/er wölle mit frölichem hertzen dahyn faren/weñ Gott jn abfordere. Denn warumb wolten oder solten wir vns für dem tod förchten/sintemal wir Gottes Heylande vnnd hilff haben/die vns in ewigkeyt nicht kan fehlen?

This is a short but very excellent sermon about the Lord Christ: what His office is, and for what we are to have the benefit of Him. Old Simeon calls Him God's Savior, that is, a help which God Himself has appointed for us men, so that we may be helped against sin, death, and Satan. Therefore we are to think and believe nothing else of God in heaven than that He is gracious and merciful, and loves us heartily, although we are poor sinners, since He Himself provides help and deliverance against sin and death for us lost and condemned men. But lest anyone should think that this help is meant for the Jews only, Simeon says that this Savior of God is prepared, that is, appointed and firmly established, for all peoples, for the heathen just as much as for the Jews. The heathen He is to enlighten, that is, to bring God's word and the knowledge of Him among them, so that they too may be saved; and to the Jews He is to be a glory, so that they come to honor, and sin and death may neither put them to shame nor overpower them. Therefore this fruit is further to follow in us, which we see in old Simeon. He is not afraid of death, but says that he will depart with a joyful heart when God calls him away. For why would we, or should we, be afraid of death, since we have God's Savior and help, which can never fail us?

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is the Song of Simeon, Luke 2:29–32, in King James wording, introduced by "Old Simeon said." Where the King James Version has "thy salvation," Luther and Dietrich have "thy Saviour" (*deinen heyland*), and the summary expounds that word, so "Saviour" is kept.
- **The heading.** *nach den sechs wochen*, "after the six weeks": the forty days of the mother's purification under the law (Leviticus 12:2–4; Luke 2:22).
- **"Glory."** *preyß* (*Preis*), praise and glory.
- **"Calls him away."** *abfordere*, summons from this life.

**Prayer (PDF p. 247)**

> HErr Got hymlischer Vater/der du deinen Sun vns zum Heylandt bereyttet hast/das er der Heyden liecht/vnnd der Juden preyß sol sein/Wir bitten dich/erleuchte vnsere hertzen/das wir dein gnad vnd Vätterlichen willen gegen vns in jm erkennen/vnd hilff vnd schutz/wider die sünd/verdamnuß vnd den Sathan an jm haben/vnd durch jn ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, who hast prepared Thy Son to be our Savior, that He should be the light of the Gentiles and the glory of the Jews: We beseech Thee, enlighten our hearts, that we may know in Him Thy grace and fatherly will toward us, and have in Him help and protection against sin, damnation, and Satan, and obtain eternal salvation through Him, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **The quotation.** *der Heyden liecht/vnnd der Juden preyß* is Luke 2:32 in Dietrich's own phrasing ("the glory of the Jews").
- **Ending.** Dietrich's closing "through Him" is moved to the end of its clause so that TLHy's "who liveth and reigneth…" can follow it; only the word order changes (§2.4c).

#### [86] St. Matthias, Apostle (February 24)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 247):** *An Sanct Matthias tag/Matthei am 9.* [On St. Matthias's Day. Matthew 9 [sic].] The text expounded (PDF p. 248, "Kombt her zu mir/alle die jr mühselig vnd beladen seydt…") is Matthew 11:25–30.

**Verse (PDF p. 248).** The heading stands at the foot of p. 247; the woodcut heads p. 248.

> KOmbt her zu mir/alle die jr mühselig vnd beladen seydt/Jch will euch erquicken. Nemet auff euch mein Joch/vnd lernet von mir/Deñ ich bin sanfftmütig/vnd von hertzen demütig/so werdet jr ruhe finden für ewre seele. Denn mein joch ist sanfft/vnd mein last ist leycht.

Come unto me, all ye that labour and are heavy laden, and I will give you rest. Take my yoke upon you, and learn of me; for I am meek and lowly in heart: and ye shall find rest unto your souls. For my yoke is easy, and my burden is light.

**Summary (PDF pp. 249–250)**

> ES lehret vnser lieber Herr Christus im heutigen Euangelio erstlich/wie das Euangelion von den weltweysen verachtet vnd verfolget werde/Denn weil sie es nicht wöllen annemen/vñ mehr auff jr vernunfft/verdienst vñ macht/denn auff Gottes gnad bawen/lest sie Gott in solcher blindheyt jm̃er hingehen/Dagegen aber werden die armen ellenden gewissen/so jre sünden erkennen/vnd Gottes zorn fülen vnd fürchten/solcher predigt fro/vnd nemen sie mit hertzen an/Sie müssen aber auch redlich drumb herhalten/vnnd sich vom Teuffel vnnd der welt plagen lassen. Darumb tröstet sie vnser lieber Herr Christus/vnnd verheysset/ob sie wol sich müssen leyden/so wölle er doch sie erquicken/vnnd jnen tragen helffen/Sie sollen nur keck vnnd getrost sein/vnd an seim Exempel lernen/erstlich sanfftmütig/das ist/gedultig sein/vnnd durche [sic, for durchs] Creutz sich nit erzüvnen [sic, for erzürnen] noch vngedultig lassen machen/vnnd [p. 250] darnach auch demütig sein/das ist/Sie sollen es erkeñen/das wir arme sünder sind/vnd durch sünd offt selb vrsach geben/das Got mit seinem rütlein muß kommen/Sollen derhalben sich schmiegen/nicht embor begeren/sonder sich lassen drucken/biß Got genedige hilff schaffet/vnnd wider embor hilfft. Also spricht der Herr/wird euch mein joch sanfft/vnnd mein last leycht werden/Denn er will mit seim Geyst/vnnd durch sein wort bey vns sein/vnnd durch die gewisse hoffnung des ewigen lebens alles vnglück leicht vnd treglich machen.

In today's Gospel our dear Lord Christ teaches, first, how the gospel is despised and persecuted by the worldly wise. For because they will not receive it, and build more on their own reason, merit and power than on God's grace, God lets them go on and on in such blindness. But on the other hand the poor, wretched consciences that acknowledge their sins, and feel and fear God's wrath, are glad of such preaching and receive it with their hearts. But they must also pay for it in full measure, and let themselves be plagued by the devil and the world. Therefore our dear Lord Christ comforts them, and promises that, although they must suffer, yet He will give them rest and help them to bear it. They are only to be bold and of good courage, and to learn from His example, first, to be meek, that is, patient, and not to let the cross make them angry or impatient; and then also to be lowly, that is, they are to acknowledge that we are poor sinners, and often by sin ourselves give occasion for God to come with His little rod. They are therefore to bow down, not to want to rise up, but to let themselves be pressed down, until God provides gracious help and helps them up again. So, says the Lord, My yoke shall become easy to you, and My burden light. For He will be with us by His Spirit and through His word, and by the sure hope of eternal life make all misfortune light and bearable.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 11:28–30 in King James wording. The Gospel for St. Matthias is Matthew 11:25–30; the heading's "Matthei am 9." is a misprint (Appendix D). *erquicken* ("refresh") is the King James "give you rest," and the summary's "give them rest" renders the same word.
- **"The worldly wise."** *weltweysen*: Matthew 11:25, "thou hast hid these things from the wise and prudent, and hast revealed them unto babes."
- **"Pay for it in full measure."** *redlich drumb herhalten*.
- **"Meek … lowly."** Dietrich explains the verse's two words: *sanfftmütig*, "that is, patient"; *demütig*, that is, acknowledging oneself a sinner.
- **"Little rod."** *rütlein*, a diminutive of *Rute*: God's fatherly chastening (compare Hebrews 12:6).
- **"Bow down … pressed down."** *sich schmiegen*, to bend, submit; *sich lassen drucken*, to let oneself be pressed down; *embor* is *empor*, "up."
- **The prayer.** The summary is proper to St. Matthias; the prayer printed after it is the Annunciation prayer (Appendix D, item 3).
- **Misprints.** *durche* for *durchs*; in *erzüvnen* a v stands for r.

**Prayer (PDF pp. 250–251)**

> HErr Got himlischer vater Wir dancken dir für deine vnaußsprechliche gnade/dz du vns arme sünder bedacht/vñ deinen Sun in vnser fleisch geschickt/vnd vmb vnsert willen hast lassen mensch werden. Vñ bitten dich du w[ö]llest [type damaged] durch deinen heyligen geist vnsere hertzen also erleuchtē/das wir vns seiner menschwerdung/leydens vnd sterbens trösten/jhn für vnsern herrn vnd ewigen König erkennen/vnd annemen/vnnd durch jn mit dir vnnd deinem heyligen Geyst/ewig leben/vnd selig werden/Amen/Amen.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee for Thine ineffable grace, that Thou hast been mindful of us poor sinners, and hast sent Thy Son into our flesh, and for our sakes hast suffered Him to become man; and we beseech Thee so to enlighten our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may comfort ourselves with His incarnation, suffering, and death, acknowledge and receive Him as our Lord and everlasting King, and through Him live eternally with Thee and Thy Holy Ghost, and be saved. Amen, Amen.

**Notes**
- **A duplicated prayer.** In this edition the Matthias prayer is the Annunciation prayer [87], differing only in spelling and in the doubled "Amen/Amen." It does not reflect the Matthias exposition, which treats the easy yoke and light burden of Matthew 11:28–30. The section's heading also misprints the chapter.
- **Likely cause.** Together these point to a printer's duplication. Comparing another edition of the *Summaria* would settle whether Dietrich wrote a distinct Matthias prayer.
- **Ending.** No TLHy ending is added (§2.4g).

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [87] The Annunciation (March 25)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 251):** *Am tag der verkündigung Marie/Luce am 1.* [On the Day of the Annunciation of Mary. Luke 1.]

**Verse (PDF p. 252)**

> DEr Engel sprach: Fürchte dich nicht Maria. Sihe/du wirst im leyb schwanger werden/vnd einen Sun geberen/Des namen soltu Jesus heyssen/der wird groß/vnnd ein Sun des höhesten genennet werden. Vnd Gott der Herr/wirdt jm den stul seines Vatters Dauid geben/vnnd er wirdt ein König sein/vber das hause Jacob ewiglich/vnd seines Königreychs wirdt kein ende sein.

The angel said, Fear not, Mary: behold, thou shalt conceive in thy womb, and bring forth a son, and shalt call his name Jesus. He shall be great, and shall be called the Son of the Highest: and the Lord God shall give unto him the throne of his father David: and he shall be King over the house of Jacob for ever; and of his kingdom there shall be no end.

**Summary (PDF pp. 252–254)**

> DJe fürnemeste lehr des heutigen Festes/ist dise/das wir mit höchstem fleyß bedencken sollen/was grosse vnmeßliche gnad Gott vns bewisen hat/das er seinen eingebornen Sun in vnser fleysch kommen/vnnd einen menschen hat lassen werden wie wir/das nun wir fürt an rhümen können/Ob gleych der Sathan vnser fleysch/durch die sünde grewlich geschendet vnd geschwechet [p. 253] hat/das doch schandt/schwachheyt/vnd alles mit ein ander/durch den sun Gottes sey hinweg genommen/sintemal Gott selb in vnserm fleysch lebet. Aber bey dem rhum soll es nit allein bleyben/wie wol wir Gott teglich für solche gnad dancken sollen/Den nutz sollen wir auch bedencken/Denn solches ist ein gewises zeugnuß/das Got vns menschen vmb der sünden willen nicht feind sey/noch vns wölle verdammen lassen. Denn wie der Engel hie sagt: Wirt Christus darumb mensche/das er sitze auff dem stul seines Vaters Dauid/das ist/das er ein König sey/vñ bey vns menschē sein reych anrichte/Das nicht/wie andere welt reych ein zeytlang bleybe/sonder das ewig sey/So nun wir menschen inn solches ewiges Reych des Sunes Gottes kommen sollen/so folget das zuuor durch disen König/vnsere sünde müssen versönet/vnnd der heylige Geyst vns geschencket/vnd wir andere vnd gar Newgeborne menschen werden. Denn wo die sünde auff vns solte bleyben [p. 254]/würden wir nicht können ewig leben/Dise gnad vñ wolthat/so durch den Sun Gottes vns widerfaren ist/das er ein ewiges Reych anrichtet/vnd durch sein wort vnnd die selige Tauff/vns dazu beruffen hat/sollen wir wol lernen/vnd Gott mit hertzen dafür dancken/vnnd vns für sünden hüten/auff das wir nicht wider auß disem ewigen Reych/in den todt fallen/vnd ist kein zweyffel/der heylige Geyst wirdt vns gnedig dazu verhelffen/wenn nur wir mit ernst darumb bitten.

The chief teaching of today's feast is this: that we are to consider with the utmost diligence what great, immeasurable grace God has shown us, in that He let His only-begotten Son come into our flesh and become a man like us. So now we can boast henceforth that, although Satan has dreadfully disgraced and weakened our flesh through sin, yet shame, weakness and all of it together has been taken away through the Son of God, since God Himself lives in our flesh. But it is not to stop at the boasting, although we are to thank God daily for such grace; we are also to consider the benefit. For this is a sure testimony that God is not the enemy of us men on account of sin, nor will let us be damned. For, as the angel says here, Christ becomes man for this reason, that He may sit on the throne of His father David, that is, that He may be King and set up His kingdom among us men, a kingdom that does not last for a time like the other kingdoms of the world, but is eternal. Now if we men are to come into this eternal kingdom of the Son of God, it follows that first, through this King, our sins must be atoned for, the Holy Ghost must be given to us, and we must become other men, wholly new-born. For if sin were to remain upon us, we could not live eternally. This grace and benefit that has come to us through the Son of God, that He sets up an eternal kingdom and has called us to it through His word and blessed baptism, we are to learn well, and thank God for it with our hearts, and guard ourselves against sins, so that we do not fall out of this eternal kingdom back into death. And there is no doubt that the Holy Ghost will graciously help us to this, if only we pray for it in earnest.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 1:30–33 in King James wording, introduced by "The angel said," without "for thou hast found favour with God," which Dietrich omits. With Luther, Dietrich has "he shall be a King over the house of Jacob" where the King James Version has "he shall reign," and the summary builds on the word "King," so it is kept.
- **"Boast."** *rhümen*, glory in.
- **"Sits on the throne."** Dietrich's *sitze auff dem stul* paraphrases the verse's "the Lord God shall give unto him the throne."
- **"Wholly new-born."** *gar Newgeborne*; compare John 3:3–5 (Trinity, [46]).
- **The prayer** that follows is the same text as at St. Matthias ([86]).

**Assignment:** The Annunciation, beside TLHy's present collect, which is not Dietrich's ("O almighty God, who of Thy great mercy didst cause Thy Son to be conceived by the Holy Ghost…"; Appendix A, [88]).

**Prayer (PDF pp. 254–255)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/Wir dancken dir für deine vnaußsprechliche gnade/das du vnns arme sünder bedacht/vnnd deinen Sun in vnser fleysch geschicket/vnnd vmb vnsert willen hast lassen mensch werden/Vnnd bitten dich/du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst/vnsere hertzen also erleuchtē/das wir vns seiner menschwerdung/leydens vnnd sterbens trösten/jn für vnserm Herrn vñ ewigen König erkennen/vnd annemen/vnnd durch jn mit dir vnd deinem heyligen Geyst/ewig lebē vnd selig werden/Amen.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee for Thine ineffable grace, that Thou hast been mindful of us poor sinners, and hast sent Thy Son into our flesh, and for our sakes hast suffered Him to become man; and we beseech Thee so to enlighten our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may comfort ourselves with His incarnation, suffering, and death, acknowledge and receive Him as our Lord and everlasting King, and through Him live eternally with Thee and Thy Holy Ghost, and be saved. Amen.

**Notes**
- **Wording.** *bedacht* and *vnaußsprechliche gnade* are rendered as TLHy renders them at Easter 2 and Easter 1. "für vnserm Herrn" (dative for accusative) is as printed.
- **Ending.** No TLHy ending is added (§2.4g).

### May to July

#### [89] Sts. Philip and James, Apostles (May 1)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 255):** *Am tag Philippi vnnd Jacobi/Johan. 14.* [On the Day of Philip and James. John 14.]

**Verse (PDF p. 256)**

> CHristus spricht: Jch bin der weg/die warheyt/vnd das leben. Niemand kombt zum Vatter denn durch mich. Wenn jr mich kennetet/so kennetet jr auch meinen Vatter/vnd von nun an kennet jr jn/vnnd habt jn gesehen/Deñ wer mich sihet/der sihet den Vatter.

Christ saith: I am the way, the truth, and the life: no man cometh unto the Father, but by me. If ye had known me, ye should have known my Father also: and from henceforth ye know him, and have seen him. For he that seeth me seeth the Father.

**Summary (PDF pp. 256–258).** The paragraph breaks follow the print.

> DJß ist ein hohe predigt/vñ lehret wie wir dazu mögen kommen/das wir Got eygentlich erkennen/Solchs ist die höchste kunst/Denn wer Gott recht erkennet/der hat das ewige leben/Johan. 7. [sic, for 17.] Vnd ist gewiß das solche kunst niemandt kan/denn nur allein die rechten Christen. Türcken vnd Juden wissen/das Gott himel vnd erden erschaffen hat/dabey lassen sie sich duncken/haben sie Gott gewiß gefasset/vnd kennen jn eygentlich/so doch bede Türcken vnd Juden/den Herrn Christum nicht allein nit [p. 257] kennen/sonder auch lestern/vnd laugnen. Nun sagt aber Christus hie: Jch bin der weg/auff dem jr müst gehen/wenn jr zu Gott wölt kommen. Die welt hat wol jr sondere weg/dadurch sie getrawet zu Gott zu kommē/aber es sind jrre weg/vnnd füren inn den ewigen todt/Wer aber den Herren Christum zum weg hat/der hat den waren weg zum rechten erkentniß Gottes/vnd dem ewigen leben.

This is a lofty sermon, and teaches how we may come to know God truly. That is the highest art; for whoever knows God rightly has eternal life, John 17. And it is certain that no one has this art but true Christians alone. Turks and Jews know that God created heaven and earth; with that they imagine that they have surely grasped God and know Him truly, whereas both Turks and Jews not only do not know the Lord Christ, but also blaspheme and deny Him. But Christ says here: I am the way on which you must go, if you would come to God. The world has indeed its own ways by which it trusts to come to God; but they are wrong ways, and lead into eternal death. But whoever has the Lord Christ as his way has the true way to the right knowledge of God and to eternal life.

> Wie gehet aber solches zu? Also. Sihe am ersten/wer Christus sey/So findest/das er Gottes Sun sey/von Gott dem Vater in ewigkeyt gezeugget/wie im andern Psalm stehet. Darnach sihe weytter was er thue? So findest/das er erstlich predigt/vnnd dich Gottes willen lehret/wie Gott durch jn/allen menschen von sünd vñ todt wölle helffen. Zum andern findest/das er sich hin gibt/an das creutz in den todt/vnd opffert sein leyb vnd leben für der gantzen welt sünde. Stehet darnach am dritten tag wider auf von dem todt/fehret gen himel/vnnd [p. 258] gibt den heyligen Geyst allen/so das Euangelion hören vnd annemen.

But how does this come about? Thus. See first who Christ is: you find that He is God's Son, begotten of God the Father from eternity, as it stands in the second Psalm. Then see further what He does: you find, first, that He preaches and teaches you God's will, how God will help all men from sin and death through Him. Secondly, you find that He gives Himself up to the cross, into death, and offers His body and life for the sin of the whole world. Then on the third day He rises again from death, ascends into heaven, and gives the Holy Ghost to all who hear and receive the gospel.

> Weil nun solches der Sun Gottes thut/vnd thuts auß des Vatters befelh vñ willen/Sage mir/was wilt du von Gott halten? oder was solt du vom [sic, for von] jm glauben? Jsts nicht war/du kanst jn anders nicht/denn für gnedig vnnd barmhertzig erkennen/der es mit vns menschen auff das freundlichest meynet/sintemal er nit allein vns erschaffen hat/sonder auch/nach dem wir durch die sünde in den todt gefallen/wider sünd vnd todt/durch seinen eingebornen Sun vns hilffet vnnd rettet? Das heyst Gott recht kennen/vnnd solche erkentniß bringt das ewige leben mit sich/Man kombt aber zu solchem erkentniß allein durch Christum. Wer den kennet/der kennet den Vater/wer jn sihet/der sihet den Vatter.

Now since the Son of God does this, and does it by the Father's command and will, tell me: what will you think of God? Or what are you to believe of Him? Is it not true that you can know Him only as gracious and merciful, as one who means it toward us men in the friendliest way, since He has not only created us, but also, after we had fallen into death through sin, helps and rescues us against sin and death through His only-begotten Son? That is to know God rightly, and such knowledge brings eternal life with it. But one comes to such knowledge through Christ alone. Whoever knows Him knows the Father; whoever sees Him sees the Father.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is John 14:6–7 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ saith," with the end of 14:9. There Luther and Dietrich have the present, "he that seeth me seeth the Father," where the King James Version has "he that hath seen me hath seen the Father"; the present is kept, because the summary ends with it.
- **"John 17."** "This is life eternal, that they might know thee the only true God, and Jesus Christ, whom thou hast sent" (John 17:3). The print's "Johan. 7." has lost the 1.
- **"Turks and Jews."** As at Trinity ([45]).
- **"The second Psalm."** Psalm 2:7: "Thou art my Son; this day have I begotten thee."
- **"Means it toward us … in the friendliest way."** *der es mit vns menschen auff das freundlichest meynet*.
- **Misprint.** *vom jm* for *von jm*.

**Prayer (PDF p. 259)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/Wir dancken dir/das du deinen gnedigen willen vnd liebhabendes hertze/vns in deinē Sun Christo Jesu so klerlich angezeyget hast/Vnd bitten dich/du wöllest vns in solchem erkentnuß von tag zu tag lassen wachsen/auff das wir in allerley anfechtung vns damit trösten/vnnd durch solche hoffnung alles vberwinden/vnnd endlich selig werden. AMEN.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee that Thou hast so clearly shown us Thy gracious will and loving heart in Thy Son, Jesus Christ; and we beseech Thee, cause us to grow from day to day in this knowledge, that in all manner of temptation we may comfort ourselves therewith, and through such hope overcome all things, and at last be saved; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [90] The Nativity of St. John the Baptist (June 24)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 260):** *An Sanct Johannes tag/was Johannes für ein ambt haben werde/Luce 1.* [On St. John's Day: what office John would have. Luke 1.]

**Verse (PDF p. 261).** The heading and a woodcut fill p. 260.

> VNd du kindlin wirdst ein Prophet des höchsten heyssen/Du wirdst für dem Herrn hergehn/das du seinen weg bereytest/vnd erkentniß des heyles gebest seinem volcke/welches ist in vergebung jrer sünden/durch die hertzliche barmhertzigkeyt vnsers Gottes/Durch welchen vns gesuchet hat/der auffgang auß der höhe.

And thou, child, shalt be called the prophet of the Highest: thou shalt go before the face of the Lord to prepare his ways; to give knowledge of salvation unto his people by the remission of their sins, through the tender mercy of our God; whereby the dayspring from on high hath visited us.

**Summary (PDF pp. 261–262)**

> DJse wort lehren/wie der heylige Johannes ein sondere predigt füren sol/der gleichen man vnter dem gesetz vor nie gehöret hat. Vnnd nennet der heylige Zacharias/solche predigt ein erkentniß des heyles/das ist/ein predigt dadurch wir selig werden. Solchs soll also zugehn/das der Barmhertzige Gott/seinen Sun von himel herundter schicket/vnd durch jn vergebung der sünden/vnd ewiges leben vns anbeutet/das [p. 262] alle die den Sun Gottes annemen/vnd sich seines todes trösten/das er damit für vnsere sünde bezalet/vnnd vns mit Gott versönet habe/sollen selig vnd kinder Gottes sein. Dise predigt bringet der heylig Johannes in die welt/ist derhalb/wie der Engel sagt/Seiner geburt von hertzen sich zu frewen/das Got einen solchen gnadenprediger vns gibt/der nicht/wie Mose allein treybt/vnd dringet was wir thun vñ lassen sollen/sonder weil wir mit Mose nicht hernach können/lehret Johannes/wie wir dennoch sollen selig werden. Nemlich/durch den glauben an Christum/welchen er heist den auffgang auß der höhe.

These words teach how holy John is to carry on a special preaching, the like of which had never been heard before under the law. And holy Zacharias calls this preaching a knowledge of salvation, that is, a preaching by which we are saved. This is to come about thus: the merciful God sends His Son down from heaven, and through Him offers us the forgiveness of sins and eternal life, so that all who receive the Son of God and comfort themselves with His death, that by it He has paid for our sins and reconciled us with God, shall be saved and be children of God. This preaching holy John brings into the world. Therefore, as the angel says, we are to rejoice at his birth from our hearts, that God gives us such a preacher of grace, who does not, like Moses, only urge and insist on what we are to do and leave undone; but, since we cannot keep pace with Moses, John teaches how we are nevertheless to be saved: namely, through faith in Christ, whom he calls the dayspring from on high.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 1:76–78, from the song of Zacharias, in King James wording. Dietrich lacks the "for" before "thou shalt go."
- **"Knowledge of salvation."** *erkentniß des heyles*; Dietrich explains it as "a preaching by which we are saved."
- **"As the angel says."** Luke 1:14: "many shall rejoice at his birth."
- **"Keep pace with Moses."** *mit Mose nicht hernach können*: we cannot follow where the law of Moses leads, that is, cannot fulfil it.
- **"He calls."** *er heist*: Zacharias, in the verse, calls Christ "the dayspring from on high."

**Prayer (PDF pp. 262–263)**

> HErr Gott himlischer Vatter/Wir dancken dir von hertzen/für deine grosse gnad/das du es bey der gesetzpredigt nicht hast lassen bleyben/sonder den heyligen Johannē geschicket [catchword on p. 262 "geschicket"; p. 263 begins "schicket"]/das er auff Christum mit seinem finger weysen/vnnd vergebung der sünde/heyligkeyt vnd gerechtigkeit durch jn vns verheyssen solt/wir bitten dich aber auch/du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst vnsere hertzen erleuchten/das wir solche gnadenpredig des heyligen Johannis mit rechtem glauben annemen/vnnd dir one forcht in heyligkeit vnd gerechtigkeit dienen vnser lebenlang/Amen.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee from our hearts for Thy great grace, that Thou didst not let it rest with the preaching of the law, but didst send Saint John, that he should point with his finger to Christ, and promise us through Him forgiveness of sins, holiness, and righteousness; and we beseech Thee also, enlighten our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may receive with true faith this preaching of grace by Saint John, and serve Thee without fear, in holiness and righteousness, all the days of our life; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **The closing clause.** It is Luke 1:74–75 (the Benedictus), given in King James wording.

#### [91] Sts. Peter and Paul, Apostles (June 29)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 264):** *Am tag Petri vnd Pauli/Matthei am 16.* [On the Day of Peter and Paul. Matthew 16.]

**Verse (PDF p. 264)**

> PEtrus sprach zum Herrn Jesu/Du bist Christus des lebendigen Gottes Sun/Jesus antwortet vnd sprach zu jm: Selig bist du Simon Jonas Sun/Denn fleisch vnd blut hat dir das mit [sic, for nit] offenbaret/sonder mein Vatter im hymel/Vnnd ich sage dir/du bist Petrus/vnd auff disen Felß will ich mein Kirch bawen/vnnd die pforten der Hellen sollen sie nicht vberweltigen.

Peter said unto the Lord Jesus, Thou art the Christ, the Son of the living God. And Jesus answered and said unto him, Blessed art thou, Simon Barjona: for flesh and blood hath not revealed it unto thee, but my Father which is in heaven. And I say unto thee, That thou art Peter, and upon this rock I will build my church; and the gates of hell shall not prevail against it.

**Summary (PDF pp. 264–266).** The paragraph breaks follow the print.

> DAs ist ein feyne lehr/von der Christlichen Kirchen/was sie sey/wo bey man sie soll kennen/vnnd wie es jr auf erden werde gehn. Die Christlich Kirch heyst das heufflein/das mit Petro bekennet/diser Jesus/der Junckfrawen Marien Sun/sey [p. 265] der Christ/vnd des lebendigen Gottes Sun/das ist/Got hab jn gesalbet/vnnd dazu gesendet/das er für aller welt sünde bezalen/den todt würgen/vnnd/alle so an jhn glauben/soll selig machen. Wer diß also glaubt von dem Herrn Christo/vnd helt es für war/vnd vnzweyffenlich/vnd tröstet sich derhalb der gnade Gottes/der ist ein glid der rechten Christlichen kirchen/vnd ist mit Petro selig/vnnd hat den heyligen Geyst/Denn fleysch vñ blut kan solches für sich selb nicht glauben/der Vater im himel muß durch seinen heyligen geyst solches liecht im hertzen anzünden.

This is a fine teaching about the Christian Church: what it is, by what it is to be known, and how it will go with it on earth. The Christian Church is the name of the little flock that confesses with Peter that this Jesus, the son of the Virgin Mary, is the Christ and the Son of the living God; that is, that God has anointed Him and sent Him to this end, that He should pay for the sin of all the world, slay death, and save all who believe in Him. Whoever believes this of the Lord Christ, and holds it for true and beyond doubt, and therefore comforts himself with God's grace, is a member of the true Christian Church, and is blessed with Peter, and has the Holy Ghost. For flesh and blood cannot believe this of itself; the Father in heaven must kindle this light in the heart by His Holy Ghost.

> Weil aber der Herr sagt/er wölle sein kirch auff solches bekendtnuß bawen/folget das die Kirch kein gewissers gemerck noch zeychen hab/denn dises bekentnuß. Wo das gehet/da ist die Christliche kirch/ob sie gleich nicht scheynet das sies sey/Widerumb/wo diß bekentnuß nit recht gehet/da ist kein Christliche kirch/ob sie gleich den scheyn vnd Namen hat. Es schliessen [p. 266] sich aber inn diß bekendtnuß mit ein/die heylige Tauff/vnd das Nachtmal des Herrn/Denn der Herr Christus wirdt in beden bekennet vnd gepreyset/das er für vnsere sünde bezalet/vnd vns mit Gott versönet habe.

But since the Lord says that He will build His Church on this confession, it follows that the Church has no surer mark or sign than this confession. Where it is made, there is the Christian Church, even though it does not appear to be so. On the other hand, where this confession is not rightly made, there is no Christian Church, even though it has the appearance and the name. But holy baptism and the Lord's Supper are included in this confession; for in both the Lord Christ is confessed and praised as having paid for our sins and reconciled us with God.

> Zum dritten/sagt der Herr/die kirch werde vmb diß bekendtnuß willen/der hellen pforten/das ist/den Sathan mit all seinem Reych vnnd vermögen/zu feynde haben/Weyl aber der Sathan ein Fürst vnd Gott der welt ist/folget/das er die welt wider diß bekentnuß erregen/vnnd es zu dempffen werde fürnemen/Solchs kan on grosse fahr vnd not der armen Christen nicht abgehen/Vnd dennoch tröstet der Herr/es soll seine Kirch bleyben/Der Teuffel aber mit all seiner macht/müsse ablassen vñ zu schanden werden. An solchen trost sollen wir vns vhest halten/vnd Got vmb schutz auch bitten.

Thirdly, the Lord says that the Church, on account of this confession, will have the gates of hell, that is, Satan with all his kingdom and power, for its enemy. And since Satan is a prince and god of the world, it follows that he will stir up the world against this confession and undertake to stifle it. This cannot happen without great danger and distress for poor Christians. And yet the Lord gives the comfort that His Church shall remain, while the devil with all his might must give up and be put to shame. To this comfort we are to hold fast, and also pray God for protection.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 16:16–18 in King James wording, introduced by "Peter said unto the Lord Jesus." Luther's "Simon, Jonas' son" (*Simon Jonas Sun*) is the King James "Simon Barjona." The print's *mit* is a misprint for *nit*.
- **"Is blessed with Peter."** *ist mit Petro selig*, taking up the verse's "Blessed art thou."
- **The mark of the Church.** Dietrich makes the confession of Christ its surest mark, with baptism and the Supper included in it; compare the Augsburg Confession, Article VII.
- **"Where it is made."** *Wo das gehet*, where it is current, where it has its course.
- **"Prince and god of the world."** John 12:31, "the prince of this world"; 2 Corinthians 4:4, "the god of this world."

**Prayer (PDF p. 267)**

> ALmechtiger/ewiger Got/wir dancken dir/das du vns zum erkentnuß deines liebē Suns hast lassen kommen/vnd bitten dich/weyl doch der Sathan auff allen seyten wider vns fichtet/vnnd von solchem erkendtnuß vns begeret zu dringen/du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst vns regieren vnd füren/das wir biß an vnser ende in solchem erkendtnuß vhest bleyben/vnd selig werden/durch Christum Jesum deinen Sun/vnnd vnsern Herrn/Amen.

**New translation**

> Almighty and everlasting God, we thank Thee that Thou hast brought us to the knowledge of Thy dear Son; and we beseech Thee, since Satan fighteth against us on every side and seeketh to drive us from this knowledge, govern and direct us by Thy Holy Ghost, that we may abide steadfast in this knowledge unto our end, and be saved; through Jesus Christ, Thy Son and our Lord, *who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Rendering choices.** *vhest* = *fest*, "steadfast." *regieren vnd füren* is TLHy's "govern and direct" (Advent 1).
- **Ending.** Dietrich's own close, "through Jesus Christ, Thy Son and our Lord," is continued with TLHy's "who liveth and reigneth…" (§2.4d).

#### [92] The Visitation (July 2)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 272):** *Am tag der heymsuchung Marie/Luc. am 1.* [On the Day of the Visitation of Mary. Luke 1.]

**Verse (PDF p. 273).** The heading and a woodcut of Mary and Elizabeth fill p. 272. The Magnificat is printed in three paragraphs.

> MAria sprach: Mein seel erhebt den Herrn/vnd mein Geyst frewet sich in Gott meinem Heylandt. Denn er hat seine ellende magd angesehen/Sihe von nun an/werdē mich selig preysen alle kinds kindt.
>
> Denn er hat grosse ding an mir gethan/der mechtig ist/vnnd des name heylig ist. Vnnd seine barmhertzigkeyt wehret ymmer für vnd für/bey denen die jn förchten. Er übet gewalt mit seinem arm/vnd zustrewet die hoffertig sind/in jres hertzen sinn.
>
> Er stosset die gewaltigen von dem stul/vnd erhebet die ellenden. Die hungerigen füllet er mit gütern/vnd die reychen lest er ler. Er denckt der Barmhertzigkeyt/vnnd hilfft seinem diener Jsrahel auff. Wie er geredt hat vnsern Vättern/Abraham vnnd seinem samen ewigklich.

Mary said: My soul doth magnify the Lord, and my spirit hath rejoiced in God my Saviour. For he hath regarded the low estate of his handmaiden: for, behold, from henceforth all generations shall call me blessed.

For he that is mighty hath done to me great things; and holy is his name. And his mercy is on them that fear him from generation to generation. He hath shewed strength with his arm; he hath scattered the proud in the imagination of their hearts.

He hath put down the mighty from their seats, and exalted them of low degree. He hath filled the hungry with good things; and the rich he hath sent empty away. He remembereth his mercy, and hath holpen his servant Israel; as he spake to our fathers, to Abraham, and to his seed for ever.

**Summary (PDF pp. 274–275)**

> ZV diser schönen Predigt/von Gott vnnd seiner gnad/vnnd wie wir vns dazu schicken sollen/wird die junckfraw Maria durch jr Mume Elisabeth verursachet/weil sies so hoch lobet vnnd preyset/vnd nennets/die gebenedeyte/oder berümbtiste vnter den Weybern/Ja/spricht die Jungfraw Maria/es ist ye war/Gott hat mir ein besonder grosse gnad gethon/der ich mich von hertzen frewen/vnnd Gott drumb loben vnd dancken muß/Denn ich ye ein armes ellendes vnansehliches Meydlin bin/Aber liebe Elisabeth/ob wol mir allein dise grosse genade widerfaren ist/So will doch Gott nit mir allein/sonder allen menschen barmhertzig vnnd gnedig sein/allein man förchte jn/vñ fleyssige sich seines worts vnd willens. Wer aber Gott nicht will förchten/vnd sich/wie der menschen vnart ist/seiner weyßheit/seines gewalts/oder reychthums [p. 275] trösten vnnd vberheben will/da will Got one alle gnad straffen/das sie zu narren werden/verderben/vnd darben müssen/Darumb so lerne yederman Gott förchten/das ist/Gottes wort hören/vnd dem selben folgen/so soll es nicht not haben/ob wir gleych arm/ellend/veracht/vnnd hartselig sind/Gott weyß vns wol empor zuheben/vnd reych zumachen/Vnnd soll endlich auch dise gnad folgen/das Got solche Gottfürchtige hertzen/zu der verheyssung Abrahe kommen lassen/vnd durch seinen Son Christum Jesum ewig will selig machen. Also lehret die Junckfraw Maria/das wir Got förchten/vnd für hoffart alß für dem grewel/welchen Gott nit leyden kan/auff das fleyssigest vns hüten sollen/so will Gott mit zeytlichem vnnd ewigem segen bey vns sein.

The Virgin Mary is moved to this beautiful sermon, about God and His grace and how we are to prepare ourselves for it, by her kinswoman Elizabeth, because Elizabeth praises and extols her so highly, and calls her the blessed, or most renowned, among women. Yes, says the Virgin Mary, it is indeed true: God has shown me an especially great grace, in which I must rejoice from my heart, and for which I must praise and thank God; for I am but a poor, lowly, insignificant girl. But, dear Elizabeth, although this great grace has befallen me alone, yet God will be merciful and gracious not to me alone but to all men, if only they fear Him and apply themselves to His word and will. But whoever will not fear God, and, as is the perversity of men, would comfort himself with his wisdom, his power or his riches, and pride himself on them, him God will punish without any grace, so that such people become fools, perish and suffer want. Therefore let everyone learn to fear God, that is, to hear God's word and follow it; then there shall be no danger, although we are poor, wretched, despised and unhappy. God well knows how to lift us up and make us rich. And at last this grace too shall follow: that God will let such God-fearing hearts come to the promise made to Abraham, and save them eternally through His Son, Jesus Christ. So the Virgin Mary teaches that we are to fear God, and guard ourselves most diligently against pride, as against the abomination that God cannot endure; then God will be with us with temporal and eternal blessing.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is the Magnificat, Luke 1:46–55, in King James wording. Luther and Dietrich put the second half in the present tense ("He sheweth strength … He putteth down the mighty"), where the King James Version has the past; the King James wording is kept.
- **Order in the book.** The Visitation (July 2) is printed after Mary Magdalene (July 22) (Appendix D, item 6).
- **"Kinswoman."** *Mume* is an aunt or female relative; the King James Version calls Elizabeth Mary's "cousin" (Luke 1:36).
- **"The blessed … among women."** Luke 1:42. *gebenedeyte* (from Latin *benedicta*) is glossed by Dietrich as *berümbtiste*, "most renowned."
- **"Unhappy."** *hartselig*, the opposite of *glückselig*.
- **"The promise made to Abraham."** *verheyssung Abrahe* (Latin genitive), taking up Luke 1:55.
- **"The abomination."** Compare Proverbs 16:5, "Every one that is proud in heart is an abomination to the LORD."

**Prayer (PDF pp. 275–276)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/Wir dancken dir für alle deine wolthat/leiblich vnnd geystlich/die du so reychlich [catchword "reichlich"] vns hast widerfaren lassen/Vnd bitten dich/behüte für hoffart/vnd sicherheyt/das wir nicht in vndanck vnd sünd gerathen/vnnd dein huld verlieren/wie die liebe Junckfraw Maria drowet/dz weder weißheyt/gewalt noch gelt die helffen sol/die dich nicht förchten/Gib aber vns ein solches hertz/das für vnnd für in deiner forcht bleybe/vnnd an deinem wort hange/auf das dein segen bey vns bleybe/vnd wir durch dein gnad ewig selig werden/AMEN.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, we thank Thee for all Thy benefits, bodily and spiritual, which Thou hast so richly bestowed upon us; and we beseech Thee, keep us from pride and carnal security, that we fall not into ingratitude and sin, and lose Thy favor; as the dear Virgin Mary threateneth, that neither wisdom, power, nor money shall help them that fear Thee not. But give us such a heart as may abide evermore in Thy fear, and cleave unto Thy word, that Thy blessing may remain with us, and we through Thy grace obtain eternal salvation; *through Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Supplied object.** *behüte* has no object in the German; "us" is supplied, as TLHy does with the same construction at Epiphany 5.
- **"Threateneth."** *drowet* (*dräuet*) means "threatens." It reads the Magnificat (Luke 1:51–53) as a threat against the proud, the mighty and the rich, here wisdom, power and money.

#### [93] St. Mary Magdalene (July 22)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 267):** *Am tag Marie Magdalene/welches Fest wir am 7. Sontag nach Trinitatis halten/Lu. 7.* [On the Day of Mary Magdalene, which feast we keep on the Seventh Sunday after Trinity. Luke 7.] In the 1548 book this section (pp. 267–271) is printed before the Visitation.

**Verse (PDF p. 268).** The heading stands at the foot of p. 267; the woodcut heads p. 268.

> DAs Weyb tratt hinden zu des Herren Jesu füssen/vnnd weynet/Vnnd fieng an seine füsse zu netzen mit threnen/vnd mit den haren jres haubts zu trücknen/vnd küsset seine füß/vnnd salbet sie mit köstlichem wasser. Vnd er sprach zu jr/Dir sind deine sünd vergeben/Dein glaub hat dir geholffen/Gehe hin mit fride.

The woman stood behind at the Lord Jesus' feet, weeping, and began to wash his feet with tears, and did wipe them with the hairs of her head, and kissed his feet, and anointed them with precious water. And he said unto her, Thy sins are forgiven. Thy faith hath helped thee; go in peace.

**Summary (PDF pp. 269–270)**

> DEr Euangelist sagt/diß weyblein sey ein sünderin aewest [sic, for gewest]/Weil nun der herr gm [sic, for am] ende jr disen trefflichē trost gibt/vnd sagt/Deine sünd sind dir vergeben/Jst diß die fürnembste vnnd beste lehre/das wir an jr sehen vnd lernen/wie sie zu vergebung der sünden sey kommen. Nu sehen wir aber/das sie mer nit thut/denn das sie zu dem Herrn Christo kombt/für jm sich demütiget/vnd bitterlich weynet/mit dem vertrawen/(wie denn der Herr hie klar anzeigt/weyl er sagt/Dein glaub hat dir geholffen) er werde jr gnedig sein/vnnd hilff beweisen. Diß ist der weg/welchen wir auch gehn müssen/wenn wir vergebung der sünden begeren. Jm Bapstumb hat man gelehrt/man müsse beichten/vnd für die sünde mit sonderlichen wercken gnugthun/der heyligen fürbit vnd verdienst suchen/vñ kauffen/ꝛc. Aber es ist eytel lügen vñ verfürung. Das sol anfenglich sein/das deine sünd dir hertzlich leyd sind/ [p. 270] vnnd du gentzlich im willen habest/in sünden lenger nicht zuuerharren/sonder dich mit Gottes hilff zu besseren. Solches sihest an disem Weyblin hie/Denn das hefftige weynen vnd seufftzen/kombt allein daher/das sie gern von sünden ledig wer. Darnach finde dich zu dem Herrn Christo Jhesu mit festem vertrawen/er werde sich deiner not annemen/vnd dir helffen/wie er darumb Jesus vñ ein Heyland heyst/das er vns in sünd vnnd todt nit will lassen stecken/Das heyst aber den Herrn Christum suchen vnd finden/wenn du sein wort mit glauben annimbst/vnd wie er dir in der tauff/im Abendmal/vnd sonst zusagt/durch seinen tod vergebung der sünden hoffest. Es soll aber auch dise frucht folgen/das du in Gottes forcht dich haltest/vnnd für sünden bewarest/denn sonst wo du in sünden woltest fort faren/würde es kein rechter ernst mit der rewe sein. Wo du aber auß schwacheyt in sünde fellest/solt du durch die Buß widerkeren/vnnd von sünden ablassen.

The evangelist says that this woman had been a sinner. Now since at the end the Lord gives her this excellent comfort, and says, "Thy sins are forgiven," the chief and best teaching is this: that we see and learn from her how she came to the forgiveness of sins. Now we see that she does no more than come to the Lord Christ, humble herself before Him, and weep bitterly, with the confidence (as the Lord clearly shows here, when He says, "Thy faith hath helped thee") that He will be gracious to her and show her help. This is the way that we too must go when we desire the forgiveness of sins. In the papacy it was taught that one must confess, make satisfaction for sin with special works, and seek and buy the intercession and merit of the saints, etc. But that is nothing but lies and seduction. The beginning is to be this: that your sins grieve you heartily, and that you are wholly resolved not to continue in sins any longer, but to amend yourself with God's help. This you see in this woman here; for her violent weeping and sighing comes only from this, that she would gladly be free from sins. Next, betake yourself to the Lord Christ Jesus with firm confidence that He will take your need to heart and help you, as He is called Jesus and a Savior for this very reason, that He will not leave us stuck fast in sin and death. And that is to seek and find the Lord Christ: when you receive His word with faith, and hope for the forgiveness of sins through His death, as He promises it to you in baptism, in the Supper, and elsewhere. But this fruit too is to follow: that you keep yourself in the fear of God and guard yourself against sins; for otherwise, if you would go on in sins, there would be no true earnestness in your repentance. But if you fall into sin out of weakness, you are to return through repentance and leave off sins.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 7:38, 48 and 50, introduced by "The woman," in King James wording, with "helped thee" for "saved thee," as at Quinquagesima ([23]), because the summary takes up the word. Where Luther and the King James Version have "anointed them with the ointment" (*mit Salben*), Dietrich has *mit köstlichem wasser*, "with precious water," a perfumed water; it is translated as he has it.
- **The feast.** The heading says that the feast of Mary Magdalene is kept on the Seventh Sunday after Trinity (Appendix D, item 6). Dietrich, like the Gospel of the day, identifies her with the sinful woman of Luke 7:36–50.
- **"Make satisfaction."** *gnugthun*: satisfaction, with contrition and confession, was one of the three parts of penance in the medieval system. Dietrich instead names the two parts of repentance of the Augsburg Confession, Article XII: contrition ("that your sins grieve you heartily") and faith ("Next, betake yourself to the Lord Christ … with firm confidence").
- **"Jesus and a Saviour."** Compare Matthew 1:21, as at New Year ([8]).
- **"Weakness."** *schwacheyt*, as printed (for *schwachheyt*).
- **Misprints.** On consecutive lines of p. 269 the a and the g have changed places: *aewest* for *gewest* and *gm* for *am*. In *annemen* (p. 270) the second n is damaged and looks like *ni*.

**Prayer (PDF p. 271)**

> O Jesu du Sun des lebendigen Gottes/der du vmb der sünder willen auff erden kom̃en/vnd am creutz für sie bist gestorben/Wir bitten dich/gib deinen heyligen Geyst inn vnsere hertzen/das er rechte rew vnser sünden in vns/vnnd vestes vertrawen auff dein verdienst vnd gnad anrichte/auff das wir vns wider die sünd vnd böses gewissen/auffhalten/vnnd mit der lieben Magdalena/durch den glauben/im fride hingehen/dich loben/preysen/vnd lieben in ewigkeyt/Amen.

**New translation**

> O Jesus, Thou Son of the living God, who for sinners' sake didst come upon earth, and didst die for them upon the cross: We beseech Thee, give Thy Holy Ghost into our hearts, that He may work in us true contrition for our sins, and firm trust in Thy merit and grace; that we may uphold ourselves against sin and an evil conscience, and with the beloved Magdalene, through faith, go in peace, and praise, glorify, and love Thee for ever, *Thou who livest and reignest with the Father and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Address and ending.** The prayer is addressed to Christ. Its opening follows TLHy's Trinity 5 pattern, and so does the ending, TLHy's form for a prayer to Christ (§2.4e).
- **Rendering choices.** *im fride hingehen* echoes Luke 7:50 ("go in peace"). *vns … auffhalten* means to uphold ourselves.

#### [94] St. James the Elder, Apostle (July 25)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 276):** *An Sanct Jacobs tage/Marci am 10.* [On St. James's Day. Mark 10.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 277–278).** The heading stands alone on p. 276; the woodcut heads p. 277.

> DEr Herr Jesus rieffe seinen jüngern zu sich/vnd sprach: Jr wisset/das die weltlichen Fürsten herrschen/vnd die Oberherrn haben gewalt/so soll es nit sein vnter euch/Sonder so yemandt vnter euch will gewaltig sein/der sey ewer Diener/vnd wer da will der fürnemest sein/ [p. 278] der sey ewer knecht. Gleych wie des menschen Son ist nit komen/das er jm dienen lasse/sonder das er diene/vnd gebe sein leben zu einer erlösung für vile.

The Lord Jesus called His disciples to Him, and said: Ye know that the worldly princes rule, and the overlords have power. But so shall it not be among you: but whosoever will be mighty among you, shall be your minister: and whosoever will be the chiefest, shall be your servant. For even the Son of man came not to be ministered unto, but to minister, and to give his life a ransom for many.

**Summary (PDF pp. 278–279)**

> DJse lehr gehet fürnemlich auff die kirchendiener/das die selben für diser anfechtung sich hütten/vnd nach gewalt vñ zeitlicher pracht nit sollen streben/Solchs gebüret denen/so inn weltlichen embtern sindt/Denn die müssen gewalt vnnd macht haben/das sie dem vnrecht vnnd ergernussen wehren/vnd jre vnterthanen befriden. Aber wem das predigampt befolhen ist/der gedenck/es müsse gedienet sein/vnd nit allein gedienet/sonder auch drüber gelitten/Deñ die welt gibt keinen andern lon. Dem wort/vnd denen so es bekennen vnd füren/ist sie feind/vñ kans noch wils nit dulden. Das sollen alle Prediger vnd kirchendiener sich erwegen/vnd auff dise fahr wol bedacht vnd gerüstet [p. 279] sein. Wer aber/wie der Bapst/Cardinal vnd Bischoffe/Pfaffen vnd München/vnd in summa/das gantze Bapsthumb durch auß/das predig oder kirchenampt zu seiner herrligkeit gewalt vnd reichthumb mißbrauchet/der ist Christus Jünger nicht/Denn hie stehets: So soll es nicht sein vnter euch/das ist/Jr solt knecht vnd nicht Herrn sein/vnd weñ jr auff das trewlichest gedienet habt/solt jr allen vndanck von der argen welt gewarten. Ob nun solches wehe thut/so sehet hieher auff mich/wie nicht allein ich diene/sonder auch drüber leyde/vnd leib vñ leben vber solchem dienst lasse/das euch geholffen vnnd gerathen werde. Solches Exempel bedencket/ob gleich jr andern leuten dienet/das ich euch vnd aller welt auch gedienet/vnd mit meinem todt euch von sünde vnd todt erlöset habe. O ein hertzliche/schöne warnung vnd vermanung/Gott gebe/das wirs alle/sonderlich aber die kirchendiener zu hertzen nemen/vnd für hoffart/geytz/neyd/vnd anderm vns hüten.

This teaching applies chiefly to the ministers of the Church: that they are to guard themselves against this temptation, and are not to strive after power and temporal pomp. That befits those who are in secular offices; for they must have power and might, so that they may restrain wrong and offenses and keep their subjects in peace. But whoever has been entrusted with the office of preaching, let him consider that he must serve, and not only serve but also suffer for it; for the world gives no other wage. It is the enemy of the word and of those who confess it and carry it on, and neither can nor will tolerate it. All preachers and ministers of the Church are to make up their minds to this, and be well prepared and armed against this danger. But whoever, like the Pope, the cardinals and bishops, priests and monks, and in sum the whole papacy throughout, misuses the office of preaching or of the Church for his own glory, power and riches, is no disciple of Christ. For here it stands: "So shall it not be among you"; that is, you are to be servants and not lords, and when you have served most faithfully, you are to expect all manner of ingratitude from the wicked world. Now if that hurts, then look here at Me: how I not only serve, but also suffer for it, and give up body and life in this service, so that you may be helped and provided for. Consider this example: although you serve other people, I too have served you and all the world, and by My death redeemed you from sin and death. O a hearty, beautiful warning and exhortation! God grant that we all, but especially the ministers of the Church, take it to heart, and guard ourselves against pride, covetousness, envy and the like.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Mark 10:42–45, from the Gospel of the sons of Zebedee, in King James wording, introduced by "The Lord Jesus called His disciples to Him." Where Dietrich differs from the King James Version in ways the summary takes up, he is followed: "the worldly princes rule, and the overlords have power" (King James "they which are accounted to rule over the Gentiles exercise lordship over them; and their great ones exercise authority upon them"); "mighty" (*gewaltig*) for "great"; and "your servant" for "servant of all."
- **The woodcut** (p. 277) shows a woman kneeling with two young sons before Christ, who holds a cup: the mother of Zebedee's children (Matthew 20:20–22).
- **"Look here at Me."** Dietrich again slips into Christ's own voice (compare [29]).
- **"Make up their minds to."** *sich erwegen*, as at Epiphany 4 ([18]).
- **"Priests and monks."** *Pfaffen vnd München*, as at Exaudi ([42]) and Trinity 23 ([69]).

**Prayer (PDF p. 280)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/Es thut vns das creutze sehr wehe/vnd wolten sein gern vberhoben sein. Aber du hast erstlich deins eingebornen Sons selb nicht verschonet/der hat durchs Creutz vnd leiden zu seiner herrligkeyt müssen kom̃en/vnnd wilt/das wir Christen alle solchem Hertzogen nachgehen/vnd jhm gleych sollen werden/darumb bitten wir dein grundlose barmhertzigkeyt/verleyhe durch dein heyligen Geyst/das wir im leyden nicht kleynmütig/noch vngedultig werdē/sonder durch die hoffnung der künfftigen herrligkeyt/alles vnglück mit starckem glauben vberwinden/AMEN.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, the cross grieveth us sore, and we would gladly be spared it. But Thou first didst not spare Thine own only-begotten Son, who must needs come to His glory through the cross and suffering; and Thou willest that all we Christians should follow such a Captain and be made like unto Him. Therefore we beseech Thy fathomless mercy, grant by Thy Holy Ghost that in suffering we become neither fainthearted nor impatient, but through the hope of the glory to come overcome all misfortune with strong faith; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Scripture echoes.** *Hertzog* is "Captain," as in Hebrews 2:10 (Luther: "Herzog ihrer Seligkeit"; King James: "captain of their salvation"). *müssen kommen* echoes Luke 24:26, and *nicht verschonet* echoes Romans 8:32.

### August to October

#### [95] St. Bartholomew, Apostle (August 24)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 281):** *An Sanct Bartholomeus tag/Luce am 22.* [On St. Bartholomew's Day. Luke 22.]

**Verse (PDF p. 282).** The heading and a woodcut fill p. 281.

> CHristus sprach zu seinen jüngern: Jch will euch das Reich bescheyden/wie mir mein Vatter beschiden hat/das jr essen vñ trincken solt/vber meinem tisch in meinem Reych/vnd sitzen auff stülen/vnnd richten die zwölff geschlecht Jsrael.

Christ said unto His disciples: I appoint unto you a kingdom, as my Father hath appointed unto me; that ye may eat and drink at my table in my kingdom, and sit on thrones, and judge the twelve tribes of Israel.

**Summary (PDF pp. 282–284)**

> DJß Euangelion ist fast ein meinung mit dem nechsten an Sant Jacobs tag/Deñ es erhube sich am letzten Abendmal ein zanck vnter den Jüngern/da sie höreten/wie der Herr würde von jhnen weg gehen/welcher alß deñ solte für den grösten gehalten werden/auff den die anderen all ein auffsehen hetten/vnd nach seim befelh sich hielten/Aber der herr gibt jn disen beschid/Herrschen gehöre weltlichen Königen vnnd Herren zu/jr aber/spricht er/nit also/sonder welcher vnter euch wil der fürnemest sein/soll sein wie ein diener. Nach solchem wort gedencke doch/ob auch der [p. 283] Bapst sich halte/der ein herr vber alle Kirchen sein/vnd allein die macht haben will sünde zuuergeben? Denn der schlüssel masset er sich allein an/alß könne die niemand habē noch brauchen/er habe sie deñ von jm/So doch der Herr alle seine Apostel gleich machet/vnd will keinen für den andern ziehen/all zumal sollen sie dienen/vnd keiner herrschen/noch der fürnemest sein. Wer nun solches befelhs mit fleiß außwartet/der soll sein belonung dafür empfahen nit hie auff erden/sonder in dem ewigen leben. Denn spricht der Herr: Jch beschide euch das reych wie mirs mein Vatter beschiden hat/das ist/Jr solt herrn sein vñ regiern/aber nit auff erden/ewer herrschafft soll in eim bessern leben sein/Denn also hat mein Vatter das reich mir auch bescheyden/das ich hie auff erden dienen vnd leyden/vnd durch vil trübsal hab zur herrligkeyt sollen eingehen. Da seyt nun mit zu friden/das jr wie meine nechste/liebste freund essen vnd trincken solt vber meinem tisch/ [p. 284] das ist/es sol euch gehn wie mir/Deñ jr füret eben das ampt das ich füre/Auff erden hie solt jr dienen/vñ euch leyden/Nach disem leben aber solt jr ewige freud vnd herrligkeyt haben.

This Gospel has almost the same meaning as the last, on St. James's Day. For at the Last Supper a dispute arose among the disciples, when they heard that the Lord was going to go away from them, which of them should then be accounted the greatest, the one to whom all the others would look and by whose command they would conduct themselves. But the Lord gives them this answer: ruling belongs to worldly kings and lords; "but ye," He says, "shall not be so: but whoever among you would be the chiefest, let him be as he that doth serve." After such a word, just consider whether the Pope too abides by it, who would be lord over all churches and alone have the power to forgive sins. For he claims the keys for himself alone, as if no one could have or use them unless he had them from him; whereas the Lord makes all His apostles equal, and will prefer none above another: all alike are to serve, and none is to rule or be the chiefest. Whoever now attends diligently to this command shall receive his reward for it, not here on earth, but in eternal life. For the Lord says: "I appoint unto you the kingdom, as my Father hath appointed it unto me"; that is, you are to be lords and to reign, but not on earth: your lordship shall be in a better life. For so My Father has appointed the kingdom to Me too: that here on earth I should serve and suffer, and through much tribulation enter into glory. Be content, then, with this: that, as My nearest and dearest friends, you are to eat and drink at My table; that is, it shall go with you as it goes with Me. For you carry on the very office that I carry on. Here on earth you are to serve and suffer; but after this life you are to have eternal joy and glory.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Luke 22:29–30 in King James wording, introduced by "Christ said unto His disciples." *bescheyden* is "appoint" (as by will or decree).
- **"The last, on St. James's Day."** *dem nechsten*, the section immediately before, on Mark 10:42–45 ([94]).
- **The dispute.** Luke 22:24–27, "there was also a strife among them, which of them should be accounted the greatest … he that is chief, as he that doth serve," the opening of this Gospel.
- **The keys.** Dietrich denies the papal claim to sole possession of the keys: Christ gave them to all the apostles (Matthew 18:18; John 20:23; see Easter 1, [36]).
- **"Through much tribulation."** Acts 14:22.
- **"Be content with this."** *Da seyt nun mit zu friden*, where *mit* stands for *damit*. Dietrich again speaks in Christ's voice.

**Prayer (PDF p. 284)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/du hast dein kirch mit trewen Dienern besetzet/das sie dein wort füren/vnd yederman durch jren dienst dahin sollen bringē/das sie dich erkennen/in deiner forcht wandlen/vñ durch Christum selig werden/Wir bitten dich/du wöllest mit deinem heiligen Geyst alle solche Kirchendiener regieren/das sie jres diensts trewlich warten/Vnd durch geytz/hoffart/oder anders/vom Sathan nicht bethöret/noch in jhrem dienst laß vnnd faul werden/auff das dein reych gemehret/vnnd des Sathans zerstöret werde/Amen.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, Thou hast furnished Thy Church with faithful servants, who should bear Thy word, and by their ministry bring every man to know Thee, to walk in Thy fear, and through Christ to be saved: We beseech Thee, govern all such servants of the Church by Thy Holy Ghost, that they may faithfully wait on their ministry, and be neither beguiled by Satan through covetousness, pride, or aught else, nor grow slack and slothful in their ministry; that Thy kingdom may be increased, and that of Satan destroyed; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Notes**
- **Rendering choices.**
  - *Dienern … Kirchendiener* are both given as "servants," to keep Dietrich's link. TLHy renders *Kirchendiener* as "pastors" at the Ascension.
  - *trewlich warten* is "faithfully wait on" (cf. Romans 12:7, King James).
  - *bethöret* means befooled or beguiled.
- **Date.** Stratman dates this feast August 25; the traditional date is August 24.

[↑ Index](#3-index-of-the-collects)

#### [96] St. Matthew, Apostle and Evangelist (September 21)

**Dietrich's heading (PDF p. 285):** *An Sanct Mattheus tag/Matthei am 9.* [On St. Matthew's Day. Matthew 9.]

**Verse (PDF pp. 285–286)**

> DER Herr Jhesus sprach: Die starcken bedürffen des Artztes nicht/sonder die krancken. Gehet aber hin/vnd lernet was da sey/Jch hab wolgefallen an barmhertzigkeyt [p. 286]/vnd nit am opffer/Jch bin kommen die sünder zur Busse zu rüffen/vnd nicht die gerechten.

The Lord Jesus said: They that be whole need not a physician, but they that are sick. But go ye and learn what that meaneth, I will have mercy, and not sacrifice: I am come to call sinners to repentance, and not the righteous.

**Summary (PDF pp. 286–288)**

> ZV diser predigt verursachen den Herrn Christum die Phariseer/deñ sie deuteten es jm vbel/das er mit den Zolnern gemeinschafft hette. Aber er will darumb vngestraffet sein/vñ recht haben/Denn/spricht er/Jch bin ein Artzt wider die sünde. Gleych nun wie ein leybes Artzet zu krancken leuten gehn/vñ vmb krancke leut seines ampts halb sol sein. Also ist es mein ampt vmb die sünder zu sein/das ich sie zur busse rüffe/vnd sie von sünden ledig mache. Dise predigt mercke mit höchstem fleyß/Denn vnser gröste anfechtung/sorg vnd kümmernuß ist/das wir der sünden halb/vns für Got förchten. So du nun ein sünder bist/was wiltu thun? Wilt du verzweiffeln? O nein/Denn höre hie/man hat einen artzt wider die sünde/Wer ist der? Es ist der Son Gottes [p. 287] vnser lieber Herr Christus. Was brauchet er für ein artzney? Dise/das er zur busse rüffet/Das ist/er vermanet wir sollen Got förchten/in sünden nit sicher fort faren/Gottes zorn vnd vrteyl bedencken/vñ von sünden ablassen/Vnnd darnach vns des trösten/das er/der Sone Gottes für vns am Creutz sich selb auffopffert/vnnd für vnsere sünde mit seim leben bezalet. Wer diß bedes thut/dem ist von sünden geholffen. Du must aber nit allein solche kranckheit erkeñen/sonder auch dem artzt folgen/vñ nit thun/wie die vnleidlichen/vngefölgigen krancken/was jn der artz gibt/wöllen sie nicht. Essen aber vñ trincken was jnen am schedlichsten ist/Dafür hüte dich/folge deinē trewen artzet/Ob es dir wehe thut/das du von der vnzucht/dem geytz/dem zorn/vnd andern sünden der du gewonet hast/solt ablassen/da ligt nit an/Gedenck aber/das gewißlich der ewige todt würde folgen/wo du in sünden woltest fort faren/solche fahr laß dich bewegen/das du disem [p. 288] artzt folgest/vnnd dem ewigen todt entrinnest.

The Pharisees give the Lord Christ occasion for this sermon, for they took it ill of Him that He had fellowship with the publicans. But He will not be rebuked for it, and will have the right on His side. For, He says, I am a physician against sin. Now as a physician of the body is to go to sick people, and by reason of his office is to be among sick people, so it is My office to be among sinners, that I may call them to repentance and set them free from sins. Mark this sermon with the utmost diligence. For our greatest temptation, care and distress is that on account of our sins we are afraid of God. If, then, you are a sinner, what will you do? Will you despair? O no! For hear this: there is a physician against sin. Who is He? It is the Son of God, our dear Lord Christ. What medicine does He use? This: that He calls to repentance; that is, He exhorts us to fear God, not to go on securely in sins, to consider God's wrath and judgment, and to leave off sins; and then to comfort ourselves with this, that He, the Son of God, offers Himself up for us on the cross and pays for our sins with His life. Whoever does both of these is helped from his sins. But you must not only acknowledge this sickness, but also follow the physician, and not do as intractable, disobedient patients do, who will not take what the physician gives them, but eat and drink what is most harmful to them. Guard yourself against that; follow your faithful physician. If it hurts you to leave off unchastity, covetousness, anger and the other sins to which you have grown accustomed, that does not matter. But consider that eternal death would surely follow if you would go on in sins. Let this danger move you to follow this physician and escape eternal death.

**Notes on the verse and summary**
- **The verse** is Matthew 9:12–13 in King James wording, introduced by "The Lord Jesus said," with Dietrich's order in the last sentence. *starcken* ("strong") is the King James "whole"; Luther's "I have pleasure in mercy" (*Jch hab wolgefallen an barmhertzigkeyt*) is the King James "I will have mercy."
- **The heading.** "Matthei am 9." is correct here: the Gospel is the call of Matthew, Matthew 9:9–13.
- **"Fellowship with the publicans."** Matthew 9:10–11: "Why eateth your Master with publicans and sinners?"
- **"Intractable, disobedient."** *vnleidlichen*, insufferable, impatient; *vngefölgigen*, not following orders.
- **"That does not matter."** *da ligt nit an*.

**Prayer (PDF p. 288)**

> HErr Got himlischer Vatter/du hast vnns durch dein liebes wort/zu deynem Reych beruffen/da vergebung der sünden/vñ alle genad vnd barmhertzigkeyt jnne ist/Jch bitte dich/du wöllest durch deinen heyligen Geyst/vnsere hertzen erwecken/vnnd erleuchten/das wir/wie Mattheus der Zolner/solchen beruff annemen/von sünden ablassen/vnd vns bessern/vnnd aller gnad durch deinen Son Christum Jesum/vns zu dir versehen/vnd also endlich selig werden/AMEN.

**New translation**

> Lord God, heavenly Father, Thou hast called us by Thy dear word into Thy kingdom, wherein is forgiveness of sins and all grace and mercy: I beseech Thee, quicken and enlighten our hearts by Thy Holy Ghost, that we, like Matthew the publican, may accept such a call, forsake sin, and amend our lives, and look to Thee for all grace through Thy Son, Jesus Christ, and so at last be saved; *through the same, Thy beloved Son, Jesus Christ our Lord, who liveth and reigneth with Thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, world without end.* Amen.

**Congregational form (TLHy practice):** Read "We beseech Thee" for "I beseech Thee"; the rest is unchanged.

**Notes**
- **Mixed number.** The German mixes "Jch bitte dich" with "vnsere hertzen … das wir"; it is kept as printed.
- **Rendering choices.** *erwecken* is TLHy's "quicken" (Trinity 3). *aller gnad … vns zu dir versehen* means "to look to Thee for all grace" (*sich zu jemandem einer Sache versehen*, to expect something confidently from someone).
